# LP64-VEHICLE-CLEAN  2026-09-01  ★★ FIVE OF EIGHT GAMETYPES RUN THEIR FULL
#                     150 s AT x86-64, AND THE THREE THAT DO NOT ARE THE ENGINE'S
#                     UI, NOT KARMA. The named open item — McdSphylTriangleList-
#                     Intersect -> GenerateTriangleContact, 6 of 8 gametypes and
#                     the arm64 device at 140 s — is CLOSED, and closing it
#                     uncovered four more, each of which was the next crash.
#
#                     (1) THE OUTGOING ARGUMENT AREA BELOW AN `alloca`, REACHED
#                     THROUGH A CURSOR. `fix_frame_slots`' fourth spelling.
#                         kd_alloca_iVar3 = (char *)alloca(n * sizeof(McdUserTriangle) + 0);
#                         puVar12 = (undefined1 *)kd_alloca_iVar3;
#                         *(int *)    (puVar12 + -0x20) = (kd_iptr)&tri;
#                         *(MeReal **)(puVar12 + -0x1c) = relPos;
#                         GenerateTriangleContact(*(McdUserTriangle **)(puVar12 + -0x20), ...);
#                     TWO defects at LP64: the slots OVERLAP (-0x1c and -0x20 are
#                     four bytes apart and hold eight-byte pointers), and `tri` is
#                     STORED through `*(int *)` and READ through
#                     `*(McdUserTriangle **)`. The callee dereferences
#                     `tri->vertices[0]` — the SEGV_MAPERR, digit for digit.
#                     ★ THE STORAGE IS THE HARD HALF. Scaling the offsets alone
#                     doubles the area to 72 bytes BELOW a block whose base is
#                     where the allocation starts, which is nobody's memory at
#                     either width. So the allocation grows by the scaled area and
#                     the base walks up past it, on ONE statement:
#                         alloca(SIZE)  ->  alloca(SIZE + PAD) + PAD
#                         PAD = (int)(sizeof(void *) / 8) * (9 * (int)sizeof(void *))
#                     `4 / 8` is ZERO in integer arithmetic, so PAD is 0 at i386
#                     AND on wasm32 and the statement folds back to `alloca(SIZE)
#                     + 0` on every shipping target. In the artefact:
#                     `add $0x48,%rax`, twice.
#                     ⚠ AND THE TYPE GROUP HAS TO ADMIT ITS OWN `*`s. Written
#                     `[\w ]*?` the site pattern matches `*(int *)` and NOT
#                     `*(McdUserTriangle **)`, so the pass saw the four-byte
#                     stores, missed every pointer load, concluded no slot carried
#                     a pointer and widened nothing — the one access the rule
#                     exists for. Zero sites, NO decline: a group that never forms
#                     has nothing to decline.
#
#                     (2) THE SAME FUNCTION'S TRIANGLE STRIDE, and the evidence
#                     for it is another pass's own conclusion.
#                     `local_5c + 0x18` is sizeof(McdUserTriangle) at i386 and 48
#                     at LP64. `fix_element_stride` could not see the file:
#                     `IxSphylPrimitives` walks its triangles through BYTE cursors
#                     so `fix_word_indexed_struct` has nothing to type in it. But
#                     the file carries `alloca(n * (int)sizeof(*(McdUserTriangle
#                     *)0))`, which `fix_baked_sizeof --field-allocas-only` wrote
#                     ONLY because the field the block is stored into is declared
#                     `McdUserTriangle *`. That is a measured conclusion, so it
#                     counts as evidence.
#                     ⚠⚠ AND IT LICENCES EXACTLY ONE RULE — MEASURED, NOT
#                     ASSUMED. Run without that restriction the table/cursor
#                     fixpoint flooded to FIFTY names and rules B and D rewrote
#                     TWELVE READS OF A TRANSFORM MATRIX:
#                         *(float *)((kd_iptr)pvVar7 + 0x30)   /* translation row */
#                      -> *(float *)((kd_iptr)pvVar7 + 2 * (int)sizeof(McdUserTriangle))
#                     0x30 is 48 is 2*24, so it survives "whole elements only"; at
#                     LP64 it becomes 96 and reads off the end of the transform.
#                     ★ ALL SIXTEEN SITES PASSED THE i386 BYTE-IDENTITY GATE,
#                     because 2*sizeof(E) IS 0x30 there. FIFTH time 32-bit
#                     byte-identity could not see a semantic change at 64 — the
#                     rule is only ever as wide as the evidence, and the gate
#                     cannot narrow it for you.
#
#                     (3) `KPerContactCB`, IN ALL SIX REMAINING GAMETYPES AT ONE
#                     ADDRESS, and it is what (1) made reachable.
#                     `fix_member_base_walk.py` is new. gcc keeps the contact
#                     array in a register pointing at the first element's
#                     `normal`, not at the element:
#                         *(...)((kd_iptr)result->contacts->normal + cur + 0x18)
#                     `McdContact` i386 sizeof 40, normal 12, element1 32,
#                     element2 36; LP64 sizeof 48, normal 12, element1 32,
#                     element2 **40**. So `+ 0x18` is byte 36, which is `element2`
#                     here and the HIGH HALF OF `element1` there — and the write is
#                     eight bytes wide, so it lands across two fields.
#                     ⚠⚠ THE OBVIOUS DECOMPOSITION IS WRONG IN A WAY i386 CANNOT
#                     SHOW YOU. `fix_element_stride`'s rule D reads a literal as
#                     `q*sizeof(E) + offsetof(member at L % sizeof(E))`; L is 24
#                     and byte 24 of McdContact is `separation`, a REAL member, at
#                     24 at BOTH widths. That compiles to the same 24 it replaced,
#                     passes the gate, and is still four bytes short at LP64. The
#                     remainder has to be taken from `b + L`, where b is the offset
#                     of the member the chain is ROOTED at.
#                     ★ ONLY REWRITE WHAT MOVES — 4 of the 8 offset sites. The
#                     same shape carries `+ 0xc` (byte 24, `separation`) and
#                     `+ 0x14` (byte 32, `element1`) and BOTH are already right.
#                     `McdBatch.c` writes `+ 0x14` and `+ 0x18` four lines apart.
#                     ⚠ AND THE LITERAL IS OPTIONAL: `McdGjk` and
#                     `McdPlaneIntersect` have no offset that moves at all — their
#                     only defect is `cur = cur + 0x28`, walking 40 bytes over
#                     48-byte elements. A rule that needed an offset to move before
#                     it looked at the stride skipped both and reported nothing.
#                     `McdPlaneIntersect` advances its cursor inside the loop's own
#                     CONDITION, so a `^...;$` pattern finds it not at all.
#
#                     (4) ★★ A CALLBACK'S `void *` CONTEXT, AND NO STATIC GATE IN
#                     THIS PROJECT CAN SEE THE CLASS. `fix_callback_context.py` is
#                     new. `MePoolxDictNodeAllocate(void *pool)` addresses every
#                     field by a baked byte offset:
#                         MePoolx i386 mem 0 isize 4 numrec 8  numfree 12 ifree 16
#                                 LP64 mem 0 isize 8 numrec 12 numfree 16 ifree 20
#                     so `0xc` is `numrec` there, `0x10` is `numfree`, and `4` is
#                     the HIGH HALF OF `mem`. Nothing is truncated and no cast is
#                     narrowed: `MePoolx` is on NEITHER the 91 aarch64 diagnostics
#                     NOR the 52 open ones. Only running the game found it.
#                     ★ THE TOMBSTONE IS THE DEFECT, FIELD BY FIELD. gdb at the
#                     SIGSEGV in MeDictInsert <- MeSetAdd <- McdConvexMeshPlaneCut
#                     — a convex mesh being sliced, i.e. a VEHICLE:
#                         nodemem      = 0x7ffffffc66d0   (MeDictNode[200])
#                         set.nodepool = { mem = 0x7ffffffc66d0, isize = 12,
#                                          numrec = 196, numfree = 32767,
#                                          ifree = -235024 }
#                     `numrec` should be 200 and is 196, decremented four times by
#                     the code that means `numfree`. `numfree` should be 200 and is
#                     **32767** — the high half of a stack address, because
#                     `*(MeDictNode **)(pool + 0x10)` stores EIGHT bytes into a
#                     four-byte index. The node handed back is 0x...cc, which a
#                     MeDictNode can never be.
#                     THE TYPE IS DECLARED IN THE REGISTRATION —
#                     `MeDictSetAllocator(d, alloc, free, p)` — and ⚠ THERE ARE TWO
#                     CANDIDATES AND POSITION DOES NOT SAY WHICH. The tie-break is
#                     a measurement: every offset must land on a top-level member
#                     START at i386, and `MeDict` has none at 4.
#                     ⚠⚠ AND THE MEMBER SPELLING IS NOT BYTE-IDENTICAL, WHICH IS
#                     WHY THIS RE-SPELLS THE ADDRESS. `->numfree` alone is
#                     identical, `->ifree` alone is identical, and the TWO TOGETHER
#                     are not — gcc schedules the member form differently once both
#                     loads are visible as fields of one object. A typed local does
#                     not rescue it. The address form keeps every expression's TYPE
#                     and changes only how the address is computed.
#                     ⚠ AND THE GREEDY RETURN TYPE ATE THE NAME AGAIN:
#                     `static void MePoolxDictNodeDeallocate(` read as a function
#                     called `e`. Its sibling survived only because a `*` forced the
#                     split — so ONE callback was typed, the other silently was not,
#                     and no decline was printed. Same family as `aiStack_9cb0` ->
#                     `iStack_9cb0`. There is a self-check over both spellings now.
#
#                     (5) A FRAME SLOT'S MEMBER HOLDING A POINTER, and a SLOT
#                     STORED NARROWER THAN THE NEXT READ OF IT.
#                     `MStack_26c.flags = (McdTriangleFlags)result->normal;` —
#                     `McdUserTriangle::flags` is four bytes at BOTH widths, so the
#                     address is cut in half. `ptrwidth_classify`'s worst single
#                     object, `IxCylinderTriList` 6 of 6 UNEXPLAINED, on the
#                     ragdoll-vs-WORLD path.
#                     ★ THE REPAIR IS STORAGE, NOT A RE-SPELLING, AND THAT WAS
#                     MEASURED: rewriting the reads as `result->normal[1]` is EXACT
#                     — the line above them already does — and is NOT
#                     byte-identical, with or without the now-dead store. Its own
#                     `kd_iptr` local IS.
#                     ⚠ AND GROUPING BY ADDRESS WOULD BE WRONG for the sixth rule:
#                     an argument slot is REUSED, so the same address carries an
#                     `int` for one call and a pointer for the next. The pairing is
#                     a STORE and the NEXT READ with no store between. 6 sites, one
#                     of them 22 in `MeFAssetCreateCopy` — the ragdoll loader.
#
#                     (6) THE `alloca`'d ARRAY OF POINTERS, which
#                     `fix_baked_sizeof` DECLINED BY NAME. Its qsort rule needs a
#                     plain identifier and `MdtLOD`'s base travels in an argslot;
#                     it said so and was right — `void` has no size. The missing
#                     piece was the ELEMENT TYPE, and `fix_alloca_elem.py` finds it
#                     written down. `alloca(group->count * 4)` holding
#                     `MdtContactID`, and `qsort(..., 4, ...)` over it.
#                     ⚠⚠ AN ARGSLOT IS NOT EVIDENCE: `kd_argslot_ffffffa8` is cast
#                     to `MeHash **`, `MeFJoint **`, `char **`, `void **` AND
#                     `MeFAssetPart **` in ONE function. Taking the first cast off
#                     it chose `MeAssetFactory *`. The ORACLE is asked first —
#                     `MeFAssetGetPartsSortedByName(const MeFAsset *const,
#                     MeFAssetPart **)`.
#                     ⚠ AND `(\w+)\s*\(([^();]*)\)` CANNOT SEE THESE CALLS AT
#                     ALL — every argument is itself parenthesised, so the oracle
#                     evidence never fired and the pass fell through to Ghidra's
#                     guess with NO DECLINE PRINTED. Twice.
#                     ⚠ `* 4` IS NOT A CLASS: eight allocas carry a literal 4 and
#                     `keaMatrix_PcSparse_vanilla`'s `numClamped * 4` is an honest
#                     int array. Four bases are declined for want of a declared
#                     type.
#
#                     ★★ THE WEB ARTEFACT IS BYTE-IDENTICAL THROUGH ALL SIX,
#                     MEASURED UNDER CLANG RATHER THAN ARGUED: SDLLaunch.wasm is
#                     sha256 12d70e6c44d951af73d8acc407db15db95ebbcdbeaeb3642448ad
#                     2448444da07 before and after. `sizeof(void *) / 8` is 0 at
#                     wasm32, `-(k * sizeof(void *))` folds to the constant it
#                     replaced, `kd_iptr` IS `int`, and every `sizeof(E)` folds to
#                     the literal. The existing web stamp still covers the engine.
#
#                     ⚠⚠ AND A RED SWEEP IS STILL NOT EVIDENCE UNTIL THE BASELINE
#                     IS CHECKED — FIVE FOR FIVE NOW. DM-Rankin and DM-DE-Ironic
#                     die in `FCanvasVertex` <- `UCanvas::DrawTileStretched`, and
#                     THE 32-BIT CONTROL DIES IN THE SAME FUNCTION on the same two
#                     maps. That is the software renderer under Xvfb, which
#                     STATE.md already recorded from the i386 ktrace run. It is not
#                     Karma and it is not 64-bit.
#                     ⚠ AND THE ASSET TREE MOVED UNDER THIS SWEEP. A JB2004 map
#                     pack was installed on 2026-08-31, so "7 gametypes clean" and
#                     this run are not measurements of the same game. The 32-bit
#                     control is the only comparable baseline, and it is what says
#                     which failures are ours.
#
#                     ★★ arm64 PLAYS FOR AS LONG AS IT IS LEFT RUNNING — ONS-Dria
#                     Onslaught with bots, vehicles and ragdolls, **1397 s** and
#                     still going, 0 signals, against a 140 s ceiling before this.
#                     ⚠⚠ AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT THAT NUMBER WAS WRONG IN THIS
#                     PROJECT'S OWN RECORDED WAY. A player run read "9+ minutes
#                     signal-free" and the screenshot at 13 minutes was
#                     PIXEL-IDENTICAL to the one before it: the player had died,
#                     the Onslaught map GUI had opened, and `ULevel::Tick` guards
#                     Karma with `IsPaused()`. The logcat kept growing throughout.
#                     ★ HUD OUTPUT IS NOT EVIDENCE THE LEVEL IS RUNNING —
#                     test/README.md says so about ktrace_run.sh and it is just as
#                     true of a device run. The number above is a SPECTATOR
#                     (?SpectatorOnly=1, so nothing can open that GUI) with a
#                     two-screenshot frame-advance check at every 60 s sample.
#
#                     ★★ AND ktrace HAS FINALLY COMPARED THE TWO WIDTHS — the
#                     first 64-bit-vs-32-bit trajectory this project has ever had.
#                     test-karma-1, no bots, 200 frames, 8 bodies, 100% overlap:
#                         7 of 8 bodies never differ by more than 0.0001
#                         KBox_Light first differs at frame 82; rest Z
#                                    0.884 (i386) vs 0.879 (LP64)
#                         ktrace_score  rest 8/8  sleep 8/8  gone 0  ->  MATCH
#                     ⚠ The 5 mm is the float floor, not physics: the shipping
#                     32-bit build computes in x87 with excess precision and LP64
#                     in SSE, and the offline scenes already establish BIT-identity
#                     once -ffloat-store holds the arithmetic fixed.
#                     ⚠ AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT AT 600 FRAMES COULD NOT BE COMPARED:
#                     the **32-bit** side stopped at 223 (37%) in FCanvasVertex
#                     under Xvfb and ktrace_diff.py correctly refused a verdict
#                     below 90% overlap. Same renderer crash as the sweep, from a
#                     third direction — and note which side is short: the LP64 run
#                     captured all 600.
#
#                     aarch64 truncations 91 -> 87, UNEXPLAINED 52 -> 48: the
#                     MStack_26c.flags repair took IxCylinderTriList — the worst
#                     single object, 6 of 6 — off the list entirely. ⚠ That number
#                     is still not a measure of what is left: MePoolx WAS the
#                     vehicle crash and is on neither list.
#
#                     ★ AND armeabi-v7a IS THE ATTRIBUTION CONTROL, RUN AT LAST.
#                     `adb install --abi armeabi-v7a` puts the 32-bit slice on the
#                     same OnePlus 6 (primaryCpuAbi=armeabi-v7a) and it played the
#                     SAME spectator ONS-Dria match for 508 s, 0 signals, frame
#                     advance verified. So arm64 is not passing because 64-bit is
#                     lenient — BOTH WIDTHS PLAY.

#                     ✅ THE WEB IS 55/55 AND AS-Convoy BOOTS. `yarn e2e --target
#                     ut2004`: 55 passed, 0 failed, 44.9m. PROBE_MAP=AS-Convoy
#                     reaches `Bringing Level AS-Convoy.myLevel up for play` and

#                     0 GetFontSizeIndex. ⚠ A BOOT verdict, not a match.

#                     1 failed, 4 DID NOT RUN" — the four were MY OWN 75-minute
#                     `timeout`, not Playwright, which has no maxFailures here. A
#                     TRUNCATED RUN IS NOT A STAMP. The one failure in it passed in
#                     isolation and passed again in the full re-run: a flake, seen
#                     once, recorded rather than retried away.
#
#                     i386 145/145 · three scenes BIT-IDENTICAL · frame bounds 0
#                     lp64_pipeline.sh -> PASS · run-standalone 12/12
#                     wasm SDLLaunch.wasm sha256 UNCHANGED (compared, not argued)

# LP64-ARM64-PLAYS  2026-08-31  ★ MAKING ARM64 *PLAY* RATHER THAN BOOT FOUND
#                     SEVEN MORE LP64 DEFECTS IN ONE SESSION, and the first of
#                     them was the ONS crash the owner reported.
#
#                     THE INSTRUMENT CAME FIRST. `GameActivity` now forwards a
#                     `com.ut2004.android.ARGS` string extra to the engine's
#                     command line (engine-ut2004, ufront 2.45), so
#                         adb shell am start -n com.ut2004.android/.MainActivity \
#                           --es com.ut2004.android.ARGS 'ONS-Dria?Game=Onslaught.
#                           ONSOnslaughtGame?bAutoNumBots=True?QuickStart=True?
#                           bPlayerMustBeReady=False'
#                     loads a map and STARTS A MATCH with no touch at all — the
#                     launcher's ten-second countdown does the rest. Before it the
#                     activity could only ever reach the menu, so "does arm64
#                     PLAY?" was not a question anyone could ask in a scripted run.
#                     ⚠ THE URL MUST BE argv[1]. UGameEngine::Init takes the FIRST
#                     token as the travel URL and, since ufront 2.38, rejects a
#                     global option in that slot and falls back to DefaultLocalMap
#                     WITHOUT looking at the next token — so `CPUSPEED=3000
#                     ONS-Dria?...` reaches the MAIN MENU. Measured on the device
#                     before the order was swapped.
#
#                     (1) `McdGjkBinarySubset + 0x3c` — THE REPORTED ONS CRASH.
#                     It is `const int *[16]`, so 0x3c is element 15 at i386 and
#                     nothing at all at LP64: an eight-byte load at byte 60 splices
#                     the low half of element[8] onto the high half of element[7].
#                     ★ THE TOMBSTONE IS THAT SPLICE, DIGIT FOR DIGIT. libUT2004.so
#                     maps around 0x75_00000000, so an entry reads
#                     0x00000075_0040546a and `fault addr 0x40546a00000075` is
#                     those two words exchanged.
#                     ★★ AND MATHENGINE'S OWN amd64 BUILD CONFIRMS THE REPAIR:
#                     win_amd64_single/McdConvex.lib reads `mov rax,[rip+0x78]` at
#                     BOTH references — 0x78 = 15*8 against our 0x3c = 15*4, in
#                     McdGjkFaceQueueInit and McdGjkPenetrationDepth, which are
#                     exactly the two sites. Two for two, off a binary.
#                     ⚠ `ptrwidth_check` CANNOT SEE THIS CLASS AT ALL — nothing is
#                     truncated. Both halves are real addresses.
#                     ⚠ AND THE SHAPE ALONE IS NOT ENOUGH: MeProfile_linux.c has
#                     six sites of it and all six are CORRECT (`frameTime` is a
#                     struct of MeI32, `clockSpeed + 4` is its own high word). The
#                     rule keys on the DECLARATION being an array of pointers.
#                     New tool: tools/fix_global_array_index.py. 3 occurrences —
#                     the third is CxSmallSort's vtable address point, which
#                     fix_vtable_offsets cannot see because it is a macro over the
#                     vtable symbol rather than a local.
#
#                     (2) `IxBoxTriList`, DECLINED SINCE LP64-TWO-COMPILERS, AND
#                     THE FIX IS A TYPE RATHER THAN A SPELLING. Bisected site by
#                     site: 4 of the 7 rewrites are byte-identical under emcc and
#                     3 are not, and the 3 are exactly those where the repair turns
#                     an INTEGER load into a POINTER one — `p[1]` yields
#                     `undefined4` cast to `float *`, `->vertices[1]` yields
#                     `MeVector3 *` directly, and clang tees one extra local three
#                     bytes away. ★ KEEP THE VALUE AN INTEGER OF POINTER WIDTH:
#                     `((kd_uptr *)p)[1]` is `unsigned int` at i386 AND wasm32, so
#                     the conversion that follows is the one that was always there.
#                     Every member spelling tried failed emcc; this one passes both.
#                     Guarded by three MEASUREMENTS — offset 4k here, 8k there,
#                     sizeof 4/8 — so `flags` (MeU32) keeps the member spelling.
#                     17 -> 24 sites, 1 decline -> 0.
#
#                     (3) `qsort(partArray, n, 4, cmp)` — THE RAGDOLL CRASH.
#                     4 is `sizeof(*partArray)` at i386 over an array of POINTERS,
#                     so qsort walks four-byte steps over eight-byte elements and
#                     the comparator is handed spliced addresses.
#                     `fault addr 0x6390f87800000073` under local_qsort, from
#                     MeFAssetGetPartsSortedByName <- KInitSkeletonKarma. Four
#                     sites in MeFAsset.c, all on ragdoll creation. The repair
#                     names no type: `sizeof(*partArray)`.
#                     ⚠⚠ AND "ONLY REWRITE WHAT MOVES" WAS BROKEN BY THIS PROJECT'S
#                     OWN STT_FILE TRAP. With no type to compare, the test compiles
#                     the file at -m64 before and after — written as `a_<fn>` and
#                     `b_<fn>` the two objects differ ON THE NAME ALONE, so it
#                     returned True for everything and silently stopped testing. It
#                     passed McdPolygonSort, which sorts MeVector3: twelve bytes at
#                     every width and already correct.
#
#                     (4) RULE E — the same narrow load with NO DESTINATION.
#                     Rule C's "the destination is declared pointer-width" was only
#                     ever a PROXY for the measurement. An inline one has nothing to
#                     inspect, and IxAggregateLineSegment+304 on the device is
#                         ldr w8, [x8, #0x20]     <- elementTable, CORRECT offset 32
#                         ldr x8, [x8, w9, sxtw]  <- fault addr 0xffffffffd3b89558
#                     — the right address, four bytes of an eight-byte pointer,
#                     sign-extended into the address the tombstone reports.
#                     ⚠⚠ AND WIDENING AN ASSIGNMENT TARGET ALONE CORRUPTS. The
#                     first version matched both sides of `=` and made
#                     `*(undefined4 *)(...mdtbody[1]) = uVar1` an EIGHT-byte store
#                     of a four-byte local, zeroing the high half of a pointer that
#                     had been valid. i386 STAYED 145/145 AND THE THREE SCENES
#                     STAYED BIT-IDENTICAL; the LP64 harness went red with an ASan
#                     error in MdtBodyGetCenterOfMassPosition and trajectories that
#                     stopped at row 52 and row 0. ★ THE LP64 HARNESS IS THE ONLY
#                     GATE THAT SEES THIS — the third time this project has been
#                     told so. A store is now widened only when its SOURCE is a
#                     load being widened too.
#
#                     (5) ⚠⚠ `fix_literal_offsets` READ THE CALLING CONVENTION AS
#                     THE RETURN TYPE. metoolkit puts a long return type on its own
#                     line:
#                         MEPUBLIC
#                         McdGeometryInstanceID
#                                       MEAPI McdModelGetGeometryInstance(...);
#                     The one-line pattern matched the SECOND line and recorded the
#                     type as `MEAPI`, which resolves to no struct — so the function
#                     read as "returns something untypeable" rather than as
#                     unmatched, AND NOTHING SAID SO. Eight accessors. One of them
#                     is a ragdoll crash: IxAggregateLineSegment reads `pvVar7 +
#                     0x30`, which is McdGeometryInstance::child at i386 and 72 at
#                     LP64, so the child instance came back NULL and the struct copy
#                     two lines later faulted on address 0. `+ 4` is `mTM`, 4/8.
#                     Confirmed by the amd64 build: `mov 0x48(%rdi),%rbp`,
#                     `mov 0x8(%rcx),%rdx`.
#
#                     (6) ⚠⚠ A SECOND ACCESSOR ON ONE TYPE IS NOT A CONFLICT, AND
#                     TREATING IT AS ONE COST THE WHOLE CONVEX-MESH FAMILY.
#                     McdConvexMesh.c exports McdConvexMeshGetTypeId AND
#                     McdConvexMeshMeshGetTypeId, both returning 7.
#                     `interaction_types` read two tags claiming one id as evidence
#                     about neither, ID 7 LEFT THE TABLE ENTIRELY, and
#                     McdSphylConvexMeshIntersect and seven siblings were never
#                     typed — so every geometry offset in IxConvexPrimitives.c
#                     stayed at its i386 value, including
#                     `(McdConvexHull *)(geom + 0x10)`: McdConvexMesh::mHull is 16
#                     here and 32 there, so ConvexHullNSegment got a hull pointer
#                     sixteen bytes short and ConvexHullVoronoiRegion faulted on the
#                     face table (LP64 vehicle, frame 27 of a ktrace run).
#                     ★ THE TIE-BREAK IS THE TYPE DATABASE: a tag naming a struct
#                     the oracle declares is a type; one that does not is an
#                     accessor. 26 -> 28 functions typed.
#                     ⚠ A LATENT CRASH CAME WITH IT: `out[fn] = None` is the
#                     dropped marker and a THIRD registration then indexed None[0].
#                     Nothing had been registered three times until now.
#
#                     (7) THE `alloca` WHOSE TYPE IS ON THE FIELD IT IS STORED INTO.
#                     `alloca(n * 0x18)` for McdUserTriangle in IxSphylPrimitives —
#                     half the array the generator is about to fill. The type is
#                     DECLARED (`McdUserTriangle *list;`), so nothing is inferred.
#                     ⚠ IT NEEDS A SECOND, LATE INVOCATION: in the raw recovery the
#                     statement is `pMVar9[3].prev = ...` and the field has no NAME
#                     until fix_literal_offsets and fix_index_layout have run.
#                     `--field-allocas-only` keeps the other five rules off.
#                     ⚠ fix_element_stride repairs the other three triangle-list
#                     allocas and CANNOT repair this one: its anchor is gated on
#                     fix_word_indexed_struct having typed an access in the same
#                     file, and IxSphylPrimitives walks its triangles through BYTE
#                     cursors. Broadening that gate is measured-unsafe
#                     (LP64-ANDROID-ARM64). This rule needs no such gate.
#
#                     ★ AND A NINTH THAT IS NOT A RECOVERY DEFECT AT ALL: the
#                     SDL thread's 1 MB DEFAULT STACK. Karma sizes several arrays
#                     on the stack from a run-time count — MeDictNode nodemem[200]
#                     in McdConvexMeshPlaneCut, an alloca of triangleMaxCount
#                     McdUserTriangles in every Ix*TriList — and at LP64 EVERY ONE
#                     OF THEM DOUBLES. ★ `code 2 (SEGV_ACCERR)` rather than
#                     SEGV_MAPERR is what names it: a guard page is mapped
#                     PROT_NONE, so hitting it is a PERMISSION fault, and that is
#                     what separates stack exhaustion from a wild pointer.
#                     SDLActivity now asks for 16 MB (reserved address space, not
#                     committed memory). CONFIRMED BY THE CRASH CLASS CHANGING,
#                     not by argument: MeSetAdd/ACCERR disappeared and the device
#                     moved on to McdSphylTriangleListIntersect/MAPERR — the site
#                     x86-64 was already stuck at. 120 s -> 140 s.
#
#                     ⚠⚠ AND A RED RESULT IS STILL NOT EVIDENCE UNTIL THE BASELINE
#                     IS CHECKED — four for four now. This session's sweep crashes
#                     6 of 8 gametypes in IxAggregateLineSegment on the tree the
#                     previous session called "7 gametypes clean". It is NOT a
#                     regression: `git show HEAD:...McdAggregate.c` has all five
#                     narrow `elementTable` loads BYTE-IDENTICALLY, including the
#                     one that faults. What changed is that ragdolls now get
#                     created at all — each repair buys the next crash, which is the
#                     pattern this whole port has followed. The recorded sweep never
#                     reached KInitSkeletonKarma.
#
#                     ⚠ ktrace_diff.py CALLED TWO BUILDS "behaviourally IDENTICAL"
#                     over TWELVE FRAMES of two hundred and twenty-three, because
#                     `first` is computed over the frames the runs SHARE and one had
#                     crashed. The frame counts were printed at the top of its own
#                     report and the verdict did not consult them. It now refuses
#                     below 90% overlap. Same family as substitute_test.sh's
#                     "substituted and ran cleanly".
#
#                     ptrwidth_classify.py is new: the 98 aarch64 diagnostics are
#                     now 91, of which 39 are in four NAMED benign classes
#                     (KD_FBITS into a 4-byte slot 10, MeDict's own void * key 15,

#                     A CLASS: IxBoxTriList's two use the same macro and write
#                     EIGHT bytes at byte 16 of a three-float vector.

# LP64-LEAKED-LOOPVAR  2026-08-31  ⚠⚠ A REFACTOR PASSED `E` WHERE THE BLOCK
#                     NAMES THE TYPE `T`, AND PYTHON DID NOT COMPLAIN. Routing all
#                     three anchors of `fix_word_indexed_struct` through one helper
#                     left the POOL block calling
#                         rewrite_sites(out, v, E, paths, ...)
#                     where that block's type variable is `T`. `E` is not undefined
#                     — it LEAKS from the loop below it — so the pass silently used
#                     the PREVIOUS FILE'S type, stopped repairing `McdCache`, and
#                     reported no decline at all.

#                     truncated stack address the McdCache repair exists to prevent.

# LP64-ARM64-MENU  2026-08-31  ✅ ANDROID arm64 REACHES THE MAIN MENU WITH KARMA
#                     ON. `UT2k4MainMenu.Opened()`, 2043 log lines, ZERO signals,

#                     TWO REPAIRS, and the second is the one that mattered.
#
#                     (1) THE `struct` KEYWORD, NARROWLY. `IxSphereTriList` writes
#                     `((struct McdTriangleList *)0)->list`, one keyword away from
#                     the anchor — and simply allowing the keyword had already been
#                     measured as unsafe (63 -> 147 rewrites, scene_ragdoll
#                     NONDETERMINISTIC and DIFFERING FROM i386). It is now accepted
#                     only behind the SAME evidence as the direct spelling —
#                     `fix_word_indexed_struct` having already typed an access as
#                     `((E *)v)->` — plus one more restriction:
#                     ⚠ A GATED SPELLING GETS WHOLE ELEMENTS ONLY. "the remainder
#                     lands on a member start" is a strong test for a 68-byte
#                     struct with two members and a VACUOUS one for an 8-byte
#                     struct with two: every 4-aligned value passes.
#                     `MeAssetDBXMLIO` reaches `PElement::childHead ->
#                     PElementNode` (sizeof 8) carrying seventeen bare `8`s that
#                     have nothing to do with any table. Result: +2 sites, exactly
#                     the alloca and the cursor, and MeAssetDBXMLIO was DECLINED by
#                     the byte-identity gate on top.
#
#                     (2) ★★ A FRAME SLOT THAT IS A SCALAR, NOT AN ARRAY — and this
#                     is what was actually crashing. `fix_frame_slots` scales the
#                     fabricated argument areas, and it DECLINED these by its own
#                     rule ("the name must appear nowhere except inside a
#                     frame-slot cast"):
#                         float fStackY_160;                       /* 4 bytes */
#                         *(MeReal (**) [3])((kd_iptr)&fStackY_160) = edge;  /* 8 */
#                     At LP64 that store runs FOUR BYTES PAST a four-byte local and
#                     into whatever the compiler put next — which was the outgoing
#                     `McdUserTriangle ct`. The tombstone landed two calls later in
#                     `McdVanillaOverlapSphereTri`, dereferencing an
#                     `inTri->vertices[0]` of 0x7200000072.
#                     ⚠ `check_frame_bounds` CANNOT SEE IT: it reads ARRAY bounds,
#                     and this slot is a scalar. It reported 0 throughout.
#                     THE REPAIR: the decline rule was right that widening changes
#                     what a bare read means — but if EVERY other use is the bare
#                     scalar, `var[0]` means exactly what `var` meant. So those are
#                     rewritten with it. 6 slots across 6 files, all in the
#                     triangle-list interactions plus MeAssetDBXMLOutput.

#                     and every element after the first is read from the middle of
#                     the one before. Two new shapes: a direct-member anchor
#                     (`triList->list`, not the offsetof spelling) and a cursor
#                     that lives in a UNION MEMBER rather than a local
#                     (`MStack_26c.triangleData.ptr = (void *)(...tag + 0x18)`).

#                     ⚠⚠ AND THE ANCHOR HAD TO BE EVIDENCE-GATED, TWICE, BECAUSE

# LP64-TWO-COMPILERS  2026-08-31  ⚠⚠ THE i386 ACCEPTANCE TEST CANNOT SPEAK FOR
#                     wasm32, AND NOTHING IN THIS PROJECT SAID SO UNTIL NOW.
#                     i386 is compiled by GCC and wasm32 by CLANG, so "byte
#                     identical" has always been a statement about ONE code
#                     generator. The triangle-list repair below is byte-identical
#                     under gcc at -m32 and NOT under emcc — same address, same
#                     width, different instruction selection. The web artifact
#                     SHIPS, so a repair only gcc certifies is not certified.
#                     `fix_word_indexed_struct` now runs emcc over the source it
#                     replaces and the candidate and requires those to match too;
#                     with no wasm baseline to compare against, a self-comparison
#                     is the no-op check. It declined IxBoxTriList and kept
#                     IxSphereTriList — the SAME repair, and only one of them
#                     survives clang. If emcc is missing the site declines rather
#                     than being assumed safe.
#
#                     THE SECOND ANCHOR, which is what needed the gate.
#                     `IxBoxTriList`/`IxSphereTriList` walk the triangle list with
#                         puVar31 = (undefined4 *)((kd_iptr)&(<list>)->mRefCtAndID + cur);
#                         pfVar26 = (float *)puVar31[1];   /* vertices[1] */
#                     `McdUserTriangle` is {MeVector3 *vertices[3]; MeVector3
#                     *normal; ...}, so those four words are four POINTERS — byte 4
#                     here and EIGHT there. The type is DECLARED:
#                     `McdUserTriangle *list;` in the oracle.
#                     ★ THE CURSOR IS RIGHT AT BOTH WIDTHS BY COINCIDENCE
#                     (`&p[1].next` is 24 here and 48 there, and so is sizeof), so
#                     nothing about the stride reveals it.
#                     ⚠ AND THE OFFLINE SCENES CANNOT REACH THIS AT ALL —
#                     `scene_ragdoll` is capsules on a PLANE; there is no triangle
#                     list in it. Only a body hitting real world geometry runs it.
#
#                     THREE MISTAKES ON THE WAY, all of the same family:
#                       * `if not pools: continue` skipped the whole FILE, and the
#                         second anchor needs no pool — every triangle-list site in
#                         the corpus was silently out of scope.
#                       * the site regex matched the DECLARATION
#                         `undefined4 *puVar31;`, whose rewrite is a syntax error,
#                         which reads back as "not byte-identical" and declined the
#                         five correct sites with it.
#                       * `flo` keys structs by TAG (`_McdUserTriangle`) and the
#                         code names the TYPEDEF, so asking once returned an empty
#                         map and `vertices` came back as the ARRAY NAME instead of
#                         `vertices[0]`.
#                     Each one presented as "0 rewritten, 0 declined" — a clean
#                     zero that reads exactly like "there was nothing to do".
#
#                     aarch64 truncations 134 -> 98 across 28 objects. Of what
#                     remains, 21 are KD_FBITS float bit-puns and 13 are an MeI32

# LP64-PIPEFAIL  2026-08-31  ⚠⚠ A PASS CRASHED AND THIS PIPELINE REPORTED PASS.
#                     Every pass in `lp64_pipeline.sh` is piped into `head`/`tail`
#                     to keep the log readable, and `|| exit 2` then tests the exit
#                     status of TAIL, which is always 0. A Python IndexError went
#                     straight through it: the acceptance test ran on a tree the
#                     pass had not finished editing and printed 145/145, three
#                     scenes BIT-IDENTICAL, PASS. The only symptom was a summary
#                     line that had gone missing from the log.
#                     `set -o pipefail` is now the second line of the script.
#                     ★ THE GATE THAT CANNOT FAIL IS THE ONE TO CHECK FIRST.
#
#                     RULE D, and it is why BR-Anubis now survives its own
#                     teardown. `McdBatchContextDestroy` frees the last of every
#                     four contact pools through `(void *)*puVar1`, where `puVar1`
#                     is an `undefined4 *` pointing AT a `contacts` POINTER. The
#                     other three in the same loop use `*(void **)(...)` and are
#                     fine; this one read four bytes of an eight-byte pointer and
#                     handed the half to free(). Rule A cannot see it — it
#                     dereferences a VARIABLE, not a `*(T *)` cast — so the repair
#                     types the POINTER instead of the load: `void **`, which is
#                     the same four-byte access at i386.
#
#                     ⚠ AND A MEMBER NAME IS NOT A TYPE. Four structs here declare
#                     a pointer called `contacts` — McdBatchEntry,
#                     McdBatchContactPool, _McdIntersectResult and MstBridge — so
#                     the first version matched on the name and correctly declined
#                     as ambiguous. `resolve_member` follows the EXPRESSION
#                     instead: `context` is declared `McdBatchContext *`, its
#                     `pools` is an `McdBatchContactPool *`, and `[3]` of that is
#                     where `.contacts` lives. Every hop is read from the oracle
#                     and an unresolvable hop declines the site.
#
#                     SWEEP after this: DM-Rankin, DM-DE-Ironic, CTF-FaceClassic,
#                     BR-Anubis, ONS-Torlan and VCTF-BE-Dystopia all clean at 120 s
#                     — SIX gametypes. ONS-Primeval still faults, now deeper, in
#                     McdGjkFaceQueueInit. AS-Convoy remains ENGINE-side.
