# LP64-SWEEP  2026-08-31  A MAP SWEEP IS A BETTER DETECTOR THAN ANY SCENE, and it
#                     took the corpus from "DM works" to five gametypes clean.
#                     Eight map/gametype combinations, 120 s each:
#                         DM-Rankin  DM-DE-Ironic  CTF-FaceClassic
#                         ONS-Torlan  ONS-Primeval      -> ALL OK
#                         BR-Anubis   -> runs 120 s, SIGSEGV in the SHUTDOWN free
#                         AS-Convoy   -> ENGINE-side, not Karma: a runaway loop in
#                                        Engine.HUD.GetFontSizeIndex
#
#                     THREE MORE REPAIRS, all measured, all byte-identical at i386.
#
#                     RULE G, a TYPED POINTER stepped by a whole element:
#                         piVar1 = piVar1 + 0x11;   /* 17 ints = 68 = i386 sizeof */
#                     `McdAggregateAddElement` scans for a free slot with an
#                     `int *`, and `fix_strides`' self-advancing rule keys on
#                     `&p->field`, so a plain `p + K` is invisible to it. The step
#                     is a whole element only if `K * sizeof(*p)` equals the i386
#                     element size, which is measured.
#
#                     ⚠ R0, AND IT WAS THE ONE THAT MATTERED. A variable stored
#                     *INTO* the field is the table too. `McdAggregateCreate` keeps
#                     the fresh allocation in a bare `void *pvVar2` and zeroes the
#                     FIRST SLOT through it, before any load of the field exists to
#                     anchor on. Every other slot was repaired; slot 0 kept a
#                     four-byte clear, so its high half held whatever the previous
#                     tenant left.
#                     ★ A WATCHPOINT NAMED THE TENANT: `tri_push` in the convex
#                     hull builder, writing 0x7fff00000000 into memory that later
#                     became the element table. That is EXACTLY the value
#                     `McdGeometryIncrementReferenceCount` then dereferenced on
#                     CTF-Face, BR-Anubis and AS-Convoy. One unfixed statement out
#                     of fifty-six, and it broke three gametypes.
#
#                     `fix_word_indexed_struct.py` is NEW. `McdCache` comes out of
#                     a pool as `void *`, Ghidra types the local `undefined4 *`,
#                     and every field is addressed by WORD INDEX. Its leading
#                     floats keep their offsets at both widths; the two POINTERS at
#                     the end move 52->56 and 56->64 and are eight bytes wide, so
#                     `puVar3[0xd] = ptr` wrote half a pointer. That is the
#                     Onslaught crash: `McdGjkFatness (ins = 0xfffcf878)`, the low
#                     half of the stack address `0x7ffffffcf878`.
#
#                     THE TYPE COMES FROM THE POOL — `m_cachedData` is declared
#                     `void *` in the oracle and the rest is a COMMENT, so
#                     `init(pool, 100, sizeof(*(McdCache *)0), 16)` is the only
#                     place it is written down at all.
#
#                     ⚠ ONLY REWRITE WHAT MOVES. Re-spelling all twelve indices was
#                     NOT byte-identical: five are `MeReal` fields, and
#                     `puVar3[9] = fVar5` through `undefined4` is an INTEGER store
#                     where `->fat1 = fVar5` is a float one. Measuring offset and
#                     width first leaves three sites — the two pointer stores and
#                     the read-back — which are the three the crash is about.
#
#                     ⚠ AND `flo.offsets_of` CANNOT SEE EITHER OF THEM. Its member
#                     regex does not parse a comma-separated declarator, and
#                     McdCache ends `McdGeometryInstanceID ins1, ins2;`. The type
#                     database has them but without array expansion. Neither source
#                     alone can resolve this struct; the union can, and the
#                     self-check is what proves it.
#
#                     i386 145/145; three scenes BIT-IDENTICAL; wasm32 146/146;
#                     pipeline PASS; run-standalone 12/12; gates 6/6.
#
# LP64-PLAYS  2026-08-31  ⚠⚠ UT2004 RUNS AT 64-BIT. A four-bot DM-Rankin and a
#                     SIX-BOT ONS-TORLAN WITH VEHICLES each ran 200 seconds to the
#                     timeout with ZERO signals. Onslaught is the strong test: its
#                     vehicles ARE Karma rigid bodies, and the stack reached
#                     `KWorldStepSafeTime -> MdtPackPartition -> MdtBclAddAngular3`
#                     — the constraint solver stepping, not initialisation.
#
#                     THREE MORE DEFECTS, AND ONE OF THEM WAS IN A GATE.
#
#                     (6) THE ELEMENT SIZE, not the offsets. `McdAggregateElement`
#                     is {MeMatrix4 mRelTM; McdGeometryID mGeometry;} — 68 bytes
#                     here, 72 there, with `mGeometry` at 64 in BOTH because
#                     sixteen floats do not grow. So every offset check passes and
#                     every stride is wrong. `fix_element_stride.py` is new.
#
#                     ⚠ AND IT WAS NOT ONLY `i * 0x44`. `McdAggregateCreate` zeroes
#                     the table with gcc's four-at-a-time unrolling and EVERY
#                     offset in it is baked — 0x40, 0x84, 200, 0x10c, step 0x110,
#                     which is `64 + k*68` and `4*68`. At LP64 the slots are at 64,
#                     136, 208, 280. The loop was zeroing the middle of the
#                     matrices and leaving every mGeometry holding malloc garbage.
#                     ★ AND THE WIDTH WAS WRONG TOO: `undefined4` clears four bytes
#                     of an eight-byte pointer. Fixing the stride alone still ships
#                     a dangling pointer; that is why the first attempt moved the
#                     crash instead of removing it.
#
#                     THE DECOMPOSITION IS THE MEASUREMENT: a literal L on the
#                     table's additive chain is an offset into it only if
#                     `L % sizeof_i386(E)` lands on a member START of E. 54 sites.
#
#                     (7) ⚠⚠ A GATE THAT COULD NOT EXPRESS THE ANSWER.
#                     `flo.accept_edits` bisects a position-ordered list, so it can
#                     only ever accept a CONTIGUOUS-IN-POSITION subset. gcc
#                     common-subexpression-eliminates the two loads of
#                     `g[1].mRefCtAndID` in `McdAggregateDestroy`:
#                         first only   byte-identical = False
#                         second only  byte-identical = False
#                         both         byte-identical = True
#                     Four `g[1].prev` edits sit between them, so every subset the
#                     bisect tried contained one without the other. It declined all
#                     six and the table pointer went on being read four bytes wide.
#                     THE TOOL HAD THE RIGHT ANSWER AND NO WAY TO SAY IT.
#                     `accept_by_field` makes the ATOM A FIELD rather than a
#                     position — sites naming the same field are exactly the ones
#                     gcc folds together. 63 -> 65 re-spelled.
#
#                     (8) RULE C, WIDENED. `MdtBcl3`'s solver holds an `MdtBody *`
#                     in `iVar15`, an `int` Ghidra also uses as a row index three
#                     statements earlier, so `fix_narrow_pointers` will not widen
#                     it and rule C's "destination is already pointer-width" test
#                     skipped it. `kd_iptr` IS `int` at 32-bit pointer width, so
#                     promoting the DECLARATION is a no-op on every shipping target
#                     by construction and correct for both uses at LP64. 2 -> 7.
#
#                     ⚠ SIGNEDNESS SURVIVES THE PROMOTION. `uint -> kd_iptr` is a
#                     SIGN CHANGE, not a widening, and it cost MdtBcl its
#                     byte-identity on three declarations that had nothing else
#                     wrong with them. Same lesson as the access type in
#                     fix_element_stride, learned twice in one session.
#
#                     i386 145/145 byte-identical; all three scenes BIT-IDENTICAL;
#                     wasm32 146/146 at the product's flags; lp64_pipeline.sh PASS;
#                     run-standalone 12/12; gates 6/6.
#
# LP64-NARROW-LOAD-C  2026-08-31  THE EARLIER REPAIR HID THE REMAINING ONE.
#                     Frame (5). `fix_narrow_loads` rules A and B both fire on
#                     clang's -Wint-to-pointer-cast, so they need the loaded value
#                     to be cast to a POINTER. Once `fix_narrow_pointers` has
#                     widened the destination local to `kd_iptr`, the assignment is
#                     integer-to-integer and CLANG SAYS NOTHING AT ALL:
#
#                         kd_iptr iVar8;                       /* already widened */
#                         iVar8 = *(int *)((kd_iptr)pvVar6
#                                 + KD_OFFSET(McdAggregate, elementTable));
#
#                     The address is right and the local is wide; the LOAD still
#                     takes four bytes of an eight-byte pointer. A pass fixing half
#                     the round trip made the other half INVISIBLE to the detector.
#
#                     RULE C IS MEASURED, NOT DIAGNOSED. It fires only when the
#                     address is an offsetof naming a real field, the destination is
#                     declared pointer-width, and sizeof(T::F) is 4 at i386 and 8 at
#                     LP64 — the field is provably a pointer that grew. TWO sites
#                     qualify, both McdAggregate::elementTable, both the crash. The
#                     other 32 narrow loads into widened locals do not name a field,
#                     so they are not measurable this way and are LEFT ALONE:
#                     widening a load of a genuine `int` field would read four bytes
#                     past it, so the measurement is the gate and not the shape.
#
#                     i386 145/145 byte-identical; three scenes BIT-IDENTICAL;
#                     wasm32 146/146 at the product's flags; pipeline PASS;
#                     run-standalone 12/12.
#
#                     ⚠ FRAME (6) IS OPEN, DIAGNOSED, AND ITS REPAIR IS DERIVABLE.
#                     `McdGeometryIncrementReferenceCount` received
#                     `g = 0x3f800000596203b0` — a SPLICED pointer: the low half is
#                     a plausible address and the high half is 0x3f800000, which is
#                     1.0f. A matrix diagonal is sitting in the top of a pointer.
#
#                         McdAggregateElement { MeMatrix4 mRelTM; McdGeometryID mGeometry; }
#                             i386  sizeof 68 (0x44), mGeometry at 64 (0x40)
#                             LP64  sizeof 72,        mGeometry at 64
#
#                     The OFFSET 0x40 is right at both widths; the STRIDE `i * 0x44`
#                     is not. 21 occurrences of 0x44 in McdAggregate.c, all of them
#                     this element stride.
#
#                     THE REPAIR IS DECLARED, NOT INFERRED: the oracle says
#                     `McdAggregateElement *elementTable;`, so any stride applied to
#                     an address rooted at KD_OFFSET(McdAggregate, elementTable) MUST
#                     be sizeof(McdAggregateElement). `i * 0x44` becomes
#                     `i * (int)sizeof(McdAggregateElement)`, which folds to 68 at
#                     i386 and is byte-identical there by construction. That is
#                     fix_baked_sizeof's class, not fix_narrow_loads'.
#
# LP64-INDEX-WIDTH  2026-08-31  AN INDEX CAN LAND ON THE RIGHT ADDRESS AND STILL
#                     BE A DEFECT. Frames (3) and (4) of LP64-RUNS-THE-GAME, both
#                     in `fix_index_layout`, both from the SAME hole: the `same`
#                     short-circuit compared only the OFFSET.
#
#                     ⚠ AND MY FIRST REPORT OF FRAME (3) WAS WRONG. I wrote that
#                     `fix_index_layout` "already reports this site by name, nine
#                     times". It does not. Those nine lines are its REPAIRS of
#                     `geom[1].next -> elementCountMax`, and a grep for "declin"
#                     had matched the declined-count HEADER and then printed every
#                     McdAggregate line under it. The surviving sites were the
#                     `.mRefCtAndID` ones, which the pass never mentioned at all —
#                     it silently counted them as "already correct". Read a tool's
#                     output by SECTION, not by grep.
#
#                     FRAME (3), McdAggregateCreate. `McdAggregate` is
#                     `{ McdGeometry m_g; void *elementTable; ... }` and
#                     `mRefCtAndID` is the FIRST member of `McdGeometry`, so
#                     `geom[1].mRefCtAndID` is the byte just past the base:
#                         i386 16 + 0 = 16   LP64 32 + 0 = 32
#                         elementTable at    16                32
#                     THE OFFSET AGREES AT BOTH WIDTHS. But `mRefCtAndID` is four
#                     bytes at both and `elementTable` is four here and EIGHT
#                     there, so the store kept the low half of a pointer and every
#                     read of it was an address with its top 32 bits gone —
#                     `0x5961f910` in the register file where every live pointer
#                     was `0x5555_5xxxxxxx`.
#
#                     THE REPAIR: the `same` test now requires the WIDTH to agree
#                     as well. `CxSmallSort` is untouched — `mPrev` and `mRep` are
#                     both pointers, so both scale and those sites still skip.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THAT REPAIR WAS WORSE THAN THE
#                     DEFECT. A field can resolve to a STRUCT that begins at the
#                     same address — `McdConvexMesh::mHull` does — and a struct has
#                     no access width, so `w` was its whole SIZE and the access
#                     type fell through to `MeU32`. It wrote FOUR bytes of an
#                     eight-byte pointer while widening the matching READ to
#                     `kd_uptr`: one truncating store became a truncating store
#                     plus a widened read of it. ONLY RUNNING THE GAME CAUGHT IT —
#                     every gate was green, i386 145/145, all three scenes
#                     bit-identical. The crash simply moved to
#                     MeBoundingSphereCalc2 with `points = 0x59620c50`.
#
#                     FRAME (4), the same site done properly: `descend_to_scalar`
#                     narrows a struct-typed field to the member that BEGINS at
#                     that address — `mHull` -> `mHull.vertex`, width 8 — and the
#                     caller VERIFIES the offset did not move at either width
#                     before using it. Store and read now both say `kd_uptr`.
#                     Anything that cannot be narrowed is skipped, which leaves
#                     the site exactly as it was rather than guessing.
#
#                     ⚠ A METHOD CORRECTION THAT CHANGED A RESULT. The wasm32 A/B
#                     must compile with the flags the PRODUCT uses —
#                     `-std=gnu99 -fno-strict-aliasing` (metoolkit_decomp's
#                     MD_C_FLAGS). Without them McdAggregate.c came back as ONE
#                     byte difference; with them it is identical. The recovery
#                     reinterprets storage, so a strict-aliasing probe is not
#                     measuring the thing that ships.
#
#                     i386 acceptance 145 object(s), 0 compile failure(s),
#                     0 byte difference(s); all three scenes BIT-IDENTICAL;
#                     wasm32 146/146 byte-identical at the product's own flags;
#                     lp64_pipeline.sh -> PASS; run-standalone 12/12; gates 6/6.
#                     Boot now reaches "Bringing Level NvidiaLogo.myLevel up for
#                     play" and the crash is in McdModelUpdate — physics RUNNING,
#                     not initialising.
#
#                     ⚠ FRAME (5) IS OPEN and is a different pass's territory:
#                     McdAggregateUpdateAABB holds the element table in an `int`
#                     local and reads it with `*(int *)` — the address is right,
#                     the LOAD is four bytes. That is fix_narrow_loads /
#                     fix_narrow_pointers, not this one.
#
# LP64-RUNS-THE-GAME  2026-08-30  ⚠ THE OFFLINE SCENES WERE NEVER GOING TO FIND
#                     THESE. `LP64-CLOSED` below is still true — all three scenes
#                     are byte-identical at both widths — and UT2004 still died at
#                     64-bit on the first frame it drew. The x86-64 LINUX vehicle
#                     (`-DUT_ALLOW_64BIT=ON`) is what closed the gap: same data
#                     model as Android, with gdb, and WITHOUT the engine's own
#                     SA_RESETHAND handler eating the tombstone.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE OPTION DID NOT DO WHAT IT SAID. `UT_ALLOW_64BIT`
#                     skipped the -m32 demotion block and then FOUR other places
#                     put -m32 back — a bare `add_compile_options(-m32)`, the exe
#                     link options in SDLLaunch and UCC, and a `CACHE ... FORCE`
#                     for gl4es that rewrote CMAKE_C_FLAGS for the whole project.
#                     The build announced "Pointer width: 8 bytes" and emitted
#                     `ELF 32-bit LSB relocatable, Intel i386`. Everything below
#                     was measured only after `file` said x86-64.
#
#                     THREE FRAMES, EACH FOUND BY RUNNING THE GAME:
#
#                     (1) SIGSEGV in __strcmp_avx2 under MeXMLElementProcess, from
#                         Handle_GeometryPrimitive_1_0 — a strcmp against a NULL
#                         `name`. The handler table it was searching had been built
#                         by a word loop and never held its MeXMLActionEnd sentinel.
#                         -> LP64-BLOCK-COPY, fixed.
#                     (2) SIGSEGV in KaFileCreate_1_0 with `e = 0x58cabf90` beside
#                         `parent = 0x555558ce4030`. Every pointer in that process
#                         is 0x5555_5xxxxxxx, so a 32-bit `e` is a MEASUREMENT of
#                         where the top half went. -> LP64-LIST-WALK, fixed.
#                     (3) SIGSEGV in McdAggregateCreate at
#                         `*(undefined4 *)(pMVar1[1].mRefCtAndID + 0x40 + iVar5)`.
#                         ⚠ FIRST REPORTED HERE as "already reported by name nine
#                         times by fix_index_layout" — THAT WAS WRONG, see
#                         LP64-INDEX-WIDTH above; those nine lines were REPAIRS of
#                         a different site and a grep had run the sections
#                         together. Fixed in LP64-INDEX-WIDTH.
#
#                     ★ THE ORDER MATTERS AND IS THE POINT. Each fix bought the
#                     next frame; none of them was reachable from the one before.
#                     Boot went XML-parse -> asset-DB built -> UGameEngine::LoadMap
#                     with per-actor collision. The remaining defect is in map
#                     load, not in start-up.
#
# LP64-BLOCK-COPY  2026-08-30  A WHOLE-STRUCT COPY GHIDRA RENDERED AS A WORD LOOP.
#                     `rep movsd` decompiles to a loop that walks ONE FIELD AT A
#                     TIME and counts in WORDS, with the cursor advanced by
#                     `p = (T *)&p->secondField` — four bytes at i386, EIGHT at
#                     LP64, while the literal trip count stays put.
#
#                     MEASURED PER SITE, and the last row is why the pass measures
#                     instead of matching:
#                       type                    n  step32/64  sizeof32/64  n*step64
#                       McdModel               30    4  8       120  208     240 over
#                       McdGeometryInstance    13    4  8        52   80     104 over
#                       MdtKeaDebugDataRequest 11    4  8        44   80      88 over
#                       MdtKeaParameters       19    4  4        76  128      76 SHORT
#                       MeXMLHandler            8    4  8        32   48      64 over
#                       MdtBclContactParams    18    4  4        72   72      72 CORRECT
#                     MdtBclContactParams holds no pointers, so it matches the
#                     pattern perfectly and MUST BE LEFT ALONE. The pass reports it
#                     as "already correct" rather than repairing it.
#
#                     TWO SHAPES. Both ends real memory -> one struct assignment.
#                     Source `&PTR_s_RADIUS_...` -> NOT a copy at all: that is
#                     `kd_relsec_rodata[]`, ONE SLOT PER ORIGINAL 4-BYTE WORD, so
#                     at LP64 the source has EIGHT slots per handler and the
#                     destination SIX words. Only a field-by-field read is correct:
#                     word j of element i is slot[i*W + j].
#
#                     ★ WHY IT IS GUARDED, AND WHY THAT IS THE STRONGER ANSWER.
#                     The single-spelling repair every other pass uses was tried
#                     FIRST AND MEASURED: the per-field expansion compiles to a
#                     different i386 object (22800 -> 22824 bytes). No one spelling
#                     can serve both widths here, because the two widths genuinely
#                     need different numbers of words per element. So the i386 text
#                     is kept VERBATIM under `#if __SIZEOF_POINTER__ == 4`.
#                     That is a proof, not a concession: __SIZEOF_POINTER__ IS 4 ON
#                     wasm32, so THE WEB ARTIFACT CANNOT HAVE CHANGED — and it was
#                     compiled and compared rather than argued, 146/146 identical.
#
#                     15 repaired, 1 already correct, 0 declined.
#                     i386 acceptance 145 object(s), 0 compile failure(s),
#                     0 byte difference(s); all three scenes still BIT-IDENTICAL;
#                     lp64_pipeline.sh -> PASS; run-standalone 12/12.
#
# LP64-LIST-WALK  2026-08-30  A LINKED LIST WALKED THROUGH A FOUR-BYTE CURSOR.
#                     `puVar1 = (undefined4 *)puVar1[1]` is right twice over at
#                     i386 — PElementNode::next really is at byte 4 — and at LP64
#                     `next` moves to byte 8, so `puVar1[1]` reads the TOP HALF of
#                     `current` and calls it the next node, while `*puVar1` keeps
#                     only the low 32 bits of a pointer. That is frame (2).
#
#                     THE NODE TYPE IS DECLARED, NOT INFERRED. The cursor is
#                     initialised from a NAMED field and the ORACLE header says
#                     what that field is: `PElementNode *childHead;`. The type
#                     comes from metoolkit/include, which is never edited.
#
#                     ⚠ NO `#if` HERE, AND THE DIFFERENCE FROM LP64-BLOCK-COPY IS
#                     THE WHOLE LESSON: `((PElementNode *)p)->next` IS `p[1]` at
#                     i386 — same address, same four-byte access — so one spelling
#                     does serve both widths. Reach for the guard only when a
#                     measurement says no single spelling exists.
#
#                     1 repaired, 2 declined and reported by name: McdAggregate's
#                     two cursors come from a BAKED offset (`+ 0x30`), not a named
#                     field, so the node type cannot be derived and the site waits
#                     on the base being typed first.
#
# LP64-CLOSED  2026-08-30  THE LP64 PORT IS DONE, AND "done" here means something
#                     stronger than the gate that was in place could express: with the
#                     ARITHMETIC HELD FIXED, two of the three scenes are BYTE-IDENTICAL at
#                     i386 and LP64 over all 901 rows. This supersedes LP64-INDEX-LAYOUT and
#                     LP64-ADDRESS-DEPENDENT, both of which are closed.
#
#                     WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG. LP64-INDEX-LAYOUT named ONE defect and
#                     specified its repair. The defect was real, the repair works, AND IT WAS
#                     NOT THE CAUSE OF THE NONDETERMINISM — it is on UT2004's path, not on
#                     `scene_ragdoll`'s. The ragdoll is capsules on a PLANE; there is no
#                     triangle list in it. Fixing the located site moved the run-to-run
#                     variance from 4 distinct outcomes in 10 to 9 in 40 and no further.
#
#                     THE READ THAT DID IT, found by census rather than by reading:
#                         McdSphylPlaneIntersect            (7,298 calls a run)
#                           pvVar9 = McdModelGetGeometry(p->model1);
#                           fVar2  = -*(float *)((kd_iptr)pvVar9 + 0x14);
#                     0x10 and 0x14 are `McdSphyl::mRadius` and `mHalfHeight` at i386 and 32
#                     and 36 at LP64. At 64-bit the capsule's radius comes out of the middle
#                     of its own base class — bytes 16..19 of `_McdGeometry`, the low half of
#                     the `prev` POINTER. An address, read as a float, straight into the
#                     contact solver. THAT is the address dependence.
#
#                     ⚠ AND IT HAD BEEN REPORTED FOR MONTHS, in a line nobody costed:
#                     fix_literal_offsets' own note, "44 declined, an Ix* function handles TWO
#                     geometries so per-file inference cannot work". Those 44 were not a tail.
#
#                     WHAT LANDED, four changes, each with its own evidence:
#                       * tools/interaction_types.py — NEW, evidence only. The type is written
#                         down in the REGISTRATION: McdSphylGetTypeId() returns 5,
#                         McdPlaneGetTypeId() returns 3, and McdSphylPlaneRegisterInteraction
#                         installs intersectFn then calls SetInteractions(frame, 5, 3). So
#                         model1 is an McdSphyl and model2 an McdPlane, inferred from nothing.
#                         26 interaction functions typed this way; two independent confirmations
#                         that the argument order IS the model order (the sphyl reads model1 at
#                         0x10/0x14, the trilist reads model2 at 40/48/52).
#                       * fix_literal_offsets.py — consumes it, and grew one more site shape:
#                         `(base + iVar12 * 4 + 0x10)`, an indexed array MEMBER, which the old
#                         pattern could not see because it required the literal to follow the
#                         base directly. That one is `McdBox::mR[i]`, and at LP64 it made
#                         McdSphylBoxIntersect produce TWO FEWER CONTACTS than the same sources
#                         at i386. 835 -> 936 rewrites.
#                       * tools/fix_index_layout.py — NEW, the residue fix_derived_fields
#                         cannot type: 38 re-spelled, 33 measured as ALREADY CORRECT at LP64,
#                         13 declined. It repairs LP64-INDEX-LAYOUT's site and every sibling.
#                       * tools/fix_narrow_loads.py — applied at last, 44 sites in 9 objects.
#
#                     ★ THE MEASUREMENT THAT REPLACED THE GATE. `-mfpmath=387` was believed to
#                     make the two widths compute the same numbers; lp64_run.sh's header said
#                     so. IT DOES NOT — it equalises the FORMAT, not the EXCESS PRECISION, and
#                     an 80-bit x87 register gets spilled at different points by the two ABIs.
#                     The old 1e-5 floor was absorbing that: the SAME sources at the SAME width
#                     differing only in -mfpmath first differ at 2.0e-07 and reach a worst of
#                     1.97e+02, the identical worst the i386/LP64 pair reached. Add
#                     `-ffloat-store` at both widths and the arithmetic is identical:
#                         scene_chain    BYTE-IDENTICAL, 901 rows
#                         scene_ragdoll  BYTE-IDENTICAL, 901 rows
#                         scene_boxes    differs at line 96 — see LP64-CONTACT-ORDER
#                     That is the gate now. There is no floor and no tolerance.
#
#                     PROVEN, on the tree this landed with:
#                         i386 acceptance   145 object(s), 0 compile failures, 0 byte differences
#                         determinism       100 runs of the plain -m64 ragdoll, ONE distinct
#                                           output; setarch -R gives the same one
#                         wasm32            all 146 shipped sources compile BYTE-IDENTICALLY
#                                           before and after — the web build is measurably
#                                           unchanged, not argued to be
#                         amd64 oracle      22 of 22 rewritten offsets confirmed against
#                                           MathEngine's own 64-bit archives
#                         arm64 control     every tag measures the same at arm64 as at x86-64
#                         truncations       181 -> 134 at aarch64, 0 at armv7a
#
#                     ⚠ WHAT IS NOT CLOSED. ptrwidth_check still counts 134 truncations — none of
#                     them now moves any scene, which is a measurement over three scenes and not a
#                     proof over the corpus. LP64-CONTACT-ORDER was the last known divergence and
#                     it closed the same day; ALL THREE scenes are byte-identical at the two widths
#                     with `-ffloat-store`.

# LP64-CONTACT-ORDER  2026-08-30 (closed the same day)  ✅ CLOSED. ALL THREE SCENES ARE NOW
#                     BYTE-IDENTICAL at i386 and LP64 with the arithmetic held fixed.
#
#                     WHAT IT WAS. `McdContactSimplify` walks the incoming contact array with a
#                     local Ghidra put in the WRONG STACK SLOT — it merged an `McdContact *` into
#                     an unrelated `McdContactLink` local and used its `next` field as the cursor:
#                         MStack_9c.next = (_McdContactLink *)(inContacts + cNum);
#                         ... *(float *)&((&(MStack_9c.next)->contact)[2]) ...
#                         MStack_9c.next = (_McdContactLink *)((kd_iptr)MStack_9c.next + 0x28);
#                     0x28 is 40 is sizeof(McdContact) — ON i386. At LP64 it is 48, because the
#                     contact's two `element` unions hold pointers, so the cursor walked 40 bytes
#                     at a time over 48-byte elements. And the index `[2]` steps in units of
#                     `sizeof(McdContact *)` — four bytes here, EIGHT there — while what it
#                     dereferences is a four-byte float.
#
#                     HOW IT PRESENTED, and why it took an instrument rather than a reading: two
#                     contacts with EQUAL separation arriving in the opposite order at the two
#                     widths, and four calls later a simplify returning 3 contacts where i386
#                     returned 4. Dumping the IN and OUT arrays of every McdContactSimplify call
#                     at both widths localised it in one run — the INPUTS were identical and only
#                     the OUTPUT differed, which is what named the function.
#
#                     THE REPAIR is `fix_strides.py`'s THIRD SHAPE, and both halves are no-ops at
#                     i386 by construction:
#                         + 0x28            ->  + (int)sizeof(*(McdContact *)0)
#                         &((&(P)->F)[k])   ->  ((char *)&(P)->F + k * (int)sizeof(ACC))
#                     The element type is not guessed: it comes from what the cursor was ASSIGNED
#                     FROM before it started stepping, and the literal must equal that type's
#                     i386 size or the site is declined. Six other self-advancing cursors in the
#                     corpus are declined by that test, correctly — `poly1 + 4` over an `MeVector3`
#                     is one float, not one element.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE GATE'S ALLOWLIST IS GONE WITH IT. `lp64_run.sh` carried this scene
#                     by name for exactly one commit. An exception that outlives its defect is a
#                     place for the next one to hide.
#
#                     ORIGINALLY FILED AS: ⚠ OPEN, PINNED, AND PRE-EXISTING. With the arithmetic held
#                     fixed (-ffloat-store) `scene_boxes_on_plane` is byte-identical at i386
#                     and LP64 for 94 steps and then differs, first by 2.0e-04.
#
#                     IT IS AN ORDER, NOT A VALUE. The contact SET is the same at both widths;
#                     two of them arrive in the opposite ORDER —
#                         m64 #98  0.131278843 -0.320444286 0.247821286
#                         m32 #98  0.136381716 -0.330199182 0.243161917
#                         m64 #100 0.136381716 -0.330199182 0.243161917
#                         m32 #100 0.131278843 -0.320444286 0.247821286
#                     — the same two positions, swapped. The solver then sums them in the other
#                     order, and the box scene amplifies that from step 94 on.
#
#                     IT PREDATES EVERY PASS IN THIS PIPELINE: the same comparison on the tree
#                     BEFORE the 2026-08-30 changes differs at the same line. It is pinned by
#                     name in lp64_run.sh so that its PRESENCE does not fail the gate and any
#                     CHANGE to it does.
#
#                     ✅ It is not a determinism defect — the 64-bit build is deterministic
#                     across 100 runs with ASLR on — and no shipping target is 64-bit.

# LP64-INDEX-LAYOUT  2026-08-30  ✅ CLOSED by LP64-CLOSED above: the repair is written,
#                     landed and confirmed. Read on for the diagnosis, which stands — and
#                     for the prediction that it explained the nondeterminism, which does not.
#                     ORIGINALLY FILED AS: ⚠ OPEN DEFECT — the mechanism behind
#                     LP64-ADDRESS-DEPENDENT, located and confirmed. THE LP64 PORT IS NOT DONE,
#                     and this contradicts what STATE.md and HANDOVER.md have implied since
#                     2026-08-29.
#
#                     THE SITE, IN THE SHIPPED SOURCES:
#                         IxBoxTriList.c:140   pcVar4 = (code *)trilistgeom[3].mRefCtAndID;
#                                              count  = (*(int (*)(...))pcVar4)(...);
#                     `trilistgeom` is an McdGeometry *; the object behind it is an
#                     McdTriangleList, and byte 48 of that object is the triangle-generator
#                     CALLBACK. Ghidra rendered `[base + 48]` as an index through the type IT
#                     chose. MEASURED with a probe over the shipped kd_types.h:
#                         sizeof(struct _McdGeometry)  i386 16   LP64 32
#                         trilistgeom[3] starts at     i386 48   LP64 96
#                     So at 64-bit pointer width the call target is read from byte 96 — a
#                     different field of a different size, whose contents depend on what the
#                     allocator put there. That is the address dependence in
#                     LP64-ADDRESS-DEPENDENT, and it is on the ragdoll's own path
#                     (IxSphylPrimitives:1527 and IxSphereTriList:129 read the same way).
#
#                     ⚠ THIS IS THE EXAMPLE layout_check.py's OWN HEADER USES, verbatim, and the
#                     tool still ends its report with "What is NOT in doubt: at least one site is
#                     measurably wrong on arm64 and nothing in the toolchain says so." It was
#                     right. lp64_pipeline.sh reported `-> PASS` over the top of it because no
#                     check it ran could see a wrong VALUE that did not also crash.
#
#                     THE REPAIR IS MECHANICAL AND IS NOT YET WRITTEN. `NAME[k].field`, where k
#                     is non-zero and sizeof(T) differs between the widths, must become
#                         *(FT *)((char *)NAME + K),  K = k*sizeof_i386(T) + offsetof_i386(field)
#                     which is a no-op at i386 BY CONSTRUCTION — same byte offset — and correct
#                     at LP64. layout_check.py already locates the sites and already measures
#                     both sizes, so the pass has its inputs. ⚠ Per this project's own standard a
#                     layout claim needs the amd64 oracle behind it: tools/amd64_oracle.py reads
#                     the real 64-bit field offsets out of MathEngine's own build, and every
#                     rewritten K should be confirmed against it rather than computed and trusted.
#
#                     WHAT WAS DONE THIS SESSION, and what it did NOT achieve:
#                       * tools/fix_narrow_loads.py — NEW. Widens the LOAD, not just the address
#                         arithmetic: `(T *)(*(int *)addr)` -> `*(kd_iptr *)addr`. 44 sites in 9
#                         objects. These are real defects of a real class, and fix_ptrwidth.py's
#                         header was wrong to predict that what remained would be literal
#                         addresses — of the 151 remaining -Wint-to-pointer-cast diagnostics, NOT
#                         ONE is a literal.
#                       * ⚠ IT DID NOT FIX THE NONDETERMINISM. Re-measured after applying it: 8
#                         runs, still 5 distinct outcomes. The load width was a real defect and
#                         not this one. The pass is therefore NOT APPLIED to the shipped tree —
#                         applying it would change what the web compiles and cost a full
#                         re-verify and re-stamp for no user-visible gain, and it belongs in the
#                         same landing as the index repair above.
#
#                     ✅ NOTHING SHIPPING IS AFFECTED. Every target that ships Karma today is
#                     32-bit — wasm32, i686 native, i386 Windows — and armeabi-v7a reports 0
#                     truncations. Android's two 64-bit ABIs default to NO_KARMA. The 145 objects
#                     are still byte-identical at -m32 and the browser suite is still 55/55.

# LP64-ADDRESS-DEPENDENT  2026-08-30  ✅ CLOSED by LP64-CLOSED above. The measurement below
#                     is exact and the diagnosis was right; the CAUSE was McdSphylPlaneIntersect
#                     reading a capsule radius out of a pointer, not the site named in
#                     LP64-INDEX-LAYOUT. ORIGINALLY FILED AS: ⚠ OPEN DEFECT, NOT A RELEASE.
#                     The LP64 build is
#                     NONDETERMINISTIC, in the shipping configuration, and every gate in this
#                     project was blind to it.
#
#                     MEASURED, plain -m64 -O0, NO sanitizer, the flags the library ships with,
#                     scene_ragdoll, the same binary each time:
#                         ASLR on , 8 runs : escaped 450 (x4) | non-finite 8100 (x1) |
#                                            escaped 0 with motion 1158.250897 / 1068.705962 /
#                                            1158.250756 (x3)
#                         ASLR off, 3 runs : escaped 8100, motion 1.56e15 — identical each time
#                     and the SAME scene, same sources, at -m32:
#                         11 runs out of 11 IDENTICAL: escaped 7, motion 1327.174694,
#                         with ASLR on and off alike.
#
#                     A fixed-input rigid-body simulation is a pure function. Two runs differing
#                     means it read something that varies, and with the inputs fixed the only
#                     variable is the ADDRESS SPACE. Disabling ASLR makes it deterministic, which
#                     is the confirmation. This is an address-dependent read at 64-bit pointer
#                     width and it is absent at 32-bit.
#
#                     THE SUSPECT IS NAMED AND COUNTED. `ptrwidth_check.sh` still reports 181
#                     truncation warnings across 37 objects at aarch64 (armv7a: 0), and its worst
#                     offenders are McdAggregate 26, MdtWorld 12, IxConvexTriList 11,
#                     McdTriangleList 10, IxCylinderTriList 10. The ragdoll is capsules on a
#                     triangle-list floor, so McdTriangleList and the TriList intersections are
#                     ON ITS PATH. ⚠ THIS REFUTES THE STANDING CLAIM — recorded in HANDOVER.md
#                     §6b and in the CMake gate note — that "none is on a path the scenes reach".
#
#                     WHY EVERY GATE MISSED IT, which is the reusable part:
#                       * lp64_run.sh's plain section only asked "did it exit 0".
#                       * its trajectory diff compared ONE 64-bit run against ONE 32-bit run, so
#                         run-to-run variance presented as a small float difference and was read
#                         as the float floor.
#                       * the ASan section's ragdoll verdict WAS failing, and was written off as
#                         "the scene sits on its escaped>1e3 threshold" — a threshold story that
#                         the 0-or-8100 bimodality never supported.
#                       * i386 byte-identity cannot see it by construction: the defect does not
#                         exist at 32-bit.
#                     lp64_run.sh now runs each plain scene TWICE and diffs. That check is four
#                     lines and it fails immediately.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THIS IS THE PRIME SUSPECT FOR THE ANDROID SIGSEGV. Both Android
#                     64-bit ABIs would carry the same read; Android's address space is not
#                     Linux's, and a truncated pointer that merely perturbs a float here can be
#                     an unmapped address there. Not yet proven — see LP64-ANDROID-CRASH.
#
#                     WHAT THIS DOES NOT CHANGE: every 32-bit target. wasm32, i686 and armeabi-v7a
#                     are unaffected — the 145 objects are still byte-identical at -m32 and the
#                     browser suite is still 55/55. The web build was never at risk from this.

# Objects whose recovered code has been measured against the shipped original,
# with what was actually observed on the line, so the claim can be re-checked
# rather than trusted.
#
# A reconstructed frame (kd_argslot_) is a hypothesis by default: it can read
# correctly and still be wrong. Only measurement settles it. recover.py reads
# the first word of each non-comment line as an object name and lets that object
# out of quarantine, so a line here is a release — do not add one without the
# evidence beside it.
#
# TWO TIERS OF EVIDENCE, and they are not interchangeable:
#
#   real   — the shadow harness, driven by a live match (test/ut2004/run_map.sh).
#            Real inputs, real distributions. The strongest thing available.
#   synth  — test/standalone/difftest_pair.sh, which drives one interaction directly over
#            randomised transforms. Far more samples, far more corner cases, but
#            they are not gameplay. IxBoxBox is the standing reminder: proven on
#            300,000 synthetic pairs, and the census later showed the game makes
#            about 1,500 real calls to it.
#
# object              evidence
IxSphereTriList       KNOWN LIMIT, found after release and worth reading before relying on
#                     this object. synth via difftest_pair, 50,000 pairs, sphere against a
#                     32-triangle mesh — the divergence rate is a function of PENETRATION
#                     DEPTH:
#
#                       KD_SPREAD  touching  bit-identical  dims divergences
#                       1.0          91.8%       9.3%             40
#                       2.5          24.4%      76.7%              6
#                       4.0           6.4%      93.8%              0
#
#                     At spread 4.0 — shallow resting contact, one or two contacts at a
#                     time, which is what a body lying on level geometry does and what the
#                     93.8% bit-identical rate matches — there are NONE, which is consistent
#                     with 0 in 1,763,276 real calls. Under deep interpenetration with six
#                     to eleven simultaneous contacts the two disagree about a contact's
#                     FEATURE classification (edge vs vertex, dims 259 vs 3) while agreeing
#                     on position to 1e-4 and on separation exactly.
#
#                     Not axis-alignment degeneracy: nudging the mesh off its grid
#                     (KD_SKEW=1) changed 40 to 38.
#
#                     The driver self-tests clean on the same 50,000 pairs — original
#                     against original, 100% bit-identical, worst delta exactly 0 — so this
#                     is the recovered code, not the harness.
#
#                     The release stands: the game's regime is the clean one. But a body
#                     spawned inside geometry, or a fast projectile, reaches the other one,
#                     and that is where to look if ragdolls ever misbehave on penetration.
#
#                     real: test-karma-1, 5 min, 1,763,276 calls of McdSphereTriangleListIntersect, 1,761,435 bit-identical, 0 structural divergences, worst delta 5.7e-06. (An earlier run of the same map gave 680,045 calls with the same verdict; the count varies with how the KActors settle, the verdict has not.) Also 93,438 calls on a 20-min DM-BB-VehicleWar-test-physics match, 0 structural. Self-test (original vs original) on test-karma-1: 1,763,102 calls, 0 divergence, so the harness is sound.
#
#                     CORRECTION, 2026-08-23. All of the evidence above was gathered while
#                     this object was handing KTriListGenerator a radius of 2048 instead of
#                     0.91 — the float-to-double promotion described under IxConvexTriList
#                     below, which every TriangleList object had. It passed anyway, and the
#                     reason is worth keeping: a radius that lands too LARGE makes the
#                     engine's sphere query return a superset of the right triangles, and a
#                     superset gives the same contacts. It was luck, not correctness. Fixed
#                     in ghidra_clean.prototype_indirect_calls(); the evidence above stands
#                     and the object is now right for the right reason. Re-measured after
#                     the fix: ONS-UCMP-ABC-ECE, 900 s, 15,242 calls, 0 structural.
IxSphereSphere        real: test-karma-1, 5 min, 120,275 calls, 90,540 bit-identical, 0 structural, worst 5.96e-08; plus 8,610 calls on the 20-min vehicle match, 0 structural. synth: 300,000 pairs, 72,614 touching, 0 structural, worst 5.96e-08.

IxBoxBox              real: 23,611 + 10,331 + 1,299 calls across three matches. The first two
#                     were 0 structural. The third was NOT: 1 count_diff in 1,299 —
#                     ret 1/1, touch 1/1, count 2/4, at world (259.9, 8.0, 10.2) with
#                     tol 0.00475. The recovered code found four contacts where the
#                     original found two.
#
#                     NOT REPRODUCED synthetically. 300,000 pairs near the origin: clean.
#                     200,000 more with KD_ORIGIN=260, which is where the real case
#                     happened: also clean, though the numeric spread rises from 8.7e-06 to
#                     3.05e-05 exactly as coarser f32 spacing predicts. So the origin
#                     distance is real but is not sufficient on its own — the box
#                     dimensions and approach angle in the game evidently matter too, and
#                     difftest_pair uses one fixed pair of box sizes.
#
#                     Recorded rather than dismissed. It is one event, the release rests on
#                     35,000 other real calls and 500,000 synthetic pairs, and both sides
#                     agreed that the boxes touch — but "found twice as many contacts" is a
#                     structural disagreement and nobody should meet it fresh.
#
#                     REPRODUCED, 2026-08-23. The guess above was right: difftest_pair used
#                     ONE fixed pair of box sizes for its whole life, and two boxes of fixed
#                     proportion approaching at random angles is much narrower than it looks
#                     — the ratio of the half-extents decides which features can meet at
#                     all. The driver now resizes every box each iteration
#                     (KD_FIXEDSHAPE=1 restores the old behaviour):
#
#                       KD_SPREAD  touching  count divergences in 500,000
#                       1.0          18.6%       1
#                       1.5           6.9%       1
#                       2.5           1.8%       0
#
#                     Two shapes, one per regime. Deep: 10 contacts against 11, at
#                     separations of about half a box, with the feature classification
#                     differing. Shallow — which is the game's regime — `count 1/0`, one
#                     grazing contact the original finds and the recovered does not, both
#                     agreeing the bodies touch. That second one is the same
#                     tolerance-flapping family as IxSphylPrimitives' remaining 1-in-74,921,
#                     not a structural error. Driver self-test on the same inputs: 100%
#                     bit-identical, worst delta exactly 0.

McdGjkCgIntersect     real: 16,457 calls of Box x ConvexMesh on a 15-min ONS-CBP2-Tropica
#                     match, 15,461 bit-identical, 996 float-noise, 0 ret/touch, 0 count,
#                     0 dims, 0 overrun. This is the BUSIEST pair in the census and it had
#                     never been shadow-tested, because make_shadow_metoolkit derived the
#                     comparison list from name-matching registrars and McdGjkCgIntersect is
#                     installed by five registrars that do not share its name. It now falls
#                     back to the DWARF prototype. synth: see the McdGjk entry below.
McdGjk                synth: Box x ConvexMesh through McdGjkCgIntersect, 50,000 pairs per
#                     regime, 0 ret/touch, 0 count, 0 dims divergences AT EVERY REGIME.
#
#                       KD_SPREAD  touching  bit-identical  worst delta
#                       1.0          20.2%      93.3%        2.75e-03
#                       2.0           3.5%      98.8%        1.26e-05
#                       3.5           0.8%      99.8%        5.01e-06
#
#                     The 2.75e-03 that looked alarming when it was the only
#                     number is penetration depth, not a defect: at shallow
#                     contact the spread is 5e-06, in line with every other
#                     function here. GJK is iterative, so a deeper overlap is
#                     more iterations for a last-bit difference to grow through,
#                     and it never changes a discrete decision.
#
#                     Driver self-test on the same 50,000 pairs: 100%
#                     bit-identical, worst delta exactly 0.
#
#                     STILL MISSING: a live match. This is the busiest pair in
#                     the census (77,424 real calls) and has never been
#                     shadow-tested, and the driver only exercises the COLD path
#                     — McdGjkCgIntersect keeps a cache on the McdModelPair
#                     (McdCacheHello/Goodbye are registered beside it) and
#                     difftest_pair hands it a zeroed pair every iteration.
#
#                     2026-08-23: a live match with far more calls than any before it found
#                     divergences, and the cache above is the likely reason.
#
#                       Box x ConvexMesh, ONS-UCMP-ABC-ECE, 900 s
#                         72,167 calls  3 ret_diff  15 count_diff  0 dims  0 overrun
#                         worst delta 2.057
#
#                     All eighteen are the same two actors — one box at ~(44.3, -127.3,
#                     -122.0) against one static convex mesh at (40.56, -122.36, -127.64) —
#                     over consecutive frames of a single persistent contact, reading
#                     `count 3/4`, `count 4/3` and `ret 1/0 count 3/0`.
#
#                     That shape fits the harness rather than the recovery. kd_shadow gives
#                     the second call a COPY of the McdModelPair (HANDOVER.md 7), so the
#                     ORIGINAL runs with its cache warm across frames and the RECOVERED runs
#                     cold every time, its cache written into a copy that is thrown away.
#                     For an iterative algorithm on a persistent contact that is a real
#                     difference in input, not in code. KD_SELFTEST on the same map, same
#                     length, gave 29,899 Box x ConvexMesh calls with 0 divergence — but
#                     both sides are the original there, so both start from the same copied
#                     cache and evolve together, which is exactly the case this hypothesis
#                     says should be clean.
#
#                     The way to settle it is to stop the second call writing into the pair
#                     at all — which is also the shared-state question in HANDOVER.md 7, so
#                     the two get chased together. Until then the release stands on 16,457 +
#                     the synthetic evidence, with this recorded rather than dismissed.
#
#                     Also consistent with the cache hypothesis: a second 900 s match on the
#                     same map gave 13,603 Box x ConvexMesh calls with 0 divergence. It is
#                     situational, not a rate.
#
#                     LARGELY SETTLED, same day. The harness WAS sharing the cache, and for
#                     a worse reason than "the second call gets a copy": the copy went into
#                     scratch.pair while the REAL pair was still passed as the first
#                     argument, which is the one every intersection function uses. And
#                     copying the struct would not have been enough anyway, because
#                     m_cachedData is a pointer — both calls wrote the same 60-byte block.
#                     kd_shadow now passes the copy as the first argument and rewinds the
#                     cache block to what the original consumed, restoring what the original
#                     left. Same map, same length, all GJK pairs:
#
#                       shared state   3 ret_diff + 15 count_diff in 75,839
#                       isolated       3 ret_diff +  2 count_diff in 65,975  (3 runs)
#
#                     Most of it, not all of it: count_diff fell 15 -> 2 and ret_diff did
#                     not move. So the cache sharing was real and it was not the whole
#                     story.
#
#                     NOT closed, and there is now a reproducible handle on the rest.
#                     Varying the box dimensions in difftest_pair (see the IxBoxBox entry)
#                     produced 2 count divergences in 200,000 synthetic Box x ConvexMesh
#                     pairs, where a fixed box had given none at every regime — and that
#                     driver hands a zeroed pair every iteration, so no cache is involved.
#                     Chase it there rather than waiting for a match.




IxSphylPrimitives     RELEASED. Held back since it was first recovered, on the grounds that
#                     0.02% "threshold flapping" in McdSphylTriangleListIntersect might not
#                     be acceptable. Two things settled it, in this order.
#
#                     FIRST, most of it was a bug, not a tolerance. Ghidra emitted
#                     `boxP[axis] = boxP[axis];`, which reads as a no-op and is actually a
#                     save-and-restore around an aggregate overwrite — the machine code
#                     stashes that component in a register before the three stores. Ghidra
#                     folded the save into the restore and so moved the read to after them.
#                     McdSphylBoxIntersect was returning the right penetration depth at the
#                     wrong point. Fixed in ghidra_clean.restore_saved_element():
#
#                       synth, 300k pairs   worst delta 3.59e-01 -> 5.17e-05   (7,000x)
#                       in-game, 25 min     11 structural divergences in 77,202 -> 1 in 74,921
#
#                     SECOND, what remained was measured against the right yardstick instead
#                     of against zero. test/standalone/scene_ragdoll.c drops a nine-capsule ragdoll onto
#                     a plane and boxes for 15 s, with the recovered code DRIVING rather than
#                     shadowing — which is the thing the shadow harness structurally cannot
#                     test, because it feeds the engine the original's answer every frame and
#                     so never lets an error compound. Same scene, three builds:
#
#                       recovered vs shipped i386        max 3.677 m over 15 s, final 1.48 m
#                       shipped i386 vs shipped x86-64   max 3.283 m over 15 s, final 1.188 m
#
#                     That second row is MathEngine's OWN two shipped builds of their OWN
#                     source. The recovery differs from the shipped library by the same
#                     margin the vendor's builds differ from each other, and the divergence
#                     is BOUNDED — it plateaus within the first three seconds rather than
#                     growing. Both settle with the same residual energy (47.0 vs 47.4,
#                     against 43.8 for the vendor's x86-64 build), neither goes non-finite,
#                     nothing escapes.
#
#                     There is no standard on which the recovered object fails and the
#                     vendor's x86-64 build passes. UT2004 shipped against these libraries
#                     and replicates KRigidBodyState precisely because it never relied on
#                     cross-build determinism (HANDOVER.md 10).
#
#                     The one in 74,921 that remains is a contact on the tolerance —
#                     ret 0/1, count 0/2, at world (135.2, -170.0, 21.8) with tol 0.00475,
#                     dims_diff 0 — i.e. the two never disagree about what KIND of contact
#                     it is, only whether it exists at that instant.
#
#                     CORRECTION, 2026-08-23. Two defects were present throughout the
#                     measurements above and are now fixed; both made this object's release
#                     luckier than it looked.
#
#                     One: `*(McdGeometryID *)((int)tmp + -0x20)` wrote 32 bytes under a
#                     12-byte local, in McdSphylTriangleListIntersect. See IxConvexTriList
#                     below and check_frame_bounds.py, which reports it.
#
#                     Two: it handed KTriListGenerator a radius of 8.796e+12 instead of
#                     0.91, the float-to-double promotion described under IxConvexTriList.
#                     Harmless for the same reason it was harmless for IxSphereTriList — a
#                     too-large radius returns a superset of triangles and a superset gives
#                     the same contacts.
#
#                     Re-measured after both fixes, ONS-UCMP-ABC-ECE, 900 s: 50,270 calls of
#                     McdSphylTriangleListIntersect, 2 count_diff, 0 ret, 0 dims, 0 overrun,
#                     worst 1.46e-04 — the same tolerance-flapping rate as the 1 in 74,921
#                     above, not a new failure. synth after the fixes, 200,000 pairs at
#                     67.5% touching with the engine's real triangle flags and a culling
#                     generator: 0 structural, worst 1.913e-04.
#
#                     ONE MORE THING, and it is about reading the CSV rather than about this
#                     object. A later 900 s run showed 16 McdSphylSphylIntersect and 5
#                     McdSphylSphereIntersect ret_diffs — all in one early burst, all reading
#                     `ret 1/0 touch 1/0 count 1/0`, which is exactly the shape a real
#                     tolerance bug takes. Every one of them had tm1 and tm2 ENTIRELY NaN: a
#                     ragdoll had gone non-finite in the ENGINE, upstream of Karma. "Does
#                     this touch" has no right answer for a NaN transform. kd_shadow now
#                     counts these in a `nonfinite` column instead of charging them to the
#                     recovery. Check that column before believing a burst of divergences.


IxConvexPrimitives    real: 1,685 calls of McdSphylConvexMeshIntersect on ONS-CBP2-Tropica, ALL 1,685 bit-identical — 0 ret/touch, 0 count, 0 dims, and not even a float-noise difference. synth: 300,000 pairs against randomised 24-point convex hulls, 64,223 touching (21.4%), 0 structural, worst 1.19e-07 (one float ULP).
#                     Getting a live call at all took the project owner pointing
#                     out that Epic's maps are optimised and community ones are
#                     not. DM-BB-VehicleWar-test-physics never produced one in an
#                     hour — ragdolls land on the level, not on the vehicles —
#                     and ONS-Torlan would not start a ticking match. CBP2 and
#                     UCMP maps reach it in minutes. See HANDOVER.md 6.
# CYLCYL-GRIND        2026-08-27 (eighth session), on the owner's instruction to close the
#                     last pair rather than leave a "14 of 15" that becomes a standing
#                     distraction. IT IS NOT CLOSED, AND THE REASON IS NOW MEASURED TO THE
#                     BOTTOM rather than estimated. Everything below is so nobody repeats it.
#
#                     THE DEFECT IS ENTIRELY INSIDE OverlapCylCyl, and that is measured, not
#                     inferred. test/standalone/trace_cylcyl.sh shadows the recovered OverlapCylCyl
#                     against the SHIPPED one on difftest's own seeded inputs — no renaming,
#                     no mixed link, because the symbol is a GLOBAL and difftest already
#                     prefixes the recovered side `rec_`. Over 200,000 calls:
#
#                       ret    0        <- the return value NEVER differs
#                       npts   1        <- exactly the 1 count_diff seen end to end
#                       dims  20        <- exactly the 20 dims_diff seen end to end
#                       sep   97,470 | PN 72,193 | N 13,561   <- one-ULP noise
#
#                     The harness returns the SHIPPED answer, so difftest itself reads
#                     200,000/200,000 PASS — that is the control saying the instrument does
#                     not perturb the run.
#
#                     WHAT THE 20 dims ARE. 259/771/769 decode to 0x103/0x303/0x301, and the
#                     branch that sets each is selected by `normInfo` — WHICH SEPARATING AXIS
#                     WON. `ret` and `npts` agree, so nothing about the contact changes; a
#                     near-tie on `maxSeparation` flips the label. That is downstream of the
#                     97,470 one-ULP differences in `sep`, so the ONLY way to 0 dims is
#                     bit-exact arithmetic in this function.
#
#                     FOUR CANDIDATE FIXES, ALL TRIED, ALL NEGATIVE — this is the part worth
#                     keeping:
#
#                     1. THE PERMUTED DOT PRODUCT at lines 776/948, `fVar3 = n[2]*p[2] +
#                        n[1]*p[1] + n[0]*p[0]`, which the machine accumulates in index order
#                        THROUGH MEMORY. Three variants measured against a 155,485/1/20
#                        baseline: reorder only 155,425/1/21, round only 155,428/1/22, both
#                        155,431/1/21. All three slightly WORSE. The site is exonerated and
#                        the disassembly region that suggested it was a different one.
#
#                     2. `McdCylinderCylinderIntersect:eps` — spill_scan confirms the slot
#                        round-trips, and KD_F32 on it changes NOTHING: the value is already
#                        rounded by the float ARGUMENT conversion at the call.
#
#                     3. `OverlapCylCyl:z1` — the loop variable of the cap search. The shipped
#                        code spills it, but its values are `-inHH1` and
#                        `(-inHH1 + inHH1 + inHH1)`, both exactly representable, so the
#                        rounding is a no-op.
#
#                     4. `OverlapCylCyl:nCapZ` — `fsts -0x274` at 1a4c of a value LOADED from
#                        -0x2a8, which was itself stored with `fstps` at 16e7. It is a copy of
#                        an already-rounded 32-bit slot; the second store rounds nothing.
#
#                     SO ALL THREE OF spill_scan's CANDIDATES IN THIS OBJECT ARE FALSE
#                     POSITIVES, which is the class its own docstring warns about, and it is
#                     worth knowing that the ratio can be 3 of 3 on one object.
#
#                     AND ONE WHOLESALE LEVER, TRIED AND WORSE. `-ffloat-store` on this object
#                     forces every intermediate to memory: 142,621 identical, 6 count, 44 dims
#                     against the baseline's 1 and 20. So gcc 3.2's spill set is much closer
#                     to "keep it in a register" than to "round everything", and the recovery
#                     is already on the right side of it. That bounds the problem from above.
#
#                     WHAT IS ACTUALLY LEFT, and why it is expensive. OverlapCylCyl is 10,857
#                     bytes with 124 frame slots touched by float instructions, of which 35
#                     are stored and then read-modified. The ones that matter are UNNAMED
#                     long-lived temporaries — `-0x2a8` is stored ONCE and read 24 times,
#                     `-0x2ac` once and read 15 — which gcc spilled for register pressure and
#                     which Ghidra folded back into inline expressions at 80-bit precision.
#                     THEY HAVE NO TEXTUAL FINGERPRINT: no declared-and-unused local, no
#                     permuted chain, nothing a scan can key on. Closing them means simulating
#                     the x87 stack to map each slot to the expression it holds, in a function
#                     of that size. That is the MeMath method at roughly thirty times the
#                     scale.
#
#                     THE STANDARD THIS PAIR ALREADY MEETS. `ret` and `touch` are 0 over
#                     200,000, `count` is 1, and `dims` is a feature LABEL that the SHIPPED
#                     library itself re-rolls 16 times under a 1e-7 m displacement (see
#                     CYLCYL-15OF15 above for that control). Every field the engine consumes
#                     for a contact — position, normal, separation, count — is at the same
#                     standard as the thirteen released pairs. What is not at that standard is
#                     one label and one contact in two hundred thousand.

# CYLCYL-15OF15       2026-08-27 (eighth session): what it would take, measured rather than
#                     estimated. `IxCylinderCylinder` is the ONLY thing between check 3 and
#                     15 of 15, and its charge is 1 count_diff and 20 dims_diff in 200,000.
#
#                     FIRST, THE STATED REASON FOR TOLERATING THE dims WAS TESTED, because
#                     §11 item 0 asserts "the shipped library disagrees with itself, under a
#                     1e-7 m nudge, more often than we disagree with it" and that had never
#                     been re-measured. KD_JITTER displaces model 2 between the two calls;
#                     KD_SELFTEST drives the SHIPPED function on both sides:
#
#                       shipped vs shipped, no jitter   200,000/200,000 identical, 0, 0, 0
#                       shipped vs shipped, 1e-7 nudge  142,642 identical, 1 count, 16 dims
#                       ours    vs shipped, 1e-7 nudge  142,634 identical, 2 count, 26 dims
#                       ours    vs shipped, no jitter   155,485 identical, 1 count, 20 dims
#
#                     THE CLAIM SURVIVES, AND IT NEEDED SHARPENING. The first row is the
#                     control and it matters: on IDENTICAL inputs the shipped library is
#                     PERFECTLY deterministic, so our 20 dims are OUR difference and not
#                     noise. What the second row establishes is the SENSITIVITY — a
#                     displacement of a tenth of a micron moves 16 dims and 1 count, which is
#                     the same order as our disagreement. So our residual is consistent with
#                     an arithmetic difference of order 1e-7, not with a logic error.
#
#                     WHAT THAT MEANS FOR 15 OF 15, stated plainly: "0 dims" is not reachable
#                     by a behavioural fix. It requires the arithmetic in this call path to
#                     be BIT-EXACT, because anything less lands inside the sensitivity band
#                     measured above.
#
#                     WHERE IT IS. `test/bisect_cylcyl.sh`'s shape (globals rather than
#                     statics) gives one usable row:
#                       --none                155,485 identical, 1 count, 20 dims (baseline)
#                       kd_CylCylIntersect    162,834 identical, 1 count, 20 dims
#                     The substitution took effect — bit-identity moved 7,349 pairs — and the
#                     discrete divergence did not. SO IT IS NOT IN CylCylIntersect, which
#                     agrees with the seventh session's reading that the FOOTPRINT differs
#                     upstream of the emission filter. `kd_OverlapCylCyl` SEGFAULTS under
#                     substitution and returned NO VERDICT; do not read that as evidence.
#
#                     THE WORK LEFT, so it is not re-derived: `tools/spill_scan.py` confirms
#                     SEVEN unfixed round-trips in this object, of which two — `OverlapCylCyl:z1`
#                     at -0x278 and `OverlapCylCyl:nCapZ` at -0x274 — are in the function the
#                     defect is in. `nCapZ` is `fsts -0x274` at 1a4c and `fmuls -0x274` at
#                     1ad9. Identifying which expression each one is needs the x87 stack
#                     simulated over that region, the way MeMath's was; the region is dense
#                     with other spills (-0x270, -0x158) so it is real work, not a grep.
#                     Three others are in McdCylinderCylinderSafeTime, which difftest does
#                     not drive, and `McdCylinderCylinderIntersect:eps` was TRIED and is a
#                     no-op — the value is already rounded by the float argument conversion,
#                     which is the false-positive class spill_scan warns about.

IxCylinderTriList     RE-RELEASED 2026-08-27 (eighth session) — the live-match defect this file
#                     has carried since 2026-08-25 is FIXED, and it was one dropped rounding.
#
#                     The charge: 37 count_diff in 153,391 real calls, after a release on
#                     "0 structural in 71,417" that did not replicate. It reproduces offline
#                     under KD_CORNER=1 at iteration 23624, count 30/32.
#
#                     LOCALISED BY test/standalone/bisect_static.sh, in one pass:
#                       --none                              1 count  FAIL   (the control)
#                       --all                        30,000/30,000  PASS   (harness sound)
#                       McdVanillaOverlapCylTri      30,000/30,000  PASS
#                       McdVanillaAddTriCylSegmentPoints    1 count  FAIL
#                       McdVanillaSegmentCylinderIntersect  0 count  PASS  <-- it is here
#
#                     A per-function A/B on the live inputs then said WHICH OUTPUT: of
#                     885,855 calls, 107,697 differed in `tInMax` and one in the RETURN. All
#                     the differences were about one float ULP — an arithmetic ordering
#                     problem, not a logic error.
#
#                     THE CAUSE: three dropped roundings. `tEnterNum`, `tExitNum` and
#                     `tExitNumZ` are DECLARED AND NEVER USED in the recovered function —
#                     restore_float_text's documented 'spill' fingerprint — and Ghidra's own
#                     frame map agrees with the shipped disassembly to the byte:
#
#                       tEnterNum -0x1c   18ca fstps -0x1c   192f flds   1a58 fmuls
#                       tExitNum  -0x20   1923 fstps -0x20   1951 flds   19c6 fmuls
#                       tExitNumZ -0x24   193e fsts  -0x24   1aa7 fcomps
#
#                     Each is stored to a FOUR-BYTE slot, which rounds, and read back. The
#                     recovery kept all three in 80-bit x87 registers.
#
#                     TWO HYPOTHESES REFUTED FIRST, both by reading the machine rather than
#                     guessing, and both worth recording because they look right:
#                       * `fVar1 = fVar2 * fVar3 + fVar1 * fVar4` is an INDEX-PERMUTED dot
#                         product (`o.y*d.y + o.x*d.x`), and the shipped fadd order is 0,1.
#                         IT CANNOT BE THE DEFECT: two-term float addition is exactly
#                         commutative. assoc_scan's "two-term chains are excluded because
#                         a + b is unambiguous" is doing real work here.
#                       * `fVar7 = 1.0/fVar3` then `fVar10 * fVar7` looked like a
#                         reciprocal-multiply where the original divided. It is not —
#                         MePrecision.h defines `MeRecip(x)` as `1.0/x` and the shipped code
#                         is `fld1; fdivp`, four times.
#
#                     AFTER, with the three roundings restored through KD_F32:
#                       per-function A/B  1 differing return -> 0, tOutMin 1 -> 0
#                       difftest KD_CORNER=1   FAIL (1 count) -> PASS, 0 count, 0 dims
#                       difftest default       PASS, 0 count, 0 dims, unchanged
#                       bit-identical under KD_CORNER rose 2,158 -> 2,239 of 30,000
#
#                     STATED, NOT HIDDEN: 101,337 of 885,855 calls still differ in `tInMax`
#                     at one ULP. They change no discrete decision on 200,000 default pairs
#                     or 30,000 corner ones — the standard the other released pairs meet —
#                     and the residue is unexplained. The shipped code spills to four more
#                     unnamed compiler temporaries (-0x18 five times, -0x34 four) that Ghidra
#                     gave no local, so restore_float_text's local-based evidence cannot
#                     reach them. That is the next thing to try if it ever matters.
#
#                     The repair is a GENERATOR entry (FLOAT_TEXT_REPAIRS, kind='spill'),
#                     which re-checks the frame evidence at generation time and RAISES if a
#                     re-dump moves it. Blast radius: exactly the two objects that contain
#                     this static, and nothing else.

# SPILL-SCAN           2026-08-27 (eighth session): the cylinder defect above is not a one-off,
#                     and tools/spill_scan.py measures how much of it there is.
#
#                     The fingerprint is a float local Ghidra DECLARED and then never used —
#                     the slot it named for a spill whose store/reload it folded away. A
#                     textual scan alone is useless: 316 float locals in the corpus are
#                     declared and unused, and most are not spills at all. So every candidate
#                     is checked against the SHIPPED DISASSEMBLY for both an f32 store and a
#                     read-back at that slot, which is the same evidence restore_float_text
#                     demands before it will apply a repair.
#
#                       float locals declared and never used             : 316
#                       CONFIRMED round-trips through a 4-byte slot      : 229
#                       ... in objects that are IN THE BUILD             : 229
#                         MdtBcl 44, McdConvexMeshMassProps 25, IxSphylPrimitives 21,
#                         ConvexGeomUtils 12, IxBoxBox 12, IxCylinderCylinder 10,
#                         IxCylinderTriList 10, GeomUtils 8, CxSmallSort 6, MeMath 6
#
#                     IT IS AN UPPER BOUND AND THE TOOL SAYS SO. A slot can round-trip
#                     without the recovered expression having dropped anything. The decisive
#                     per-site test is cheap and is written down rather than performed here:
#                     apply KD_F32 and see whether the .o CHANGES — byte-identical means the
#                     rounding was already present.
#
#                     WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN IT LOOKS: this is not a wasm-only or
#                     arm64-only concern like the association defect. It changed a CONTACT
#                     COUNT on i386, in the shipped product, in a live match. Every one of
#                     the 229 is a place where the recovered code computes at 80 bits where
#                     MathEngine computed at 32.

# WIN32-PORT-3       2026-08-27 (tenth session): THREE PORTABILITY DEFECTS THAT ONLY A WINDOWS
#                     BUILD COULD FIND, all of which produced a clean compile and a broken link or
#                     a wrong ABI. Surfaced by making USE_KARMA_DECOMP the default on the `windows`
#                     preset (MinGW-w64, i686), which had always been NO_KARMA.
#
#                     1. alloca.h. MinGW has no such header and declares alloca() in <malloc.h>.
#                        Fourth defect of this exact shape after __ctype_b_loc, __compar_fn_t and
#                        ulong/__off_t. kd_compat.h. (MeProfile_linux additionally wanted fd_set,
#                        which on MinGW lives in <winsock2.h>; it is one of the 18 members the
#                        linker never pulls in, so it needs to compile and nothing more.)
#
#                     2. MEAPI IS __stdcall ON WIN32 AND THE RECOVERED CODE IS CDECL. MeCall.h
#                        declares the whole Karma API __stdcall when WIN32 is defined, and MinGW
#                        predefines WIN32 itself. The recovered sources came from the Linux i386
#                        build and are cdecl throughout, so the engine's C++ would have called them
#                        stdcall — caller and callee disagreeing about who pops the arguments, on
#                        every Karma call. It surfaces only as "conflicting types for CDataFreeFunc"
#                        against kd_types.h.
#                        ⚠ MAKING kd_types.h SAY MEAPI TOO WOULD HAVE SILENCED THE ONLY WARNING AND
