#                        KEPT THE BUG. Fixed by forcing the CONVENTION instead: ME_FORCE_CDECL,
#                        defined globally beside WITH_KARMA. MathEngine's Windows archives are not
#                        linkable here in any case — they are 32-bit Linux ELF.
#
#                     3. ASM LABELS DROP THE USER LABEL PREFIX. `__asm__("name")` is literal and
#                        bypasses the automatic prefixing the platform's C ABI applies. On ELF that
#                        prefix is empty; on i686 PE it is `_`, so the library exported
#                        `MdtBSJointCreate` while every caller referenced `_MdtBSJointCreate` —
#                        100+ undefined references from objects that had all compiled cleanly.
#                        KD_MANGLED now prepends __USER_LABEL_PREFIX__, the compiler's own answer.
#                        gen_vtables.py's 42 raw __asm__ labels were routed through it too.
#                        BLAST RADIUS: all 145 i386 objects BYTE-IDENTICAL, because stringifying an
#                        empty macro yields "" and the ELF spelling does not move.
#
#                     AND A FOURTH, IN THE ENGINE RATHER THAN THE RECOVERY: KSkeletal.cpp and
#                     KUtils.cpp guard wcstombs() with `#if WIN32`, but TCHAR is wide only under
#                     _UNICODE (Core.h:219) and this tree is ANSI everywhere. Linux never saw it.
#                     The original author's own comment on those lines reads "!!! FIXME: this
#                     conflicts on Linux. --ryan." — the same defect, from the other side.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE WINDOWS KARMA BUILD DOES NOT RUN, WHICH IS A REAL DEFECT AND NOT
#                     A PRE-EXISTING ONE. This entry first recorded the opposite, from a run that
#                     used `-GL4ESRENDERER` — the WRONG renderer for Windows, which has no GLES.
#                     With `-OPENGLRENDERER`, the owner's tested configuration:
#
#                       windows-nokarma   exit 124 — a full 330 s ONS-Torlan match, no fault
#                       windows (Karma)   EXCEPTION_ACCESS_VIOLATION during startup
#
#                     So Windows works and the Karma build breaks it. Corrected here rather than
#                     quietly, because the wrong-renderer run had produced a confident and wrong
#                     conclusion in both directions at once.
#
#                     WHERE, AND WHY IT IS A SYMPTOM RATHER THAN THE BUG: it faults in
#                     MeFAssetPartEnableCollision (MeFAssetPart.c:465) reading address 0x50 —
#                     which is `((MeFAsset *)0)->maxParts`. ghidra_clean.absolute_data_refs'
#                     docstring shows the SHIPPED machine code doing exactly the same thing
#                     (`mov 0x50,%ecx` at 0x775): gcc rematerialised `asset->maxParts` on the branch
#                     where it has proved `asset == NULL`, so the ORIGINAL dereferences NULL there
#                     too. Reproducing it reproduces the program. The defect is therefore UPSTREAM —
#                     `p1->asset` is NULL on Windows and is not on Linux or wasm — and the first
#                     place to look is Karma's file layer, because the only file implementation in
#                     the corpus is `MeSimpleFile_linux`.
#
#                     ⚠ THAT DOCSTRING IS ALSO NOW STALE: it says the two objects with this shape
#                     are "both held out of the build on it". MeFAssetPart is IN the build.
#
#                     WHERE IT SITS, from the crash's own stack scan:
#                       KCreateAssetDB -> MeAssetDBXMLInputRead -> Handle_KaFile_0_1
#                                      -> KaFileCreate_1_0 -> ... -> MeFAssetPartEnableCollision
#                     i.e. the `.ka` XML parse, during KInitGameKarma, before any level loads. So
#                     it reproduces on a plain menu boot and needs no map.
#
#                     ONE HYPOTHESIS TESTED AND REJECTED, recorded so it is not retried: MeOpen
#                     bakes LINUX O_* values in as literals (0x241 == O_WRONLY|O_CREAT|O_TRUNC at
#                     O_CREAT==0x40; MinGW's is 0x100) and carries no O_BINARY, because on Linux
#                     there is no text/binary distinction — so every Karma read on Windows was
#                     CRLF-translated. That is a REAL portability defect and is fixed
#                     (ghidra_clean.PORTABILITY_CONSTANTS + KD_O_BINARY, all 145 i386 objects
#                     byte-identical because the macros fold to the same constants). It is NOT this
#                     crash: the fault address does not move.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE USUAL INSTRUMENT IS NOT AVAILABLE HERE. Every localisation in this
#                     file rests on substituting MathEngine's shipped member — and those are 32-bit
#                     LINUX ELF, so on Windows there is nothing to substitute.
#
#                     ── FOUND AND FIXED: A NULL-BRANCH-ONLY LOAD, HOISTED BY A COMMA OPERATOR ──
#
#                     Not a Windows defect at all — a landmine in every build, which only Windows
#                     was unlucky enough to step on. The shipped code reads address 0x50 ONLY where
#                     it has proved `asset == 0` (`je 775; ... 775: mov 0x50,%ecx`), because gcc
#                     rematerialised `asset->maxParts` on that branch. Ghidra flattened it into a
#                     comma operator:
#
#                       (pMVar2 = p1->asset, iVar5 = _DAT_00000050, pMVar2 == 0 || ...)
#
#                     so the read happens on EVERY call where p1 != p2, live asset or not. 0x50 is
#                     below Linux's mmap_min_addr (65536), so it is a fault wherever it executes.
#
#                     WHY IT DID NOT KILL EVERYTHING: at -O2 gcc sinks the dead load back onto the
#                     null branch by itself, and the recovery's own objects are built -O2 — which is
#                     why every offline gate and every kd_build object is clean, and why the two
#                     forms are BYTE-IDENTICAL there. The ENGINE is a different matter (below).
#                     On wasm it is doubly hidden: address 0x50 is ordinary readable linear memory,
#                     so there is nothing to trap.
#
#                     ★ AND THE REASON THE ENGINE IS EXPOSED IS WORTH ITS OWN LINE: CMakeLists'
#                     optimisation block ends in an `else()` marked "Match Phase 1's -O0
#                     debug-oriented build for now", and EVERY native build lands there —
#                     RelWithDebInfo included, because the test is `STREQUAL "Debug"`. The flag line
#                     reads `... -O2 -g -DNDEBUG ... -m32 -O0 -g ...` and the last -O wins, so the
#                     whole engine AND the recovered physics compile at -O0 on Linux and Windows.
#                     That is pre-existing and not Karma's doing, but it is what turns this
#                     particular defect from dormant into fatal, and it is a standing performance
#                     question for the native targets.
#
#                     FIXED by restoring the branch: the read is now inside a conditional, which is
#                     what the machine code does. Verified in the emitted Windows code —
#                     `cmpl $0x0,-0x10(%ebp); jne ...` now precedes it. i386 objects: ZERO changed,
#                     because -O2 already produced this. Native ONS-Torlan still clean at 180 s, and
#                     the wasm binary is BYTE-IDENTICAL before and after, so neither working target
#                     moved.
#
#                     RESULT ON WINDOWS: KCreateAssetDB now completes, the level loads, and
#                     ONSOnslaughtGame starts. The build gets a great deal further and then hits a
#                     DIFFERENT fault — see below.
#
#                     ── AND THE LAST ONE: `#ifndef __thiscall` ──
#
#                     THE DEFECT WITH THE WIDEST REACH IN THIS ENTRY, and the shortest fix.
#                     kd_compat.h defined the MSVC calling-convention keywords away like this:
#
#                       #ifndef __thiscall
#                       #  define __thiscall
#                       #endif
#
#                     On Linux none of them are defined, so the define-away applies and the
#                     annotation is the no-op the docstring describes. MinGW PREDEFINES them:
#
#                       #define __thiscall __attribute__((__thiscall__))
#                       #define __fastcall __attribute__((__fastcall__))
#
#                     so `#ifndef` is FALSE, the define-away is SKIPPED, and all 140 recovered C++
#                     methods acquire MSVC's real thiscall — `this` in ECX rather than on the stack.
#                     Callers push it, so every argument after `this` arrives one word early.
#
#                     MEASURED, by instrumenting both sides of one dispatch rather than reasoning
#                     about ABIs:
#
#                       linux    caller np=0 ncp=0x7b08c378 nb=1
#                                callee np=0 ncp=0x7b08c378 nb=1     intact
#                       windows  caller np=0 ncp=0ceee900  nb=1
#                                callee np=2289596 ncp=0ceee8fc nb=0  shifted 4 bytes
#
#                     The callee reads the field its caller had 4 bytes further on, and num_bodies
#                     arrives as 0. That is what made `constraints.num_constraints_partition` read
#                     as ~1 and killed vanillaAllocateMemory at keaMemory.c:124.
#
#                     FIXED by `#undef` first and defining away unconditionally — the recovered code
#                     is decompiled from a Linux i386 build where all of these are plain cdecl, so
#                     the annotation must be inert on EVERY target, not merely where the platform
#                     happens not to claim the keyword.
#
#                     BLAST RADIUS: ZERO i386 objects change, because on Linux the two spellings do
#                     the same thing, and the wasm binary is BYTE-IDENTICAL. Which is also why
#                     nothing but a Windows build could ever have found it.
#
#                     ── RESULT: ALL THREE 32-BIT TARGETS PLAY ONSLAUGHT ──
#
#                       wasm32       ONS-Torlan + bots, 330 s in the browser, no trap
#                       native i386  ONS-Torlan 300 s x2, ONS-Adara 240 s, ONS-Ascendancy 240 s
#                       windows      ONS-Torlan + bots, 330 s under wine, -OPENGLRENDERER
#
#                     Android is the one target still without Karma, by the owner's decision to fix
#                     the LP64 struct layouts first rather than ship an APK whose physics depends on
#                     the ABI.
#
#                     ── HISTORICAL: the solver fault this section chased ──
#
#                       vanillaAllocateMemory (keaMemory.c:124), READ of address 0x00000001
#                         <- keaFunctions_Vanilla::allocateMemory
#                         <- MdtKeaAddConstraintForces <- KWorldStepSafeTime
#                         (ASVehicle::execUpdateVehicle on the stack — a vehicle physics step)
#
#                     `constraints.num_constraints_partition` is ~1, i.e. the by-value
#                     `MdtKeaConstraints` arrives wrong or arrives with garbage in it. Struct LAYOUT
#                     is ruled out and measured: sizeof is 92 with num_constraints_partition@16,
#                     Jsize@80, num_partitions@0 on BOTH toolchains. So it is not MinGW's double
#                     alignment, and the next thing to look at is how the by-value struct crosses
#                     the vtable dispatch in MdtKeaAddConstraintForces.

# ONS-CRASH-GJKPD    2026-08-27 (tenth session): McdGjkPenetrationDepth CRASHES A LIVE ONSLAUGHT
#                     MATCH, on native i386 AND on wasm32, and it is the ONLY one of the 145 that
#                     does. It is IN the build and has never been in this file — which is the
#                     "not quarantined does not mean validated" hazard, in its purest form.
#
#                     FOUND by the acceptance run the owner asked for when the recovered Karma
#                     became the default on every 32-bit target: a 5-minute ONS-Torlan match per
#                     build (decomp/test/ut2004/ons_smoke.sh). ONS is the gametype that spawns
#                     VEHICLES, which is most of what Karma exists for; the ut2004 browser suite is
#                     51/51 and is almost entirely DM, so it never touched this path.
#
#                     native i386, ONS-Torlan, Onslaught.ONSOnslaughtGame:
#                       SIGSEGV at McdGjkPenetrationDepth.c:143, in McdGjkFaceLoad
#                         McdGjkFaceLoad <- McdGjkPenetrationDepth <- McdGjkTest
#                         <- McdGjkCgIntersect2 <- McdGjkCgIntersect <- KIntersect
#                         <- KHandleCollisions <- KUpdateContacts <- KTickLevelKarma
#                     wasm32, same map and gametype:
#                       RuntimeError: memory access out of bounds
#
#                     LOCALISED TO THIS ONE OBJECT BY SUBSTITUTION, four measurements:
#                       recovered, all 145            crash at 26s / 25s / 23s (three runs)
#                       `legacy-karma` preset          300 s, no fault
#                       recovered + SHIPPED McdGjkPenetrationDepth.o   300 s, no fault
#                       restored to pure recovered     crash again
#                     So 144 of 145 objects survive a five-minute ONS match and this one does not.
#
#                     ⚠ THE FIRST SUBSTITUTION TEST WAS VACUOUS AND SAID SO ONLY BECAUSE IT WAS
#                     CHECKED. Swapping the member inside libKarmaDecomp.a and re-running
#                     `cmake --build` looked like a clean reproduction of the crash — but cmake
#                     REBUILDS the archive from its objects, so the swap was discarded and the test
#                     measured nothing. `ar t` showed `McdGjkPenetrationDepth.c.o` still there.
#                     Replacing the compiled .o and touching it works WITH the build system.
#                     A second check in the same session was vacuous too: `nm | grep kd_McdGjkFaceLoad`
#                     can never match, because KD_MANGLED's asm label REPLACES the kd_ identifier.
#
#                     ── NARROWED TO TWO FUNCTIONS, AND ONE OF THEM DIAGNOSED (same session) ──
#
#                     test/ut2004/gjk_bisect.sh asks "does taking function F from the shipped object fix
#                     it?" — and every one of the nine answered NO while the whole object answered
#                     YES. That is the signature of MORE THAN ONE defective function: swapping one
#                     leaves the other, so no single swap can pass.
#
#                     test/ut2004/gjk_bisect_complement.sh asks the complement, which has no such blind
#                     spot: keep exactly ONE function of OURS, take the rest from theirs.
#
#                       (positive control: nothing of ours)  survived      <- harness is sound
#                       McdGjkFaceLoad                       survived
#                       McdGjkFaceAdd                        survived
#                       McdGjkFaceAddPoint                   survived
#                       McdGjkFacePop                        survived
#                       McdGjkFaceCompare                    survived
#                       McdGjkFaceQueueInit                  CRASHES 27s   <- DEFECTIVE
#                       McdGjkFindNextInflatePoint           survived
#                       McdGjkResultIsReasonable             survived
#                       McdGjkPenetrationDepth               CRASHES 26s   <- DEFECTIVE
#
#                     Corroborated independently: a hybrid of the SHIPPED driver calling OUR helpers
#                     (weak symbols rather than renaming, so the calls reach ours) also crashes,
#                     which rules the driver in and is consistent with FaceQueueInit being bad.
#
#                     McdGjkFaceQueueInit's DEFECT, read off the shipped machine code rather than
#                     guessed: `MePoolxGet` returns the new face and Ghidra typed it `MeReal *`, so
#                     the recovered code writes FLOATS into fields the original writes as INTEGERS.
#                     _McdGjkFace is { MeVector3 v; MeReal v_len; int depth; int bits; int fi[4];
#                     int slant; }, i.e. v_len at 0xc, depth 0x10, bits 0x14, fi[] 0x18..0x24:
#
#                       shipped                              recovered
#                       5c3: mov   %esi,0x14(%eax)   bits    pMVar3[5] = MVar5;          (float)
#                       5f0: fstps 0xc(%ebx)         v_len   pMVar3[3] = MVar6;          (float, RIGHT)
#                       5f6: mov   %ecx,0x18(%ebx)   fi[0]   pMVar3[6] = (MeReal)q->si[0];
#                       5fc: mov   %esi,0x1c(%ebx)   fi[1]   pMVar3[7] = (MeReal)q->si[1];
#
#                     `(MeReal)q->si[0]` is an int->float CONVERSION where the original is a plain
#                     move: si[0]==1 stores 0x3F800000 into an int field instead of 1. The mask is
#                     the same defect one level earlier — `*(MeReal *)(McdGjkBinarySubset+0x3c...)`
#                     against the shipped `mov 0x4(%ebx,%eax,4),%esi`. So `f->bits` and `f->fi[]`
#                     are float bit patterns, which is precisely the garbage McdGjkFaceLoad's loop
#                     needs to walk off the end of `q->s->point[4]`.
#
#                     Note `pMVar3[4] = 0.0` (depth) and `pMVar3[10] = 1.4013e-45` (slant) are
#                     RIGHT BY ACCIDENT: 0.0f is 0x00000000 and 1.4013e-45 is 0x00000001, so those
#                     two happen to lay down the integer the original does. Only the fields whose
#                     integer value is neither 0 nor a denormal-sized constant are corrupted.
#
#                     ── FIXED, AND THE MATCH SURVIVES (same session) ──
#
#                     ghidra_clean.FLOAT_TYPED_INT_FIELDS retypes the seventeen stores: the mask
#                     gets its own integer local (MVar5 cannot serve — the shared tail reassigns it
#                     to MeVector3Normalize's float result), the table load becomes an int load, and
#                     bits/depth/fi[]/slant are written through `(int *)pMVar3`.
#
#                       native i386, ONS-Torlan   300 s x2, no fault   (was: SIGSEGV at ~25 s)
#                       native i386, ONS-Adara    240 s, no fault
#                       native i386, ONS-Ascendancy 240 s, no fault
#                       wasm32,      ONS-Torlan   330 s in the browser, no trap, 12 .ka loaded
#
#                     BLAST RADIUS: one source file; McdGjkPenetrationDepth.o is the only i386
#                     object that moves, and it is the object being repaired.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE COMPLEMENT BISECT'S SECOND ANSWER DID NOT SURVIVE CONTACT.
#                     It named McdGjkPenetrationDepth defective as well, on the strength of "our
#                     driver + their helpers crashes". Repairing FaceQueueInit ALONE takes the match
#                     to 300 s, and the diff touches only lines 223-371 — entirely inside
#                     FaceQueueInit, with the driver (line 432 on) untouched. So the driver reading
#                     as defective was an artefact of MIXING implementations, not a defect in it:
#                     the useful lesson is that a substitution harness measures a COMBINATION, and
#                     a mixed pair can fail where either whole pair succeeds. Read a positive from
#                     one as a lead, not a verdict.
#
#                     STILL OPEN: nothing on this crash. Windows is a SEPARATE fault — see
#                     WIN32-PORT-3.
#
#                     WHAT IS KNOWN ABOUT THE ORIGINAL FAULT SITE: the faulting statement writes
#                     `q->s->point[]` at a running byte offset while reading `q->point[f->fi[i]]`,
#                     so either `f->bits` (documented 0..15) or `f->fi[]` is garbage by the time
#                     FaceLoad runs — i.e. the face is corrupt BEFORE this function, and the bug may
#                     be in any of the object's eight functions rather than in FaceLoad itself.
#                     The object carries ZERO of the usual markers — no kd_argslot_, no extraout_,
#                     no in_stack_, no alloca, check_frame_bounds clean — which is exactly why
#                     every offline gate passed it.
#
#                     NO FALLBACK EXISTS OFF x86. The substitution above is only possible on i386
#                     Linux, because the shipped member is 32-bit Linux ELF; wasm32 and Windows have
#                     nothing to fall back to, so this has to be fixed rather than worked around.

McdSphere             real: test-karma-1 (ONS gametype), 600 frames at a pinned 30 fps, the whole
#                     level's Karma state diffed against a `legacy-karma` run of the SAME engine
#                     source — 14 of 15 bodies BIT-IDENTICAL for the entire run, the 15th
#                     (ONSHoverBike3, wedged against a ramp) differing by 0.005 in final Z and
#                     coming to rest in the same place. Held over 3,000 frames / 100 simulated
#                     seconds: 13 of 15 bit-identical, all 15 at identical rest positions.
#                     Localised BY SUBSTITUTION with both controls — see MCD-GEOM-FLOAT-FIELDS.
McdTriangleList       real: same run, same evidence. These two objects account for the ENTIRE
#                     behavioural defect the owner reported; taking both from the shipped library
#                     restored the reference behaviour exactly, and taking either alone did not.

# MCD-GEOM-FLOAT-FIELDS  2026-08-28 (eleventh session): A NUMERIC CONVERSION WHERE THE ORIGINAL
#                     MOVES FOUR BYTES. Seventeen sites in six geometry objects, every one of them
#                     compiling cleanly, passing all nine gates, and destroying the value it
#                     touched. This is the defect behind the owner's report that vehicles sink
#                     through terrain, boxes fall through the floor and friction "feels too high".
#
#                     THE SHAPE. UT2004's geometry types derive from `McdGeometry` by PREFIXING it,
#                     so a sphere's radius lives past the base struct and Ghidra — which has only
#                     `McdGeometry *` — addresses it as `g[1].<field-of-the-base>`. The byte offset
#                     is right; the TYPE is whatever field of a second `McdGeometry` happens to sit
#                     there. Three of those four fields are POINTERS and one, `mRefCtAndID`, is a
#                     `MeU32`. The pointer-typed ones were already repaired (KD_FBITS). The integer
#                     one was not, and C obligingly CONVERTS instead of reinterpreting:
#
#                       McdSphereGetRadius   return (MeReal)g[1].mRefCtAndID;   shipped: flds 0x10(%edx)
#                       McdSphereGetBSphere  *radius = (MeReal)g[1].…;          shipped: mov  0x10(%ecx),%edx
#                       McdGjkFatness        MVar1 = (MeReal)pMVar3[1].…;       shipped: flds 0x10(%eax)
#                       McdTriangleListSetBoundingBox
#                                            g[1].mRefCtAndID = (MeU32)(*max + *min);
#                                                                              shipped: fstps 0x10(%edx)
#
#                     A 0.91 radius comes back as 1.06e9. A bounding-box centre of 512.0 is stored
#                     as the INTEGER 512 and read back as a denormal. The world triangle list — the
#                     geometry every body in every map collides with — had its X centre and Y
#                     half-extent destroyed at construction.
#
#                     ⚠ EVERY EXISTING GATE IS BLIND TO IT, AND THE difftest IS BLIND FOR A REASON
#                     WORTH KNOWING. `difftest_pair.sh McdSphereTriangleListIntersect` reads
#                     IDENTICALLY before and after the repair — 4,837 bit-identical, 13 dims, worst
#                     2.645e-05, twice — because the driver substitutes the Ix* INTERACTION object
#                     and links the geometry objects from the shipped library. The broken accessor
#                     was never in the loop. Nothing that substitutes one object at a time can see a
#                     defect in the object it did not substitute, and "145 objects pass" is a
#                     statement about 145 separate links, not about the program.
#
#                     HOW IT WAS FOUND, and the instrument is new: test/ut2004/ktrace_run.sh records one
#                     CSV row per simulated body per frame from inside KTickLevelKarma, and
#                     test/ut2004/ktrace_subst.sh rebuilds the engine with a chosen SET of objects taken
#                     from the shipped library and scores the result against a `legacy-karma`
#                     reference (test/standalone/ktrace_score.py). Both controls first: ALL 145 from shipped
#                     scored MATCH, none scored 12 mismatches. Then the COMPLEMENT — keep one family
#                     of ours, take the rest from theirs:
#
#                       keep Mdt   MATCH        keep Ix   MATCH
#                       keep kea   MATCH        keep Me   MATCH
#                       keep Mcd   12 MISMATCH  <- and the same 12, to four significant figures
#
#                     Four bisection steps inside Mcd reached two objects, and the FORWARD direction
#                     confirmed them independently: recovered + shipped McdTriangleList = 8
#                     mismatches, + shipped McdSphere = 12, + BOTH = MATCH.
#
#                     ⚠ THIS OVERTURNED THE RANKED SUSPECTS, WHICH WERE REASONED AND WRONG. The
#                     handover's list led with MdtContactParams (23 friction references, not in this
#                     file), then McdGjkPenetrationDepth. Both are innocent: `keep Mdt` MATCHES.
#                     The reasoning was sound — friction lives in Mdt, and 25 of 27 Mdt objects have
#                     never been measured — and the answer was still in the other family. The
#                     symptom named a subsystem and the subsystem was not where the defect was.
#
#                     TWO GENERATOR BUGS, both of which had a rule that should have caught this:
#
#                     1. FLOAT_CAST matched `(float)` and Ghidra emits `(MeReal)`. `fix_float_load_
#                        of_int` was written for exactly these sites — its docstring's example is
#                        `(float)g[1].mRefCtAndID` — and `MeReal` is the spelling the geometry
#                        objects actually carry. This is the identical mistake INT_STORE records
#                        against ITSELF eight lines away ("Ghidra spells the destination with
#                        whatever integer type the SLOT has, not with `int`"): the store side was
#                        widened, the load side was not.
#                     2. `_expr_float_type` resolves ONE lvalue, and the constructors store a
#                        COMPUTED value — `(MeU32)(*max + *min)`. The rule fired on the right lines
#                        and declined because it could not type the operand. It now splits a
#                        top-level arithmetic expression and requires EVERY part to resolve, which
#                        keeps a comparison from being typed as a sum — the failure that silently
#                        changed IxCylinderCylinder once.
#
#                     ⚠ AND A THIRD, WHICH IS THE SIXTH INSTANCE IN ghidra_clean.py OF ONE PATTERN.
#                     Widening FLOAT_CAST changed NOTHING, because the rule's early-out still read
#                     `if '(float)' not in region`. A cheap guard that disagrees with the rule it
#                     guards is a rule that silently declines. It was caught only because the blast
#                     radius came back as ONE object where six were intended — the count disagreed
#                     with the intent, and that disagreement is the whole reason to check it.
#
#                     BLAST RADIUS, checked before correctness: 9 source files move, 7 change the
#                     compiled object. Six are the intended repairs. McdBox and MeFAsset change
#                     SPELLING only (`(*(MeReal *)&x)` -> `*(float *)&(x)`) and compile
#                     BYTE-IDENTICAL. The seventh is a bonus and was verified separately —
#                     McdGjkPenetrationDepth's `MVar2 = (MeReal)s->next_bit`, which feeds
#                     `_McdGjkFace.slant`, an INT field the shipped code writes with
#                     `mov %eax,0x28(%esi)`. That is a previously-unrepaired instance of
#                     ONS-CRASH-GJKPD's own class, in the DRIVER rather than in FaceQueueInit — the
#                     function that entry's complement bisect accused and then exonerated.
#
#                     NOT MEASURED INDIVIDUALLY, and the difference matters: McdSphyl, McdCylinder,
#                     McdConvexMesh and McdGjkMaximumPoint carry the same defect and are REPAIRED on
#                     the same machine-code evidence (flds / mov at the field, and no conversion
#                     instruction anywhere in the function), but test-karma-1 did not isolate them —
#                     they are not released above. Repaired is not the same as validated.

# UNICODE-GUARD-REGRESSION  2026-08-28 (eleventh session): A CORRECTION TO THIS FILE'S OWN
#                     PREVIOUS ENTRY, AND THE DEFECT WAS MINE. An earlier version of this entry was
#                     headed "NO NATIVE BUILD HAS EVER LOADED A .ka ASSET" and concluded that every
#                     native acceptance run ever recorded here had missed the ragdoll path. That is
#                     WRONG. The original code worked; the tenth session broke it; I then found the
#                     breakage and mistook it for archaeology.
#
#                     WHAT I CHANGED, and why the reasoning was wrong. WIN32-PORT-3 records a fourth
#                     defect "IN THE ENGINE RATHER THAN THE RECOVERY": KSkeletal.cpp and KUtils.cpp
#                     guarded wcstombs() with `#if WIN32`, and MinGW rejected `char*` against
#                     `const wchar_t*`. I changed the guard to `#if defined(_UNICODE)` on the stated
#                     grounds that "TCHAR is wide only under _UNICODE and this tree is ANSI
#                     everywhere". THE SECOND HALF IS FALSE. CMakeLists defines UNICODE and _UNICODE
#                     on every target EXCEPT the two that set NO_UNICODE_OS_SUPPORT — the web build
#                     and MinGW. So on Linux _UNICODE IS defined, TCHAR is a 2-byte UNICHAR, and the
#                     new guard routed Linux into wcstombs, where glibc's FOUR-byte wchar_t cannot
#                     read a 2-byte UNICHAR at all.
#
#                     ⚠ THE SYMPTOM I DIAGNOSED WAS REAL AND THE CAUSE I ASSIGNED WAS NOT. Both
#                     sites really did produce garbage; I traced the .ka one to the "nasty little
#                     hack" beside it (a TArray<TCHAR> copy taken through a char*) and rewrote that.
#                     The hack is pointless and the rewrite is an improvement, but it was NEVER the
#                     bug — the round trip is the identity whatever TCHAR is. Rewriting the innocent
#                     line made the guilty `#if` disappear from view for another hour.
#
#                     MEASURED, three ways:
#
#                       #error inside the branch     _UNICODE IS DEFINED in this TU
#                       raw / conv / copied          raw='Male2' conv='Male2' cSkelName='\u0404\xa1a\x9cG\xbfZ'
#                       pre-tenth-session sources    0 "Asset not found", 5,190 ragdoll bone rows
#
#                     That last row is what settles it: checking KSkeletal.cpp and KUtils.cpp out at
#                     bd3b80d and rebuilding gives working ragdolls. The regression is the change,
#                     not the codebase.
#
#                     ⚠ AND A COMPILE PROBE TOLD ME THE OPPOSITE FIRST. I tested for _UNICODE by
#                     scraping -D flags out of flags.make with a grep and compiling a scratch file
#                     with them; it printed "_UNICODE not defined" and I believed it for three
#                     measurements. A `#error` INSIDE the real translation unit is the only form of
#                     this question that cannot be answered by a mis-assembled command line.
#
#                     FIXED by removing the #if from all three sites rather than re-choosing it:
#                     appToAnsi IS the engine's own TCHAR->ANSI conversion, correct on every target
#                     whatever TCHAR is, so no platform predicate is needed. Verified natively —
#                     0 "Asset not found", 3,453 bone rows, and the scripted actors still MATCH.
#
#                     THE LESSON WORTH KEEPING: a portability guard is a claim about the platform,
#                     and this one was never tested against the platform it was rewritten for. The
#                     Windows build compiled, which is all the change was checked against, and the
#                     property it asserted about Linux was simply assumed.

# ASSOC-SURVEY       2026-08-28 (eleventh session): THE ASSOCIATION QUESTION NOW HAS AN ORACLE,
#                     AND THE ANSWER IS MOSTLY "WE ARE ALREADY RIGHT". Record in
#                     decomp/assoc-survey.txt, generated by tools/assoc_survey.py.
#
#                     ASSOC-ON-I386 and assoc_scan.py's own docstring both say the association half
#                     "decides nothing" — Ghidra decompiles the i386 build, x87's 80-bit registers
#                     make a+(b+c) and (a+b)+c exactly equal, so the binary carries no evidence.
#                     The owner supplied the missing evidence: UT2004 v3369's win_amd64_single
#                     *.lib archives are real x86-64 COFF, NOT stripped, 1,089 symbols. MSVC x64 has
#                     no x87, so every add is an explicit `addss` in a fixed order.
#
#                     ★ AND THE FIRST THING IT SETTLED WAS THAT THE OBVIOUS FIX IS WRONG. Having
#                     read 2,1,0 out of MeVector3Dot and MeMatrix4TMInverseTransform, a blanket
#                     "reorder every chain to 2,1,0" looked certain. It is wrong three ways:
#
#                       MeMatrix4TMInverseRotate  row 0 is 2,1,0; rows 1 and 2 are 2,0,1 — the
#                                                 order differs per ROW of one function
#                       McdGjkMaximumPoint        our two live sites are 2,0,1 and already correct
#                       MeQuaternionFromTM        i386 sums 1,0,2, amd64 sums 0,1,2 — the two
#                                                 SHIPPED builds disagree, so there is no single
#                                                 "original order" to match
#
#                     ⚠ AND COMPARING THE SEQUENCE IS THE WRONG COMPARISON. IEEE addition is
#                     COMMUTATIVE; only associativity fails. The tool's first version compared
#                     left-to-right order and called its very first site a mismatch — ours 2,1,0
#                     against amd64's 1,2,0, which is the same computation. For a three-term chain
#                     the only question is WHICH TWO ARE ADDED FIRST: {0,1}, {0,2} or {1,2}.
#
#                     WHAT THE SURVEY FOUND, over the 25 sites in functions a real match enters
#                     (575 candidates -> 66 index-permuted -> 25 live):
#
#                       4  readable mechanically:  2 SAME grouping, 2 DIFFERENT
#                       21 need a hand audit — the register tracker cannot follow them
#
#                     The two differing are both IxSphereTriList:McdVanillaOverlapSphereTri, ours
#                     {0,2} against amd64 {1,2}. NOT ACTED ON, for two reasons: that object is
#                     RELEASED above on real-match evidence, and the criterion requires checking
#                     i386 too — where our recovery came from and where it presumably agrees.
#
#                     ⚠ THERE IS NO ACCEPTANCE TEST FOR THIS CLASS RIGHT NOW, WHICH IS THE REAL
#                     REASON NOTHING WAS CHANGED. wasm reproduces i386 to THREE DECIMAL PLACES in
#                     position and 0.007 degrees in orientation on test-karma-1 (ktrace-probe.cjs
#                     over the console channel, 3,000 ticks). An association edit therefore moves us
#                     away from the only reference that can be measured, toward one that cannot be
#                     run here, with nothing to validate it. That is exactly what ASSOC-ON-I386
#                     already records from the other direction: reversing 41 flat chains in one
#                     object changed the object file and changed nothing measurable.
#
#                     THE SURVEY IS A RECORD. Act on a site when a defect appears that a change to
#                     it can be shown to fix, and check BOTH shipped builds before touching it.

# COVERAGE-CENSUS    2026-08-28 (eleventh session): 464 OF 2,025 RECOVERED FUNCTIONS (22.9%) HAVE
#                     EVER EXECUTED IN A REAL MATCH, measured across four maps and two gametypes
#                     with -finstrument-functions inside the engine (CMake -DKD_CENSUS=ON,
#                     tools/census_report.py). Everything else in this file is evidence about the
#                     functions the game CALLS; this is the denominator.
#
#                     WHY IT MATTERS MOST AFTER A PASS. The whole-library A/B on test-karma-1 reads
#                     bit-identical against MathEngine for 13 of 15 bodies over 3,000 frames. That
#                     is strong evidence for the paths the map exercised and says NOTHING about the
#                     rest. "The recovered Karma matches the original" and "it matches on the 23%
#                     of its functions anything has ever called" are different claims.
#
#                     THE RAGDOLL ANSWER THE OWNER ASKED FOR IS IN THIS TABLE:
#
#                       MdtLimit      34 functions   NEVER ENTERED   <- the joint-limit machinery
#                       MdtHinge       7 functions   NEVER ENTERED
#                       MdtSkeletal    1/25 entered
#                       MdtConeLimit   6/11 entered
#                       MdtBSJoint     2/5  entered
#                       MeFJoint      89 functions   NEVER ENTERED
#                       MeFAsset       1/66 entered  (and the 1 is KD_FBITS, an inline helper)
#
#                     "Limbs open 180 degrees and rotate freely, as if there were no limits" is
#                     exactly what an unvalidated MdtLimit would look like, and MdtLimit has never
#                     executed on ANY native run — so on wasm the game is running 34 functions that
#                     nothing has ever measured. That is a LEAD, not a verdict: it has not been
#                     bisected, and the ranked-suspect lesson in MCD-GEOM-FLOAT-FIELDS is that a
#                     good hypothesis about which subsystem is at fault can be confidently wrong.
#
#                     ⚠ THE FIRST CENSUS READ "0 of 2025 FUNCTIONS ENTERED", which is
#                     indistinguishable from "the physics never ran". The binary was a PIE, so the
#                     runtime addresses kd_instr.c records differ from nm's link-time offsets by the
#                     load bias and nothing resolved. -no-pie must go on the EXECUTABLE; putting it
#                     on the Engine static library changes nothing and the build still succeeds.
#                     census_report.py now REFUSES a zero rather than printing one.
#
#                     ⚠ TWO CORRECTIONS TO THIS ENTRY, both from COLD-TRIAGE below.
#                     (a) 148 of the "2,025 functions" are `__x86.get_pc_thunk.*`, three
#                         instructions gcc emits per object. The recovery has 1,877.
#                     (b) "MdtLimit has never executed on ANY native run" WAS AN ARTEFACT OF THE
#                         RUN SET, not a property of the code. With `SpectatorOnly=1` and bots
#                         that actually die, MdtLimit enters 8 or 9 of its 34 functions on four
#                         maps out of five. The lead it was offered as is withdrawn; the joint
#                         limits were separately root-caused (a `.ka` bool parsed as a float) and
#                         that fix stands on its own evidence.

# COLD-TRIAGE        2026-08-28 (twelfth session): THE COLD TWO THIRDS IS MOSTLY CODE UT2004
#                     CANNOT REACH. 301 functions — 16.0%, not 64.1% — are reachable, referenced
#                     and never measured. tools/cold_triage.py, five census runs against the
#                     build at karma/decompile HEAD.
#
#                     COVERAGE-CENSUS gave the numerator and left the denominator looking awful.
#                     reachable.py answers the other half at object level and retires 31 members.
#                     Neither number is the one to reason about; their intersection is:
#
#                       symbols nm reports                        2025
#                       …of which __x86.get_pc_thunk.*             148   <- NOT recovered code
#                       functions the recovery actually has       1877
#                       ENTERED on some recorded run               673   35.9%
#                       cold                                      1204   64.1%
#                         DEAD-OBJECT   member is unreachable        71
#                         DEAD-SYMBOL   no relocation names it      682
#                         FILE-LOCAL    static; no symbol to name   150
#                         UNVALIDATED   reachable and never run     301   16.0%
#
#                     ⚠ FIRST, A CORRECTION TO THE DENOMINATOR EVERY ENTRY HERE HAS USED. 148 of
#                     the "2,025 recovered functions" are `__x86.get_pc_thunk.bx` and siblings —
#                     three instructions gcc emits per object to load the PC. They are in every
#                     `nm`-based count this project has ever printed, including COVERAGE-CENSUS's.
#                     The recovery has 1,877 functions.
#
#                     ⚠ SECOND, COVERAGE-CENSUS'S RAGDOLL LEAD IS WITHDRAWN. It recorded "MdtLimit
#                     34 functions NEVER ENTERED … MdtLimit has never executed on ANY native run"
#                     and offered it as the explanation for limbs with no joint limits. Measured
#                     again with `SpectatorOnly=1` and bots that actually die:
#
#                       test-karma-1 (2 bots)   MdtLimit 8/34    MdtSkeletal 13/25
#                       test-karma-1 (1 bot)    MdtLimit 8/34    MdtSkeletal 13/25
#                       ONS-Torlan              MdtLimit 8/34    MdtSkeletal 13/25
#                       DM-Rankin               MdtLimit 8/34    MdtSkeletal 13/25
#                       ONS-Adara               MdtLimit 0/34    MdtSkeletal  1/25
#
#                     The joint-limit machinery runs. The earlier zero was a property of the RUN
#                     SET, not of the code, and the ragdoll defect was separately root-caused
#                     (a `.ka` bool parsed as a float, MCD-GEOM-FLOAT-FIELDS' session). ONS-Adara
#                     is the outlier for the reason test/README.md already records about
#                     bot-dependent maps headless: it enters 528 functions against 648-665 for the
#                     others and reaches almost no ragdoll code.
#
#                     ⚠ AND ADDING MAPS BUYS ALMOST NOTHING. test-karma-1 alone enters 658 of the
#                     union's 673 — 97.8%. Four maps and two gametypes is not four times the
#                     coverage of one; the ceiling is set by what the GAME does, not by which map
#                     it does it on. Do not read a bigger map list as a bigger measurement.
#
#                     WHAT THE TWO WEAK VERDICTS ARE WORTH. DEAD-SYMBOL inherits reachable.py's
#                     own blind spot: a table the ENGINE fills in, and anything entered only
#                     through a function pointer the engine computed, has no relocation to find.
#                     It is a strong prior, not a licence to delete. FILE-LOCAL is not a verdict
#                     at all — a static has no external symbol, so symbol reachability has nothing
#                     to say, and folding those 150 into "dead" would be an artefact of the
#                     instrument. They are counted apart for that reason.
#
#                     AND THE CENSUS SIDE IS A LOWER BOUND. A function absent from every recorded
#                     run was not reached BY THOSE RUNS. That asymmetry is why the verdict is
#                     UNVALIDATED and not "unreachable": the claim is about our measurements.
#
#                     WHERE THE 301 ARE, worst first: CxSmallSort 18, MeFAsset 16, McdSpace 15,
#                     MeFAssetPart 14, keaPrintBasicTypes 14, MdtCarWheel 12, MdtLimit 11,
#                     MeFGeometry 10, MeFPrimitive 10, McdModelPairManager 9. Two readings worth
#                     having: the MeF* asset-construction family is large and cold because a
#                     match READS assets rather than building them, and `McdSpace`/`CxSmallSort`
#                     is 33 unvalidated functions in the broadphase — the same object LP64-REBUILT-
#                     DATA shows is the next thing to fail at 64-bit.
#
#                     ⚠ RE-BUILD THE INSTRUMENTED BINARY FIRST. A census trace is a list of
#                     RUNTIME ADDRESSES and means nothing except against the binary that produced
#                     it. This entry's first run used a census tree whose objects were four
#                     commits old; it resolved cleanly, reported plausible numbers, and described
#                     a build that no longer existed. `cmake` will not rebuild what it thinks is
#                     current, so the failure is silent. Check the newest `.c.o` against `git log`.

# LP64-ANDROID-CRASH 2026-08-29 (thirteenth session, fifth leg): ★ THE OFFLINE LP64 WORK IS DONE
#                     AND ANDROID STILL CRASHES WITH KARMA ON. Two different things, and this
#                     entry exists so the second is not read as the first coming undone.
#
#                     WHAT IS DONE: `metoolkit_decomp/src` now carries the LP64 post-passes — it used
#                     to be the raw `recover.py` output — and `lp64_pipeline.sh` ends `-> PASS`.
#                     All 145 objects still compile byte-identical at -m32, so wasm32, i686 and
#                     armeabi-v7a get exactly the object code they had. The CMake UT_PTR_BYTES
#                     hard-error is a STATUS line now.
#
#                     WHAT IS NOT: turning Karma on in the Android build produces an APK that
#                     SIGSEGVs during startup. Measured on the API-34 x86_64 emulator, with BOTH
#                     ARMS VERIFIED BY SYMBOL COUNT IN libUT2004.so rather than by which flag was
#                     passed — which matters, because the first two attempts at this comparison
#                     built the wrong thing (see below):
#
#                       Karma ON   SIGSEGV on the engine thread the moment
#                                  "Initializing GL4ESDrv..." returns.   243 log lines
#                       Karma OFF  same APK, same ini, same map: reaches
#                                  UT2k4MainMenu.Opened().              1713 log lines
#
#                     ⚠ THE OBVIOUS SUSPECT IS IN BOTH LOGS AND IS NOT THE CAUSE. `Warning:
#                     Package not found: SDL2Drv` / `GL4ESDrv` appears five times in the run that
#                     REACHES THE MENU. I read the first failure as a renderer problem on that
#                     evidence and was wrong; only the control said otherwise. `[GL4ES-DBG] Init:
#                     complete, returning 1` is in the crashing log too — the render device
#                     initialises fine and the fault is just after it.
#
#                     ⚠ AND IT IS NOT KNOWN WHETHER THIS IS LP64 OR ANDROID. Karma has never been
#                     enabled on Android at ANY pointer width, so a 32-bit ABI separates the two
#                     — and neither is reachable here: the API-34 system image refuses x86 ("ABI
#                     x86 not supported on this device") and the x86_64 emulator cannot execute
#                     ARM, so armeabi-v7a needs the real OnePlus 6, which was not attached.
#                     THAT IS THE NEXT MEASUREMENT, and it is one command on a connected device.
#
#                     ⚠ THE ENGINE SWALLOWS ITS OWN BACKTRACE. UT2004 installs a SIGSEGV handler
#                     that prints "Crash information will be saved to your logfile" and exits, so
#                     there is no tombstone and no `DEBUG` frames in logcat. Getting a stack means
#                     disabling that handler or attaching lldb-server first.
#
#                     ⚠⚠ AND THE GRADLE CACHE LIES ABOUT WHAT IT BUILT, TWICE. `option()` reads an
#                     existing CMake cache before its default, so flipping USE_KARMA_DECOMP and
#                     re-running gradle reported "BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 1s, 32 up-to-date" and
#                     shipped the PREVIOUS physics. CMakeLists' own comment warns about exactly
#                     this. Every arm of the comparison above was checked with
#                     `llvm-nm libUT2004.so | grep -c McdBoxPlaneIntersect` before it was believed,
#                     and `rm -rf app/build/intermediates/cxx` is what actually reconfigures.
#
#                     THE DEFAULT STAYS NO_KARMA, because an APK without vehicles beats an APK
#                     that does not start. `./gradlew assembleDebug -PwithKarma` reproduces it.

# LP64-THREE-CLEAN  2026-08-29 (thirteenth session, fourth leg): ★★★ THE LP64 PIPELINE PASSES.
#                     All three scenes run clean on the plain build — 901 rows each, exit 0, five
#                     runs of five — and every trajectory now first differs at the float floor.
#                     Where the leg started: chain clean, boxes SIGSEGV after 51 rows, ragdoll
#                     SIGSEGV at row 0. Eight more defects, each invisible until the one before it
#                     was repaired, which makes seventeen in the chain.
#
#                     THE READING, and `lp64_pipeline.sh` now says PASS:
#
#                       plain LP64 vs plain i386, first difference / magnitude
#                         scene_chain            step   4   9.0e-10   noise, amplifying
#                         scene_boxes_on_plane   step  52   1.9e-09   noise, amplifying
#                         scene_ragdoll          step   0   2.2e-07   noise, amplifying
#
#                     boxes reached that in two more steps: 1.5e-01 at step 45 (the geometry AABB
#                     family), then 1.5e-03 at step 52 (the McdHello swap), then 1.9e-09 — level
#                     with the collision-free scene. Nothing in the corpus first differs above the
#                     float floor any more.
#
#                     ★ THE FIRST DIFFERING STEP IS THE DISCRIMINATOR, NOT THE MAXIMUM, and on
#                     a contact scene the maximum is worthless — every one of these reaches
#                     metres by step 900. chain and ragdoll first differ at 1e-9 and 1e-7, which
#                     is last-bit float noise amplifying through a chaotic scene; boxes was
#                     BIT-IDENTICAL for 44 steps and then jumped to 1.5e-01 in one step, which is
#                     not amplification. Repairing that took it to step 52 at 1.5e-03 — 100x
#                     smaller and seven steps later, and still five orders above the floor.
#
#                     ★★ AND THE REFERENCE HAD BEEN THE WRONG BUILD. `lp64_run.sh` diffed the
#                     trajectory against an i386 control built WITH AddressSanitizer, then ran
#                     the plain scenes against no reference at all. ASan changes the numbers —
#                     its heap layout and its poison are not the plain build's — so the plain
#                     rows have to be diffed against a PLAIN i386 control, which the harness now
#                     builds. That is what makes the first-differing-step reading above mean
#                     anything.
#                     ⚠ AND `0.00e+00 over 901 rows` WAS AN ARTEFACT OF THE COMPARATOR: `nan > x`
#                     is False, so every non-finite sample failed the divergence test AND left the
#                     worst delta at zero. It read "matches the control over all 901 rows" on a
#                     trace that was 8,100 NaNs. Non-finite samples are counted and reported now.
#
#                     ⚠⚠ AND I DREW THE WRONG CONCLUSION FROM THAT BLIND COMPARATOR, WHICH IS THE
#                     LESSON. On the strength of `0.00e+00` I recorded that the sanitized ragdoll
#                     blew up "at BOTH widths" and called it an uninitialised read ASan made
#                     visible. The delta was zero because NaN compares equal to nothing, not
#                     because the two agreed. Repairing the McdHello swap below removed the NaNs
#                     and the spinning entirely: measured over five runs, the sanitized scenes now
#                     read chain 5/5 clean, boxes 5/5 clean, ragdoll 901 rows with 0 sanitizer
#                     errors every time. THE NaN STORM WAS DOWNSTREAM OF A REAL LP64 DEFECT.
#                     A conclusion drawn through a broken instrument is worth exactly what the
#                     instrument is worth.
#
#                     WHAT IS TRUE ABOUT ragdoll UNDER ASan, measured rather than inferred: its
#                     own verdict is UNSTABLE, and it is unstable AT i386 TOO — the i386 ASan
#                     build reads BLOWN UP 5 times out of 5 with zero sanitizer errors, where the
#                     LP64 one reads BLOWN UP 3 of 5. The scene sits on its "escaped > 1e3"
#                     threshold and ASan's layout is enough to tip it. So an ASan ragdoll verdict
#                     is not a pointer-width signal in either direction; the plain rows are.
#
#                     ★★★ THE BIGGEST SINGLE FINDING: AN ENTIRE TYPE FAMILY WAS INVISIBLE TO THE
#                     OFFSET MAP. The SDK declares every concrete geometry as
#                     `typedef struct { McdGeometry m_g; MeReal mR[3]; ... } McdBox;` — NO TAG
#                     before the brace — and `fix_literal_offsets`' map is built by scanning for
#                     `struct NAME {`. So McdBox, McdSphere, McdCylinder, McdSphyl,
#                     McdConvexMesh, McdAggregate and McdNull all had EMPTY offset maps, every
#                     site addressing one read as "out of scope, the base carries no structure",
#                     and 412 sites across 16 objects sat in that bucket unexamined.
#
#                     What it cost: `McdBoxUpdateAABB` read the box half-extents at the i386
#                     `+0x10/0x14/0x18`. `_McdGeometry` is 16 bytes here and 32 there, so at LP64
#                     those land INSIDE the base and read zero — the box's AABB came back as a
#                     POINT. The broadphase then never reported the box/plane pair until the box
#                     CENTRE was through the plane, two steps late, and that is the whole of the
#                     step-45 divergence. Measured, not inferred: the AABB reads
#                     `-0.505,-0.405,z-0.305 .. +0.505,+0.405,z+0.305` at i386 and
#                     `-0.005,-0.005,z-0.005 .. +0.005,+0.005,z+0.005` at LP64 — half-extents
#                     exactly zero plus the 0.005 margin — and a call counter on
#                     `McdBoxPlaneIntersect` reads 0,1,2,3,4 across steps 43-47 at i386 against
#                     0,0,0,0,1 at LP64. The narrow phase was never wrong; it was never CALLED.
#
#                     WHICH CONCRETE TYPE IS NOT GUESSED EITHER: `McdBox.c` calls
#                     `McdFrameworkRegisterGeometryType(frame, 2, "McdBox", ...)` and hands it
#                     that file's own destroy/updateAABB/getBSphere, so the object under every
#                     geometry pointer in that file IS an McdBox — the LP64-OPAQUE-API installer
#                     rule one indirection shallower. Eight files register exactly one type; the
#                     `Ix*` interaction objects register none and handle TWO geometries
#                     (IxSphylPrimitives has 227 such sites), so they are reported and left to
#                     per-site typing. 122 sites repaired, byte-identical.
#
#                     ⚠ AND A DECLINE IS ONLY A DECLINE IF NOTHING LATER TYPES THE VARIABLE. The
#                     RETURNS rule declines `pvVar7` correctly — `_McdGeometry` genuinely has no
#                     field at +0x10 — and the registration rule then types it. Printed as
#                     written, the report claimed a decline on a site repaired in the same run.
#                     Declines are keyed by variable and emitted only if still untyped.
#
#                     ★★ TWO OF THE OTHER FOUR WERE RULES THAT ALREADY EXISTED AND HAD SILENTLY
#                     STOPPED MATCHING, both because they keyed on a SPELLING AN EARLIER PASS
#                     REWRITES:
#
#                       fix_frame_slots     FRAME_OFF2 required a literal `(int)` cast;
#                                           fix_ptrwidth writes `(kd_iptr)` four passes earlier.
#                                           20 sites, no match, NO DECLINE PRINTED.
#                       fix_vtable_offsets  VSLOT read `(*(char **)&obj)` while VPTR_STORE, its
#                                           own partner, accepted `char|undefined4|void`.
#
#                     A pattern that does not fire has nothing to report, so both read as objects
#                     with nothing to do. Both matchers are structural now and both REFUSE rather
#                     than half-apply.
#
#                     10  `puVar9 = auStack_3c + -uVar6;` — LP64-COLLISION-CHAIN item 9 in a
#                         second spelling. `NEG_MUL` matched `* -N` only; a unary minus on a
#                         `uint` is the identical defect and so is `auStack_3c[iVar2 - uVar6]`.
#                         27 sites. ⚠ AND IT ADDRESSES A DIFFERENT ARRAY FROM THE LINE ABOVE IT:
#                         `+ -uVar6` is ELEMENT arithmetic on a `uint *` and lands at -4*uVar6;
#                         `(kd_iptr)auStack_3c - uVar6`, five lines earlier, is BYTE arithmetic
#                         and lands at -1*uVar6. MEASURED both ways — the shipped i386 `solveLCP`
#                         does SIX successive `sub %edi,%esp`, one per block, so the frame is a
#                         partition of six and reading the two spellings alike would have aliased
#                         two live arrays. The cast goes on the FIRST IDENTIFIER of the chain:
#                         `(kd_uptr)((uVar3 - 2) - order)` is the defect with a cast on it.
#
#                     11  `*(int **)((int)(kd_frame + 0) + 8) = clamped;` — the trailing argument
#                         word, outside the base's own parentheses. `this` goes to word one and
#                         `clamped` to word two, so at LP64 the write of `this` lands on top of
#                         `clamped`'s low half and the read comes back `0x00007fff00007fff`, the
#                         two pointers' high halves interleaved. ★ THE ARITHMETIC PREDICTS THE
#                         REGISTER EXACTLY.
#
#                     12  the vtable SLOTS in `keaLCPSolver`, with the address point ALREADY
#                         scaled — half-applied is worse than not applied. `+ 8` reached slot ONE,
#                         so `makeFromColMajorPSM` (nine arguments, called with nine) dispatched
#                         into `makeFromJMJT` (seven) and left `NAZ[0]` NULL for a SEGV two
#                         functions later. The i386 slots are +0=allocate(1 arg),
#                         +8=makeFromColMajorPSM(9), +12=factorize(0), +16=solve(2) and the four
#                         call sites pass exactly 1, 9, 0 and 2 — which is what makes it a
#                         measurement. ⚠ The same pass read ONE site of the `*(int *)A + K`
#                         spelling where the corpus has twenty-two, because its object group
#                         matched a bare identifier and every real one is `this->A`.
#
#                     13  `if (pool_max < (uint)(size + (kd_iptr)pool_ptr))` — a pointer compared
#                         against a 32-bit-NARROWED ADDRESS, so the kea pool reads as exhausted
#                         the moment it is handed memory above 4 GB. ⚠ NOTHING IN THE COMPILER
#                         CAN SEE THIS: measured under `-Wall`, `-Wextra`, `-Wint-conversion` and
#                         `-Weverything`, clang says nothing, which is why `fix_ptrwidth` — whose
#                         site list IS clang's diagnostics — structurally cannot reach it. ⚠ And
#                         it is an ADDRESS, not a DIFFERENCE: 31 sites share the shape and
#                         `(int)((kd_iptr)p - uVar3)` is a COUNT, correct at every width. What
#                         separates the one is that it is compared against a POINTER.
#
#                     14  and the pool was then genuinely too small: `MdtKeaMemoryRequired`
#                         reserves the same arrays a second time at the i386 element size.
#                         tools/fix_pool_reserve.py, 8 sites.
#                         ★ THE ADDEND IS ELEMENTS TOO, AND SCALING ONLY THE STRIDE CHANGED THE
#                         POOL BY NOT ONE BYTE. `(uVar27 & 0xfffffff0) * 4 + 0x40` is
#                         `((n + 15) & ~15) * 4`, so the `0x40` is `16 * 4` — and with these
#                         matrices `n` is under 16, `uVar27 & 0xfffffff0` is ZERO, and the addend
#                         IS the whole reservation. The first patch read exactly like a wrong
#                         diagnosis and was a half-right one.
#                         ⚠ The discriminator reached for first over-matched: "the count is a
#                         self-multiplication, so it is the blocks*blocks pointer array" also
#                         fits `iVar2 = iVar12 * 4` in the same function — the A matrix, a
#                         squared count holding FLOATS. What separates them is that a rounded
#                         reservation is a PAIR of branches assigning the same local.
#
#                     15  and the geometry AABB family above, which is the one that moved the
#                         boxes trajectory.
#
#                     16  `iVar10 = iVar10 + 0x30` in `McdBatchFlatten` — a table walked by a
#                         BYTE CURSOR with a baked stride. `0xc` is one `McdBatchContactPool` and
#                         `0x30` is four, 12 bytes each here and SIXTEEN there. tools/fix_strides.py
#                         grew a third shape for it; six cursors across four objects.
#                         ⚠ IT IS LATENT AND IT IS WRITTEN DOWN BECAUSE OF THAT: repairing it alone
#                         changed not one number in any scene. Real by measurement, and NOT the
#                         cause of the divergence it was found while chasing. Bisect before
#                         believing — four for four.
#
#                     17  ★ AND THE ONE THAT CLOSED THE TRAJECTORY: `puVar4[4]` in `McdHello`.
#
#                           undefined4 *puVar4;
#                           puVar4 = McdFrameworkGetInteractions(frame, type1, type2);
#                           if (puVar4[4] != 0) { swap p->model1 and p->model2; }
#
#                         A STRUCT ADDRESSED AS AN ARRAY OF 4-BYTE WORDS. `_McdInteractions` opens
#                         with four function pointers, so `swap` is the fifth WORD at i386 and
#                         `[4]` is exactly it; at LP64 those pointers take 32 bytes, `swap` moves
#                         to `[8]`, and `[4]` reads the upper half of `intersectFn` — non-zero, so
#                         McdHello SWAPPED EVERY PAIR IT WAS GIVEN. LP64-VTABLE-WORDS' family on an
#                         ordinary struct, invisible to every offset rule because an array
#                         subscript is the whole expression: no cast, no `+ K` to key on.
#
#                         ★★ HOW IT WAS FOUND, and the method is the reusable part. The symptom
#                         was a box pair arriving at `McdBoxBoxIntersect` with an exactly NEGATED
#                         normal and the same penetration to seven digits. Five instruments, each
#                         eliminating one layer: the AABBs (identical through step 52), the sweep's
#                         own reports (`MoveStartMarkerDown a=1 b=2`, identical), `CxSmallSort::
#                         Insert` (ids 0..8 in the same order), the pair-manager's 56 creations
#                         (identical, `(1,2)` created as `(box0, box1)` at both widths), and
#                         finally the same PAIR OBJECT read back in `McdBatchFlatten` as (8,7) at
#                         LP64 having been created as (7,8). Same object, different contents:
#                         that is what said a WRITER had swapped it, and there was exactly one.
#
#                         ⚠ AND AN INSTRUMENT LIED FIRST. Reading `p->model1->mSpaceID` came back
#                         as garbage at BOTH widths and proved nothing; the box's own `mR[0]` is
#                         stable across widths and is what made the swap legible. When an
#                         instrument disagrees with itself, change the instrument.
#                         ⚠ AND TWO EMPTY FILES COMPARED EQUAL. A link failure left both AABB
#                         dumps empty and `diff -q` reported them identical — "★ AABBs IDENTICAL"
#                         on zero lines of evidence. Assert the measurement is non-empty first.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE FIRST HYPOTHESIS FOR THAT SWAP WAS WRONG, AS USUAL. It read as a
#                     broadphase ordering difference — `CxSmallSort` is the object census_report
#                     flags with 33 unvalidated functions, and its sweep is what names the pair.
#                     Every one of those layers measured IDENTICAL. The writer was two subsystems
#                     away, in `McdHello`, and only the same-object-different-contents reading
#                     could have pointed at it.
#
#                     WHAT THIS IS NOT. Necessary, not sufficient: x86-64 tolerates unaligned
#                     access where arm64 FAULTS, these are OFFLINE SCENES, and nothing at 64-bit
#                     has yet exercised a real match, the drop-in link or the .ka asset path.

# LP64-COLLISION-CHAIN 2026-08-29 (thirteenth session, third leg): ★ THE COLLISION HALF WAS
#                     DEAD AT LP64 — `McdIntersect` dispatched through a NULL — and unpicking
#                     it turned into a CHAIN of nine defects, each hidden behind the last.
#                     Where it stands: memory-safe under ASan on all three scenes, `scene_chain`
#                     matching the i386 control over all 900 rows, and the two CONTACT scenes
#                     still failing in `keaLCPSolver::solveLCP`.
#
#                     ★★ THE INSTRUMENT THAT MADE IT TRACTABLE, and it is the reusable part.
#                     `lp64_run.sh` counted AddressSanitizer errors and nothing else. It now
#                     also (a) builds the i386 control for EVERY scene and diffs the
#                     TRAJECTORY, and (b) runs the same scenes WITHOUT the sanitizer.
#
#                     ⚠ (b) IS NOT BELT-AND-BRACES — ASan HIDES THIS ENTIRE CLASS. A pointer
#                     that lost its top 32 bits addresses somewhere in the low 4 GB, and under
#                     ASan that region is MAPPED (its shadow and allocator live there), so the
#                     load succeeds and the run reads CLEAN. Measured: with ASan reporting "no
#                     sanitizer error" on all three scenes, the plain build segfaulted on two —
#                     `UpdateHull`, `MdtConstraintGetRowCount`, `getClampIndices`, each a live
#                     pointer arriving as two interleaved halves. Three real defects the
#                     sanitized run could not see. The plain build is also 20x faster.
#
#                     ⚠ AND A SHORTER RUN IS NOT A MATCHING RUN. The first trajectory check
#                     zipped the two traces, so a scene that timed out at row 20 reported
#                     "matches" on 20 rows out of 900. It reports the length now.
#
#                     THE CHAIN, in the order it had to be unpicked — every one of these was
#                     invisible until the one before it was repaired:
#
#                     1  `*(code **)(pvVar4 + 8)` in `McdIntersect` — the interaction table's
#                        `intersectFn`, at 8 here and SIXTEEN there, so every dispatch went
#                        through `goodbyeFn`, which is NULL for most interactions. NOTHING
#                        collided at LP64. ⚠ The cause was one `\s` in a regex: `MEPUBLIC` sits
#                        on its own line above the declaration, `\s` crosses newlines, and the
#                        return type came out as `MEPUBLIC McdInteractions*`, which resolves to
#                        no struct. A line-local pattern fixed the whole collision half.
#                     2  `MdtContactGroupID MEAPI MdtConstraintDCastContactGroup(...)` — the
#                        star is NOT WRITTEN when the SDK returns a handle typedef, so the
#                        return-type scan never saw it. `MdtUpdatePartitions` kept reading
#                        `contactCount` at `+0x160` and reported ZERO contacts on every step
#                        while the packer packed them; the row budget it sized the kea arena
#                        from was 24 where the run needed 74.
#                     3  the kea arena itself: `iVar2 = maxPartitions * 0xc + 0x5c`, and `0x5c`
#                        is `sizeof(MdtKeaConstraints)` — 92 here, 152 there. ⚠ proven.txt said
#                        this carve-up needed "a static analysis, not a pattern match"; MEASURED,
#                        every other term is width-independent and it is ONE literal.
#                     4  `alloca(n * 0x10 + 0x10)` in `McdContactSimplify` — `0x10` is
#                        `sizeof(McdContactLink)`, 32 at LP64, so the link array came back half
#                        size. The type is in the cast that consumes the block on a LATER line,
#                        which is why it was one of the five "alloca: no type for the block".
#                        ⚠ The trailing ADDEND is an element too when it equals the stride.
#                     5, 6, 7  three more spellings of the invented argument area:
#                        `kd_argarea_X[N]` addressed forwards, the same addressed DOWNWARDS
#                        from a derived pointer, and `char *kd_frame = alloca(...)` — a whole
#                        materialised frame whose TRAILING constants are the argument words.
#                     8  `iVar1 = *(int *)(pvVar2 + KD_OFFSET(MdtContactGroup, first))` — a
#                        four-byte read of an eight-byte pointer field, then `iVar1 + 0x1dc`
#                        with no cast on the base for any pattern to key on. Both halves needed
#                        the OTHER pass's output to be recognisable, which is why
#                        `fix_literal_offsets` is now run TWICE.
#                     9  `C = (int *)((kd_iptr)auStack_3c + uVar6 * -2)` — `uVar6` is a `uint`,
#                        so the product is UNSIGNED and comes out large-positive. At i386 the
#                        addition wraps in 32 bits and is right; at LP64 the product is
#                        ZERO-EXTENDED and the pointer moves four gigabytes the wrong way.
#                        ⚠ `(kd_iptr)uVar6` is the WRONG repair and was the first tried — it
#                        makes the multiply SIGNED, gcc schedules it differently and all eight
#                        sites decline. `(kd_uptr)` IS `unsigned int` at i386, so it is
#                        byte-identical by construction. Preserve signedness.
#
#                     WHAT IS LEFT: `keaLCP_new.c:270`, `*puVar9 = 0` inside `solveLCP`, on the
#                     plain build only. Same neighbourhood as 9 — the LCP solver's hand-laid
#                     frame — and it is the next thing to read.

# LP64-COLLISION-SIDE 2026-08-29 (thirteenth session, later): ★ MEMORY SAFETY IS CLEAN ON ALL
#                     THREE SCENES AT 64-BIT POINTER WIDTH. What is left is a TRAJECTORY
#                     divergence, on the collision side, and the control that says so is
#                     scene_chain — which is COLLISION-FREE and matches the i386 build over all
#                     900 steps.
#
#                       scene_chain            0 ASan errors   trajectory MATCHES (worst 4.9e-04)
#                       scene_boxes_on_plane   0 ASan errors   diverges >1% at step 33
#                       scene_ragdoll          0 ASan errors   diverges >1% at step 1, BLOWN UP
#
#                     Both divergences begin at the scene's FIRST CONTACT and chain never has
#                     one, so the remaining defect is in the collision half — not in the
#                     dynamics, not in the solver, and not in arithmetic.
#
#                     ⚠ THE ERROR COUNT WAS ONLY HALF OF WHAT THE HARNESS COULD SAY. A pointer
#                     that is wrong but still IN BOUNDS is invisible to AddressSanitizer and
#                     perfectly visible in the numbers. `lp64_run.sh` now builds the i386
#                     control for EVERY scene, keeps its trajectory, and diffs — and it fails on
#                     a non-zero exit as well as on an error count, because ragdoll's own
#                     "BLOWN UP" verdict was being reported as PASS.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE FLAGS MUST MATCH DOWN TO -O AND -mfpmath, WHICH I GOT WRONG
#                     FIRST. Comparing an -O2 i386 build against the -O1 LP64 one measures x87
#                     excess-precision rounding: it read "boxes diverges at step 45" either way,
#                     and only the matched-flag reading meant anything. The control builds with
#                     the identical CF now.

# LP64-FRAME-SLOTS   2026-08-29 (thirteenth session, later): GHIDRA'S INVENTED ARGUMENT AREA,
#                     ADDRESSED IN 4-BYTE WORDS — 22 areas, tools/fix_frame_slots.py. ★ AND THIS
#                     ENTRY CORRECTS LP64-TWO-REMAIN, WHICH CALLED THE CLASS UNFIXABLE UNDER THE
#                     BYTE-IDENTITY GATE.
#
#                       int aiStack_9cb0 [6];
#                       *(McdBatchContext **)((kd_iptr)aiStack_9cb0 + 0x14) = b->context;
#                       ... = McdBatchIntersectEach(*(void **)((kd_iptr)aiStack_9cb0 + 4), ...);
#
#                     Every offset is an argument WORD and every word is four bytes because that
#                     is what a pointer weighed on the shipped target. At LP64 slots 4 and 5
#                     overlap and the write to slot 5 runs four bytes off the end of a 24-byte
#                     array. That is `MstUtils.c:91`; `McdBatch.c:829` is its consequence one
#                     call deeper, `*resultCount = 0` through a pointer from a clobbered slot.
#
#                     ★ WHY THE PREDICTION WAS WRONG, AND IT IS THE FOURTH TIME THIS SESSION.
#                     LP64-TWO-REMAIN reasoned: "It is a PIPELINE change, so metoolkit_decomp/src
#                     moves and byte-identity CANNOT be the gate — the repair gives the scratch
#                     its own storage, which is a different stack layout by construction." True
#                     of the repair it had in mind — converting the shape upstream in
#                     recover.py — and not true of scaling the offsets AND the object TOGETHER
#                     in a post-pass:
#
#                       + 0x14                ->  + (5 * (int)sizeof(void *))
#                       int aiStack_9cb0[6];  ->  int aiStack_9cb0[6 * (int)(sizeof(void *) / 4)];
#
#                     Both fold to the original text at 32-bit pointer width, so `generated`
#                     never moves and the i386 object cannot change. Measured on MstUtils — the
#                     object the upstream attempt "knocked out of the build entirely" — 123
#                     offsets scaled, all three areas widened, byte-identical. NEVER DECLINE A
#                     REPAIR ON A PREDICTION.
#
#                     WHICH LOCALS, and the two refusals are the whole safety: only Ghidra's own
#                     `<type><Stack|StackY>_<hex>` names; every offset must be a multiple of
#                     four; and the name must appear NOWHERE except inside one of these casts,
#                     because if the recovery ever uses `fStack_9c98` as the float it is
#                     declared to be then widening it changes what that read means. Seven areas
#                     decline on that test and are reported.
#
#                     ⚠ ALL OR NOTHING PER AREA, AND RECOMPUTE BETWEEN AREAS. A half-scaled area
#                     overlaps DIFFERENTLY rather than not at all. And the areas INTERLEAVE —
#                     MstUtils writes `aiStack_9cb0` and `aMStack_9c94` in alternate statements
#                     — so offsets taken against the original text are stale the moment one area
#                     lands, and the next area's declaration edit falls in the middle of an
#                     expression. It reads as "not byte-identical", which is a DECLINE ON A
#                     REPAIR THAT WAS NEVER TRIED: `aiStack_9cb0` declined exactly that way
#                     while the same three areas, edited together by hand, were identical.
#
#                     ⚠ AND A GREEDY REGEX ATE THE FIRST LETTER OF EVERY NAME. `aiStack_9cb0`
#                     was captured as `iStack_9cb0`, a name that appears nowhere — so the area
#                     had no sites, so it was skipped SILENTLY. The tool reported nine clean
#                     repairs while the object it was written for was untouched. A `\b` before
#                     the name group fixes it; the lesson is that a skip with no note is
#                     indistinguishable from nothing to do.
#
#                     ★★ AND IT BLINDS A GATE IF YOU LET IT — MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED.
#                     `check_frame_bounds.py` exists for exactly this defect and reads CONSTANT
#                     offsets and CONSTANT array bounds; after this pass both are constant
#                     EXPRESSIONS. A deliberate `+ 0x400` into `aiStack_9cb0` is caught before
