#                     the post-pass and MISSED after it, on the same detector, which is how the
#                     blinding was confirmed rather than argued. The gate has been taught both
#                     spellings and re-checked against the same deliberate violation, and
#                     `lp64_pipeline.sh` now runs it after the post-passes so the zero is stated
#                     out loud rather than assumed.

# SCENE-RAGDOLL-DANGLING 2026-08-29 (thirteenth session, later): ★ THE RAGDOLL SCENE HAS BEEN
#                     READING DEAD STACK FOR AS LONG AS IT HAS EXISTED, at every pointer width,
#                     and no gate in this project had ever run the corpus under AddressSanitizer
#                     at i386 to hear it.
#
#                       for (int i = 0; i < NBOX; i++) {
#                           MeMatrix4 tm = { ... };
#                           MstFixedModelCreate(u, (McdGeometryID)McdBoxCreate(fw, ...), tm);
#                       }
#
#                     `MstFixedModelCreate` keeps the POINTER — `McdModelSetTransformPtr` — so
#                     every obstacle's transform is a loop-iteration local that dies at the
#                     closing brace, and the collision code reads it for the whole 900 steps.
#                     ASan names it 24 times, all in `McdSphylBoxIntersect`, which is the ragdoll
#                     path exactly. Fixed in `scene_ragdoll.c` and `scene_ragdoll_lod.c` by
#                     giving the transforms storage that outlives the loop.
#
#                     ⚠ HOW IT WAS MISSED, AND IT IS A GATE-DESIGN LESSON. `lp64_run.sh` built
#                     its i386 control for the FIRST SCENE ONLY — "it is a control, not a
#                     survey" — and the first scene is `scene_chain`, which creates no collision
#                     models at all. Extending the control to every scene found this on its
#                     first run.
#
#                     ⚠ AND I NEARLY REPORTED THE OPPOSITE. The control binary exits 0 and its
#                     own verdict reads "plausible", so a first pass read it as clean and used
#                     it as the reference for a trajectory comparison. It had 24 sanitizer
#                     errors in a file nobody had looked at. CHECK THE STDERR, not the exit code
#                     — the scene's verdict and the sanitizer's are different questions.

# LP64-ONE-REMAIN    2026-08-29 (thirteenth session): ★ scene_chain RUNS CLEAN AT 64-BIT
#                     POINTER WIDTH, and the other two scenes fail in ONE class between them —
#                     the invented stack frames, which is a DECOMPILATION defect and not a
#                     layout one. Measured over five consecutive runs of all three scenes:
#
#                       scene_chain            exit 0   no sanitizer error       5 of 5
#                       scene_boxes_on_plane   exit 1   MstUtils 91,97 + McdBatch 829   5 of 5
#                       scene_ragdoll          exit 1   the same three (3 of 5) or
#                                                       BLOWN UP with no ASan error (2 of 5)
#
#                     Where this started the session: `MdtBcl.c:519` on chain and ragdoll,
#                     `MstUtils` + `McdBatch` on boxes. Where the LP64 work started twelve
#                     sessions ago: the FIRST STATEMENT of the first scene.
#
#                     ⚠ AND FIVE RUNS IS THE MEASUREMENT, NOT ONE. Three separate times this
#                     session a single run read "clean" and the next read three errors, on
#                     sources that had not changed — ASLR moves what an out-of-bounds write
#                     lands on, and `-fsanitize-recover` means how far a scene gets changes
#                     what it reports. A clean run is a sample. Take five.
#
#                     ⚠ ragdoll's `exit 1` with NO sanitizer error is a REAL FAILURE and the
#                     harness used to call it PASS. Every scene returns non-zero when its own
#                     verdict fails — ragdoll's is "BLOWN UP", bodies past 1e3 — and at i386
#                     the identical sources read "plausible" (measured: link scene_ragdoll.c
#                     against /tmp/kd_build at -m32, exit 0, 0 escaped). A wrong pointer that
#                     stays IN BOUNDS is invisible to the sanitizer and visible to the scene.
#                     `lp64_run.sh` now fails on a non-zero exit as well as on an ASan count.
#
#                     WHAT IS LEFT, and it is one thing seen from four call sites:
#                     `MstUtils.c:91,97,105`, `McdBatch.c:829`, `MstBridge.c:168` — all of
#                     them Ghidra's fabricated `aiStack_N + K` frames. LP64-TWO-REMAIN's
#                     reading of that class still stands: it belongs upstream in recover.py's
#                     frame conversion, it is 124 sites across 8 objects, five of those
#                     objects are RELEASED, and byte-identity cannot gate it because giving
#                     the scratch its own storage is a different stack layout by construction.

# LP64-OPAQUE-API    2026-08-29 (thirteenth session): ★ A `void *` API IS TYPED BY ITS
#                     INSTALLER, and that closed MdtBcl — 600 sites in one object, every one
#                     of them a baked byte offset, all of them repaired and byte-identical.
#
#                     `MdtBcl.h` declares `void *const constraint`, so this is the ORIGINAL
#                     API and no header can type it. Each of the sixteen `MdtBclAdd*` is
#                     registered as a callback on the very type it takes —
#
#                       MdtBSJoint.c:54   (pMVar1->head).bclFunction = MdtBclAddBSJoint;
#
#                     — and `pMVar1` is an `MdtBSJointID`. Corpus-wide, because the installer
#                     and the function it names are in different objects.
#
#                     WHICH PARAMETER is not guessed either: the one every one of whose baked
#                     offsets lands on a real field of that type. `constraint` reads 21 of 21
#                     in MdtBclAddBSJoint and `params` has no baked offsets at all. ONE HOP
#                     further does MdtBclAddContactGroup, which walks the group's contact list
#                     and hands each element to MdtBclAddContact — so the local it walks with
#                     is an `MdtContact`, named by the call.
#
#                     ⚠ LP64-TWO-REMAIN SAID THE BLOCKER WAS THE OFFSET MAP AND IT WAS RIGHT,
#                     but for two reasons rather than one. The nesting depth was the second:
#                     `MdtBSJoint` is NOTHING but `MdtConstraintHeader head`, so a flat map of
#                     its own members has one entry. The first was a MEASUREMENT BUG —
#                     `int bodyindex[MdtKeaMAXBODYCONSTRAINT];` did not match `\[\d*\]`, so
#                     +0xb4, the field every one of the sixteen reads FIRST, was not in the map
#                     at all. With both fixed the sixteen read 339 of 346 offsets landing, and
#                     the seven that do not are `constraint_00`'s, which the one-hop rule types.
#
#                     ⚠ AND RAISING THE DEPTH IS NOT ENOUGH ON ITS OWN — IT COSTS TWENTY
#                     REPAIRS. The concrete-type inference asks which struct every offset lands
#                     on; three levels of nesting means almost anything covers almost anything.
#                     `McdSpace.c`'s six offsets went from fitting ONE type to fitting
#                     TWENTY-TWO, declined as ambiguous, and the tool fell from 22 rewrites to
#                     2 — a silent regression the byte-identity gate cannot see, because a
#                     DECLINE COMPILES PERFECTLY. The fit is now asked in three tiers, narrowest
#                     first: direct member starts, then array elements, then the nested map.
#                     Expanding arrays ALONE was enough to bring the ambiguity back (three
#                     types fit), which is why the narrowest tier is members' own offsets.
#
#                     ★ THE REPAIR CHANGED SHAPE, AND THE OLD ONE WAS NOT BYTE-IDENTICAL.
#                     `*(T *)&p->FIELD` — this tool's documented rewrite — re-spells the whole
#                     address, and on MdtBcl gcc then commons the base pointer across the
#                     function: the object came back 268 bytes SMALLER. What works is to
#                     substitute THE LITERAL AND NOTHING ELSE,
#
#                       *(int *)((int)constraint + ((int)((char *)&((struct MdtBSJoint *)0)
#                                                          ->head.bodyindex[0] - (char *)0)))
#
#                     leaving the expression Ghidra wrote — including the `(int)` cast that
#                     fix_ptrwidth will widen later — exactly where it was. All 600 sites in
#                     one compile, byte-identical. It also removes the need to parse the cast
#                     at all, so `(MeReal (*) [4])((int)p + K)` and the 52 bare address
#                     computations get the same repair as the 548 dereferences.
#
#                     A THIRD SOURCE OF TYPE fell out of the same machinery and is the only
#                     one that is not an inference: the vendor's own declaration.
#                     `MeDictFirst` returns `MeDictNode *`, and `MdtWorldStep` reads
#                     `*(void **)((kd_iptr)pvVar5 + 0x14)` — `data`, at 20 here and FORTY at
#                     LP64. ⚠ Per FUNCTION, not per variable: Ghidra assigns `pvVar5` from
#                     `MeChunkGetMem` at line 218 and from `MeDictFirst` at 284, and a function
#                     where two known assignments name different structs is declined.
#
#                     RESULT: 643 rewritten, 13 declined and reported, up from 22.

# LP64-VTABLE-WORDS  2026-08-29 (thirteenth session): AN ITANIUM VTABLE ADDRESSED IN 4-BYTE
#                     WORDS. 26 sites, tools/fix_vtable_offsets.py.
#
#                       (*(char **)&vanillaFunctions) = (char *)((char *)&vtable_for_...[0] + 8);
#                       (**(void (**)(void *))((char *)(*(char **)&vanillaFunctions) + 16))(...);
#
#                     The address point is `2 * sizeof(void *)` and slot n is `n * sizeof(void *)`.
#                     At LP64 `+ 8` leaves the vptr at `&vtable[1]`, and `+ 16` from there is
#                     `&vtable[3]` — so the call meant for `platformInit`, which takes NO
#                     arguments, enters `calcIworldandNonInertialForceandVhmf`, which takes
#                     seven. It reads `blist` out of a register nobody set:
#
#                       #0 keaFunctions_Vanilla::calcIworldandNonInertialForceandVhmf ...:52
#                       #1 MdtKeaAddConstraintForces  keaRbdCore_unified.c:163
#
#                     and line 163 is the platformInit call. ★ THE ARITHMETIC PREDICTS THE
#                     OBSERVED CALLEE EXACTLY, which is what makes it a measurement.
#
#                     ⚠ NOTHING IN THE GATE SET CAN SEE THIS. No truncation, no struct changes
#                     size, and `code_call_check.py` reads 0 because the dispatch goes through
#                     a concrete `void (**)(...)` prototype rather than through `code` — the
#                     exact case that tool's own docstring records it cannot catch.
#
#                     ⚠ THERE ARE TWO SPELLINGS AND THE SECOND WAS FOUND A DAY LATER, by
#                     which time both chain and ragdoll had been chased through three
#                     downstream functions. `kd_types.h`'s own note warns about it:
#
#                       (**(...)(*(int *)A + 0x10))(A,this->x,b)
#
#                     — the object arrives as a POINTER, so there is no vptr store to key on,
#                     and the vptr is read as an `int` (truncating) before the slot arithmetic.
#                     `0x10` is slot 4, which is `solve`; the code was reaching slot 2,
#                     `makeFromColMajorPSM`, a NINE-argument function called with three. What
#                     says `A` is polymorphic is its own struct: the first member is
#                     `code **_vptr_`. `*(int *)contact2 + 0x178` has the identical shape, is
#                     a field read, and is correctly left alone.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE REFUSAL RAN AFTER THE EDITS. "Not one address point in the
#                     corpus" is what this tool prints rather than a zero — and it printed it
#                     on the SECOND run over the same tree, having already rewritten every
#                     literal on the first, leaving a half-edited tree behind each refusal.
#                     The count is now taken before any file is written, and it counts the
#                     repaired spelling too. A search has to recognise its own output.

# LP64-ALIGN-MASK    2026-08-29 (thirteenth session): AN ALIGNMENT MASK FROZEN AT 32 BITS.
#                     36 sites, tools/fix_align_masks.py.
#
#                       paiVar7 = (MdtKeaBodyIndexPair *)
#                                 (maxPartitions * 4 + 0x3f + (kd_iptr)(piVar8 + maxPartitions)
#                                  & 0xffffffc0);
#
#                     `0xffffffc0` is `~63` IN 32 BITS and its type is `unsigned int`, so at
#                     LP64 the usual arithmetic conversions widen it with ZEROS and the AND
#                     does not round the pointer down — it CUTS THE TOP HALF OFF IT.
#
#                     ★ THE FIRST EVIDENCE WAS A REGISTER DUMP, AND THAT IS THE LESSON:
#
#                       SEGV on unknown address 0x00004c1e01c0 ... WRITE
#                       rbx = 0x000000004c1e01c0   rdi = 0x000072b84c1e0134
#
#                     the faulting address IS the low half of a live pointer. ⚠ THE REPORT
#                     LANDED TWO FILES AWAY on `clist->Jbody[...] = ...`, the first statement
#                     of `MdtBclAddBSJoint` — correct code writing through a pointer
#                     `MdtKeaConstraintsCreateFromChunk` had computed. Every baked offset in
#                     that statement was repaired first, on the strength of the file and line
#                     in the report, and the scene failed at exactly the same place afterwards.
#                     READ THE REGISTERS, not the line number.
#
#                     THE REPAIR IS ANCHOR-AND-CORRECT in its simplest form yet:
#
#                       0xffffffc0   ->   (0xffffffc0 | ~(kd_uptr)0xffffffffU)
#
#                     zero at 32-bit pointer width, `0xffffffff00000000` at LP64. The
#                     self-check COMPILES IT BOTH WAYS and reads the two values back rather
#                     than asserting the claim.
#
#                     ⚠ WHICH MASKS, and the discriminator is narrower than it looks. The
#                     literal must have the shape of `~(align - 1)` — `flags & 0xfffffffd` is
#                     a bit being cleared in an integer and is correct everywhere — AND the
#                     `&`'s own operand must be pointer-derived. `(void *)((kd_iptr)pMVar5 +
#                     (uVar8 & 0xfffffffc))` has a pointer in the statement, a mask of the
#                     right shape and a pointer cast around the result, and is CORRECT: the
#                     mask is on a count. The scan walks back from the `&` to its own
#                     enclosing parenthesis and asks about that span alone.

# LP64-NARROW-PTR    2026-08-29 (thirteenth session): A POINTER HELD IN SOMETHING FOUR BYTES
#                     WIDE. 139 sites, tools/fix_narrow_pointers.py — and this is the class
#                     `fix_ptrwidth.py` has been reporting as "N diagnostic(s) remain" all
#                     along. It widens the punned CASTS at the columns clang names; here there
#                     is no cast to rewrite, or the cast is already as wide as it can be and
#                     what is narrow is the VARIABLE.
#
#                     SIX SHAPES, each with its own evidence and none of them a pattern:
#
#                       A  int iVar3; iVar3 = keaPoolAlloc(...);   -Wint-conversion names it
#                       B  *(undefined4 *)&this->mLP = ...         the member is a MeReal *
#                       C  the value stored into a widened pointer must be pointer-wide
#                       D  (T *)iVar10 where iVar10 is an int      -Wint-to-pointer-cast
#                       E  iVar2 = *(kd_iptr *)&this->NAZ;         reading one truncates too
#                       F  *(MeReal **)(NCZ + z * 4)               an array OF POINTERS at 4
#                       G  keaPoolAlloc((n + 0xf & ...) << 2)      ... allocated at 4 as well
#
#                     ⚠ B IS DECIDED BY MEASURING THE TARGET, NOT BY ITS NAME.
#                     `*(int *)&this->m_blocks` has the identical shape and is CORRECT.
#                     153 sites have the shape; 50 have a target that grows.
#
#                     ⚠ AND F WITHOUT G IS WORSE THAN NEITHER. Repairing the stride while the
#                     block is still allocated at four bytes an element makes the walk correct
#                     and runs it off the end; what it reads back is a NULL where a matrix
#                     block should be, and the SEGV is at `*AcholMatrix = ...` three hundred
#                     lines from the allocation. Both halves or neither.
#
#                     ★ THE RULES FEED EACH OTHER, SO THE PASS RUNS IN ROUNDS. Widening an
#                     access (B) is what reveals that the value stored through it is a narrow
#                     local (C); widening that local reveals the next. A single pass leaves
#                     `*(kd_uptr *)&this->matrixChol = uVar1;` with `uVar1` four bytes wide,
#                     which is the same defect one step back and reads as a clean run.
#
#                     ⚠ ORDER AGAINST fix_align_masks: AFTER fix_ptrwidth (which writes the
#                     `kd_iptr` both key on) and BEFORE the mask pass, because a mask on a
#                     WIDENED LOCAL has no cast in it to recognise — `iVar3 + 0xf & 0xfffffff0`
#                     with `iVar3` declared `kd_iptr`. That ordering is worth five more masks.
#
#                     THE SITE THAT PROVES THE CLASS IS `keaPopPoolFrame`:
#
#                       pool_ptr = *(undefined4 *)((char *)poolstack + (-4) + poolstack_ptr * 4);
#
#                     `void *poolstack[3]` — the element size, the element OFFSET and the
#                     access width all frozen at four. So every allocation after the first
#                     pool-frame restore comes back with its top half gone, on the SECOND
#                     simulation step, which is why the first one looked clean and why a gdb
#                     watchpoint on `pool_ptr` found in one command what reading could not.
#
#                     ⚠ HOW IT WAS ACTUALLY FOUND, because it is the reusable part: a
#                     conditional hardware watchpoint. `watch -l pool_ptr if
#                     ((unsigned long)pool_ptr) < 0x100000000` names the exact line that
#                     truncates, first try, on a defect whose symptom is three files away.
#                     Two rounds of reading the allocator had not.

# LP64-STRIDE-AS-ADDR 2026-08-28 (twelfth session, later): A POINTER STRIDE SPELLED AS AN ADDRESS
#                     INSIDE THE STRUCT — one line, and it was the last thing between the collision
#                     side and a clean LP64 run.
#
#                       pCVar5 = (CxSmallSortRep *)&pCVar5->mAABBMarkers[0].mOrdinate;
#
#                     `_Update` walks three axes reading `mAABBMarkers[0]` and `[1]` off an
#                     advancing pointer, so each iteration must step TWO markers. Ghidra spells
#                     that step as whatever address happens to be two markers along, and at i386
#                     the two coincide — offsetof(mAABBMarkers)=32 plus offsetof(mOrdinate)=8 is
#                     40, and 2*sizeof(CxSmallSortMarker) is also 40. At LP64 they diverge: 56+16
#                     is 72 against 2*40 = 80. From the second axis on every marker is 8 bytes
#                     adrift, and the SEGV lands in MoveStartMarkerDown reading `inMarker->mRep`,
#                     three hundred lines and two calls away.
#
#                     CONFIRMED, NOT INFERRED: the shipped amd64 `_Update` does `add $0x50,%rbx`.
#                     0x50 is 80.
#
#                     ⚠ AND A CONFIDENT HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG FIRST, AGAIN. The obvious suspect was
#                     the unrolled init loop in `CxSmallSort::New`, which walks the rep array with
#                     a baked `iVar9 + 0x98` (0x98 = sizeof(CxSmallSortRep) at i386) and a baked
#                     `-0x18`. It looks exactly like the defect and it is one. Patching all ten of
#                     its substitutions changed NOTHING — same SEGV, same address. The single line
#                     above, alone, cleared it. Bisect before believing: that is three for three
#                     this session.
#
#                     ★ THE SPELLING THAT KEEPS BYTE-IDENTITY, AND IT IS THE REUSABLE PART.
#                     `(char *)p + 2 * sizeof(marker)` is the obvious repair and it is NOT
#                     byte-identical — gcc re-allocates registers across the whole of `_Update`,
#                     508 differing instructions, for what is the same address. Four such spellings
#                     were tried and all four differed. What works is to KEEP THE EXPRESSION GHIDRA
#                     WROTE and add a correction term that is provably zero on the shipped target:
#
#                       (char *)&p->mAABBMarkers[0].mOrdinate
#                           + (2 * (int)sizeof(*(CxSmallSortMarker *)0)
#                              - (int)((char *)&((CxSmallSortRep *)0)
#                                        ->mAABBMarkers[0].mOrdinate - (char *)0))
#
#                     At i386 the delta is 40 - 40 = 0 and gcc folds it away entirely, so the
#                     object is unchanged; at LP64 it is 80 - 72 = +8 and the stride is right.
#                     ANCHOR ON THE ORIGINAL AND CORRECT BY A ZERO-AT-i386 DELTA is a general
#                     technique for this whole family, and it is what makes the byte-identity gate
#                     survivable where a rewritten expression would not be.
#
#                     ⚠ A TOOL THAT REPORTED A REPAIR IT DID NOT MAKE. `path` was the output file
#                     and the new rule shadowed it with the FIELD path it was building, so the
#                     repaired text went to a file named `mAABBMarkers[0].mOrdinate` in the working
#                     directory. The tool printed "4 repaired", CxSmallSort.c was untouched on
#                     disk, and the harness kept reporting the same SEGV against a fix that the
#                     report said had landed. Diff the SOURCE; do not read the summary.
#
#                     WHERE THIS LEAVES THE SCENES. All three now converge on the SAME remaining
#                     work, having run through world creation, the framework, every pool, the
#                     interaction table and the whole broadphase: MdtPartition 7, MdtMainLoop 5,
#                     McdBox 5, MstUtils 4, McdBatch 2, McdSphyl 1. The McdBox and McdSphyl sites
#                     are the derived-field accessors of LP64-DERIVED-FIELDS, so that class is now
#                     ON the critical path rather than merely counted.

# LP64-FIELD-OFFSETS 2026-08-28 (twelfth session, later): A STRUCT FIELD ADDRESSED BY A BAKED BYTE
#                     OFFSET. 22 sites repaired, and the class turned out to be a quarter the size
#                     layout_check.py reports because that column is two classes added together.
#
#                       *(code **)((char *)pMVar1 + 200) = McdModelUpdatePathCompatible;
#                       McdModelPairManagerSetPoolFullHandler(*(void **)((char *)pMVar1 + 0x2c), ...);
#
#                     200 and 0x2c are CxSmallSort's `mAABBUpdateFn` and `mManager` ON i386; at
#                     LP64 they are 392 and 80. The repair is a PURE ADDRESS RE-SPELLING —
#                     `*(T *)((char *)p + K)` becomes `*(T *)&p->FIELD`, so the cast and therefore
#                     the expression's type are untouched and only the address computation moves
#                     from a number to something the compiler recomputes per target.
#                     tools/fix_literal_offsets.py.
#
#                     ⚠ layout_check.py's "OFFSET 128 across 10 objects" IS TWO CLASSES. Only 27
#                     are struct-field offsets (21 in McdSpace.c, 6 in ReadWriteKeaInputToFile.c).
#                     The other ~100 are Ghidra's INVENTED STACK FRAMES — `(int)&local + K`, MdtBcl
#                     70 and MdtMainLoop 25 — which are a decompilation defect, not a layout one,
#                     and do not belong in that bucket at all. Quoting 128 as LP64 exposure
#                     overstates it by 4.7x.
#
#                     THE DECLARED TYPE IS THE WRONG ONE, FOR THE FOURTH TIME. `pMVar1` is declared
#                     `McdSpaceID` = `McdSpace *`, and McdSpace is an OPAQUE handle — the object is
#                     really a CxSmallSort. So the concrete type is inferred from the offsets: the
#                     struct on which EVERY offset used against that declared type in the file
#                     lands on a real field. For McdSpace.c that set is {4, 0xc, 0x18, 0x1c, 0x24,
#                     0x2c, 0xc8, 0xcc} and CxSmallSort is the only complete type covering it.
#                     Requiring ALL of them is what makes it a measurement — one offset alone fits
#                     dozens of structs, as the tool's own decline lines show.
#
#                     CONFIRMED AGAINST THE SHIPPED 64-BIT BUILD: amd64 McdSpaceAxisSortCreate
#                     touches displacements 0x50 and 0x188, which are exactly mManager (80) and
#                     mAABBUpdateFn (392) measured from kd_types.h's own declaration. Two
#                     independent 64-bit sources agreeing on a layout the i386 build cannot express.
#
#                     ⚠ PARAMETERS ARE NOT DECLARATIONS, and missing that repaired 2 of 21 sites.
#                     Ghidra writes locals as `T x;` at the top of a body and parameters in the
#                     signature; a locals-only lookup found `pMVar1` and missed `s` and `space` in
#                     every other function of the same file. The other 19 sites were reported as
#                     "declined" for want of a type written down three lines above them.
#
#                     ⚠ AND 1,545 SITES ARE OUT OF SCOPE, NOT DECLINED. Most `*(T *)(x + K)` bases
#                     are `void *` or `undefined4 *` — a type that carries no structure tells you
#                     nothing, and reporting them as refusals buries the ones that can be repaired.
#                     They are counted on their own line.

# LP64-TWO-REMAIN    2026-08-29 (twelfth session): WHERE THE LP64 WORK ACTUALLY STANDS. All three
#                     scenes are down to TWO distinct defects between them, from a harness that
#                     started the session failing on the FIRST STATEMENT of the first scene.
#
#                       scene_chain              1   MdtBcl.c:519
#                       scene_boxes_on_plane     3   MstUtils.c:91,97 + McdBatch.c:829
#                       scene_ragdoll            1   MdtBcl.c:519
#
#                     ⚠ McdBatch.c:829 is a CONSEQUENCE, not a defect: `*resultCount = 0` where
#                     resultCount was handed in through one of MstUtils' frame slots. Repairing
#                     MstUtils removes it. The same was true of ragdoll's MstUtils pair, which
#                     vanished when McdBox's derived-field reads were repaired — most of what looks
#                     like breadth here is one defect seen from several call sites.
#
#                     1. MdtBcl — A `void *` API, AND THE HEADERS CANNOT TYPE IT.
#
#                          if (... *(int *)((int)constraint + 0xb4) ...)
#
#                     `MdtBcl.h` declares `void *const constraint` — MathEngine passes an opaque
#                     pointer and the Bcl layer knows the layout by offset, so this is not Ghidra
#                     losing a type, it is the ORIGINAL API. Measured: no struct with a complete
#                     body covers the offsets, per file (54 of them) OR per function (18 for
#                     MdtBclAddBSJoint), even with array elements and nested members expanded. The
#                     per-file pooling is wrong here anyway — each MdtBclAdd* function takes a
#                     DIFFERENT constraint type — so the inference has to be per function, and per
#                     function it still finds nothing.
#
#                     ★ THE TYPE *IS* DERIVABLE, AND THE EVIDENCE IS THE INSTALLER — this is the
#                     lead to pick up, not the disassembly alignment below. Each MdtBclAdd* is
#                     installed as a callback on the very type it takes:
#
#                       MdtBSJoint.c:54   (pMVar1->head).bclFunction = MdtBclAddBSJoint;
#
#                     so `constraint` is an `MdtBSJoint *`. That resolves ALL SIXTEEN of them
#                     mechanically — MdtBclAddContact <- MdtContact, MdtBclAddHinge <- MdtHinge,
#                     and so on — with no guessing and no cross-compiler alignment.
#
#                     ⚠ WHAT BLOCKS IT IS THE OFFSET MAP, NOT THE TYPE. With the installer types
#                     the offsets still do not all land: MdtBclAddContact maps 27 of 31,
#                     MdtBclAddBSJoint only 4 of 18, and `0xb4`/`0xb8` miss for EVERY ONE of the
#                     sixteen — a uniform miss on a shared `head` field, which is a nesting-depth
#                     limit in `field_paths` rather than a wrong type. It recurses ONE level into a
#                     nested struct; `MdtConstraintHeader` needs more. ⚠ Raising the limit naively
#                     breaks the mapper outright (it returns ZERO offsets) because the cycle guard
#                     caches an empty dict under `(tag, depth)` and a deeper walk re-enters it.
#                     Fix the guard first, then raise the depth, then re-measure the sixteen.
#
#                     The disassembly route — `MdtBclAddBSJoint` is a plain C name in
#                     MdtBcl/MdtBcl.obj with its LP64 displacements right there — is the FALLBACK,
#                     and it is worse: aligning two compilers' instruction streams is guesswork
#                     that fails silently. Prefer the installer.
#
#                     ⚠ AND MstUtils IS NOT A PASS-SIZED JOB — MEASURED, NOT ESTIMATED. Its slots
#                     are the SINGLE-SHIFT case that ghidra_clean.py's AREA rule deliberately
#                     excludes, and that exclusion is load-bearing: the rule's own note records
#                     that extending it to one shift "changed five of those seven objects and
#                     knocked MstUtils out of the build entirely." The shape is 124 sites across 8
#                     objects, FIVE of them released and validated (IxConvexTriList,
#                     IxCylinderTriList, IxSphylPrimitives, IxBoxTriList, IxSphereTriList). It is a
#                     PIPELINE change, so `metoolkit_decomp/src` moves and byte-identity CANNOT be the
#                     gate — the repair gives the scratch its own storage, which is a different
#                     stack layout by construction. Validating it means the nine gates plus the
#                     behavioural harnesses on those five. Do not attempt it as an increment.
#
#                     2. MstUtils — GHIDRA'S INVENTED STACK FRAMES.
#
#                          *(McdBatchContext **)((kd_iptr)aiStack_9cb0 + 0x14) = b->context;
#
#                     Arguments routed through a fabricated local array at computed offsets — the
#                     class `check_frame_bounds.py` exists for. At LP64 the slot offsets are wrong
#                     because the pointers doubled. ⚠ THIS IS A DECOMPILATION DEFECT, NOT A LAYOUT
#                     ONE, and the fix belongs upstream in the dump: recover.py already converts
#                     this shape to named `kd_frameslot_` arrays, which are in bounds at both
#                     widths — it converted all 44 of MdtPartition's. MstUtils' were not converted,
#                     so the question is why that conversion declined here, not how to patch the
#                     output.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE ERROR COUNTS MOVE BETWEEN RUNS. ASan runs with -fsanitize-recover,
#                     so how far a scene gets changes how many errors it reports; ragdoll has shown
#                     1 and 3 on consecutive runs of the same binary. READ THE SITE LIST.

# LP64-VISITED-FLAG  2026-08-29 (twelfth session): ★ ONE LITERAL BYTE OFFSET WAS THE WHOLE
#                     PARTITIONER FAILURE. scene_chain went from 25 AddressSanitizer errors to ONE.
#
#                       if ((*(byte *)((int)pMVar9->data + 0x1ec) & 2) == 0) goto ...
#
#                     0x1ec is 492 is `offsetof(MdtBody, flags)` at i386. At LP64 it is 556. So
#                     `MdtUpdatePartitions`'s VISITED test reads a byte 64 short of the flag, no
#                     body is ever seen as visited, the root loop re-seeds one already placed in
#                     partition 0, and the failure surfaces as `addedBodies[12]` overflowing a
#                     12-element buffer.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THAT SYMPTOM LOOKS NOTHING LIKE A LAYOUT DEFECT. This entry's own
#                     predecessor recorded the remaining errors as "a COUNT overrunning ... the
#                     traversal is visiting some of them more than once, so the next defect is in
#                     the partitioner's BOOKKEEPING — arrayIdPartition, the `flags & 2` visited
#                     test, or the MeDict walk — and NOT in any allocation." The mechanism named
#                     was right and the CATEGORY was wrong: it is the visited test, and the reason
#                     it fails is a baked byte offset, which is a layout defect after all. Measuring
#                     `offsetof(MdtBody, flags)` at both widths settled it in one command; two
#                     rounds of reading the traversal logic had not.
#
#                     ⚠ TWO PROBES THAT MISLED BEFORE THAT ONE, both worth knowing:
#                       * `local_3c = (MdtBody *)((kd_iptr)&(local_3c->keaBody).tag + 1);` is an
#                         integer increment spelled as an in-struct address and looks exactly like
#                         LP64-STRIDE-AS-ADDR. It is NOT a defect: offsetof(MdtBody, keaBody.tag)
#                         is 0 on BOTH targets, so it really is `+ 1` everywhere. Measured, not
#                         assumed.
#                       * adding an fprintf moved the failure, which reads like stack corruption
#                         and sent me looking at Ghidra's invented frames. MdtPartition has none of
#                         the raw `aiStack_N + K` form — recover.py had already converted all 44 of
#                         its slots to named `kd_frameslot_` arrays, which are in bounds at both
#                         widths. The probe was simply perturbing WHICH out-of-bounds byte got hit.
#
#                     THE REPAIR is fix_literal_offsets.py extended to a MEMBER-EXPRESSION base.
#                     `pMVar9->data` is a `void *` and carries no type of its own; what names it is
#                     the SAME FIELD read into a typed local elsewhere in the function —
#                     `pMVar3 = rootNode->data;` with `pMVar3` declared `MdtBody *`. The FIELD is
#                     the key, not the variable: `rootNode`, `node` and `pMVar9` are three names for
#                     the same kind of node.
#
#                     ⚠ AND THE CAST MAY NOT BE ELIDED FOR THAT SHAPE. The tool drops the cast when
#                     the base's type already IS the concrete type, which is right for a bare
#                     variable and wrong here — the inferred type belongs to what the member POINTS
#                     AT, so eliding it emits `(pMVar9->data)->flags`, which does not compile. The
#                     site then declines, and a decline reads like the repair being wrong rather
#                     than the spelling. All three candidate spellings were byte-identical once the
#                     cast was kept.
#
#                     WHERE THE SCENES STAND: scene_chain ONE error (MdtBcl.c:519),
#                     scene_boxes_on_plane three, scene_ragdoll three — and ragdoll's three are all
#                     the invented-stack-frame class in MstUtils, which is a DECOMPILATION defect
#                     and belongs upstream in the dump. Every MdtPartition and MdtMainLoop site is
#                     gone. One sibling site remains at MdtPartition.c:334, in a function that has
#                     no typed read of `->data` to infer from.

# LP64-ALLOCA-STRIDE 2026-08-29 (twelfth session): THE BAKED ELEMENT SIZE ON THE STACK. `alloca` is
#                     not `MeMemoryAPI.create`, so none of fix_baked_sizeof's patterns saw it:
#
#                       addedBodies = (MdtBody **)(kd_alloca_x =
#                           (char *)alloca((size_t)(po->maxBodies) * 4 + 0));
#
#                     `4` is `sizeof(MdtBody *)` at i386. These are arrays of POINTERS, so at LP64
#                     they come back HALF SIZE and every write past the midpoint runs off the end.
#                     That is what `MdtUpdatePartitions` was still failing on after its arena was
#                     repaired — at four different lines, none of them near the allocation.
#
#                     The element type is written down in the CAST that consumes the block
#                     (`(MdtBody **)`) or in the declaration of the variable it lands in, so nothing
#                     is guessed. 24 sites carry a baked multiplier; 5 are repaired, and the
#                     declines are honest: `float * is 4, the stride is 12` is a MeVector3 buffer
#                     whose variable is typed `float *`, and five have no type to take at all.
#
#                     ⚠ CONFIRM-ONLY, NEVER VETO, because the count is a RUNTIME value and MSVC
#                     strength-reduces the multiply — the same rule LP64-WRONG-TYPE records.
#
#                     EFFECT: scene_chain 11 -> 3 errors and scene_ragdoll's collision sites are
#                     gone entirely.
#
#                     ★ AND WHAT IS LEFT IN MdtUpdatePartitions IS NOT THIS CLASS. The arena is now
#                     sized EXACTLY right — 624 bytes for 12 bodies and 12 constraints, which is
#                     96 + 12*36 + 12*8 to the byte, and every array lands where it should. The
#                     three remaining errors are a COUNT overrunning: `numAddedBodies` passing
#                     `maxBodies` and `po->info + po->nPartitions` reaching the end of the block.
#                     With twelve bodies the traversal is visiting some of them more than once, so
#                     the next defect is in the partitioner's BOOKKEEPING — `arrayIdPartition`,
#                     the `flags & 2` visited test, or the MeDict walk — and not in any allocation.
#                     Diagnose it fresh; do not assume it is another baked size.

# LP64-ARENA-CARVE   2026-08-29 (twelfth session): ONE ALLOCATION CARVED INTO ARRAYS WITH BAKED
#                     STRIDES — and ★ A CORRECTION: this file and STATE.md both predicted the class
#                     would "almost certainly not" survive the byte-identity gate. IT DOES. That is
#                     the THIRD wrong prediction of that kind in one session, all three made by
#                     reasoning about what gcc would emit instead of asking it.
#
#                     `MdtPartOutCreateFromChunk` asks for one block and cuts it into seven arrays:
#
#                       pMVar1 = MeChunkGetMem(chunk, maxBodies * 0x20 + 0x40 + maxConstraints * 4);
#                       pppMVar2 = &pMVar1[1].constraints + maxBodies;   /* MdtBaseConstraint *** */
#                       pMVar1->bodiesSize = (int *)pppMVar2;
#                       pppMVar2 = pppMVar2 + maxBodies;                 /* steps sizeof(ptr) */
#
#                     Three things frozen at i386, all wrong at LP64:
#                       0x40  sizeof(MdtPartitionOutput)      64 here,  96 there
#                       0x20  the per-body total              32 here,  36 there — `bodies` is a
#                             POINTER array and the other four are `int`
#                       the cursor is POINTER-sized, so `+ maxBodies` steps 4 here and 8 there:
#                       right for the one pointer array it walks, wrong for the four `int` ones
#
#                     Every downstream `po->bodies[i]` is CORRECT CODE indexing arrays that overlap
#                     each other, which is why the reports land in MdtUpdatePartitions and
#                     MdtPackAllPartitions with nothing pointing at the carve-up.
#
#                     ★ WHY IT IS BYTE-IDENTICAL, AND WHY THE OBVIOUS ATTEMPT IS NOT. Rewriting each
#                     expression outright fails: `info` and `constraints` SHARE the subexpression
#                     `(pppMVar2 + maxBodies)`, and rewriting both changes what gcc
#                     common-subexpression-eliminates. Measured: five of the six edits compose and
#                     the sixth breaks it; each of the six is byte-identical ALONE. Keeping the
#                     original expression verbatim and adding a delta that is ZERO at i386 —
#
#                       (char *)(pppMVar2 + maxBodies)
#                           + maxBodies * (int)(sizeof(int) - sizeof(MdtBaseConstraint **))
#
#                     — leaves the shared subexpression intact, folds away here, and corrects the
#                     stride elsewhere. All six then compose. ANCHOR-AND-CORRECT, the same
#                     technique LP64-STRIDE-AS-ADDR found, is now two for two on cases that looked
#                     unfixable under the gate.
#
#                     THE RULE, DERIVABLE AND NOT YET GENERALISED: `X->field = (T *)cursor;` NAMES
#                     the element type of the array at the cursor, so the next advance must be
#                     `count * sizeof(T)`. Every element type in a carve-up is written down in the
#                     cast that consumes it; nothing needs guessing. The total is their sum plus the
#                     header.
#
#                     ⚠ SCOPE IS ONE OF SIX. `MeChunkGetMem` carve-ups exist in MdtPartition (this
#                     one, repaired), MdtMainLoop's MdtKeaConstraintsCreateFromChunk, and FOUR in
#                     MdtWorld. The others are NOT this shape — MdtMainLoop accumulates a dozen
#                     sizes across thirty lines with alignment rounding and branches before it
#                     allocates — and covering them needs a static analysis, not a pattern match.
#                     tools/fix_arena_carve.py keys its edits to exact source text and applies them
#                     ALL OR NOTHING, because a half-carved arena overlaps differently rather than
#                     not at all and would still pass byte-identity piece by piece.
#
#                     EFFECT: scene_chain 25 -> 3 errors, scene_ragdoll 19 -> 3, and ragdoll's three
#                     are ALL the invented-stack-frame class (MstUtils, with McdBatch as a
#                     consequence), which is a DECOMPILATION defect and belongs upstream in the dump
#                     rather than in any post-pass.

# LP64-DERIVED-FIELDS 2026-08-29 (twelfth session): ★ REPAIRED — 186 of 281 sites, and the scenes
#                     now fail ONLY in dynamics. Supersedes the entry of the same name below, which
#                     characterised the class and predicted the shape correctly.
#                     tools/fix_derived_fields.py.
#
#                     UT2004's geometry types derive from McdGeometry by PREFIXING it and Ghidra
#                     has only `McdGeometry *`, so it addresses a derived field as an index past
#                     the base: byte `k * 16 + {0,4,8,12}` at i386, which lands exactly right. At
#                     LP64 the base is 32 and its fields are at {0,8,16,24}, and those move
#                     DIFFERENTLY from the derived struct's own layout:
#
#                       McdBox at LP64   m_g 0..32   mR[3] 32..44   mRadius 44..48
#                         g[1].mRefCtAndID -> 32   mR[0]   correct, BY LUCK
#                         g[1].prev        -> 40   mR[1] is at 36        WRONG
#                         g[1].next        -> 48   past the end of the struct
#                         g[1].frame       -> 56   past the end of the struct
#
#                     ★ THE ACCESS TYPE HAS TO CHANGE TOO, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS NOT AN ADDRESS
#                     RE-SPELLING LIKE LP64-FIELD-OFFSETS. The recovery stores through the BASE
#                     field's type — `pMVar4[1].prev` is a POINTER — into what is really a 4-byte
#                     MeReal. At i386 both are four bytes and only the bytes matter; at LP64 the
#                     pointer is eight and would overwrite the next field. So three shapes each
#                     need the VALUE adapted, and the candidates are offered to the compiler rather
#                     than chosen by reasoning:
#
#                       *(float *)&(g[1].F)          -> *(float *)&((C *)g)->PATH   (cast survives)
#                       p[1].F = (T)KD_FBITS(EXPR);  -> ((C *)p)->PATH = EXPR;
#                       p[1].F = VAR;                -> ((C *)p)->PATH = *(__typeof__(...) *)&VAR;
#                       VAR = p[1].F;                -> VAR = (__typeof__(VAR))KD_FBITS(((C *)p)->PATH);
#
#                     The last two are mirror images: Ghidra types the local as `McdGeometryID`
#                     because the base field is, and every use spells it `*(float *)&pMVar1` — a
#                     float in a pointer's clothing. Re-laundering keeps the bits, and at 64-bit
#                     they sit in the low four bytes where `*(float *)&` finds them.
#
#                     ★ AND HERE THE BYTE-IDENTITY GATE IS STRONG, unlike in LP64-WRONG-TYPE. There
#                     any type of the right i386 size passed, which is how seven wrong ones shipped.
#                     HERE a wrong field is a different ADDRESS and a wrong type is a different
#                     STORE WIDTH, and either changes the i386 object. Every one of the 186 was
#                     compiled and compared; the 95 declines are declines, not guesses.
#
#                     ⚠ TWO MEASUREMENT BUGS THE SELF-CHECKS CAUGHT, both of which would have read
#                     as "there is nothing here":
#                       * `MeVector3 center;` is a `MeReal[3]` BEHIND A TYPEDEF, so scanning for
#                         `TYPE NAME[N];` recorded only its first element. McdTriangleList's map
#                         then had holes at +20 and +24, its nine offsets could not all land, the
#                         concrete type came back AMBIGUOUS and all 70 sites declined — a decline
#                         that looks like a decision. Array-ness is now measured (`sizeof(f)` over
#                         `sizeof(f[0])`), with an `esz >= 2` floor because GNU C gives
#                         `sizeof(void)` and `sizeof(function)` as 1 and a function-pointer member
#                         would otherwise read as an array of its own byte count.
#                       * registering the bare member name at the same offset as element 0 let `mR`
#                         win over `mR[0]`, and `p->mR` is not an assignable lvalue.
#
#                     WHAT IS LEFT, 95 sites in two groups:
#                       44  the concrete type is genuinely AMBIGUOUS — IxBoxTriList,
#                           IxSphereTriList, IxSphylPrimitives, McdPlaneIntersect,
#                           McdGjkMaximumPoint. An Ix* function handles TWO geometries, so the
#                           per-file inference cannot work and these need per-SITE typing.
#                       51  inside resolved objects, declined on byte-identity: McdAggregate 28,
#                           McdTriangleList 12, McdConvexMesh 6, McdCylinder 3, McdBox 2.
#
#                     THE RESULT THAT MATTERS: scene_ragdoll now fails ENTIRELY in
#                     MdtPartition/MdtMainLoop with no collision site at all, and scene_boxes has
#                     one Mcd site left. The whole Mcd half of the library is effectively LP64-clean
#                     under these scenes; what remains is dynamics.

# LP64-DERIVED-FIELDS-ORIG 2026-08-28 (twelfth session, later): ★ THE 281-SITE COUNT IN
#                     LP64-BAKED-SIZES IS AN UNDERCOUNT, and the class is not about geometry.
#                     That figure came from grepping for the FIELD NAMES of `_McdGeometry`
#                     (mRefCtAndID / prev / next / frame), which finds the geometry instances and
#                     nothing else. The shape is general: wherever Ghidra holds a BASE-CLASS
#                     pointer and the object is a DERIVED struct, it addresses the derived fields
#                     as an index past the base.
#
#                     The instance that now blocks scene_ragdoll and scene_boxes_on_plane — the
#                     LAST error in either, down from a first-statement abort — is not geometry at
#                     all. CxSmallSort::MoveStartMarkerDown holds a `Link *` and the object is a
#                     `CxSmallSortMarker`:
#
#                       *(float *)&(pLVar11[1].mNext)     i386 byte 8   -> mOrdinate
#                       *(char  *)&pLVar11[2].mNext       i386 byte 16  -> mType
#
#                     ⚠ AND BOTH OF THOSE HAPPEN TO BE RIGHT AT LP64 BY COINCIDENCE — Link doubles
#                     to 16 bytes and CxSmallSortMarker's fields move to 16 and 32, so the same
#                     indices still land. That is worth knowing before "fixing" them: the SEGV is
#                     at line 1020 reading `inMarker->mRep`, i.e. the marker handed in is already
#                     wrong, so the defect is upstream in how the marker table is built. Bisect
#                     before believing, as MCD-GEOM-FLOAT-FIELDS records.
#
#                     So the next session's search should be for the SHAPE — `base[k].field` where
#                     the pointer's declared type is a base class — not for the geometry names. The
#                     repair is the same one fix_literal_offsets.py already performs, and its
#                     concrete-type inference (the struct on which every offset lands) is the
#                     mechanism that names the derived type.

# LP64-WRONG-TYPE     2026-08-28 (twelfth session, later): ★ A CORRECTION TO LP64-BAKED-SIZES, AND
#                     THE COMMIT THAT ENTRY DESCRIBES SHIPPED SEVEN WRONG REWRITES. Every one of
#                     them passed the i386 byte-identity gate. The gate cannot see this class and
#                     never could.
#
#                     THE BUG. `fix_baked_sizeof.py` took the allocation target's type from
#
#                       re.search(r'^[ \t]*(TYPE)[ \t]*VAR[ \t]*;', text)     <- the whole FILE
#
#                     and Ghidra names a local `pMVar1` in every function it decompiles. In
#                     McdModelPairManager.c `pMVar1` is declared FOUR ways — McdModelPairManagerHash
#                     *, McdModelPairManagerID, McdModelPairManagerLink * — and a site at line 87
#                     was typed from the declaration at line 43.
#
#                     WHY THE GATE IS BLIND TO IT BY CONSTRUCTION, which is the part worth keeping.
#                     The rewrite is only OFFERED when `literal == sizeof_i386(T)`. So any type of
#                     the right i386 size is accepted, produces the identical i386 object — that is
#                     what the gate checks — and a DIFFERENT size at 64-bit, which is the entire
#                     point of the rewrite. A gate that can only see the target you are not
#                     changing proves nothing about the change.
#
#                     WHAT WAS ACTUALLY WRONG, found by diffing the two runs:
#
#                       McdBatch.c:732          4 * McdBatchPairData   -> McdBatchContext
#                       McdConvexMesh.c:281     4 * McdCnvVertex       -> 4 * McdConvexMeshID
#                       MeAssetDBXMLIO.c:294    MeAssetDBXMLInput      -> MeAssetDBXMLOutput
#                       MeFAsset.c:1464         MeFGeometryNode        -> MeFAssetPartNode
#                       MeFGeometry.c:167       3 * MeFPrimitiveNode   -> MeFGeometry
#                       MeXMLTree.c:285/286     Attribute and AttributeNode SWAPPED WITH EACH OTHER
#
#                     THE FIX IS TWO THINGS, and the second is what makes it sound rather than
#                     merely better:
#
#                       1  the declaration is read from the site's OWN FUNCTION (region_of), not
#                          the file. That alone took 102 rewrites to 126.
#                       2  the chosen type is CONFIRMED against the shipped amd64 build, exactly as
#                          the pool strides already were: this type's win64 sizeof must be a
#                          constant that same function passes. 107 of 120 confirm; 13 sit in
#                          functions that build does not contain and are counted separately rather
#                          than quietly called confirmed.
#
#                     ⚠ THE CONFIRMATION MAY ONLY VETO WHEN THE COUNT IS A COMPILE-TIME CONSTANT.
#                     `create(sizeof(T))` and `create(4 * sizeof(T))` reach the allocator as an
#                     immediate; `create(n * sizeof(T))` does NOT, because MSVC strength-reduces a
#                     runtime multiply — `n * 48` comes out as `lea (%rax,%rax,2)` then `shl $4`
#                     and the number 48 is nowhere in the function. Vetoing on those rejected five
#                     sites that were correct, including McdInit's interaction table.
#
#                     ★ AND THE CONFIRMATION FOUND A DEFECT OF ITS OWN — THE THIRD FACE OF THE
#                     BASE-VERSUS-DERIVED PROBLEM. Ghidra declares the target with the BASE handle
#                     while the code allocates the DERIVED struct:
#
#                       McdBoxCreate         reads as 2 * sizeof(McdGeometry) = 32   amd64 passes 0x30
#                       McdNullCreate        reads as 2 * sizeof(McdGeometry) = 32   amd64 passes 0x30
#                       McdConvexMeshCreate  reads as 4 * sizeof(McdGeometry) = 64   amd64 passes 0x68
#                       McdCylinderCreate    McdCylinderID is 16 and does not even divide 28
#
#                     All four are right at i386 and all four allocate too little at LP64. The
#                     two-build pin names the concrete type — the type whose i386 size IS the
#                     literal and whose 64-bit size that function is seen to pass — and all seven
#                     concrete geometry types now allocate their own sizeof. ⚠ The pin is often
#                     NOT unique and does not need to be: McdBox and McdNull are declared
#                     identically, so no size on any target separates them. The function's own
#                     name settles it, and a site where it does not is declined.

# LP64-WORD-LOOPS    2026-08-28 (twelfth session, later): A TABLE-ZEROING LOOP COUNTING IN 4-BYTE
#                     WORDS. Two in the corpus, both repaired, and the entry above them predicted
#                     this could not be done under the byte-identity gate. THAT PREDICTION WAS
#                     WRONG, and it was never measured.
#
#                     Ghidra renders `memset(table, 0, n)` as a loop that walks ONE FIELD at a time:
#
#                       for (uVar7 = (uint)(count * count * 0x1c) >> 2; uVar7 != 0; uVar7--) {
#                           pMVar4->helloFn = 0;
#                           pMVar4 = (McdInteractions *)&pMVar4->goodbyeFn;    <- steps 4 bytes
#                       }
#
#                     That is correct on i386 for one reason: every field of McdInteractions is
#                     four bytes there. At LP64 the step is EIGHT and `>> 2` still divides by four,
#                     so the loop walks twice the length of the table.
#
#                     ★ THE REASONING THAT SAID THIS WAS UNFIXABLE: the i386 code is correct only
#                     by a coincidence of field widths, so a repair must change the ARITHMETIC
#                     rather than re-spell it, and the gate rejects changed arithmetic. The premise
#                     is true; the conclusion does not follow. The trip count is a constant-folded
#                     expression, and
#
#                       (uint)(A * A * 0x1c) >> 2
#                       (uint)(A * A * (int)sizeof(*(McdInteractions *)0))
#                           / (uint)((char *)&((McdInteractions *)0)->goodbyeFn - (char *)0)
#
#                     compile BYTE-IDENTICALLY, because both fold to `A * A * 7`. Two spellings
#                     were tried and both reproduced the object exactly. ASK THE COMPILER. This
#                     project has now recorded that lesson three times in one session — the
#                     spelling of a `sizeof` product, this, and the `_UNICODE` probe before it.
#
#                     The repair is derived rather than pattern-matched: the step is
#                     `offsetof(T, F2)` — the second field the loop actually names — and the total
#                     is `n * sizeof(T)`, both compile-time constants on every target.
#                     tools/fix_strides.py. The other site is MeAssetDBXMLInput_1_0's
#                     MeXMLHandler table, whose count is a bare literal 0x30.

# LP64-BAKED-SIZES   2026-08-28 (twelfth session): AN ALLOCATION SIZE FROZEN AT THE i386 VALUE IS
#                     THREE DEFECTS, NOT ONE, AND THE THIRD IS AN ELEMENT STRIDE. The class
#                     fix_baked_sizeof.py was written for is closed; 102 allocations and 7 pool
#                     strides are now `sizeof(T)`, every one compiled and compared.
#
#                     WHERE IT STARTED. `lp64_run.sh` FAILED ON THE FIRST STATEMENT OF THE FIRST
#                     SCENE (MdtWorld.c:98) and, once that was repaired, on `MeDictInsert` —
#                     two files and one call from anything that looked wrong. The cause was
#
#                       (MePoolAPI.init)(&w->bodyPool, w->maxBodies, 0x240, 0x10);
#
#                     0x240 is 576 is `sizeof(MdtBody)` at i386. It is an ELEMENT STRIDE, so at
#                     LP64 every body in the pool overlaps the one before it, `constraintDict`
#                     comes back as garbage, and the crash surfaces in a red-black tree walk with
#                     nothing in the backtrace pointing at the pool. `ALLOC` could not see it
#                     twice over: the call is not an assignment, so there is no target to take a
#                     type from, and the size is the THIRD argument. The same regex also required
#                     the count to be ONE identifier, which missed
#                     `create(count * count * 0x1c)` — the interaction table, 58% of the size it
#                     needs, whose ASan report lands in `McdFrameworkSetInteractions`, correct
#                     code indexing a short array two hundred lines away.
#
#                     ★ THE NEW ORACLE IS WHAT MAKES THE POOL FORM TRACTABLE, AND IT IS LLP64.
#                     UT2004 v3369's `metoolkit/lib.rel/win_amd64_single/*.lib` is a 64-bit build
#                     of this same source: real x86-64 COFF, not stripped, 1,089 symbols.
#                     `MdtWorldCreate` passes 0x2b8 and 0x270 where ours passes 0x240 and 0x1ec.
#                     tools/amd64_oracle.py reads it. BUT IT IS MSVC FOR WINDOWS, WHERE `long` IS
#                     FOUR BYTES:
#
#                       sizeof(MdtBody)    i386 576   win64 696   android 704
#                       sizeof(MdtWorld)   i386 564   win64 856   android 880
#
#                     so its constants are the right oracle for WHICH TYPE a site means and the
#                     WRONG NUMBER TO PASTE. The repair is always `sizeof(T)`. x86_64-w64-mingw32
#                     reproduces the win64 column exactly, which is what makes the check
#                     mechanical rather than a matter of opinion.
#
#                     THREE FACTS PIN THE ELEMENT TYPE, AND THE PRIMARY ONE IS WRONG FOR ONE OF
#                     THE SEVEN — which is the whole reason there are three:
#
#                       1  what getStruct hands back, corpus-wide (the pool is initialised in one
#                          object and drawn from in another), following a local through the member
#                          it was just stored into
#                       2  the i386 literal must equal that type's sizeof
#                       3  the shipped amd64 build must pass its win64 sizeof in that function
#
#                     `constraintPool`'s getStruct is typed as the base `MdtConstraint` (352) and
#                     the pool is sized for the largest variant, `MdtContact` (492 -> 624). A tool
#                     that trusted its primary fact would have sized the constraint pool for the
#                     base class and corrupted every contact in the game. Facts 2 and 3 catch it
#                     and the fallback names MdtContact uniquely. ⚠ FACT 2 ALONE WOULD NOT:
#                     fourteen metoolkit types are 20 bytes at i386, and four of those are 40 at
#                     64-bit, so `linkPool`'s stride could have been rewritten as McdSphere.
#
#                     ★ AND THE ACCEPTANCE TEST TURNED OUT TO BE A PER-SITE QUESTION, WHICH IS THE
#                     PART WORTH KEEPING. This file's own tool said the rewrite is "no-op on i386
#                     by construction" and prescribed ONE spelling, `count * (int)sizeof(T)`,
#                     because CxSmallSort grew 64 bytes under the unsigned form. That is true and
#                     it is not general. The very next site wanted the opposite:
#
#                       pMVar1->bucketCount * (int)sizeof(*(T **)0)      DIFFERS
#                       (int)(pMVar1->bucketCount * sizeof(*(T **)0))    identical
#
#                     Both compute the same value on every target; which one gcc schedules the
#                     same way is a property of the surrounding function. So the tool now COMPILES
#                     each candidate and keeps whichever reproduces the baseline object byte for
#                     byte, and declines the site if neither does. "No-op by construction" is a
#                     claim, and the compiler is the only thing that can check it.
#
#                     ⚠ A HARNESS ARTEFACT THAT COSTS A BISECTION ROUND: gcc records the source
#                     BASENAME in an STT_FILE symbol, so compiling identical text out of /tmp/t.c
#                     produces a different object. The disassembly was byte-identical and `cmp`
#                     still disagreed, which reads exactly like a codegen change.
#
#                     WHERE lp64_run.sh STANDS NOW — still FAIL, and the movement is the result:
#
#                       before          MdtWorld.c:98, the first statement of the first scene
#                       now             scene_chain -> MdtPartition / MdtMainLoop
#                                       boxes + ragdoll -> one site, McdFrame.c:84
#
#                     WHAT IS LEFT, COUNTED RATHER THAN ESTIMATED. None of these is started and
#                     all three are invisible to `ptrwidth_check.sh`:
#
#                       281 sites / 12 objects   DERIVED GEOMETRY FIELDS. `*(float *)&g[1].
#                                                mRefCtAndID` is `((McdSphere *)g)->mRadius`:
#                                                Ghidra has only `McdGeometry *`, so it addresses
#                                                a derived field as an index past the base struct.
#                                                At LP64 BOTH halves move — sizeof(McdGeometry)
#                                                goes 16 -> 32 AND the field inside it moves.
#
#                                                THE MAPPING IS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN AND ALREADY
#                                                CONFIRMED, which is what makes this mechanical.
#                                                McdGeometryTypes.h defines every concrete type;
#                                                the i386 byte a site addresses is
#                                                `k*16 + offsetof(McdGeometry, FIELD)`, and the
#                                                field of the concrete struct covering that byte
#                                                is the answer. For McdTriangleList:
#
#                                                  g[1].mRefCtAndID  16  center[0]     lp64 32
#                                                  g[1].frame        28  radius[0]     lp64 44
#                                                  g[2].next         40  triangleMax   lp64 56
#                                                  g[2].frame        44  userData      lp64 64
#                                                  g[3].mRefCtAndID  48  generator     lp64 72
#
#                                                and the shipped amd64 build AGREES FIELD FOR
#                                                FIELD: McdTriangleListSetMaxTriangles reads
#                                                0x38, SetGenerator 0x48, SetBoundingBox 0x20 and
#                                                0x2c. Same for the primitives —
#                                                McdSphereGetRadius reads 0x20 there against 0x10
#                                                here, McdBoxGetRadii 0x20. So the public headers
#                                                ARE the original's 64-bit layout, measured, not
#                                                assumed.
#
#                                                Distribution: McdTriangleList 70, McdAggregate
#                                                45, McdBox 34, McdConvexMesh 33, McdCylinder 32,
#                                                McdPlaneIntersect 16, McdSphere 12, McdSphyl 11,
#                                                IxSphylPrimitives 11, IxSphereTriList 7,
#                                                IxBoxTriList 6, McdGjkMaximumPoint 4. The first
#                                                eight resolve from the object's own name; the
#                                                44 sites in the last four are per-site work
#                                                because an Ix* function handles two geometries.
#                                                Byte-identical at i386 by construction: it is a
#                                                re-spelling of the same offset, not new
#                                                arithmetic.
#                       128 sites / 10 objects   literal byte offsets `*(T *)(x + K)`
#                                                (layout_check.py's OFFSET column). ⚠ THAT COUNT
#                                                IS TWO CLASSES ADDED TOGETHER. Only 27 are
#                                                STRUCT-FIELD offsets — `*(T *)((char *)p + 0x2c)`,
#                                                21 of them in McdSpace.c and 6 in
#                                                ReadWriteKeaInputToFile.c. The other ~100 are
#                                                Ghidra's INVENTED STACK FRAMES (`(int)&local + K`,
#                                                MdtBcl 70 and MdtMainLoop 25), which are a
#                                                decompilation defect rather than a layout one and
#                                                do not belong in this bucket at all.
#
#                                                The struct-field 27 are the LAST thing between
#                                                scene_boxes_on_plane / scene_ragdoll and a clean
#                                                LP64 run: they reduce to ONE surviving site,
#                                                McdSpace.c:160, `*(void **)((char *)pMVar1 + 0x2c)`.
#                                                Everything needed is already measured —
#                                                `kd_types.h` defines CxSmallSort with real fields
#                                                and 0x2c is `mManager`, 200 is `mAABBUpdateFn` —
#                                                so the repair is `*(T *)&p->FIELD`, a pure address
#                                                re-spelling that keeps the expression's type and
#                                                is byte-identical at i386 by construction.
#
#                                                ⚠ IT NEEDS ONE NEW MECHANISM: the base is declared
#                                                `McdSpaceID`, i.e. `McdSpace *`, and McdSpace is
#                                                OPAQUE — the object really is a `CxSmallSort`.
#                                                That is the base-versus-derived problem for the
#                                                THIRD time (after the geometry allocations and the
#                                                constraint pool), so whatever resolves it should
#                                                be written once. The evidence is already there:
#                                                the value comes from `CxSmallSort::New`, and every
#                                                offset used fits CxSmallSort's layout.
#                                                One live example: McdGeometryInstance.c:135 does
#                                                `(int)frame + 0x60` for &frame->instancePool,
#                                                where the amd64 build uses 0x90.
#                        19 sites /  8 objects   WORD-WALKING LOOPS, `p = (T *)&p->field` with a
#                                                trip count of bytes/4. ⚠ SUPERSEDED — see
#                                                LP64-WORD-LOOPS above. Only TWO of the nineteen
#                                                are the defect (the rest are ordinary array
#                                                iteration, where the step IS the whole struct and
#                                                scales on its own), both are repaired, and the
#                                                warning below that the class might not be closable
#                                                under the byte-identity gate was wrong.
#
#                     ⚠ layout_check.py's NUMBERS DID NOT MOVE, AND THAT IS NOT A FAILURE. It
#                     counts offset and index SITES; it has never counted a baked allocation size
#                     at all. The static bound and the class closed here are disjoint, and
#                     `lp64_run.sh` is the only instrument that sees this one.

# LP64-REBUILT-DATA  2026-08-28 (twelfth session): A REBUILT DATA SYMBOL IS A `void *[]`, AND
#                     HALF OF ONE STRUCT READS AS ZERO AT 64-BIT. Root-caused, not fixed; this is
#                     what `lp64_run.sh` hits next on scene_boxes_on_plane and scene_ragdoll once
#                     the baked-size class is closed, and it is small.
#
#                     gen_prelude.py rebuilds an exported `.data`/`.rodata` symbol from the
#                     object's relocations and emits it with POINTER-SIZED SLOTS:
#
#                       /* MstUniverseDefaultSizes — EXPORTED .rodata symbol, 36 bytes */
#                       void *kd_MstUniverseDefaultSizes[9] = {
#                           (void *)0x64u, (void *)0x1f4u, (void *)0x0u, ... };
#
#                     MstTypes.h declares it `extern const MstUniverseSizes` — seven ints and two
#                     floats, THIRTY-SIX BYTES ON EVERY TARGET, no pointer in it. As `void *[9]`
#                     that is 36 bytes at i386 and **72 at LP64**, so a consumer reading it as the
#                     struct gets field 0 from the low half of slot 0, field 1 from the HIGH half
#                     — which is zero — field 2 from slot 1, and so on. Every other field is 0.
#
#                     MEASURED, and this is the whole chain from symptom to cause:
#
#                       ASan   SEGV writing to address 0xc8 in McdSpaceAxisSortCreate
#                              <- MstUniverseCreate <- scene_ragdoll:65
#                       i.e.   CxSmallSort::New returned NULL and the caller wrote at +200
#                       probe  KDPROBE CxSmallSort::New axes=7 nobj=0 npairs=0
#                              <- collisionModelsMaxCount and collisionPairsMaxCount are
#                                 fields 3 and 4, both on high halves
#
#                     THE DISCRIMINATOR IS THE RELOCATION, AND IT IS AUTHORITATIVE. A slot that
#                     holds a real pointer HAS a relocation in the shipped object and is correctly
#                     pointer-sized at LP64 by construction; a slot holding an integer has none.
#                     `readelf -r` on the shipped member settles it per symbol:
#
#                       McdGjkBinarySubset       2 relocations  -> genuinely 16 pointers, fine
#                       MstUniverseDefaultSizes  0 relocations  -> pure data, wrongly typed
#
#                     THE WHOLE CLASS IS FIVE SYMBOLS IN TWO OBJECTS — every rebuilt data symbol
#                     in the corpus whose slots are all literals:
#
#                       McdSpace       McdXAxis, McdYAxis, McdZAxis, McdAllAxes   void *[1]
#                                      (McdSpace.h declares each `extern int`)
#                       MstUniverse    MstUniverseDefaultSizes                    void *[9]
#
#                     The four axis constants still READ correctly on a little-endian LP64 target
#                     — the value is in the low half — so only their SIZE is wrong. The struct is
#                     actively broken. Fix: emit a 4-byte slot type when no slot carries a
#                     relocation. i386 stays byte-identical by construction (4 * N either way),
#                     which is the acceptance test.
#
#                     ⚠ NO EXISTING GATE CAN SEE THIS. check_symbol_bindings.py compares each
#                     recovered symbol's size against the SHIPPED i386 object, where `void *[9]`
#                     is 36 bytes and matches exactly. The defect only exists on a target nothing
#                     in the gate set builds for.
#
#                     ⚠ IT IS IN THE 95-SECOND PIPELINE, NOT A POST-PASS. Repairing it changes
#                     `metoolkit_decomp/src`, so it needs the full nine gates and a re-check that all
#                     145 objects still recompile byte-identical — which is why it is recorded
#                     here rather than done in the same session that found it.

# WASM-INDIRECT-SIGS  2026-08-27 (tenth session): THE INDIRECT HALF OF WASM-SIGMISMATCH,
#                     WHICH THAT ENTRY CLOSES BY PREDICTING. It was right. Found by RUNNING
#                     the engine on wasm for the first time — nothing recovered had ever
#                     executed on wasm32 before this session — and all nine i386 gates read
#                     green on every one of these, before and after.
#
#                     THE CLASS. wasm-ld type-checks DIRECT calls only. A `call_indirect`
#                     type-checks AT RUNTIME against the callee's exact type, and on wasm a
#                     function's type includes its RESULT and its ARITY. Both are free on
#                     i386 cdecl: the caller cleans the stack, so a surplus argument is never
#                     read, and a caller that ignores %eax may call a void function through
#                     any prototype it likes. So this whole class is inert on i386 BY
#                     CONSTRUCTION and no gate on the recovery side can see it.
#
#                     49 CALL SITES IN SIX OBJECTS, all reached the same way — Ghidra
#                     reconstructs an i386 call from the stack slots written before it, and
#                     `code` is `typedef int code();`, an UNPROTOTYPED type returning int.
#                     tools/code_call_check.py counts 47 of them (calls THROUGH `code`) on the
#                     pre-fix tree and 0 after; the other two are MeXMLParser's, which were
#                     already typed and merely over-supplied.
#
#                       MeXMLParser        MeXMLHandler.fn, both branches. Rendered with FOUR
#                                          arguments where MeXMLParser.h:105-106 declares two
#                                          (MeXMLCallback) and three (MeXMLParseFn).
#                       McdInteractions    helloFn (+0), goodbyeFn x2 (+4) and safetimeFn
#                                          (+0xc), through `code *` off
#                                          McdFrameworkGetInteractions' result.
#                       keaLCPSolver       17 lines of keaMatrix vtable dispatch.
#                       keaLCP_new         keaMatrix::solve (+0x10), inside solveLCP.
#                       keaRbdCore_unified platformInit (+0x10), allocateMemory (+0x14),
#                                          calculateConstraintAndResultantForces (+8),
#                                          calculateAcceleration (+0xc) and allocate (+0).
#                       keaMatrix_tester   18 lines — NOT linked, typed anyway so the gate can
#                                          read a true zero instead of a zero with a footnote.
#
#                     REPRODUCED, THREE TIMES, IN THE ENGINE — not reasoned about:
#
#                       RuntimeError: function signature mismatch
#                         at MeXMLElementProcess <- MeXMLInputProcess
#                            <- MeAssetDBXMLInputRead <- KCreateAssetDB <- KInitGameKarma
#
#                     i.e. on the `.ka` path that instances every ragdoll and vehicle, right
#                     after KarmaData/Alien.ka was read. Fixing it moved the fault to
#
#                       at MdtKeaAddConstraintForces <- KWorldStepSafeTime <- KTickLevelKarma
#
#                     the first physics tick; fixing THAT moved it back to the same function at
#                     keaRbdCore_unified.c:225, reachable only once a match has real constraints
#                     (`?map=DM-Rankin&Game=XGame.XDeathMatch&NumBots=2&QuickStart=1` — a bare
#                     `?map=` loads the level without starting a match and never reaches it).
#                     The link emitted ZERO wasm-ld diagnostics every time.
#
#                     ⚠ THE METHOD LESSON, AND IT IS THE MOST USEFUL LINE IN THIS ENTRY:
