#                     THIS CLASS WAS DECLARED CLOSED TWICE ON THE STRENGTH OF A `grep`, AND A
#                     RUNNING ENGINE FOUND A SITE BOTH TIMES. The greps were for
#                     `(\*\*(code \*\*)`. Ghidra spells one dispatch at least five ways, and the
#                     site that trapped is spelled
#
#                       (*(*(code **)((*(char **)&vanillaAMatrix))))(&vanillaAMatrix, uVar10)
#
#                     — the two dereferences split and the base in its own parentheses, which
#                     that pattern does not match. tools/code_call_check.py replaces the pattern
#                     with a PARSE: it walks parentheses, takes every group in callee position,
#                     and asks whether it dispatches through `code`. A sixth spelling is caught
#                     for free. Enumerating spellings is how this was got wrong twice.
#
#                     AND THE ONE THAT DID NOT TRAP IS THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE. McdInteractions'
#                     helloFn is slot +0, `MeBool (McdModelPair *)`, and `MeBool` is
#                     `typedef int` — so the `code` call site's `(i32) -> i32` IS the callee's
#                     real type and McdHello has always worked. It worked by luck, not by
#                     construction, and it is typed now for that reason.
#
#                     THE ONE THAT WAS AN i386 BUG TOO, which was not expected. Ghidra
#                     rendered McdSafeTimeIntersect's dispatch with a surplus argument AND its
#                     `MeReal maxTime` went through the unprototyped call, so gcc promoted it:
#
#                       before   push %ecx / flds 0x38(%esp) / fstpl (%esp)   <- float as f64
#                       after    push 0x2c(%esp)                             <- float as f32
#
#                     the exact `flds`+`fstpl` signature §4b names. Dormant in UT2004 only
#                     because McdSafeTime is not linked — KDynStep.cpp reimplements the
#                     safe-time stepper — so it was wrong on both targets and reachable on
#                     neither. This is the second time Ghidra's over-count has been recorded
#                     as harmless; it is harmless only in the cdecl sense.
#
#                     ARITY OVER-COUNT IS SELF-REVEALING, and that is what makes these safe
#                     to trim rather than a judgement call: two call sites of keaMatrix's
#                     +0x18 slot in the SAME object disagree, 8 arguments at keaLCPSolver.c:278
#                     against 6 at :651, and keaMatrix.h — which carries a _Static_assert on
#                     every slot offset — declares 6. Same shape at +0x14 (4 vs 3) and in
#                     MeXMLParser (4 vs the header's 2 and 3). The corpus was inconsistent
#                     with ITSELF, not merely with the vendor.
#
#                     FIXED AT THE GENERATOR, never in a generated file. Three changes, each
#                     taking ARITY and RESULT from a declaration in this repository and each
#                     leaving parameter TYPES alone where they were already right:
#                       ghidra_clean.VOID_PTR_CALL_ARITY        (MeXMLHandler.fn)
#                       ghidra_clean._slot_fnptr_type           (dropped its float-only
#                                                                restriction — that was an
#                                                                i386 statement)
#                       ghidra_clean.type_code_slot_dispatches  (new pre-pass; the shapes
#                                                                neither prototype_indirect_calls
#                                                                nor fix_vptr_store can reach)
#
#                     BLAST RADIUS, and it is most of the argument. Six objects change SOURCE
#                     and 140 of 145 i386 objects are BYTE-IDENTICAL. keaLCP_new is
#                     byte-identical despite its source changing — i386 cannot encode the
#                     difference — which is the same acceptance argument WASM-SIGMISMATCH
#                     makes. substitute_test on scene_chain and scene_ragdoll reads
#                     IDENTICALLY to baseline: 143/1 and 140/5, same objects, same deltas,
#                     and all six changed objects trajectory bit-identical on both.
#                     recover.py's classification table does not move (136/9/0/3/5).
#
#                     END TO END: the ut2004 browser suite is 51/51 against a NO_KARMA control
#                     built from the same tree, and 51/51 with the recovered Karma linked.
#
#                     THE RESIDUE, stated because two zeroes here would otherwise mislead.
#                     TWO gates now exist and they see different halves, and NEITHER would have
#                     caught every defect above:
#                       tools/code_call_check.py    reads the SOURCE; 47 -> 0. Catches sites
#                                                   nothing has executed, misses a call typed
#                                                   through a wrong-but-concrete prototype.
#                       tools/wasm_indirect_check.py reads the LINKED .wasm; 0 unsatisfiable on
#                                                   both presets. Catches a type NO table
#                                                   function has — so it would NOT have caught
#                                                   goodbyeFn, whose `(i32) -> i32` hundreds of
#                                                   table functions do have.
#                     goodbyeFn was caught by reading the vendor's struct; keaMatrix::allocate
#                     by running the engine. Both gates are floors, and the engine is still the
#                     only instrument that has found every one of these.

# WASM-SIGMISMATCH    2026-08-27 (ninth session; the eighth and ninth ran either side of
#                     midnight and share a date — order these by SESSION, not date):
#                     THE FIRST DEFECT THAT ONLY EXISTS OFF
#                     i386, found by BUILDING the engine against the recovered library for
#                     wasm32 for the first time. Every i386 gate reads green on it and
#                     always would have.
#
#                     gen_prelude.py could not find a prototype for `operator delete(void*)`
#                     — kd_protos*.h has no entry, because the symbol is the C++ RUNTIME's
#                     and not MathEngine's — so it fell through to its stub:
#
#                       /* TODO: fill in the C signature matching the demangled form above. */
#                       extern int operator_delete()   KD_MANGLED("_ZdlPv");
#
#                     ON i386 THAT IS EXACTLY INERT. cdecl puts the argument on the stack
#                     and lets the caller ignore %eax, so `int f()` and `void f(void *)`
#                     assemble to the same three instructions. The recovered CxSmallSort.o
#                     is BYTE-IDENTICAL either way — measured, see the blast radius below.
#
#                     ON wasm32 A FUNCTION'S SIGNATURE IS PART OF ITS TYPE. wasm-ld sees
#                     (i32)->i32 here against libc++'s (i32)->void and does NOT error: it
#                     warns, and replaces the call with a TRAPPING STUB.
#
#                       wasm-ld: warning: function signature mismatch: _ZdlPv
#                       >>> defined as (i32) -> i32  in libKarmaDecomp.a(CxSmallSort.c.o)
#                       >>> defined as (i32) -> void in libc++-debug-mt.a(new.o)
#
#                     REPRODUCED RATHER THAN REASONED ABOUT, in two files — a C caller
#                     declaring `extern int op_del() __asm__("_ZdlPv")` and a C++ main that
#                     calls it. Node:
#
#                       before
#                       RuntimeError: unreachable
#                           at wasm-function[6]:0x2dd
#
#                     CxSmallSort IS the broad-phase space's sort — McdSpace's implementation
#                     — and `CxSmallSort::Delete()` is on the engine's path, so this would
#                     have trapped the first time a collision space was torn down. It is
#                     precisely the standing order's failure mode: it compiles, no gate
#                     complains, and it is much harder to find later.
#
#                     FIXED AT THE GENERATOR, never in a generated file: gen_prelude.py now
#                     carries ITANIUM_ABI_FUNCS, four entries whose C shape is READ OFF THE
#                     MANGLED NAME (operator new/new[]/delete/delete[]) rather than inferred,
#                     and treats _ZTI/_ZTS as the DATA they are rather than as functions.
#
#                     BLAST RADIUS: all 145 i386 objects BYTE-IDENTICAL, which is the
#                     acceptance test and here it is also the argument — the fix cannot have
#                     changed i386 behaviour because i386 could not encode the difference.
#                     recover.py: 12 prelude TODOs -> 9, still 145 objects in the build.
#                     Relink of wasm-karmadecomp-debug: zero wasm-ld diagnostics.
#
#                     THE BOUND ON THE CLASS, so it is not re-derived: across all 153 SHIPPED
#                     objects the only C++ runtime imports are __gxx_personality_v0 (66),
#                     __cxa_pure_virtual (2), _ZdlPv (1) and _Unwind_Resume (1). The first
#                     and last never survive the pre-passes; __cxa_pure_virtual is already
#                     emitted `void (void)` by gen_vtables.py, which is correct. So _ZdlPv
#                     was the whole of it.
#
#                     AND WHAT THIS DOES NOT COVER, because it is the next hazard and it is
#                     bigger: wasm-ld can only check DIRECT calls. Karma dispatches through
#                     function-pointer tables (the API structs, every vtable), and a wasm
#                     `call_indirect` type-checks AT RUNTIME — a wrong prototype there is a
#                     trap nothing static will report. Nothing recovered has yet executed on
#                     wasm, so that class is unmeasured, not clean.

# VACUOUS-GATES        2026-08-26 (ninth session): TWO INSTRUMENTS THAT REPORTED THE ANSWER
#                     THE PROJECT WANTS TO HEAR WHEN GIVEN THE WRONG INPUT. Neither was a
#                     regression; both had always done it. Found by pointing each at a
#                     plausible-but-wrong directory on purpose.
#
#                     1. tools/dropin_gap.py — AND THIS ONE IS THE HEADLINE METRIC.
#                        Its seed is "every symbol the ENGINE's objects import that metoolkit
#                        defines". An engine built WITHOUT Karma imports none of them, so the
#                        seed is empty, the walk visits nothing, and it prints
#
#                          0 shipped member(s), 0 symbol(s); 0 recovered member(s) in the closure
#
#                        Every preset except native-karma and karma-ref is a NO_KARMA build.
#                        Point it at build-native and the number that measures the entire
#                        deliverable reads ZERO for the wrong reason. The only tell was the
#                        third figure — a real closure is 134 — and nothing drew attention to
#                        it. Now exits 1 with the reason. CONTROL: a directory holding one
#                        engine object that touches no Karma symbol; it refuses. POSITIVE: the
#                        real build-native-karma still reads 0 members / 0 symbols / 134.
#
#                     2. tools/reachable.py — given the FLAT object directory that every other
#                        tool in this project takes (karma-lab/allobj), it printed a correctly
#                        formatted empty table and exited 0: "0 members, 0 reached, 0 dead".
#                        It wants one SUBDIRECTORY PER ARCHIVE. Now exits 1 and says which
#                        shape it wants. Against the right tree: 147 reachable, 31 dead, which
#                        is 3b's number exactly.
#
#                     AND THE 31 CROSS-CHECK AGAINST SOMETHING COMPLETELY DIFFERENT.
#                     tools/wasm_members.py asks which archive members the WASM LINKER pulls
#                     into the binary. MdtFixedPath, MdtLinear1, MdtLinear2, MdtSpring and
#                     MdtUserConstraint are unreferenced there, and reachable.py calls the same
#                     five dead from a symbol-closure walk over the engine's objects. A linker
#                     and a static walk agreeing is worth more than either alone.
#
#                     3. AND THE NEW TOOL DID IT TO ITSELF ON ITS FIRST RUN. wasm_members.py
#                        was pointed at build-wasm-karmadecomp-perf and reported "143 of 146
#                        contribute nothing" — because -g0 strips the name section, so llvm-nm
#                        returns nothing and every member scores zero. A measurement artefact
#                        in the exact shape of a catastrophic finding, produced by the tool's
#                        own first invocation. It now refuses a build with no usable symbol
#                        table rather than printing it.
#
#                     THE PATTERN, said once so it is not re-derived: in all three the failure
#                     mode is not a wrong number, it is a WELL-FORMED number that nothing was
#                     at risk of contradicting. HANDOVER.md 12's checklist already says "make a
#                     silent check fail on purpose before believing it"; these are three more
#                     instances, and the cheapest test is to give the tool the WRONG directory
#                     and see whether it notices.

# FULL-SWEEP           2026-08-27 (ninth session): EVERY tool in tools/ and test/ run, including
#                     the ones needing a built engine and a live match. The whole state
#                     re-measured in one pass, on the owner's instruction. What it found:
#
#                     THE FRONT END IS REPRODUCIBLE, and nobody had ever checked. A
#                     single-object Ghidra re-dump of McdSpace.o — same Ghidra 12.1.3, same
#                     gscripts, same kd_protos11.h — came back BYTE-IDENTICAL to out14's
#                     McdSpace.o.c AND .locals. HANDOVER.md 5 warns that a re-run "changes
#                     every object at once" and that out5..out14 must be kept; that is true
#                     when the PROTOS or the SCRIPTS change, which is what those re-runs were.
#                     With the inputs held fixed the decompiler is deterministic. out14 is a
#                     cache, not an irreplaceable artefact.
#
#                     THE FULL DIFFTEST REPRODUCES THE DOCUMENTED BASELINE EXACTLY. 14 pairs,
#                     200,000 each: IxBoxBox 1 count, IxSphereTriList 137 dims,
#                     IxCylinderCylinder 1 count + 20 dims, the other eleven 0/0/0. The
#                     aggregate exit is 1 — those three FAILs ARE the baseline, not a
#                     regression, and a future session should not read them as one.
#
#                     AND KD_JITTER PUTS THE CYLINDER RESIDUE IN PROPORTION. The SHIPPED
#                     library against ITSELF, inputs differing by 1e-7 m:
#                         64,033 ret_diff   9,744 count_diff   975 dims_diff
#                     Our recovery against it reads 0 ret / 1 count / 20 dims. THE REFERENCE
#                     IS AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE LESS SELF-CONSISTENT THAN THE REPLACEMENT.
#                     CYLCYL-GRIND's conclusion is not just "not worth closing"; it is
#                     "closing it would demand more consistency than MathEngine has".
#
#                     CHECK 2 RE-VERIFIED END TO END, not carried. The drop-in tree rebuilt
#                     (146 members: 145 ours + kd_convexhull, ZERO MathEngine, 33 ar d'd), the
#                     engine LINKED against it (42,813,220 bytes, 0 undefined), and PLAYED
#                     test-karma-1 for 180 s with 0 SIGSEGV and 0 Critical Error — beside a
#                     STOCK control on the same map in the same session with an IDENTICAL
#                     Karma warning profile (4 KInitActorDynamics, 2+2 KAggregateGeomInstance,
#                     2 KInitConstraintKarma). crash_ab.sh then alternated the two arms
#                     A,B,A,B at 120 s: 0 crashes, both.
#
#                     THE SHADOW HARNESS, WHICH IS THE TRUTH TIER, ON REAL INPUTS:
#                         test-karma-1, 240 s, 1,571,707 real calls
#                         Sphere/Sphere        121,121   ret 0  count 0  dims 0  overrun 0
#                         Sphere/TriangleList  1,450,586 ret 0  count 0  dims 0  overrun 0
#                         nonfinite 0 on both; worst fp deltas 5.96e-08 and 5.72e-06
#                     and KD_SELFTEST=1 on the same map: 850,177 calls shipped-vs-shipped,
#                     100% identical, worst delta EXACTLY 0. That control is what makes the
#                     row above evidence rather than a coincidence of the harness.
#
#                     try_subst.sh with all 145 substituted: RUNS, reached START MATCH.
#
#                     THREE OPERATIONAL FACTS, all of which cost time to rediscover:
#                       * try_subst.sh hardcodes $ROOT/build-subst108 and only BUILDS it — it
#                         does not configure it. Create it first with
#                         cmake --preset native-karma -B build-subst108 -DMETOOLKIT_DIR=/tmp/mt_subst
#                       * run_map.sh hardcodes -GL4ESRENDERER and IT WORKS HERE. HANDOVER.md
#                         7d and 3c both say to use -SOFTWARERENDERER because GL4ES faults at
#                         the first HUD frame "for stock too". That did not happen in any of
#                         the EIGHT matches run this session, stock or recovered. Stale or
#                         environment-dependent; the script's default is fine.
#                       * BOT-DEPENDENT MAPS PRODUCE NO KARMA TRAFFIC HEADLESS HERE.
#                         ONS-CBP2-Tropica (240 s and 360 s) and DM-BE-Clearing all loaded,
#                         reached "Finished precaching textures" and exercised 0 of 38 pairs
#                         with 0 faults. test-karma-1 is reliable precisely because 6 says it
#                         needs no bots — its load is scripted actors.
#
#                     Everything else reproduced its documented figure: layout_check
#                     151/128/23/128/369/631; assoc_scan 575 sites / 51 objects; ptrwidth
#                     armv7 0, arm64 7,714/95; wasm_check 145/145; frame bounds 0; bindings 0;
#                     drop-in gap 0; scene census 103/311/312; gate_sensitivity matching 4a's
#                     table; vptr_ab control bit-identical with BOTH wrong versions crashing
#                     on the two scenes that reach the dispatch; lp64_run failing as designed
#                     with a clean i386 control.

# THE-DELIVERABLE      2026-08-27 (eighth session): CHECK 2 OF THE DEFINITION OF DONE, WHICH
#                     HAD NEVER BEEN ATTEMPTED. The engine LINKS against a metoolkit that
#                     contains no MathEngine object code at all, and PLAYS.
#
#                     test/standalone/make_dropin_metoolkit.sh builds the tree: the qhull archive
#                     replaced wholesale by src/McdConvexCreateHull/kd_convexhull.c
#                     (1,416,194 -> 10,382 bytes, exported symbol set identical), all 145
#                     recovered objects substituted in place of their shipped members, and
#                     then every member still MathEngine's `ar d`'d out — 33 of them, listed
#                     in /tmp/kd_dropin_deleted.txt and dominated by MathEngine's demo
#                     viewer (MeViewer, Render_*, RMenu, RGeometry), MeASELoad and
#                     McduDebugDraw, all of which §3b retires.
#
#                     `ar t` over the finished tree: 145 members, ours; plus our hull; and
#                     ZERO shipped members. That is the check that makes the link mean
#                     something — there is nothing left for it to fall back on.
#
#                       cmake -B build-dropin-karma -DMETOOLKIT_DIR=.../dropin-metoolkit
#                       -> ut2004-karma-pixo.bin, 42,813,220 bytes, links clean
#
#                     And tools/dropin_gap.py PREDICTED this: it reports 0 members and 0
#                     symbols by walking the symbol closure. Prediction and measurement
#                     agree, which is the standard §3c set for itself.
#
#                     THE MATCH. test-karma-1, xDeathMatch, 8 bots, -SOFTWARERENDERER,
#                     300 s, against a STOCK control on the same map in the same run:
#
#                                        drop-in        stock control
#                       reached START MATCH    yes            yes
#                       ran to the timeout     yes (124)      yes (124)
#                       SIGSEGV/SIGABRT/assert   0              0
#                       log lines          3,381,245      2,689,312
#                       Karma warnings     4 KInitActorDynamics, 2 KAggregateGeomInstance,
#                                          2 KInitConstraintKarma — IDENTICAL on both
#
#                     The only `Signal:` line in either log is `SIGTERM [terminate]`, which
#                     is the timeout. The line-count difference is run-to-run scheduling on
#                     a 300 s match, not a physics difference; the warning profile is what
#                     to read and it matches exactly.
#
#                     A SECOND MAP WAS RUN AND IS NOT CLAIMED AS EVIDENCE. On
#                     ONS-CBP2-Tropica the drop-in build ran the full 300 s with 0 faults
#                     and THE STOCK CONTROL CRASHED — in AHUD::eventPostRender / UCanvas,
#                     which is §7d's documented environment fault. A run whose control dies
#                     is not a comparison, and neither build ticked much there (893 and 99
#                     log lines against 3.4 M on test-karma-1), which is the "START MATCH
#                     but does not tick" case §6 warns about. Recorded so nobody re-runs it
#                     expecting a result.

# ASSOC-ON-I386        2026-08-27 (eighth session): THE ASSOCIATION DEFECT IS NOT INERT ON
#                     i386, and this file and §11 item 2a have both said it is.
#
#                     The claim was "on x87 the defect is EXACTLY inert, so no gate in this
#                     project can falsify a repair — measured 0 differences in 2,000,000
#                     samples". That measurement is real and it is about REASSOCIATION:
#                     `(a+b)+c` versus `a+(b+c)` over the SAME terms in the same order. The
#                     index-PERMUTED case is a different thing and it is measurable here.
#
#                     THE SITE, with the machine read rather than inferred.
#                     McdVanillaOverlapOBBTri stores the plane offset:
#
#                       Ghidra:  *outPN = fVar9 * V0[2] + fVar4 * V0[0] + fVar5 * V0[1];
#                                                 ^[2]           ^[0]           ^[1]
#                       machine (0xa5c-0xa84, x87 stack simulated):
#                                a72 fmuls 0x4(%edi)   N1*V0[1]
#                                a77 fmuls (%edi)      N0*V0[0]
#                                a7b fmuls 0x8(%edi)   N2*V0[2]
#                                a80 faddp %st,%st(2)  -> (N0*V0[0] + N1*V0[1])
#                                a82 faddp %st,%st(1)  -> ... + N2*V0[2]
#                       so the ADD order is 0,1,2 and Ghidra printed 2,0,1.
#
#                     MEASURED with a per-function A/B against the shipped static on live
#                     difftest inputs: `*outPN` differs from the shipped answer on 1,108 of
#                     5,131 calls — 22% — at one float ULP. Reordering the two permuted
#                     sites in that object to the machine's order takes the whole body
#                     divergence 1,149 -> 206.
#
#                     AND IT WAS STILL NOT APPLIED, WHICH IS THE PART TO READ. End to end,
#                     over 200,000 difftest pairs, the reordered variant is WORSE:
#
#                       reciprocals only              48,380 bit-identical, 0 count  PASS
#                       reciprocals + both reorders   42,088 bit-identical, 1 count  FAIL
#
#                     Both are the same code with two `+` chains permuted. The explanation
#                     is that source order is not the whole story on x87: gcc 15 chooses its
#                     own stack schedule, and WHERE a temporary is spilled to memory — and
#                     rounded to 24 bits — is what actually decides the last bit. That is
#                     the same phenomenon `restore_float_text` exists for.
#
#                     SO THE CORRECTED POSITION, and it is worse news than the old one:
#                       * on i386 these permutations DO change results, so "no gate can see
#                         it" is false — difftest_pair sees it;
#                       * but the i386 gate cannot ARBITRATE it either, because the source
#                         order that matches the machine measured worse than the one that
#                         does not. Both readings are confounded by spill placement.
#                       * on wasm32/armv7/arm64 there is no 80-bit intermediate and no
#                         spill question, so source order IS the whole story and the
#                         machine's order is simply correct.
#                     The consequence for whoever settles the 575 sites corpus-wide: an
#                     i386 A/B is not the arbiter, a wasm-vs-native A/B is, and
#                     tools/assoc_scan.py's docstring saying otherwise is now wrong.
#
#                     A SECOND, SMALLER CORRECTION: assoc_scan.py under-counts. It requires
#                     a chain to mention a float-typed LOCAL, so it reported 1 permuted site
#                     in IxBoxTriList when a scan that also accepts struct-field and
#                     pointer-deref terms finds 7. Its docstring already calls the number a
#                     lower bound; this is how big the gap is on one object.

# LP64-SIZEOF         2026-08-27 (eighth session): the first LP64 defect class repaired, and
#                     the iterate-and-fix loop that test/standalone/lp64_run.sh makes possible.
#
#                     tools/fix_baked_sizeof.py. THE DEFECT is an allocation whose SIZE was
#                     frozen at the i386 value, in three spellings:
#                       w      = (MeMemoryAPI.create)(0x234);          sizeof(MdtWorld) here
#                       ppvVar2= (MeMemoryAPI.create)(poolSize << 2);  sizeof(void *) here
#                       pMVar3 = (MeMemoryAPI.create)((n + 9) * 0x28); sizeof(a vtable) here
#
#                     THE REWRITE IS DERIVED FROM TWO INDEPENDENT FACTS AND REFUSES UNLESS
#                     THEY AGREE. The ASSIGNMENT TARGET names the type — `w` is `MdtWorldID`,
#                     which is `MdtWorld *` — and the LITERAL confirms it: sizeof measured at
#                     i386 must equal the constant, or divide it exactly, or equal the scale.
#                     Neither alone is enough: many structs are 0x50 bytes, and the type
#                     alone would let a byte buffer become an array of something. 98
#                     rewritten, 34 DECLINED AND REPORTED.
#
#                     `(int)sizeof(...)`, AND THE CAST IS LOAD-BEARING. `sizeof` is `size_t`,
#                     so `count * sizeof(T)` is UNSIGNED where `count * 0x98` was `int` —
#                     different overflow rules, and gcc 15 emits a different loop. THE i386
#                     ACCEPTANCE TEST CAUGHT IT: CxSmallSort's object grew by 64 bytes and
#                     the branch targets all moved. Disassembled before assuming, as the rule
#                     says; it was a real semantic change, not drift. With the cast, all 145
#                     objects recompile BYTE-IDENTICAL.
#
#                     WHAT IT BOUGHT, run after fix_ptrwidth.py on the same copy:
#                       before          MdtWorld.c:98      heap overflow, first statement
#                       after sizeof    MallocCreateAligned  SEGV on a truncated pointer
#                       + fix_ptrwidth  MePoolFixedInit / MeDictInsert / McdInit
#                       + scaled sizes  McdFrameworkSetInteractions / McdInit:85
#                     Five sites cleared in four rounds, each one named with a file, a line
#                     and a stack. That is the loop, and it is the thing this project did not
#                     have for arm64 an hour earlier.
#
#                     THE NEXT CLASS, named so it is not re-derived: a HAND-ROLLED MEMSET.
#                     Ghidra renders gcc's word-wise zeroing as pointer arithmetic over
#                     struct FIELDS —
#                         for (i = (max * 5 & 0x1fffffff) << 1; i != 0; i--) {
#                             p->destroy = 0;
#                             p = (McdGeometryVTable *)&p->getAABB;   /* advance one word */
#                         }
#                     — where the trip count is "N * 10 words" and the step is "one 4-byte
#                     field", both of which are i386 numbers. The repair is a `memset(p, 0,
#                     N * sizeof(T))`, and the shape is recognisable: a loop whose body sets
#                     one field to zero and whose increment is the address of the NEXT field.
#                     McdFrame.c:75 and :85 are the two live ones.
#
#                     NOT CLAIMED: the scenes still do not run to completion at LP64, and
#                     x86-64 is a proxy for arm64 rather than a substitute (arm64 also faults
#                     on unaligned access). This is a repaired defect class and a working
#                     method, not a port.

# LP64-EXECUTES        2026-08-27 (eighth session), and it came from the project owner asking
#                     one question: "for the pointer issue, would testing x64 give any
#                     advantage?" IT IS THE BEST INSTRUMENT THIS PROJECT HAS FOR arm64, IT
#                     TOOK TWENTY MINUTES TO BUILD, AND EVERY DOCUMENT HERE SAID IT COULD NOT
#                     EXIST.
#
#                     THE STATED REASON, and it is wrong in the way §12's closing checklist
#                     keeps describing: HANDOVER.md and HANDOVER-WEB.md both say "nothing on
#                     this machine can execute arm64", and conclude that only a static gate
#                     is possible. The premise is true. The conclusion does not follow.
#                     **x86-64 IS THE SAME DATA MODEL AS arm64** — LP64, 64-bit pointers,
#                     8-byte alignment — so every struct that changes size on arm64 changes
#                     size here, and this machine runs it natively. The question was never
#                     "can we execute arm64", it was "can we execute LP64".
#
#                     AND IT ONLY BECAME POSSIBLE TODAY. A 64-bit link has to resolve every
#                     symbol from our own code, because the shipped metoolkit is i386-only.
#                     The drop-in gap reaching zero is the precondition. That is worth
#                     noticing: closing the gap did not just finish check 2, it unlocked the
#                     measurement for check 5.
#
#                     WHAT IT FOUND, IN ABOUT A SECOND, on the FIRST STATEMENT of the first
#                     scene — after all 145 objects compiled at -m64 with no errors:
#
#                       MdtWorld.c:98  heap-buffer-overflow, WRITE of size 4
#                       48 bytes after a 564-byte region allocated at MdtWorld.c:95
#                           w = (MeMemoryAPI.create)(0x234);
#                       0x234 == 564 == sizeof(MdtWorld) on i386.  At LP64 it is 880.
#
#                     A `sizeof` FROZEN INTO AN ALLOCATION — and that is a THIRD defect
#                     class, counted by neither static gate: not a truncated pointer, not a
#                     byte offset, not a struct index. 105 literal-sized allocations exist
#                     across 41 objects in the build.
#
#                     THE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES IT ATTRIBUTABLE, and it is built into
#                     test/standalone/lp64_run.sh so it cannot be skipped by accident:
#
#                       same sources, same sanitizer, -m32 : 900 steps, exit 0, 0 errors
#                       same sources, same sanitizer, -m64 : abort, 5 errors
#
#                     These sources are decompiled from a 32-bit binary and could simply
#                     contain bugs; the clean i386 run is what says these five are POINTER
#                     WIDTH and not the recovery. Without it the rows are unattributed and
#                     the tool says so.
#
#                     `-mfpmath=387` IS LOAD-BEARING AND DELIBERATE. x86-64 defaults to SSE,
#                     which is storage precision — the same thing wasm32 and arm64 give, and
#                     the regime the association defect shows up in. Forcing x87 holds the
#                     arithmetic identical to i386 so that any divergence is pointer width
#                     and nothing else. DROPPING the flag afterwards is how the association
#                     defect gets measured on its own, on this machine, today — which
#                     ASSOC-ON-I386 says an i386 A/B cannot do and HANDOVER-WEB.md hands to
#                     the web agent. It does not have to wait for wasm any more.
#
#                     WHAT IT IS NOT, stated so nobody over-reads a future clean run:
#                       * x86-64 is a PROXY for arm64, not a substitute. arm64 faults on
#                         unaligned access where x86-64 tolerates it, so clean here is
#                         necessary and not sufficient.
#                       * UT2004 is a 32-bit binary, so this gives no IN-ENGINE evidence —
#                         only the offline scenes. It is the depth tier, not the breadth one.
#                       * it says nothing about wasm32, which is a 32-bit target with its
#                         own separate question.
#
#                     AND IT DEMOTES THE TOOL WRITTEN EARLIER THE SAME DAY. layout_check.py
#                     bounds the exposure and cannot name a defect; this names them with a
#                     file, a line, the allocation they overflowed and a stack. Use
#                     layout_check.py to know how big the job is and lp64_run.sh to do it.

# ARM64-LAYOUT         2026-08-27 (eighth session): A CORRECTION. arm64 IS NOT 95% DONE, AND
#                     THE NUMBER THAT SAID SO WAS MEASURING THE EASIER HALF.
#
#                     §6b has said since the seventh session that pointer truncation went
#                     7,771 -> 411 and that the remainder is "an integer local or struct
#                     field holding an address, needing a widened declaration". The first
#                     half is true and reproduces (at 145 objects: 7,714 raw, 3,864 widened
#                     by fix_ptrwidth.py, 435 left across 48 objects). THE SECOND HALF IS
#                     WRONG ABOUT WHAT THE REMAINDER IS, and the diagnosis matters because
#                     it says widening declarations would finish the job. It would not.
#
#                     Classifying all 435 by what is actually inside the cast:
#                       351  a plain local or expression — dominated by *(int *)(base + K),
#                            i.e. A POINTER READ AT FOUR BYTES out of a struct
#                        59  a struct FIELD the header types as an integer
#                        24  a load through *(int *) used directly as an address
#                     Every one of them reads a pointer out of a struct AT A HARDCODED
#                     OFFSET. Widening the load to eight bytes without fixing the offset
#                     reads eight wrong bytes instead of four.
#
#                     THE DEFECT UNDER THEM, and it is invisible to the truncation gate by
#                     construction:
#
#                       struct _McdGeometry { MeU32 mRefCtAndID; McdGeometryID prev, next;
#                                             McdFrameworkID frame; };
#                         i386   4 + 4 + 4 + 4        = 16 bytes   (measured, not assumed)
#                         arm64  4 + pad + 8 + 8 + 8  = 32 bytes
#
#                     IxBoxTriList reads the triangle generator out of `trilistgeom[3]`.
#                     On i386 that is byte 48; on arm64 it is byte 96. NOTHING IS
#                     TRUNCATED — the cast is width-correct and clang is silent — and the
#                     address is somebody else's memory.
#
#                     MEASURED, by tools/layout_check.py, which needs no arm64 hardware
#                     because every layout question is a compile-time constant: the i386
#                     sizes are read by running a probe, then asserted at arm64 with
#                     _Static_assert.
#
#                       structs with a complete definition            : 151
#                       SIZE DIFFERS between i386 and arm64           : 128
#                       size IDENTICAL (the control)                  :  23
#                       BAKED   a literal byte offset *(T *)(x + K)   : 128 across 10
#                         worst: MdtBcl 70, MdtMainLoop 25, MdtPrismatic 13, MeMath 6
#                       SUSPECT NAME[k].field over a pointer-ful type : 369 across 20
#                         worst: CxSmallSort 209, McdConvexMesh 26, McdContact 22
#                       SAFE    the same over a pointer-free type     : 631 across 18
#
#                     THE 10 THAT DO NOT DIFFER ARE THE CONTROL and they are why the 52
#                     means anything: an assertion mechanism that failed for everything
#                     would report "all of them differ" and read like a finding.
#                     MdtBclContactParams, MdtPartitionParams and the rest are pointer-free
#                     and their assertions PASS.
#
#                     AND THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG, BY 4x. It
#                     reported "1,339 layout-baked sites" because its size probe emitted the
#                     literal text `%d` — a plain concatenation with a `%%` in it — so the
#                     comparison never ran and EVERY type read as differing. A check that
#                     cannot pass reports everything as a finding, which is this project's
#                     own lesson arriving from the inside. Most of that 1,339 was `row[2].v`
#                     over `lsVec3`: three floats, twelve bytes on both targets.
#                     `layout_check.py` now REFUSES TO REPORT unless it can first show both
#                     answers — `lsVec3` must read "same" and `struct _McdGeometry` must read
#                     "differs" — and re-introducing the exact bug makes it exit instead of
#                     printing.
#
#                     WHAT IS AND IS NOT SAID HERE. BAKED is a defect count: a literal byte
#                     offset was derived from a 32-bit layout and no compiler will revisit
#                     it. SUSPECT is an EXPOSURE — legitimate array indexing lives there too,
#                     and only per-site judgement separates it from Ghidra indexing a pointer
#                     through a type it CHOSE. `../metoolkit_decomp/include/kd_types.h` declares real fields with
#                     real types, so anything that NAMES a field recompiles correctly at any
#                     pointer width; that is why SAFE is reported at all.
#
#                     SO THE HONEST arm64 STATUS IS: it compiles, its symbol set matches,
#                     truncation is 95% closed, and it would not RUN, for a reason no gate
#                     was looking at until today. armv7 is unaffected — 32-bit pointers, so
#                     every layout is the one the recovery encodes, and ptrwidth_check reads
#                     0 there. wasm32 likewise. THE PORT TARGET THAT WORKS IS 32-BIT.

MdtLOD                RELEASED 2026-08-27 (eighth session), and NOT on the reachability
#                     argument the seventh session left ready to sign. That argument is
#                     still true — MdtLODLastPartition's one call site is guarded by
#                     `rowCount > params->maxMatrixSize`, MdtWorldCreate sets that to
#                     0x7ffffffc, nothing anywhere lowers it, and a breakpoint fires ZERO
#                     times in a 235 s match against a control at >100,000. IT WAS ALSO
#                     ABOUT TO SHIP A FUNCTION THAT SEGFAULTS ON ITS FIRST REAL CALL.
#
#                     `MdtWorldSetMaxMatrixSize` is a PUBLIC API, so the guard does not have
#                     to be reasoned about — it can be made true. test/standalone/scene_ragdoll_lod.c
#                     is scene_ragdoll with one line added, and test/standalone/ab_lod.sh builds it
#                     against stock and against a metoolkit with ONLY MdtLOD substituted,
#                     then diffs the trajectories:
#
#                       KD_MAXMATRIX  MdtLODLastPartition / ResizeConstraint   verdict
#                       128                     0 /     0    (the control)     identical
#                        40                     2 /    48                      identical
#                         8                   900 / 7,200                      identical
#
#                     The 128 row is the negative control on the SCENE: it says the switch,
#                     not the harness, is what makes the function run — and on the
#                     unmodified scene_ragdoll both breakpoints are 0 too. The 8 row exits
#                     non-zero on BOTH sides: LOD discards the contact constraints and the
#                     ragdoll free-falls, the scene's plausibility check fails, and that is
#                     the code working. Bit-identical is bit-identical.
#
#                     WHAT THE HARNESS FOUND, AND IT IS THE WHOLE POINT OF RUNNING IT.
#                     Before this session's repairs the object read 128 identical, 40 and 8
#                     both SEGFAULT. Three separate defects, none visible to any of the nine
#                     gates:
#
#                       1. `pdataArray = (MdtLODPartitionData *)((int)&local_6c);` — the
#                          SECOND of two chained allocas, collapsed onto a 20-byte local and
#                          then written once per body. The chain rule declined because
#                          Ghidra spells one defining use with parentheses and the other
#                          without, so it saw a single set {iVar2,iVar6} introducing two new
#                          vars at once. `iVar2` is also two live ranges — a constraint
#                          count, then the second block's size.
#                       2. `kd_alloca_iVar3` INDEXED `count` times by a loop ABOVE the
#                          statement that allocates it. ghidra_clean.hoist_early_alloca, with
#                          a matching detector in recover.py so an unplaceable one is held.
#                       3. `&(*kd_argslot_ffffffd0) + uVar7 * -2` — a frame shift left on
#                          storage that is no longer in the frame, and POINTER arithmetic on
#                          a `void **`, so it moved 8*uVar7 bytes into the alloca'd blocks
#                          the function was filling. Every store and its load carried the
#                          same shift, so it read bit-identical while doing it.
#                     Plus ResizeConstraint's block 2 (`(int)aiStack_2c + i*4 + uVar7*-2`),
#                     which is materialise_secondary_blocks.
#
#                     THE HARNESS WAS MADE TO FAIL A SECOND TIME, on purpose, so the clean
#                     rows are evidence: qsort's `nmemb` and `size` argument slots swapped
#                     reads 128 identical, 40 SEGFAULT. (8 stays identical for that one —
#                     the contact groups are too small there for the sort to matter — so 40
#                     is the discriminating setting and 8 is the volume setting.)
#
#                     LIMIT, STATED, UNCHANGED FROM THE SEVENTH SESSION: if a future engine
#                     build ever calls MdtWorldSetMaxMatrixSize, this stops being a function
#                     the game cannot enter and becomes one it can. It is now measured
#                     either way, which is the difference this entry records.

MeFAsset              CORRECTION 2026-08-27 (eighth session), and it is a repair rather than
#                     a release — MeFAsset has been IN THE BUILD since the sixth session.
#                     proven.txt already recorded that `MeFAssetCreateCopy` indexes a
#                     twelve-byte local with an asset's part count and that "what stopped it
#                     mattering is that the function cannot be called — luck, not design".
#                     Under the standing order that is exactly the shape to fix. Both halves
#                     are now fixed by the rules MdtLOD needed anyway:
#                       * `copyArray` was `(int)apMStack_3c + uVar24 * -2`, the second
#                         alloca'd block collapsed onto a local, written `copyArray[i]` for i
#                         up to the part count. It is now a real alloca.
#                       * 166 `&(*kd_argslot_X) + uVar24 * -2` references wrote 8*uVar24
#                         bytes below their own storage. The shift is dropped.
#                     Blast radius: MeFAsset.o is the only object that changed, and the diff
#                     is exactly those two repairs and nothing else. The .ka path is where
#                     MeAssetDBXMLIO passed every offline gate and killed the engine at init,

McdContact            RELEASED 2026-08-27 (eighth session). It was ATTEMPTED AND REVERTED in
#                     the seventh — it compiled, passed ALL NINE GATES, and indexed an
#                     eight-byte scratch slot as (count+1) sixteen-byte structs. What is
#                     different this time is not a better argument, it is an INSTRUMENT that
#                     can see the defect the gates could not.
#
#                     test/standalone/ab_contact.sh + test/standalone/ab_contact.c drive the recovered
#                     McdContactSimplify and the SHIPPED one with identical random contact
#                     sets in one process and compare the WHOLE destination buffer bitwise —
#                     not `ret` entries, because a write past the count is exactly the smash
#                     this exists to catch. The inputs are shaped like a real contact set
#                     (coplanar about a shared normal) with one in five left as noise, and
#                     `dims` and both `element` words are filled so the comparison is not
#                     vacuous.
#
#                       1,000,000 calls, 100.000% bit-identical, 0 return, 0 contacts.
#
#                     FOUR CONTROLS, and two of them are the point:
#                       KD_SELFTEST=1 (shipped vs shipped)        20,000  0 differences
#                       block base 0x10 HIGH — the exact defect
#                         the seventh session shipped and reverted   SEGFAULT
#                       two argument-area offsets swapped            SEGFAULT
#                       the whole argument area shifted one word     0 differences, and that
#                         is correct rather than a blind spot: the region is self-contained,
#                         so a uniform relabelling of it changes nothing.
#
#                     THE REPAIR IS A GENERATOR RULE, not an edit:
#                     ghidra_clean.materialise_unrounded_alloca. It recognises the alloca gcc
#                     did not have to round (`-(count * 0x10 + 0x10)`, both terms multiples
#                     of 16, one site corpus-wide), takes the block base from the MINIMUM
#                     constant of the anchor group rather than the bare form, tells the block
#                     from the outgoing argument area by which one is ASSIGNED to a pointer,
#                     and keys every rewrite on the ANCHOR AND THE VARIABLE TOGETHER so the
#                     other two live ranges of `iVar30` — a copy-loop counter and `list.ct` —
#                     are not reachable by it. Blast radius zero: all 143 pre-existing .o
#                     byte-identical.
#
#                     LIMIT, STATED. This is a synthetic oracle. McdContactSimplify is on the
#                     live contact path but the engine imports it and nothing else from this
#                     member, and no census has yet shown it executing; the evidence above is
#                     depth, not breadth.
IxBoxTriList          RELEASED 2026-08-27 (eighth session), and the reason it was REFUSED for
#                     four sessions was correct at the time and is now answered on its own
#                     terms. The charge was "difftest measures it diverging on 139,961 of
#                     200,000 pairs, and its address is taken by a live dispatch table". The
#                     divergence is gone. The dispatch-table fact is unchanged, which is why
#                     the release had to be a MEASUREMENT and not a reachability argument.
#
#                     synth: difftest_pair, 200,000 pairs, 163,581 touching (81.8%), 48,380
#                     bit-identical, 0 ret/touch, 0 count, 0 dims, worst delta 1.995e+00
#                     (amplified through a mesh; read the discrete columns).
#                       before this session: 1,463 ret/touch, 139,961 count, 12,060 dims.
#
#                     THE FRAME ITSELF WAS MEASURED, which is what the nrecon detector asks
#                     for and what no synthetic run had ever given it. test/standalone/bisect_static.sh
#                     substitutes ONE file-static at a time by forwarding it to a `sh_`-
#                     prefixed copy of the shipped object, so inlining cannot defeat it:
#
#                       recovered top-level only (all four statics shipped)  20,000/20,000
#                         bit-identical, 0 ret/touch, 0 count, 0 dims
#
#                     Every one of the 12 `kd_argslot_` references is in that top-level
#                     function, McdBoxTriangleListIntersect, so that row IS the frame under
#                     test with nothing else in the way. The alloca that hands the engine's
#                     KTriListGenerator its triangle buffer is in the same function and in
#                     the same row. NEGATIVE CONTROL on the same harness: the unmodified
#                     object reads 13,910 count differences in 20,000, so the instrument can
#                     fail and does.
#
#                     THE DEFECT, because it is worth knowing what four sessions of
#                     "known-wrong collision function" actually was, and it was NOT the
#                     frame: Ghidra deleted two of three stores in McdVanillaBoxTriIntersect
#                     and pointed the vector eight bytes past the end of `lsVec3 axb[3]`.
#                     ghidra_clean.X87_RECONSTRUCTIONS shape 2 has the machine code and the
#                     two sibling branches that spell the idiom out. Blast radius zero — all
#                     142 pre-existing .o byte-identical.
#
#                     A per-function A/B against the shipped static, on the live inputs
#                     difftest generates, went 1,022 differing point counts and 25 differing
#                     return values to ZERO of 5,131 calls.
#
#                     WHAT IS STILL NOT EXACT, stated rather than hidden: 1,149 of those
#                     5,131 calls still differ in `*outPN` or `*outSep` at one float ULP.
#                     That is the association defect (SEE THE 2026-08-27 ENTRY BELOW — it is
#                     NOT inert on i386, which this file used to say), it changes no discrete
#                     decision, and reordering the two sites to the machine's order measured
#                     WORSE end to end. Recorded, not applied.
IxCylinderTriList     RELEASED 2026-08-24 (third session), on the pair the census had just
#                     shown to be live. It had been quarantined since it was recovered, by
#                     the reconstructed-frame detector — "frame reconstructed by inference,
#                     unproven in a real match" — and the detector's own condition is what
#                     is now satisfied.
#
#                     real: test-simple-physics, 300 s, 71,417 calls of
#                     McdCylinderTriangleListIntersect — 9,898 bit-identical, 61,519 float
#                     noise only, 0 ret_diff, 0 count_diff, 0 dims_diff, 0 overrun.
#
#                     synth: difftest_pair, 200,000 pairs, 165,267 touching (82.6%), 46,896
#                     bit-identical, 0 ret/touch, 0 count, 0 dims, worst delta 4.292e-06.
#                     The row did not exist before this session; the pair was believed
#                     unreachable, so it had never been driven by anything.
#
#                     SELF-TEST on the same map, original against original: 186,750 calls,
#                     100% bit-identical, 0 divergences of any kind. The harness is sound,
#                     which is what makes the two zeros above mean something.
#
#                     Dead end 8 says a clean SYNTHETIC run is not enough for anything
#                     holding kd_argslot_, and that is why the real tier was run rather
#                     than the release being argued from the 200,000. This is the same
#                     evidence shape IxConvexTriList was released on.
#
#                     CAVEAT ON THE MAP. test-simple-physics.ut2 is a custom test map, not
#                     shipped content — real engine, real inputs, but not stock gameplay.
#                     Cylinder collision elements DO ship (114 of them, in stock maps —
#                     tools/find_cylinder_geom.py), and whether Epic's own maps simulate
#                     one is still unmeasured. If one is ever found, re-run this.
IxConvexTriList       RELEASED 2026-08-23, and the tenth and last pair the census says the
#                     game calls. It spent two sessions quarantined, at 46% then 18% then
#                     24% wrong in a live match, always with the same signature:
#                     `ret 1/0 touch 1/0 count 3/0` — the recovered code finding NO contacts
#                     where the original finds three.
#
#                     The cause was one argument. `code` is `typedef int code();`, so the
#                     indirect call to the engine's McdTriangleListFnPtr was unprototyped
#                     and C promoted the float `radius` to a double. The callee, which does
#                     have a prototype, read the low half:
#
#                       radius the generator received      -0     (should be 1.019)
#
#                     KTriListGenerator turns that into a sphere query against the level, so
#                     a radius of zero returns no triangles and there is nothing to collide
#                     with. The ~76% of calls that agreed anyway are the actors that carry a
#                     cached KTriList, where KTriListGenerator skips the query and ignores
#                     radius entirely. All four TriangleList objects had this; see the
#                     CORRECTION notes on IxSphereTriList and IxSphylPrimitives above, whose
#                     garbage radius happened to land enormous and therefore harmless.
#
#                     real: ONS-UCMP-ABC-ECE, 900 s, 6,857 calls of
#                     McdConvexMeshTriangleListIntersect — 5,888 bit-identical, 969
#                     float-noise, 0 ret_diff, 0 count_diff, 0 dims_diff, 0 overrun, worst
#                     delta 3.05e-05. Ten other functions clean in the same run. Three more
#                     independent matches on the same map added 2,369, 5,393 and 6,172 calls
#                     with the same verdict and every other pair clean too — 20,791 real
#                     calls, 0 structural, against 272 ret_diff and 172 count_diff in 1,876
#                     before the fix. (The last two of those ran with the harness cache
#                     isolation described under McdGjk, so the result holds under both
#                     harness configurations.)
#
#                     KD_SELFTEST on the SAME map and length, which this pair had never had:
#                     7,150 calls, 0 divergence, everything 100% bit-identical, and the
#                     match ran the full 900 s without the KHandleCollisions crash. So the
#                     harness is sound for this pair on this map, and the 46/18/24% figures
#                     were the recovery, not the measurement.
#
#                     synth: 200,000 pairs at 76.6% touching, with the engine's real
#                     triangle flags and a generator that culls to the query sphere —
#                     1 count divergence, 0 ret, 0 dims, worst 1.737e-05. Driver self-test
#                     on the same 200,000: 100% bit-identical, worst delta exactly 0.
#
#                     TWO THINGS THIS OBJECT ESTABLISHED, both about the tests rather than
#                     the code, and both now fixed in difftest_pair.c:
#
#                     The generator ignored `pos` and `radius`. Every TriangleList
#                     interaction computes a bounding sphere for the other body and hands it
#                     over, so those two arguments are an OUTPUT of the recovered code that
#                     the contact comparison cannot see. With a generator that returns the
#                     same 32 triangles regardless, a completely wrong radius is invisible.
#                     KD_GENARGS=1 now compares them directly, which is what found this
#                     after a day of reading frames.
#
#                     The triangles carried flags = 0. UT2004 sets
#                     UseSmallestPenetration | UseEdges on every level triangle
#                     (KTriListGen.cpp), and with 0 the entire edge-contact half of
#                     GenerateTriangleContact — two of its three AccumulateSphylContacts
#                     sites, and ConvexHullNSegment — never executed in any test.
#
#                     A separate defect was fixed in the same session and is worth not
#                     re-introducing: `(int)afStack_11c + -0x1c` and `-0x20`, two writes 28
#                     and 32 bytes below an 8-byte local, from collapsing the alloca frame
#                     shift off a negative offset. See check_frame_bounds.py. It did not
#                     cause the divergence above — difftest_pair gave byte-identical results
#                     with and without it, because GCC happened to leave those eight bytes
#                     free — which is precisely why it needs a text-level check rather than
#                     a behavioural one.


# --- 2026-08-24 (third session): THE ENGINE RUNS ON ALL 108 ------------------
#
# Not a per-object release; a whole-build result, and it is HANDOVER.md 7c.
#
# All 108 recovered objects substituted into metoolkit at once — up from the 8
# of 7b — and the engine initialises, loads DM-Insidious, reaches START MATCH
# under xDeathMatch and plays for 300 s. Against a stock control on the same map
# and duration it is indistinguishable: same exit status, same gametype, and the
# same PRE-EXISTING HUD fault (UCanvas::DrawTileStretched -> FCanvasUtil::
# DrawTile -> FCanvasVertex) at the same four frames.
#
# Substitution verified in the machine code, not from the link:
#   McdBoxBoxIntersect                     416 -> 1116 instructions
#   McdSphereSphereIntersect               239 ->  204
#   keaFunctions_Vanilla::calcJinvMandRHS  145 ->  355
# so recovered SOLVER ARITHMETIC is in that binary too, not only collision.
#
# WHAT IT DOES NOT SAY. No trajectory comparison — the substituted build carries
# no shadow instrumentation, so "indistinguishable" means it starts, runs and
# ends like stock, NOT that its contacts match. The four kea objects of 11 item
# 2 are still shipped, so this is not definition-of-done item 7.
#
# THE DEFECT IT FOUND, which is the part worth keeping. At 108 the engine
# segfaulted in fread inside KCreateAssetDB, before any match, with a backtrace
# made entirely of SHIPPED functions. MeFileSearch's MeDefaultFileLocations is
# `const char *[22]` and its .rodata bytes are relocation ADDENDS, so emitted raw
# the file-search loop got 0, 1, 0xc, 0x1a as directory prefixes and handed them
# to the opener. It PASSED ALL SEVEN GATES before and after — the offline scenes
# never open a .ka file, so no gate could see it. Same shape as IxSphereTriList:
# compiles, gates clean, dies on first real use. Fixed in
# gen_prelude.static_reloc_definition; test/ut2004/try_subst.sh is the bisect harness.


# --- 2026-08-24 (third session): CYLINDER PAIRS ARE LIVE ---------------------
#
# This is not a release. It is the opposite: a measurement that puts an object
# BACK in scope, and it re-opens HANDOVER.md 12 item 1.
#
# Two pairs came off the "never called on any map, in any run" list of 3:
#
#   Cylinder x TriangleList   59,366 calls   object IxCylinderTriList  QUARANTINED
#   Cylinder x Cylinder       24,267 calls   object IxCylinderCylinder compiles, never measured
#
# KD_CENSUS=1 on test-simple-physics, 300 s, alongside Sphyl x TriangleList
# 95,544 and Sphere x TriangleList 31,088 — so the match was ticking properly
# and the counts are in the same range as the pairs we already trust.
#
# WHY THIS MATTERS. 3 said of Cylinder x TriangleList: "quarantined; never
# measured. Genuinely unreached rather than bypassed — same posture, unknown
# magnitude." The "unreached" half is now false. IxCylinderTriList is held by
# the reconstructed-frame detector (kd_argslot_) — the same detector that
# exists because IxSphereTriList compiled, passed the substitute gate, and then
# segfaulted on its first live call. An unmeasured reconstructed frame on a
# pair with 59,366 real calls is the exact risk profile this ledger is for.
#
# HOW THE PAIR WAS FOUND, because the route matters more than the number.
# 3's map ask was "does any shipped asset define a cylinder collision volume?"
# and it had stood open for sessions. It is answerable from the assets:
#
#   * KFarfield.cpp's KAggregateGenericIntersect RECURSES into KIntersect once
#     per element (line 878). So 3a's "the Aggregate family cannot reach Karma"
#     is true of the AGGREGATE PAIR and false of its ELEMENTS: a cylinder inside
#     an aggregate arrives at KIntersect as Cylinder x whatever, matches neither
#     interception, and goes straight to Karma's intersectFn. Every cylinder in
#     UT2004 is built this way — KUtils.cpp:762 always wraps elements in an
#     McdAggregate, with no single-element shortcut.
#   * tools/find_cylinder_geom.py parses the packages. 114 cylinder collision
#     elements across the shipped tree (vs 107 sphere, 172 box, 32,191 convex),
#     in stock maps including AS-BP2-Acatana, DM-Insidious, ONS-CBP2-Mirage,
#     DOM-UCMP2-1on1-Smelter, DM-Urotsukidoji, DM-2019-XS-2006.
#
# GREP CANNOT ANSWER THIS and the failed attempt is worth recording, because it
# looked like an answer. FKAggregateGeom is serialised by a hand-written
# operator<< with no field names, so "CylinderElems" appears in NO package —
# and neither does "BoxElems", nor "KarmaProps". A search returning 0 on all
# 2,015 packages read exactly like "UT2004 ships no cylinders". The control that
# caught it was searching for something that MUST be there and getting 0 too.
#
# WHAT IS NOT YET SHOWN. test-simple-physics.ut2 is a custom test map, not
# shipped content (96 KB, mode 755, dated apart from the rest of the tree). A
# 300 s census on DM-Insidious — a STOCK map that my parser says contains a
# cylinder element — ticked properly (6 pairs, 49,785 calls) and produced ZERO
# cylinder calls: the mesh is there and is not simulated. So:
#
#   proven      a Cylinder pair reaches Karma and executes in volume
#   proven      shipped maps contain cylinder collision elements
#   NOT proven  that stock gameplay ever simulates one
#
# That third line is the only part still open, and it no longer blocks anything:
# whether or not Epic's own maps do it, the pair is reachable, a map that drives
# it exists in the tree, and the object serving it is unmeasured.
#
# NEXT, and it is now a runnable task rather than a request for content: stage
# IxCylinderTriList and IxCylinderCylinder into a shadow build and run
# test-simple-physics WITHOUT KD_CENSUS. Note the census reported
# Cylinder x TriangleList as function "(unidentified)", shadowed "no" — the
# harness could not name the pair's intersectFn, which has to be sorted out
# before it can compare anything. Cylinder x Cylinder already reads
# shadowed "yes".


# --- NOT proven, with what was observed --------------------------------------
# --- compiles, evidence gathered, NOT released ------------------------------
# IxCylinderCylinder  MEASURED WRONG on a pair the game calls, and — read this part —
#                     NOTHING IS HOLDING IT. It compiles clean, no detector objects, so it
#                     is in /tmp/kd_build and in the 108-object substituted build (7c).
#                     That is the gap this entry exists to flag: the quarantine can only
#                     hold an object some DETECTOR recognises. An object that is simply
#                     never measured, and is wrong, walks straight into the build.
#
#                     real: test-simple-physics, 300 s, 24,111 calls of
#                     McdCylinderCylinderIntersect — 16,545 bit-identical, 6,638 float
#                     noise, 0 ret_diff, but 3 count_diff and 925 dims_diff. That is a
#                     3.8% structural divergence rate IN THE GAME'S OWN REGIME.
#
#                     synth: 200,000 pairs, 57,381 touching, 0 ret/touch, 1 count, 20 dims,
#                     worst delta 6.914e-06.
#
#                     SELF-TEST, original against original, same map: 45,169 calls, 100%
#                     bit-identical. So the 925 are real, not harness noise.
#
#                     WHAT THE DIVERGENCE IS. ret, touch, count, position and separation
#                     all agree; only `dims` differs — 259 vs 771, i.e. 0x103 vs 0x303, one
#                     bit of the feature index in the high byte. Same contact, same place,
#                     different FEATURE LABEL. The logged cases are grid-aligned transforms
#                     (rows of exactly 1/0/-1), the axis-aligned regime.
#
#                     WHY THIS IS WORSE THAN IxSphereTriList'S KNOWN LIMIT, which is the
#                     same shape. That one was released because the game's regime was the
#                     CLEAN one — 0 divergences in 1,763,276 real calls, with the
#                     disagreement confined to deep interpenetration the game does not
#                     reach. Here the game's own inputs produce 925 in 24,111. The
#                     comparison that justified the earlier release does not transfer.
#
#                     dims feeds contact identity, so a label that flips frame to frame is
#                     a warm-starting/caching question, not a position error. Whether that
#                     matters is unmeasured. DO NOT RELEASE, and consider whether it should
#                     be pulled from the substituted build until it is understood.
#
#                     LOCALISED, so the next attempt does not start from nothing.
#                     OverlapCylCyl writes outDims at seven sites; 0x103 and 0x303 are both
#                     real values, at IxCylinderCylinder.c:1389 and :1413, and the branch
#                     between them is `if ((normInfo & 3U) == 0)`. normInfo is set once, at
#                     :1275, as `minAxis1 | cVar10 << 2` — inside
#                     `if (maxSeparation < fVar11)`, i.e. it records WHICH SEPARATING AXIS
#                     WON. So the divergence is a SAT tie-break, and grid-aligned cylinders
#                     (the logged transforms have rows of exactly 1/0/-1) produce ties
#                     constantly. That is the same class as the threshold flapping in 8,
#                     at a much higher rate because the geometry keeps hitting ties.
#
#                     EXCESS PRECISION RULED OUT, and this is worth reading before trying
#                     it. The shipped OverlapCylCyl has 216 `fsts` against our 96 — it
#                     rounds intermediates to 32 bits far more often, while ours keeps them
#                     at 80 bits in x87 registers, which is exactly what flips a tie. Both
#                     use x87, so it is not an SSE/387 mismatch. Rebuilding with
#                     -ffloat-store made it WORSE, not better:
#
#                       baseline        1 count   20 dims   worst 6.914e-06
#                       -ffloat-store   6 count   44 dims   worst 8.618e-01
#
#                     and it took `fsts` DOWN to 69 rather than up. So the flag does not do
#                     what the instruction counts suggest, and the 80-bit intermediates are
#                     not the cause. See dead end 17.
#
#                     RE-FRAMED 2026-08-24 (fifth session), and the re-framing is
#                     the result: `dims` IS NOT A MEASUREMENT FOR THIS PAIR IN
#                     THIS REGIME. The shipped library does not reproduce it
#                     either.
#
#                     Two new switches in difftest_pair.c made this measurable in
#                     1.3 seconds instead of a map run:
#
#                       KD_GRID=1    axis-aligned rotations -- one of the 24
#                                    proper rotations whose rows are exactly
#                                    1/0/-1 -- which is the regime the real
#                                    divergences were logged in. Translations
#                                    stay random.
#                       KD_JITTER=e  displace model2 by e BETWEEN the two calls.
#                                    With KD_SELFTEST=1 that runs the ORIGINAL
#                                    against ITSELF on inputs differing by e.
#                       KD_DIMSHIST=1 histogram of (orig dims -> recovered dims)
#                                    instead of four sample dumps.
#
#                     WHY THE SYNTHETIC TIER WAS READING 0.01% AND THE GAME 3.8%.
#                     It was testing the wrong DISTRIBUTION, not the wrong value:
#
#                       random rotations   20 dims in 200,000    (0.03% of touching)
#                       KD_GRID=1        6,385 dims in  52,925 touching  (12.1%)
#
#                     a factor of 380. SELFTEST on the same 200,000 grid pairs is
#                     100% bit-identical, so the harness is sound in this regime.
#
#                     WHAT IS ACTUALLY HAPPENING. Instrumenting the recovered
#                     OverlapCylCyl to print every separating-axis candidate:
#
#                       cand 10 sep -0.301695902
#                       cand  6 sep -0.301695973
#                       cand  9 sep -0.301695914
#                       cand  8 sep -0.301695913
#                       cand  2 sep -0.301695922
#
#                     Five candidates agreeing to seven significant figures. Over
#                     57,079 calls with two or more candidates, the gap between
#                     the winner and the runner-up is:
#
#                       exact tie                   4,236   7.42%
#                       < 1e-7 relative (1 ULP)    10,255  17.97%
#                       1e-7 .. 1e-6                2,458   4.31%
#                       1e-6 .. 1e-4                  833   1.46%
#                       1e-4 .. 1e-2                2,658   4.66%
#                       > 1e-2 (well separated)    36,639  64.19%
#
#                     29.7% of calls pick their separating axis on a margin of
#                     1e-6 relative or less. For an axis-aligned cylinder pair the
#                     candidate axes are geometrically DEGENERATE -- they measure
#                     the same separation -- so the winner, and therefore
#                     normInfo, and therefore dims, is decided by rounding.
#
#                     THE YARDSTICK, and this is the part that settles it. Run the
#                     SHIPPED ORIGINAL against ITSELF with model2 displaced by
#                     1e-7 m -- a ten-millionth of a metre, below f32 resolution
#                     at ordinary UT2004 world coordinates -- on the same 200,000
#                     grid pairs:
#
#                       nudge     count_diff  dims_diff  worst delta
#                       0              0           0     0.000e+00
#                       1e-12          0           0     0.000e+00
#                       1e-9           0          10     1.788e-07
#                       1e-7           0       7,798     7.997e-01
#                       1e-5           5       7,821     8.000e-01
#
#                     compared with the recovered code against the original, with
#                     NO nudge at all:
#
#                       recovered      1       6,432     8.000e-01
#
#                     The shipped library disagrees with ITSELF, under a
#                     displacement smaller than float precision, MORE often than
#                     our recovery disagrees with it -- and produces the same
#                     8.0e-01 worst delta that the recovery is charged with. The
#                     0 and 1e-12 rows are the controls: the probe is inert when
#                     the input does not move.
#
#                     AND IT IS THIS PAIR ONLY. Same probe, same nudge, grid
#                     regime, other pairs in the driver:
#
#                       McdBoxBoxIntersect                 0 of  28,401 touching
#                       McdSphereSphereIntersect           0 of  76,407
#                       McdSphylSphylIntersect             0 of  90,168
#                       McdSphylBoxIntersect               0 of  62,078
#                       McdCylinderTriangleListIntersect   0 of 147,389
#                       McdCylinderCylinderIntersect   7,798 of  52,925
#
#                     So this is not "float comparisons are unstable", it is a
#                     property of THIS function's separating-axis set on
#                     axis-aligned input. Note the sibling that was RELEASED the
#                     same week, McdCylinderTriangleListIntersect, is perfectly
#                     stable -- which is what makes the contrast evidence rather
#                     than an excuse.
#
#                     THE TIE-BREAK HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED, and it was the obvious
#                     one. Changing `<` to `<=` at all six `maxSeparation <` sites
#                     and at both sites of the endpoint min-search, one at a time,
#                     under KD_GRID=1: no change at all except at
#                     IxCylinderCylinder.c:1008, which goes 6,385 -> 7,563, i.e.
#                     WORSE. The shipped code agrees: at 0x26aa it does
#                     `fcoms -0x268(%ebp); fnstsw %ax; test $0x45,%ah; jne` --
#                     take the new candidate iff STRICTLY greater, which is what
#                     the recovery already does.
#
#                     WHAT THIS DOES NOT SHOW, and it matters:
#                       * NOT that the recovered arithmetic matches the original's.
#                         It does not -- the 1-ULP differences are real. What is
#                         shown is that the quantity being compared cannot
#                         DISCRIMINATE between two implementations in this regime.
#                       * NOT an explanation for the 3 count_diff in 24,111 real
#                         calls. count is far more stable than dims under the same
#                         probe (0 at a 1e-7 nudge, 5 at 1e-5). That residue is
#                         0.012%, against the 0.0013% IxSphylPrimitives was
#                         released on, so it is small and it is not zero.
#
#                     DISPOSITION. Do NOT pull it from the substituted build: the
#                     divergence it is charged with is a label the shipped library
#                     re-rolls whenever a body moves a ten-millionth of a metre,
#                     which is every frame. Do NOT release it either -- the
#                     count_diff residue has not been accounted for. What would
#                     settle it is a live match scoring ret/touch/count/position/
#                     separation, with dims reported SEPARATELY and read as
#                     non-discriminating for this pair, the way `nonfinite` is
#                     already read separately for NaN input.
#
#                     AND THEN THE STRONGER ANSWER, which is not statistical at
#                     all: FOR THIS PAIR THE DIVERGING FIELD IS NEVER READ.
#
#                     `dims` has exactly three consumers, and they were found by
#                     reading every one rather than by sampling:
#
#                     1. KarmaSupport.cpp:303  `triFlags = colC->dims & 0x00FF`
#                        — inside `if(!a1)`, and the comment above it is explicit:
#                        "NB: The ONLY model without an Actor must be the 'world',
#                        which must be the tri-list." So: model1 is the level
#                        TriangleList.
#                     2. KarmaSupport.cpp:319  `triFlags = colC->dims>>8`
#                        — the mirror case, model2 is the TriangleList.
#                     3. KarmaSupport.cpp:535  inside `#if 0`. Dead.
#
#                     A Cylinder x Cylinder contact is two Actors and no
#                     TriangleList, so neither live branch runs and `triFlags`
#                     stays 0. For this pair UT2004 does not read `dims` at all.
#                     (Note what the field actually carries there: the TRIANGLE
#                     FLAGS, not the "0/1/2 point/line/surface" the header
#                     documents. Karma overloads it per side.)
#
#                     AND INSIDE KARMA, across all 153 out10 dumps rather than
#                     the 109 that compile, `dims` is written by the intersection
#                     functions, copied by McdContactSimplify, and BRANCHED ON in
#                     exactly one place — McdContactSimplify, under
#                     `if (inFaceNormalsFirst != 0)`:
#
#                       if (((char)pMVar22->dims == '\x02') || (pMVar22->dims >> 8 == 2))
#
#                     i.e. "is either side a SURFACE contact". Evaluate it on the
#                     values that actually diverge:
#
#                       dims   (char)dims  dims>>8   branch
#                       0x103       3         1      false
