# test/standalone/bisect_object.sh IS THE NEW INSTRUMENT and it is what made this findable.
# substitute_test.sh works one OBJECT at a time; this drives the same scene with
# exactly ONE recovered function in the link -- the shipped member's globals
# WEAKENED, all but one of the recovered object's LOCALISED -- so a divergence
# names the function. It carries its own control and reads the FIRST differing
# step. On keaLCPSolver it read `identical` for thirteen of fifteen and NaN for
# PrincipalSubmatrix and makeXandW, and makeXandW calls PrincipalSubmatrix.
#
# ONE LIMIT OF IT, so nobody is misled: localising a symbol does not REMOVE it,
# so intra-object calls still bind to the recovered copy. "One function in" is
# really "this function and everything it calls". An `identical` row is
# therefore strong; a divergent row names a subtree, not a leaf.
#
# --- 2026-08-25 (third session): keaIntegrate_pc, narrowed not fixed ----------
#
# first@13 = 1.500e-08 on scene_chain, growing to 4.900e-04 over 900 steps. That
# is a LAST-BIT difference amplified, not a gross defect -- a different class
# from keaLCPSolver's, and the first-differing-step column is what separates
# them. Its two functions report identically because MdtKeaIntegrateSystem calls
# KeaIntegrateSystem_vanilla.
#
# THE DOUBLE LITERALS ARE REFUTED. `* 0.5 *` and `1.0 / SQRT(...)` are C double
# constants where the original has floats; rebuilding with `0.5f` and `1.0f`
# changes NOTHING (still first@13 = 1.500e-08). 0.5 and 1.0 are exact, and x87
# computes at 80 bits either way.
#
# What is left is EXPRESSION REASSOCIATION in the quaternion update. The four
# qrot lines are associated differently from one another --
# `((a - b) - c)`, `((a + b) - c)`, `(a + -b + c)`, `(c + (a - b))` -- and float
# addition is not associative, so any one of them reordered against the machine
# code moves the last bit. Read the `fadd`/`fsub` order out of
# KeaIntegrateSystem_vanilla and compare.
#
# --- 2026-08-25 (third session): keaLCP_new, SIZED and not attempted ---------
#
# 37 references to `auStack_3c` in about ten distinct shapes, plus 24 `stack0x`
# sites, and the strides are MIXED: `(int)auStack_3c + uVar6 * -3` is BYTE
# arithmetic while `auStack_3c[iVar2 - uVar6]` is ELEMENT arithmetic on a
# `uint[3]`. Repairing it means deciding the stride at each of 61 sites against
# the disassembly, and the failure mode of getting one wrong is a silent
# out-of-bounds write, not a compile error. The layout is recorded above; this
# note is the size of the job.

# --- 2026-08-25 (third session): BOTH CYLINDER DEFECTS NOW REPRODUCE OFFLINE --
#
# IxCylinderTriList -- THE BLOCKER IN "WHAT REMAINS" ROW 7 IS DISCHARGED. That
# row said the divergence "diverges in a live match and the synthetic tier
# cannot see it", and asked for "a test mesh that can be a corner".
# `KD_CORNER=1` IS that mesh, and it works:
#
#   default mesh (4x2 patch)   200,000 pairs, 165,267 touching   0 count_diff
#   KD_CORNER=1                200,000 pairs, 188,416 touching   1 count_diff
#
#   !! contact count differs at iteration 23624 (ret 0/0 touch 1/1 count 30/32)
#
# The shipped library returns THIRTY contacts and we return THIRTY-TWO, with
# `ret` and `touch` agreeing. So the live defect -- 37 count_diff in 153,391,
# off-by-one contacts on a cylinder resting in a corner -- is now DETERMINISTIC
# and debuggable without a match. The synthetic rate (1 in 200,000) is far below
# the live one (0.024%), which is expected: the corner mesh puts a cylinder in a
# corner sometimes, and the game does it constantly.
#
# TWO HYPOTHESES REFUTED, both cheaply, both worth not repeating:
#
#   * The accumulation of the average normal reads
#     `*(float *)(MStack_26c.flags + 4) = diff.v[1] + ...` -- component 1 and 2
#     of `result->normal` written through what looks like ANOTHER OBJECT'S
#     field. It is not: rewriting all three as `result->normal[i] +=` gives a
#     BYTE-IDENTICAL result, so `MStack_26c.flags` already holds
#     `&result->normal[0]` there. An alias, not a defect.
#   * Our object defines ONE local where the shipped one defines FOUR
#     (`MeVector3Normalize`, `McdVanillaOverlapCylTri`,
#     `McdVanillaAddTriCylSegmentPoints`, `McdVanillaSegmentCylinderIntersect`).
#     Also not a defect: all four are `static` in the recovered source and GCC
#     inlined three of them. Note that check_symbol_bindings compares GLOBALS,
#     so it would not have caught a genuinely missing local -- worth knowing.
#
# Where to look next: the count comes from a range, `footprint` to `verts`, that
# `McdVanillaOverlapCylTri` fills, and every contact in it is emitted. Two extra
# contacts means two extra footprint points.
#
# IxCylinderCylinder -- THE COUNT DIVERGENCE IS LOCATED, and it is NOT the
# rounding instability that explains its dims.
#
#   !! contact count differs at iteration 194376 (ret 0/0 touch 1/1 count 4/2)
#      [0] sep -0.244169116 / -0.244169161
#      [1] sep -0.0574037619 / -0.244169116
#
# The shipped library keeps FOUR contacts and we keep TWO. Our contact [1]
# carries the separation the shipped library gives its contact [0], so the
# FOOTPRINT differs -- this is upstream of the emission filter, which is
# `if (sep < separation * 0.01)` and would have kept -0.0574 against a threshold
# of -0.00244.
#
# AND IT IS A REAL DEFECT, WHICH IS WORTH STATING BECAUSE §11 ITEM 0 MIGHT
# SUGGEST OTHERWISE. That item establishes that this pair's SEPARATING AXIS is
# decided by rounding -- 29.7% of calls choose on a margin of 1e-6 or less --
# and that the shipped library, nudged by 1e-7, disagrees with itself on `dims`
# 7,798 times in 200,000. But the same probe reports **0 count_diff**. The
# shipped library does not disagree with ITSELF about how many contacts there
# are. So the one count divergence is ours, and `dims` being unmeasurable for
# this pair does not extend to `count`.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fourth session): keaLCP_new — THE LAST MODULE ---------------
#
# `keaLCPSolver::solveLCP` and `checkForCycles` are BIT-IDENTICAL to the shipped
# library on scene_chain, scene_ragdoll AND scene_boxes_on_plane, and
# bit-identical on each function taken alone (test/standalone/bisect_object.sh). The object
# is held out of the build by the `extraout_EDX` detector; see the end of this
# entry, which is where a release decision would start.
#
# THE FRAME IS MATERIALISED, and the earlier "61 sites with MIXED strides" note
# was WRONG in a way worth correcting. `auStack_3c[iVar2 - uVar6]` looks like
# element arithmetic against a byte count, but `auStack_3c` is a `uint *`, so it
# is `(int)auStack_3c + 4*iVar2 - 4*uVar6` -- region FOUR in byte terms, exactly
# consistent with `(int)auStack_3c + uVar6 * -3` beside it. Every one of the 61
# sites is consistent byte arithmetic; two of them are simply spelled in
# elements. Nothing was mixed.
#
# So the layout is one contiguous run, read from `<object>.locals`:
#
#   -96 aiStackY_60  (12)   -84 uStackY_54  -80 iStackY_50
#   -76 stack0xffffffb4     -72 stack0xffffffb8   -68 stack0xffffffbc
#   -64 uStack_40           -60 auStack_3c  (12)
#
# with every shifted reference at `frame offset + K*(-uVar6)`, K in 1..6. One
# block of `6*uVar6 + 48` bytes reproduces every relative offset:
#
#   block offset = (frame offset - -96) + (6 - K) * uVar6
#
# and the two areas cannot overlap, by construction: the alloca regions occupy
# `[auStack_3c - 6*uVar6, auStack_3c)` and every other base sits BELOW
# auStack_3c, so shifted by the same 6*uVar6 they stay below the regions.
# `materialise_shifted_frame` emits
#
#   char *kd_frame = (char *)alloca(uVar6 * 6 + 48);
#   uint *auStack_3c = (uint *)(kd_frame + uVar6 * 6 + 36);
#
# and redeclaring the base as a POINTER means its own uses need no rewriting at
# all -- `auStack_3c[2]`, `(int)auStack_3c - uVar6` and `auStack_3c[i - uVar6]`
# all keep working.
#
# AND THEN THE OBJECT COMPILED AND WAS WRONG, which is what the gate is for:
# 7.844e-03 at step 0 of the collision-free scene. bisect_object.sh named
# `solveLCP` (checkForCycles was already exact), and the defect was ONE CALL:
#
#     (**(code **)(*(int *)A + 0x10))();          /* three arguments dropped */
#
# against a shipped
#
#     mov (%edi),%esi ; push %ecx ; mov 0x2c(%edx),%eax ; push %eax
#     push %edi ; call *0x10(%esi)
#
# i.e. `A->solve(this->x, b)`. §5a's machinery could not see it for TWO
# independent reasons, both now fixed in gen_vtable_callsites.py:
#
#   * it walks back from `call *N(%reg)` to a `mov -off(%ebp),%reg` -- a vptr
#     out of a FRAME SLOT. Here the vptr comes out of the OBJECT a parameter
#     points at, `mov (%edi),%esi`, and there is no frame offset to look up.
#   * and the whole loop is gated on the function CONSTRUCTING a polymorphic
#     local. solveLCP constructs nothing, so it was skipped entirely.
#
# The parameter's class comes from the enclosing function's DEMANGLED signature
# (`keaLCPSolver::solveLCP(keaMatrix*, …)`, so ebp+0xc is a `keaMatrix *`), and
# only four-byte parameters are stepped over -- a by-value aggregate would make
# the position a guess, so it declines instead.
#
# `keaMatrix` is ABSTRACT and declares slot +0x10 `__cxa_pure_virtual`, which
# carries no signature. The Itanium ABI puts a derived class's override at the
# SAME slot, so `_concrete_slot` follows through to the subclasses and REQUIRES
# them to agree: `keaMatrix_pcSparse_vanilla::solve(float*, float const*)` and
# `keaMatrix_tester::solve(float*, float const*)` do, and three arguments is
# what the three pushes say.
#
# Adopted as out13: ONE of 153 dumps changed, and one `.locals` row -- Ghidra no
# longer needs `pMStack_44` to model the outgoing argument, so that slot is now
# spelled `stack0xffffffbc`, which the frame rule reads from the name.
#
#   scene_chain / ragdoll / boxes_on_plane   BIT-IDENTICAL
#   per-function (bisect_object.sh)          both functions identical
#   positive control                         drop those three arguments again
#                                            and step 0 is 7.844e-03 out, the
#                                            exact failure from before
#
# WHY IT IS STILL HELD, and what a release would rest on. The `extraout_EDX`
# detector fires on two stores:
#
#     *(undefined4 *)((int)(kd_frame + 32)) = extraout_EDX;
#     *(undefined4 *)((kd_frame + 28))      = extraout_EDX;
#
# Both are DEAD. The shipped `commonPivot` call pushes TWO padding words for
# stack alignment before its six arguments -- `push %edx; push %edx` at 4ec/4ed
# -- and slots 28 and 32 are that padding: beyond the six arguments the callee
# reads, and slot 32 is never read anywhere in the function. So the detector's
# complaint is true (Ghidra could not account for EDX) and inert (the value goes
# into padding). The object is proven exact; releasing it is one line, and the
# more principled alternative is to make the pass drop a store of an
# `extraout_*` value into a slot beyond the callee's arity.
#
# NOTE THAT RELEASING IT DOES NOT UNLOCK §12 ITEM 7 ON ITS OWN. keaIntegrate_pc
# is still measured wrong (first@13 = 1.500e-08) and still held, and the engine
# calls MdtKeaIntegrateSystem directly, so a link with no shipped libMdtKea
# needs that one too.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): keaIntegrate_pc — THE DROPPED ROUNDING ------
#
# keaIntegrate_pc is BIT-IDENTICAL to the shipped library on scene_chain,
# scene_ragdoll AND scene_boxes_on_plane, and on 450,000 bodies of a direct
# per-function A/B on BOTH of its code paths. libMdtKea is now recovered whole:
# every object in it reproduces the shipped library exactly.
#
# THE DEFECT WAS A ROUNDING GHIDRA DROPPED, and the reason it took a session to
# find is that it does not look like a defect. The shipped function declares
# `MeReal dq[4]` at ebp-0x38 and SPILLS three of its four elements:
#
#     2ca: fstps -0x34(%ebp)          dq[1], rounded to 32 bits
#     327: flds  -0x34(%ebp)          reloaded
#     32c: fmuls 0x14(%ebp)           * stepsize
#
# Ghidra folds that store and its reload into one expression -- `qrot[1] = EXPR
# * 0.5 * stepsize + qrot[1]` -- so the local is left DECLARED AND UNUSED and
# the round-to-storage-precision is gone. On i386 gcc then keeps the whole
# chain in an 80-bit x87 register, and the last bit drifts: first@13 =
# 1.500e-08, growing to 4.900e-04 over 900 steps. Routing dq[1..3] back through
# KD_F32() (kd_compat.h) takes the A/B from 52,902 divergent bodies in 400,000
# to ZERO. dq[0] must NOT be rounded -- the shipped code stores it with `fsts`
# and keeps using st(0), so that store is dead.
#
# The same shape is on the fast-spin path: `rot` is spilled as a float across
# the MeQuaternionFiniteRotation call (fstps -0x48 / flds -0x48) and all three
# `myw[i] -= fastSpinAxis[i] * rot` results are stored with fstps. Note the
# asymmetry -- the multiply that forms the CALL ARGUMENT happens BEFORE the
# spill, so it uses the 80-bit value and only the post-call uses see the
# rounded one. The engine reaches this path: KConstraint.cpp:357 and
# KSkeletal.cpp:267 both call MdtBodySetFastSpinAxis.
#
# THIS IS NOT -ffloat-store (dead end 17). That flag rounds everywhere,
# including the many places the original did not, and it made
# IxCylinderCylinder five orders of magnitude worse. This rounds at three
# sites, each one shown by the disassembly to be a 4-byte spill AND reload of a
# slot the DWARF names. ghidra_clean.restore_float_text checks that evidence
# before it fires and RAISES if it is absent.
#
# AND A SECOND DEFECT, WHICH NO GATE IN THIS PROJECT CAN SEE. Ghidra prints a
# right-leaning float `+` chain FLAT -- `a + b + c` for a tree that is
# `a + (b + c)` -- so C re-parses it left-leaning over the wrong operands.
# Three sites in this function: the fast-spin dot product, dq[2], and the
# normalisation sum. Ground truth is not an inference: UT2004's own
# KDynStep.cpp:618-690 contains `KIntegrateSystem`, which is MathEngine's
# integrator COPIED VERBATIM into the engine, and its association is the
# shipped fadd order and not Ghidra's.
#
#   Ghidra   dq[2] = (q1*w2 + -q3*w0 + q0*w1) * 0.5
#   source   dq[2] = 0.5*( -q3*w0 + q0*w1 + q1*w2 )        KDynStep.cpp:657
#
# WHY NO GATE CAN SEE IT, and why it was fixed anyway. A float product needs 48
# mantissa bits and the x87 register has 64, so on i386 these sums are EXACT
# whatever the association -- measured 0 differences in 2,000,000 samples under
# -mfpmath=387. The SAME probe under -mfpmath=sse, which is the arithmetic
# wasm32, armv7 and arm64 all give, differs in 31% of them (621,410 and
# 624,401 of 2,000,000 on the two sites tested). So every scene gate, every
# difftest and the whole 450,000-body A/B are structurally blind to expression
# association, and the port target is exactly where it bites.
#
# RELEASE. keaIntegrate_pc was held by `live_unmodelled` on `in_stack_ffffffa0`.
# That name is the OUTGOING by-value argument area of the tail call to
# KeaIntegrateSystem_vanilla: it is DECLARED, 76 bytes, an MdtKeaParameters
# exactly, and the copy loop fills all 19 MeReal of it before it is read, so
# the dataflow is complete and only the spelling is unmodelled. Released
# through recover.RELEASED_UNMODELLED, which names the identifier rather than
# the object, so a new unmodelled read still quarantines it.
#
#   scene_chain / ragdoll / boxes_on_plane   BIT-IDENTICAL (all three)
#   A/B, 450,000 bodies, 1..8 per call       0 qrot / 0 vel / 0 velrot / 0 T
#   A/B with fastSpin set, 450,000 bodies    0 / 0 / 0 / 0
#   positive controls                        perturbing vel[1] in the unrolled
#                                            loop reports vel=17,328; in the
#                                            odd-body peel vel=2,175; R2[2]
#                                            reports T=105,716. The FIRST
#                                            attempt at the peel control read
#                                            zero because the driver only ever
#                                            passed 8 bodies -- an even count
#                                            never enters that block. The
#                                            driver now sweeps 1..8.
#   negative control                         the unrepaired object reads 52,902
#                                            divergent bodies in 400,000
#
keaIntegrate_pc       RELEASED 2026-08-25 (fifth session). Bit-identical on all three scenes
#                     and on 450,000 bodies of a direct A/B on both code paths, with the
#                     positive controls above proving the driver discriminates. See the
#                     paragraph immediately above for the two defects and for why the
#                     association one is invisible to every gate here.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): keaLCP_new — RELEASED -----------------------
#
# Proven exact last session and held by two detectors, not one. The
# `extraout_EDX` complaint is inert (see the fourth-session entry above: both
# stores land in the alignment padding the shipped `commonPivot` call pushes
# before its six arguments, and slot 32 is never read). Released through
# recover.RELEASED_UNMODELLED by IDENTIFIER.
#
# THAT UNCOVERED A SECOND DETECTOR, which is the part worth writing down.
# GHIDRA_STACK_GUESS then fired on 20 sites of the form
# `(int)auStack_3c + uVar6 * -3` -- and every one of those is the OUTPUT of
# materialise_shifted_frame, not a defect. That detector's premise is "Ghidra
# INVENTED the array, so the storage is not there"; the repair falsifies the
# premise by redeclaring the base as a pointer into an alloca'd block sized
# from <object>.locals and big enough by construction. The repair now marks
# each base it rewrites (KD_MATERIALISED_BASE) and the detector skips those, in
# the FUNCTION that carries the marker. MdtPartition -- the object the detector
# was written for -- still reports its 30 sites, which is the control.
#
#   scene_chain / ragdoll / boxes_on_plane   BIT-IDENTICAL (all three)
#   per-function (test/standalone/bisect_object.sh)     both functions identical
#   scene_census                             solveLCP ran 900 times; it is not
#                                            a never-ran verdict
#   blast radius of both releases            all 113 previously-built objects
#                                            byte-identical; 2 new
#
keaLCP_new            RELEASED 2026-08-25 (fifth session). Bit-identical on all three scenes
#                     and per-function; both detectors that held it are accounted for above.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): THE ENGINE ON THE RECOVERED SOLVER ----------
#
# build-subst115: the engine linked against /tmp/mt_subst115, in which 115 of
# the recovered objects REPLACE the shipped members -- 14 of libMdtKea's 20.
#
# SUBSTITUTION VERIFIED AT THE MACHINE-CODE LEVEL, not from the link:
#
#                                    stock   subst115
#   MdtKeaIntegrateSystem               23        40   instructions
#   KeaIntegrateSystem_vanilla         434       415
#   keaLCPSolver::solveLCP             314       499
#   MdtKeaAddConstraintForces          471       405
#
# AND IT EXECUTES. gdb breakpoint counts, test-karma-1, xDeathMatch:
#
#   MdtKeaAddConstraintForces      301 calls   (recovered keaRbdCore_unified)
#   keaLCPSolver::solveLCP          85 calls   (recovered keaLCPSolver/keaLCP_new)
#   MdtKeaIntegrateSystem            0 calls   <-- and this is the finding
#
# HANDOVER.md SAID "THE ENGINE CALLS MdtKeaIntegrateSystem DIRECTLY". IT DOES
# NOT, IN NORMAL PLAY. KDynStep.cpp:869 branches on `KGData->bUseSafeTime`,
# which KarmaSupport.cpp:1721 initialises to 1 and only the console command
# `KSAFETIME 0` (KUtils.cpp:610) clears. The safe-time branch integrates with
# the engine's OWN KIntegrateSystem (KDynStep.cpp:618) and calls Kea's
# integrator only in the else. Forcing the other branch with -EXEC:
#
#   -EXEC=kd_exec.txt containing `KSAFETIME 0`  ->  "Karma Safe-Time Disabled."
#   MdtKeaIntegrateSystem                            51 calls
#
# So keaIntegrate_pc is REACHABLE and not on the default path. It still has to
# be correct -- it is in the link, the console toggle is a shipped feature, and
# an object that is wrong is wrong -- but it is the third member of the §3a
# family (MdtWorldStep, KIntersect's two intercepts) where UT2004 reimplements
# what it also links. Check the branch, not just the call, before calling
# something load-bearing.
#
# The same read explains why KDynStep.cpp contains KIntegrateSystem verbatim:
# Epic needed a variable-timestep version, copied MathEngine's, and left the
# original reachable behind a toggle. That copy is what gave this session
# ground truth for the association defect above.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): keaMatrix_PcSparse_vanilla, 5 of 6 ----------
#
# NOT RELEASED. Recorded because the object went from "one number, no idea
# where" to "five of six functions exact and the sixth located", and because
# getting there required fixing the instrument first.
#
# THE INSTRUMENT WAS BROKEN AND THE OBJECT IS WHAT SHOWED IT.
# test/standalone/bisect_object.sh reported all six functions with the SAME
# `first@0=4.700e-10 max=4.280e-04`, including `allocate`, which does no
# floating-point arithmetic and therefore cannot produce 4.7e-10. Cause:
# `objcopy --localize-symbol` does not touch a WEAK OBJECT symbol (nm type
# `V`), so the recovered `_ZTV26keaMatrix_pcSparse_vanilla` stayed global; being
# in plain `.data` rather than the shipped copy's COMDAT section it won the
# weak-vs-weak contest, and its slot relocations resolved against the recovered
# object's own now-LOCAL methods. So every configuration ran ALL the recovered
# code. Confirmed by linking the object with EVERY function localised: the
# trajectory still moved, while the SHIPPED object given identical treatment
# changed nothing (its COMDAT duplicate is discarded), and an unrelated dummy
# object with .data and .bss changed nothing either — so it was not link
# layout. Fixed by renaming the ABI symbols out of the way; `--abi-only` is the
# new control that would have caught it. Six objects in the corpus carry their
# own vtable; keaLCPSolver is NOT one, so the bisect that found
# PrincipalSubmatrix's three casts stands.
#
# WITH THE FIX, the divergence is TWO functions of six:
#
#   solveUnits  identical      makeFromJMJT  identical
#   allocate    identical      multiply      identical
#   solve       first@0 =4.700e-10           factorize  first@10=3.800e-09
#
# AND `solve` IS NOW EXACT, by the same repair keaIntegrate_pc needed.
# `MeReal tmp[4]` at ebp-0x28: gcc 3.2 accumulates the running total THROUGH
# MEMORY -- sixteen `fsts` and nineteen `fadds` against those four slots, and
# NOT ONE `flds`. Routing every `tmp[i] = ...` through KD_F32 takes the
# function to IDENTICAL with both controls clean. Note that a check written to
# look for `fstps`/`flds` pairs would have DECLINED here and looked like a rule
# that does not apply; `_F32_SLOT` now counts `fadds`/`fsubs`/`fmuls` from a
# slot as a read-back, which is what gcc 3.2 actually emits.
#
# `factorize` IS NOT FIXED AND THE OBVIOUS ATTEMPT MAKES IT WORSE. Its
# arithmetic is not in `factorize` at all -- the shipped function is 0xcc bytes
# with ZERO float instructions and calls the file-statics `vc_strip_Cholesky`
# (0x48d) and `vc_dstrip_Cholesky` (0x48a), which gcc inlined in the recovery
# (0x7a2). Those two have no `%ebp`-relative float spills at all: they are
# flds/fmuls/fadds/fstps throughout, i.e. every intermediate goes to memory
# through a POINTER. Wrapping all 62 of their float stores in KD_F32 moves the
# divergence from `first@10` to `first@7` -- WORSE. That is dead end 17 in
# miniature: the rounding has to go where the original spilled, not everywhere.
# Next step is to read the two statics' float traffic against the recovery
# statement by statement, with bisect_object.sh's `factorize` row as the gate.
#
# AND THE ASSOCIATION HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED AS THE CAUSE HERE, which is worth
# recording because the object is full of the shape. Reversing all 41
# unparenthesised 3+-term float `+` chains changed the object file and changed
# NOTHING measurable -- the same x87 exactness that makes §11 item 2a invisible.
# So on i386 a corpus-wide association rewrite is UNFALSIFIABLE: it cannot be
# shown right and it cannot be shown wrong. Do not ship one on this evidence.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): four 340 s engine runs, no crash ------------
#
# test-karma-1, xDeathMatch, -SOFTWARERENDERER, 340 s each:
#
#   build            branch        exit  SIGSEGV  START MATCH  NaN/Critical
#   stock-karma      default        124        0            1             0
#   stock-karma      KSAFETIME 0    124        0            1             0
#   subst115         default        124        0            1             0
#   subst115         KSAFETIME 0    124        0            1             0
#
# exit=124 is the timeout, i.e. all four ran to the end. So the engine on 115
# recovered objects -- including the whole recovered solver, and including
# keaIntegrate_pc on the branch that reaches it -- is as stable as stock over
# 340 s of live physics. This is NOT a trajectory comparison: neither binary
# carries shadow instrumentation.
#
# -GL4ESRENDERER and -OPENGLRENDERER both fault at the first HUD frame in this
# environment (`REALLOC FAILED ... NewSize=1351188168`, viewport created 2x1),
# and STOCK DOES IT TOO -- same map, same URL, logs identical modulo timestamps
# and addresses, 523 lines each. Environment regression, not recovery. Run the
# stock control before reading a crash as yours.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): TOWARD A DROP-IN LIBRARY --------------------
#
# The goal is the engine linking against recovered source and NO shipped `.a`
# member. Object counts do not measure that, so tools/dropin_gap.py does: it
# walks the symbol closure from the ENGINE's own objects, resolving each symbol
# against the RECOVERED build first and only then against the shipped archives,
# and reports every shipped member the walk still had to reach.
#
# CHECKED AGAINST GROUND TRUTH, not asserted. A real link of the engine with
# every shipped member deleted reports 111 undefined symbols; the walk predicts
# all 111. (gcc prints demangled names and nm mangled ones, so the comparison
# demangles both sides — without that three C++ symbols look like a hole in the
# walk when they are a hole in the comparison.)
#
#   at the start of this run   27 members / 192 symbols
#   after this run             21 members / 160 symbols
#
# Members closed: MeDict, McdBox, McdSphyl, McdTriangleList, MeSimpleFile_linux,
# MeAssetDBXMLIO. 121 objects in the build, from 115.
#
# AND FOUR OBJECTS THAT ALREADY COMPILED WERE SILENTLY WRONG. McdSphere computed
# a sphere's mass properties from its radius read as an INTEGER; McdCylinder,
# McdConvexMesh and McdPlaneIntersect had the same defect. `_McdGeometry` is 16
# bytes and `McdCylinder::mR` is at +16, exactly where Ghidra's `g[1].mRefCtAndID`
# lands, and the shipped code reads it with `flds 0x10` — there is not one `fild`
# or `cvtsi2*` in any function of those objects. No gate had caught them: the
# scenes barely exercise those AABB and mass-property paths and difftest reads
# contacts, not radii.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): MeAssetDBXMLIO — §7c's DEFECT, AGAIN --------
#
# WORTH READING IN FULL, because everything about it is the failure mode this
# project keeps rediscovering.
#
# The object was RELEASED on a correct argument — the `extraout_EAX` it was held
# for is a dead store, provably, once the variadic padding word that used to
# read it was removed. It then passed EVERY offline gate: 121/121 clean on three
# scenes, frame bounds 0, symbol bindings 0 errors, difftest at the documented
# baseline. And the engine died before a match started:
#
#   #0  0x00000000 in ?? ()
#   #1  MeXMLElementProcess (…) at MeXMLParser.c:656
#   #2  MeXMLInputProcess (…)
#   #3  MeAssetDBXMLInputRead ()
#   #4  KCreateAssetDB (…) KUtils.cpp:1374
#
# Located by bisecting the eleven objects that had changed since the last
# engine-verified build, four link-and-run cycles, ~4 minutes each.
#
# THE DEFECT: `materialise_relocated_data` emitted the XML handler table with
#
#     (void *)0x0u /* Handle_KaFile_0_1 */,
#
# — it knew the relocation's target and wrote a NULL, because the symbol is
# defined in a SIBLING object rather than in this one. Its docstring defended
# that: "emitting a reference to a name that is not declared would trade a wrong
# value for a link error, and neither is better than leaving the object visibly
# failing."
#
# THAT REASONING IS SOUND AND ITS PREMISE IS FALSE. The object was not visibly
# failing — it was invisibly failing, which is worse than either alternative. A
# link error is loud and immediate; a null handler in a table is silent and
# fatal. `Handle_KaFile_0_1` is a global `T` in MeAssetDBXMLInput_1_0.o, so the
# third option — emit the reference — was available all along.
#
# TWO ATTEMPTS AT THE FIX, and the first was wrong in an instructive way.
# Emitting `extern void Handle_KaFile_0_1();` beside the reference is a
# `conflicting types` error: the prelude already declares it with a real
# prototype, and MeXMLParser.h declares MeXMLParseInt the same way. That took
# TWO objects out of the build. The declaration is now omitted entirely — every
# symbol observed is already declared by the prelude or by a public header, and
# an undeclared one now fails loudly, which is the outcome the docstring wanted.
#
#   engine, test-karma-1, 200 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER
#     before   SIGSEGV in KCreateAssetDB, never reached START MATCH
#     after    ran to the timeout, 0 SIGSEGV, START MATCH reached
#   blast radius of the fix   1 object, no classification changed
#
# THE LESSON IS §7c'S AND IT NEEDS SAYING AGAIN: for anything on the `.ka` path,
# the offline gates are not evidence. Run the engine.

# --- 2026-08-25 (fifth session): McdFrame — one name, two API slots ----------
#
# 13 symbols of the drop-in gap stood on a single ambiguity. `McdTerm` calls
# `MePoolAPI.destroy(MePool *)` twice and `MeMemoryAPI.destroy(void *)` four
# times, and Ghidra spells BOTH `_McdGeometryGetReferenceCount`, because
# `MePoolAPI+4` and `MeMemoryAPI+12` are the same address in its invented memory
# map. `relocation_targets` correctly offered both candidates and
# `_resolve_external` correctly declined — a tie it had no way to break.
#
# THE ARGUMENT BREAKS IT, and both halves of the comparison are READ:
#
#   the slot's first parameter type   from the public header. MePool.h declares
#                                     `void (*destroy)(MePool *)`; MeMemory.h
#                                     declares destroy through a TYPEDEF'D
#                                     function pointer, which the first version
#                                     of the header reader could not see — so it
#                                     found nothing for the one struct the
#                                     ambiguity is about, and declined
#   the argument's type               from the object's own DWARF.
#                                     `&frame->cachePool` is a `MePool *`
#                                     because `cachePool` is a `MePool` member
#                                     of what `frame` points at
#
# Result: pools go to MePoolAPI.destroy, memory blocks to MeMemoryAPI.destroy,
# and the object compiles. A site the typer cannot resolve leaves the WHOLE
# object in review — half a resolution is not a resolution.
#
# TWO SELF-INFLICTED DECLINES ON THE WAY, both found by calling the rule
# directly on the offending line rather than reasoning about it:
#
#   * `McdFrameworkID frame` is a POINTER TYPEDEF with no `*`, so
#     `_declared_pointee` returned None and every `&frame->pool` site went
#     unresolved — five of eight sites typed cleanly and the object still stayed
#     in review;
#   * the rewrite resumed at the call's CLOSING paren instead of its opening
#     one, so it dropped the argument. gcc then said `too few arguments`, the
#     error count went UP, and the repair loop reverted the edit — which from
#     the outside is indistinguishable from a rule that declined.
#
#   122 objects · 122/122 clean on three scenes, all bit-identical on chain
#   difftest at the documented baseline · frame bounds 0 · bindings 0 errors
#   engine, test-karma-1, 200 s: ran to the timeout, 0 SIGSEGV, START MATCH
#   drop-in gap 21 -> 20 members, 161 -> 148 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26 (sixth session): asking the compiler whether a value matters ---
#
# `live_unmodelled` reports that the recovery READS a value Ghidra could not
# account for. That is the right thing to report and the wrong question to stop
# on: Ghidra also invents such a read where the machine pushed a live register
# as stack ALIGNMENT PADDING, or where it merged a dead definition into a local
# that is reassigned before anything looks at it. Eight members of the drop-in
# gap were held on that, and no regex can tell the two apart — it needs to know
# which definition reaches which read, on every path.
#
# THE COMPILER ALREADY KNOWS. recover.prove_inert gives the value a defined
# constant, compiles, and does it again with a different one. Byte-identical
# objects mean the emitted code does not depend on the value, which is exactly
# what live_unmodelled is trying to decide — decided by GCC's dataflow instead
# of by a pattern. Three probes, not two, because two arbitrary constants can
# coincide: `if (v)` compiles the same for 1 and 3 but not for 0. The set is
# {0, -1, 0x5a5a5a5a}.
#
#   controls, before believing any of it (test the test):
#     dead    v = extraout_EAX; v = 7; sink(v);            -> INERT   (right)
#     live    v = extraout_EAX; if (c) v = 7; sink(v);     -> held    (right)
#     subtle  v = extraout_EAX; sink(v ? 1 : 2);           -> held    (right)
#     arr     MeReal (*unaff_ESI)[3];  (no typeable decl)  -> held    (right)
#
#   PROVEN INERT and initialised, 5 values in 4 objects:
#     MeAssetDBXMLInput_1_0  extraout_EDX
#     MeAssetDBXMLOutput_1_0 extraout_ECX, extraout_EDX
#     keaDebug               extraout_EDX
#     keaPrintBasicTypes     extraout_EDX
#
# AND THE FOURTH TIME A DETECTOR TURNED OUT TO BE SHIELDING SOMETHING ELSE.
# MeAssetDBXMLInput_1_0 passed all nine gates and killed the engine:
#
#     Signal: SIGSEGV
#     [3] ut2004-subst126.bin(+0xba8e6d)          <- no symbol: not a function
#     [4] ut2004-subst126.bin(MeXMLElementProcess+0x232)
#     [5] ut2004-subst126.bin(+0xa97f9d)          <- Handle_Geometry_1_0+0x7d
#
#   with the STOCK control on the same map, same URL, reaching START MATCH and
#   running to the timeout with 0 faults. The defect is §7c's for the THIRD
#   time. materialise_relocated_data resolved EVERY section-relative relocation
#   to an offset in a raw byte copy of that section, which is right for a string
#   pool and wrong for `.text`:
#
#     rodata+0xc8   R_386_32  .text   addend 0x1310
#     .text+0x1310  =  Handle_GeometryPrimitive_1_0   (a file-static, `t`)
#     emitted as    (void *)&kd_relstr_text[0x1310]   <- a const array of bytes
#
#   A relocation against .text is a FUNCTION POINTER. For a file-static there is
#   no symbol in the relocation to name it with — the relocation says `.text`
#   and the addend is the offset — so the name has to come from the symbol
#   table. _function_at does that; an offset that resolves to no function now
#   emits an undeclared name, because a compile error is loud and a byte-pool
#   address links, passes every gate here, and segfaults on a real `.ka`.
#
#     engine, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER
#       before   SIGSEGV in MeXMLElementProcess, never reached START MATCH
#       after    exit 124 (ran to the timeout), START MATCH reached, 0 faults
#       stock    exit 124, START MATCH reached, 0 faults  -- the control
#     one object in the corpus had the shape, and it was the one just released
#
# THE SPLITTERS WERE NOT STRING-AWARE, so fix_printf_extra_args had been
# declining silently on every format containing a comma — which is most of them:
#
#     sprintf(file->error,"line %d, char %d: expected %d MeReals, found %d\n",...)
#     -> ten arguments with the format in three pieces, so the rule saw
#        something that was not a literal and left the site alone
#
# _resolve_arity then follows a format held in a VARIABLE, and both of its
# guards are load-bearing. MeXMLParser has one call reached by two definitions
# with DIFFERENT arities (4 and 3): taking the nearer one textually would drop
# three arguments and lose `puVar9`, which the other path reads. It takes the
# LARGEST arity over all literal definitions, and declines outright where any
# definition is not a literal — that site is declined, and the object stays out.
#
# AND ARMV7 WAS NEVER COMPILING. ptrwidth_check.sh counts warnings, and a file
# clang REJECTS emits none, so "0 truncation warnings across 0 objects" was
# indistinguishable from a target that builds nothing. Two objects were failing
# outright on glibc spellings bionic does not extend — MeDict on `ulong`,
# MeSimpleFile_linux on `__off_t`. Both are now in kd_compat.h, and the gate
# reports DID NOT COMPILE separately, with a broken-input control that makes it
# exit 1. HANDOVER.md §12 item 6's "armv7 DONE, 122/122" was wrong; so was
# "wasm32 122/122" — the baseline measures 121 compiled and 1 failed.
#
#   wasm32   121/122 -> 126/126, exported symbol sets identical
#   armv7    124/126 -> 126/126, 0 truncation warnings
#   arm64    124/126 -> 126/126, 3025 truncations across 77 (was 2680/73 at 122)
#
#   126 objects · 126/126 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 · bindings 0
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline (10 PASS, 4 known FAIL)
#   vptr_ab bit-identical with both wrong controls seen · census/sensitivity ok
#   drop-in gap 20 -> 18 members, 148 -> 145 symbols

MeFAsset              real, and the release is about REACHABILITY, not about the frame — read
#                     this before relying on it. 38 symbols of the drop-in gap, the largest
#                     single member, held by GHIDRA_RECONSTRUCTED_FRAME.
#
#                     ALL 169 kd_argslot_ REFERENCES ARE IN ONE FUNCTION, and it is
#                     `MeFAssetCreateCopy`. The other 58 exported functions carry no
#                     reconstructed frame at all:
#
#                       MeFAssetCreateCopy      169 argslot refs
#                       every other function      0
#
#                     AND NOTHING CAN CALL IT. Three independent checks, because "0 calls in
#                     one run" is a sample size and this file says so about IxCylinderTriList:
#
#                       * it is called from exactly two places, both in MeAssetDB.o —
#                         MeAssetDBCreateCopy and MeAssetDBInsertCopy (objdump -dr, the two
#                         R_386_PC32 sites at 0x276 and 0x2be);
#                       * no engine object and no other metoolkit object imports either of
#                         those two, or MeFAssetCreateCopy itself — 0, 0 and 0;
#                       * none of the three has its ADDRESS taken anywhere in the corpus (no
#                         R_386_32 relocation names them), so none is reachable through a
#                         function pointer either. That is the check that makes the first two
#                         mean something.
#
#                     MEASURED IN THE ENGINE, with MeFAsset substituted (build-subst-mf,
#                     127 objects, substitution verified at the machine-code level:
#                     MeFAssetCreate 48 vs 51 instructions, MeFAssetGetPart 7 vs 9):
#
#                       gdb breakpoint counts, test-karma-1, xDeathMatch, ~150 s
#                         MeFAssetCreate           13 calls
#                         MeFAssetCreateCopy        0 calls
#                       engine, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER
#                         exit 124 (ran to the timeout), START MATCH reached, 0 faults
#                         stock control, same map and URL: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#
#                     SO WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT SAY. It does NOT say the reconstructed
#                     frame is right — the function carrying it never executed, and a clean
#                     run over code that did not run is the §4a trap. It says the frame is
#                     confined to a function the engine cannot reach by any route, so the
#                     object can be linked for its other 37 needed symbols. If anything ever
#                     calls MeFAssetCreateCopy the frame is a hypothesis again, and the
#                     169 references are where to start.

# --- 2026-08-26: `_DAT_00000050` is an address, and the original derefs NULL ---
#
# MeFAssetPart was 21 symbols of the drop-in gap, held on a name that is not a
# name. ghidra_memory_map lays an object's sections out from 0x10000, so an
# address BELOW that is in none of them — it is a literal absolute address, and
# the machine code has exactly that instruction:
#
#   70f: mov 0x4(%eax),%ebx      ebx = p1->asset
#   712: test %ebx,%ebx
#   714: je  775                 asset == NULL: skip the range check
#   716: mov 0x50(%ebx),%ecx     ecx = asset->maxParts
#   719: cmp %ecx,0x4c(%ebx)
#   71c: jg  761                 partCount > maxParts: return
#   71e: ...                     BODY, and it needs ecx
#   775: mov 0x50,%ecx           <-- ecx = *(int *)0x50
#   77b: jmp 71e
#
# MathEngine wrote `asset == NULL || asset->partCount <= asset->maxParts`, which
# lets NULL through into a body that then uses `asset->maxParts`, and gcc
# rematerialised that load on the branch where it knows the base is zero. So
# `mov 0x50,%ecx` IS `((MeFAsset *)0)->maxParts` with the zero folded in, and
# the shipped function dereferences NULL on that path — at 0x775 and again at
# 0x74b. Reproducing the load reproduces the program, fault included, which is
# the standard everything else here is held to. HANDOVER.md's diagnosis said
# "correct the control flow"; the control flow is right and it is the ADDRESS
# that needed resolving.
#
#   two sites in the corpus, both in objects held out of the build on them:
#     MeFAssetPart   _DAT_00000050   MeCommandLine   _DAT_00000000
#
#   engine, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER, 129 objects substituted
#     gdb breakpoint count   MeFAssetPartEnableCollision  252 calls
#       — the function carrying the resolved load, in a real match
#     exit 124 (ran to the timeout), START MATCH reached, 0 faults
#     stock control, same map and URL: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#     substitution verified at machine-code level: EnableCollision 43 vs 54
#     instructions, IsCollisionEnabled 27 vs 42
#
#   129 objects · 129/129 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 · bindings 0
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 129/129 · armv7 129/129, 0 truncations · arm64 3264 across 79
#   drop-in gap 17 -> 16 members, 107 -> 86 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: MdtBcl — a rebased frame pointer, and dead code below it ----
#
# 15 symbols of the drop-in gap, and it took two separate things.
#
# ONE: THE `stack0x` SITE IS REPAIRABLE AND MeMath'S IS NOT, and the difference
# is now a check rather than a judgement. frame_offsets.py --cover separated
# three cases; both of these are the third — the name is right and Ghidra
# declared NO local at that offset. MdtBcl's accesses land inside
# `MeReal ref2world[4][4]` at -156, which the line above fills via
# MeMatrix4MultiplyMatrix; MeMath's land inside `MeReal eR[3][3]`, which is
# DECLARED AND NEVER WRITTEN because Ghidra emitted fcos/fsin and discarded the
# results.
#
# The pointer is at frame -12 and the array starts at -156, so pointing it at
# the array and keeping the displacements puts the BASE 144 bytes past a 64-byte
# object — which is what check_frame_bounds.py exists to reject. So it is
# REBASED: pointed at the covering local, with every displacement through it
# shifted by the same constant. Every access keeps its address exactly:
#
#   local_124 = &stack0xfffffff4;          ->  local_124 = (undefined1 *)ref2world;
#     *(MeReal *)(local_124 + -0x80)       ->    *(MeReal *)(local_124 + 0x10)
#
#   the six accesses come out at ref2world[1][0..2] and [2][0..2], and the three
#   statements above the loop write [0][0..2] — one 3x3 rotation block copied
#   into clist->Jstore, which is what MdtBclAddSpring6 is for. That agreement is
#   the evidence the offsets are right.
#
#   THE TRIP COUNT IS READ, NOT ASSUMED: the pointer is incremented in the loop,
#   so the bounds check has to cover every iteration. `uVar20` starts at 1 and
#   steps by 2 against `< 3`, so 1 — and forcing the reader to return 3 makes
#   the rule DECLINE, because iterations 1 and 2 leave the array. The bound is
#   live, not decoration.
#
#   AND MeMath DECLINED FOR THE WRONG REASON FIRST, which is why there is a
#   second guard. Its loop is not the counted shape (`iVar7 = iVar6 + 1` rather
#   than `v = v + K`), so it was refused before the real objection was reached.
#   Measured: with the trip count forced, MeMath's targets -60..-28 all fit
#   inside eR at [-60,-24) — coverage PASSES. What refuses it is that the
#   covering local must be WRITTEN: `eR` is mentioned ONCE in its function, its
#   declaration; `ref2world` is mentioned 69 times and assigned element by
#   element. A guard that holds by accident is not a guard.
#
# TWO: THE unaff_* COMPLAINT IS IN CODE THAT IS NOT IN THE OBJECT. With the
# stack0x site repaired, MdtBcl compiled and moved to live_unmodelled on
# `unaff_EBP` used as a FRAME BASE — `*(float *)(unaff_EBP + -0x25c)` with
# `int unaff_EBP = 0;`. All 578 such references are in five functions Ghidra
# invented at addresses that already belong to real ones:
#
#   FUN_00020000 / 30 / 1a0   inside MdtBclAddRPROJoint (.text 0x0e5a0-0x10341)
#   FUN_00020720              inside LimitSingleAxis
#   FUN_00020ee0              inside MdtContactWriteRow
#
# They are static, nothing calls them, and gcc eliminates them: `nm` on the
# built object reports ZERO FUN_0002* symbols. prove_inert says the same thing
# without being told — the object is byte-identical for all three probe
# constants, because the code carrying them is not emitted at all. It needed one
# generalisation to see it: initialise_unmodelled_locals had already given the
# name a value, so the probe has to REPLACE an initialiser rather than decline
# on finding one. Controls re-run, plus two new ones for the initialised form.
#
#   MdtBcl, scene_ragdoll: TRAJECTORY BIT-IDENTICAL
#   scene_census, scene_chain: 42,300 calls across 7 functions
#     MdtBclAddBSJoint 9,900 · MdtBclEndConstraint 9,900
#     MdtBclAddSphericalRowsWorld 9,900 · EndPartition/Init/StartPartition 900 each
#   LIMIT, stated: MdtBclAddRPROJoint — the function Ghidra split — is NOT among
#   the seven the chain scene runs. It is a car-suspension joint. Its recovery is
#   unmeasured, and the fragments being dead does not make the MAIN body proven.
#
#   engine, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER, 130 objects substituted
#     exit 124 (ran to the timeout), START MATCH reached, 0 faults
#     stock control, same map and URL: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#     substitution verified: AddSpring6 1122 vs 1447 instructions,
#     AddBSJoint 190 vs 204, AddRPROJoint 1460 vs 2064
#
#   130 objects · 130/130 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 · bindings 0
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 130/130 · armv7 130/130, 0 truncations
#   arm64 3264 -> 6506 truncations across 80; MdtBcl alone is 3242 of the rise
#   drop-in gap 16 -> 15 members, 86 -> 71 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: the argument-less indirect call, and the hole it exposed ----
#
# `iVar7 = (*pcVar1)();` is the defect that crashed IxSphereTriList (§8): Ghidra
# has no signature for a call through a function pointer, so it drops every
# argument and the callee reads whatever is on the stack. Two sites were left in
# the corpus.
#
# EVERYTHING NEEDED IS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN, in four different places:
#
#   1. McdFrame.h    McdFrameworkGetInteractions returns McdInteractions*
#   2. kd_types_fields.json   _McdInteractions +8 is `intersectFn`
#   3. McdCTypes.h   that member is a McdIntersectFn
#   4. McdCTypes.h   McdIntersectFn is int (*)(McdModelPair*, McdIntersectResult*)
#
# and the arguments are in the slots the call sits on: the return-address store
# marks the base, argument i is at base + 4*(i+1), and the stores that filled
# them carry their own casts. MstUtils takes a shorter route — its pointer comes
# straight out of MstBridgeGetPerPairCB, whose RETURN type is the typedef.
#
#   McdInteractions  (*(McdIntersectFn)pcVar1)(p, result)
#   MstUtils         (*(MstPerPairCBPtr)pcVar12)(local_48, group)
#
#   both argument lists match the typedef's parameter types exactly, which is
#   the check that the slots were read in the right order — and the two words
#   ABOVE them are gcc's padding, which drop_padding_arg_stores then removed for
#   free, confirming the arity from the other side.
#
# AND MstUtils IS RELEASED AND McdInteractions IS NOT, because restoring the
# call exposed something the frame-bounds detector could not see:
#
#   pMVar6 = (McdContact *)&type1;              /* type1 is an int, 4 bytes */
#   if (...) { pMVar6 = aMStackY_501c; }
#   *(void **)((int)pMVar6 + -4)  = pvVar5;     /* FIVE WORDS BELOW type1 */
#   *(McdModelPair **)((int)pMVar6 + -0x10) = p;
#
# `McdIntersect`'s outgoing-argument area is UNMATERIALISED: Ghidra rendered it
# as negative offsets from the address of a local, and check_frame_bounds.py
# reported 0 for the object because the offsets go through a POINTER, which its
# docstring deliberately ignores — `p + 0x14` on a `void *p` really is someone
# else's memory. Not when p was handed `&local` two lines earlier.
#
#   pointer_aliases() closes it: a pointer assigned the address of a local is a
#   frame reference, but only under BYTE arithmetic — `(int)ptr + K` — because
#   `ptr + K` without the cast is element arithmetic and genuinely is not.
#   Where a pointer takes several locals the SMALLEST is used.
#
#     corpus-wide: 9 violations, ALL in McdInteractions, none in any object
#     already in the build. So it shields nothing that was validated, and it
#     holds the one object that needed holding.
#
#   MstUtils by contrast writes IN BOUNDS, and Ghidra sized the arrays to say so:
#     int aiStack_9cb0[6]   24 bytes, accessed at +0x14
#     float fStack_9c98      4 bytes, accessed at +0
#     MeReal aMStack_9c94[2] 8 bytes, accessed at +0 and +4
#
# THE GATE NOW SPLITS ITS REPORT, because a held object's violations are the
# detector working and only a violation in the BUILD is a failure. Controls:
# the same source with its object present reports "1 in the build" and exits 1;
# with the object absent it reports "1 HELD" and exits 0.
#
#   LIMIT, stated: neither repaired call was EXERCISED by anything run here.
#   McdIntersect is 0 calls in scene_ragdoll (only McdGoodbyeEach 900 and
#   McdHello 630 ran) and 0 in the engine on test-karma-1 — §3a's intercept,
#   and the engine does not reference McdIntersect at all; it arrives through
#   McdAggregate. So McdInteractions' scene verdict is about the LINK. The
#   evidence for the repair is static: the typedef's parameter types, the slots
#   the return address marks, and the padding rule agreeing on the arity from
#   above. What it still needs is materialise_shifted_frame over that area.
#
#   131 objects · 131/131 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 IN THE BUILD
#   bindings 0 · difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 131/131 · armv7 131/131, 0 truncations · arm64 6,877 across 81
#   engine, test-karma-1, 260 s: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#   drop-in gap 15 -> 14 members, 71 -> 68 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: McdInteractions — an argument area anchored on a local ------
#
# The other spelling of the outgoing-argument area, and the one
# materialise_shifted_frame does not answer. Ghidra could not name the base, so
# it anchored the area on whatever local sat just above it and addressed
# everything at a NEGATIVE offset from that — picking a DIFFERENT anchor on each
# of two paths, which is the giveaway that the anchor is arbitrary:
#
#   pMVar6 = (McdContact *)&type1;              /* an int, 4 bytes */
#   if (...) { pMVar6 = aMStackY_501c; }        /* a 20,440-byte array */
#   *(void **)((int)pMVar6 + -4)  = pvVar5;
#   ...
#   *(undefined4 *)((int)pMVar6 + -0x24) = 0x1027d;
#
# All nineteen uses of the pointer are `(int)pMVar6 + K` with K negative, so
# what it names is a 0x24-byte region below itself and nothing else. That gets
# real storage and the pointer is set to its top:
#
#   undefined1 kd_argarea_pMVar6 [36];
#   pMVar6 = (McdContact *)(kd_argarea_pMVar6 + 0x24);
#
# AND IT IS CHECKED BY THE SAME CHECKER THAT CAUGHT IT, which is the point.
# check_frame_bounds.pointer_aliases reads the `+ 0x24`, so it follows the
# pointer into the buffer instead of losing sight of it:
#
#   before   9 references outside a 4-byte int
#   after    0
#
# TWO SELF-INFLICTED DEFECTS ON THE WAY, both caught by the blast-radius diff or
# by looking before running, and both worth recording because they are the same
# mistake in two places — a rule that recognised a SHAPE without the fact that
# makes the shape mean something:
#
#   * the first version of materialise_pointer_arg_area required only that every
#     use be a negative-offset read or an assignment. It fired on MeHeap, where
#     `pvVar2 = (MeMemoryAPI.create)(capacity * 4);` is a REAL ALLOCATION, and
#     replaced it with a four-byte stack buffer — in an object that is validated
#     and in the build. The missing half is that every assignment must be the
#     ADDRESS OF A LOCAL. A pointer that came from a call is somebody's real
#     object; inventing storage for it is dead end 3.
#   * the first version of PTR_TO_LOCAL_OFF matched `(T *)(i + 2)` where `i` is
#     an int LOOP COUNTER, and reported MeFAsset's counter as a 4-byte object
#     addressed at +5 — which took MeFAsset back out of the build. A scalar's
#     name is its VALUE, not its address, so the offset form now requires an
#     array or an explicit `&`.
#
#   controls for the extended checker, both fire and the in-bounds access does not:
#     ptr = &local;             *(int *)((int)ptr + -0x10)   -> reported
#     ptr = (int *)(buf + 0x10); *(int *)((int)ptr + -0x14)  -> reported
#                                *(int *)((int)ptr + -0x10)  -> silent (offset 0)
#
#   132 objects · 132/132 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 in the build
#   bindings 0 · difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 132/132 · armv7 132/132, 0 truncations · arm64 6,948 across 82
#   engine, test-karma-1, 260 s: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#   drop-in gap 14 -> 13 members, 68 -> 63 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: MeAssetFactory is held for the RIGHT reason now -------------
#
# Not a release. A detector correction, recorded because the object's stated
# cause was a symptom and the real one was invisible.
#
# MeAssetFactory was held on `extraout_EAX, extraout_ECX`. Following those:
# `uVar20` is assigned ONLY from extraout_ECX_01..06 and read ONLY at
#
#   *(undefined4 *)((int)(kd_alloca_iVar5) + -4) = uVar20;
#
# which is the outgoing-argument area again, in a THIRD anchoring — four bytes
# BELOW an alloca'd block, because alloca lowers esp and the arguments go under
# it. Fifteen such sites, all at -4, and check_frame_bounds reported ZERO for
# the object twice over: `kd_alloca_iVar5` is a pointer local, and the reference
# is spelled `(int)(name) + -4` with the name PARENTHESISED, which REF cannot
# match because it expects the `+` immediately after the name.
#
#   * alloca_pointers() finds the base, including where the assignment is
#     embedded — `*(int *)(&(*kd_argslot_ffffffa8)) = (kd_alloca_iVar5 =
#     (char *)alloca((size_t)(count) * 4));`
#   * a NEGATIVE offset from an alloca'd block needs no size to be a violation:
#     alloca(n) yields n bytes and -4 is outside them however large n is
#   * REF_PAREN is a separate pattern rather than a loosening of REF, because
#     allowing a bare `(x) + 4` there would read `foo(arr) + 4` as a reference
#     to `arr`. The `(int)` prefix is required.
#
#   corpus-wide: MeAssetFactory only, and it was already held — so this shields
#   nothing that was validated and changes no object in the build. All 132 .o
#   files stay byte-identical.
#
#   three controls, all in-build, all fire, and the in-bounds access does not:
#     ptr = &local;              *(int *)((int)ptr + -0x10)      -> reported
#     ptr = (int *)(buf + 0x10); *(int *)((int)ptr + -0x14)      -> reported
#     p = alloca(n);             *(int *)((int)(p) + -4)         -> reported
#                                *(int *)((int)(p) + 4)          -> silent
#
# WHAT MeAssetFactory ACTUALLY NEEDS is the treatment McdInteractions got —
# real storage for the area below the anchor — with the anchor being an alloca
# pointer rather than the address of a local. materialise_pointer_arg_area
# REFUSES that shape on purpose: its guard is that every assignment to the
# pointer is the address of a LOCAL, which is what stopped it replacing MeHeap's
# `MeMemoryAPI.create(capacity * 4)` with a four-byte stack buffer. Extending it
# to alloca needs that distinction made explicitly, not by loosening the guard.

MeAssetFactory        real, and like MeFAsset the release is about REACHABILITY, not about the
#                     frame — with a stronger argument, because this one is a FIFTH member of
#                     the §3a family: code UT2004 links and REIMPLEMENTS.
#
#                     It took two fixes to get here and the first diagnosis was wrong.
#
#                     ONE: `(int)(kd_alloca_iVar5) + -4` IS AN ARGUMENT SLOT, NOT PADDING.
#                     Fifteen sites, and it looked like the padding family until the READ
#                     was found:
#
#                       *(MeMatrix4Ptr *)((int)(kd_alloca_iVar5) + -4) = tm;
#                       *(MdtWorldID *)(&(*kd_argslot_ffffffac)) = world;
#                       *(McdGeometryID *)(&(*kd_argslot_ffffffa8)) = pMVar11;
#                       *(MeAssetFactory **)(&(*kd_argslot_ffffffa4)) = pMVar21;
#                       *(undefined4 *)((int)auStackY_70 + 0x10) = 0x104e4;
#                       pMVar16 = (*p_Var3)(*(MeFAssetPart **)(&(*kd_argslot_ffffffa4)),
#                                           *(McdGeometryID *)(&(*kd_argslot_ffffffa8)),
#                                           *(MdtWorldID *)(&(*kd_argslot_ffffffac)),
#                                           *(MeMatrix4Ptr *)((int)(kd_alloca_iVar5) + -4));
#
#                     The return address is at -0x60, so the four arguments are -0x5c, -0x58,
#                     -0x54 and -0x50 — and `kd_alloca_iVar5 - 4` IS -0x50, read back by the
#                     call as its fourth argument. gcc puts an alloca'd block immediately
#                     above the outgoing area, so Ghidra spelled that one slot relative to
#                     the allocation and materialise_shifted_frame never saw it; the other
#                     three are `kd_argslot_*` and already have storage.
#                     materialise_alloca_relative_slots gives the fourth the same, rewriting
#                     base and offset together so the write-then-read is preserved exactly.
#                     Only NEGATIVE offsets, never the pointer's value: the block itself is
#                     real memory passed to MeFAssetGetPartsSortedByName as its output array.
#
#                       check_frame_bounds  before  1 reference below an alloca'd block
#                                            after  0
#
#                     TWO: ALL 155 kd_argslot_ REFERENCES ARE IN ONE FUNCTION, and it is
#                     `MeAssetInstanceCreate` — which UT2004 DOES NOT USE:
#
#                       KSkeletal.cpp:387   guard(MeAssetInstanceCreate);
#                       KSkeletal.cpp:388   skelAssIns = KAssetInstanceCreate(...);
#
#                     The `guard()` merely NAMES the MathEngine function the engine is
#                     replacing — Unreal's macro takes a scope label — and the call beside it
#                     is Epic's own. Same shape as KDynStep.cpp's integrator and KIntersect's
#                     two intercepts. Checked four ways, because "0 calls in one run" is a
#                     sample size:
#
#                       * the drop-in walk needs exactly five MeAssetFactory symbols, and
#                         MeAssetInstanceCreate is NOT one of them: MeAssetInstanceDestroy,
#                         MeAssetInstanceGetJoint, MeAssetInstanceGetModel,
#                         MeAssetInstanceGetNextGeometry, MeAssetInstanceInitGeometryIterator
#                       * no engine object imports it — 0
#                       * NOTHING IN THE WHOLE CORPUS calls it, imports it, or takes its
#                         address. Not one R_386_PC32, not one R_386_32, not one U. That is
#                         stronger than MeFAssetCreateCopy, which at least had two callers
#                       * gdb, test-karma-1, xDeathMatch, ~150 s: MeAssetInstanceCreate
#                         0 calls, MeAssetFactoryCreate 0 calls
#
#                     engine, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER, 133 objects substituted
#                       exit 124 (ran to the timeout), START MATCH reached, 0 faults
#                       stock control, same map and URL: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#                       substitution verified: MeAssetInstanceCreate 556 vs 477 instructions
#
#                     WHAT THIS DOES NOT SAY: that the reconstructed frame is right. The
#                     function carrying it never executed and cannot be reached, so the
#                     object links for its other five symbols. If anything ever calls
#                     MeAssetInstanceCreate the frame is a hypothesis again, and the 155
#                     references are where to start.

# --- 2026-08-26: an index into the alloca'd block, collapsed onto its ANCHOR --
#
# THE FIFTH TIME A CHANGED OBJECT TURNED OUT TO HAVE BEEN SILENTLY WRONG, and
# this one was in the build.
#
# materialise_alloca_frame collapses `(int)BASE + shiftVar` to the materialised
# block, because BASE + shift IS the block. It only recognised the base and the
# shift ADJACENT. Where an INDEX sits between them —
#
#   raw     *(undefined4 *)((int)apMStack_3c + iVar14 * 4 + iVar2)
#   was     *(undefined4 *)((int)apMStack_3c + iVar14 * 4)
#   now     *(undefined4 *)((int)(kd_alloca_iVar2) + iVar14 * 4)
#
# — the generic rule at the bottom of the pass dropped the `+ iVar2` and left an
# index into the ANCHOR. Ghidra's own text says which is meant:
#
#   iVar2 = -uVar24;                                   the alloca shift
#   *(int *)(&stack0xffffffb8 + uVar24 * -2) = (int)apMStack_3c + iVar2;
#   MeFAssetGetPartsSortedByName(asset, THAT);         the block, as an output array
#
# and the anchor is `MeFAssetPart *apMStack_3c[3]` — TWELVE BYTES — indexed by
# `iVar14`, the asset's part count. A UT2004 ragdoll has far more than three
# parts, so every part past the third read and wrote past an unrelated local.
#
#   MeFAsset         12 sites, all in MeFAssetCreateCopy
#   MeAssetFactory    8 sites, all in MeAssetInstanceCreate
#
# BOTH ARE IN THE ONE FUNCTION EACH OBJECT'S REACHABILITY LINE ALREADY RETIRES,
# which is why nothing observable moved and why the two releases stand: MeFAsset
# is bit-identical on the ragdoll scene before and after, and difftest is
# byte-identical to the documented baseline. That is a coincidence worth stating
# plainly rather than leaning on — the defect was real, it was in the build, and
# what stopped it mattering was that the function cannot be called.
#
# check_frame_bounds CANNOT see this shape: the index is a VARIABLE and it only
# reads constant offsets. `stack_guesses` is what was holding MeAssetFactory,
# and it was right.
#
#   133 objects · 133/133 clean on three scenes · MeFAsset, MeFAssetPart,
#   MeAssetFactory and MeAssetFactoryCreateFuncs all trajectory bit-identical
#   frame bounds 0 in the build · bindings 0
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 133/133 · armv7 133/133, 0 truncations · arm64 7,094 across 83
#   engine, test-karma-1, 260 s: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#   drop-in gap 13 -> 12 members, 63 -> 58 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: MeXMLParser — two call sites Ghidra merged into one ---------
#
# 12 symbols, and the blocker was an arity that could not be bounded FROM THE
# TEXT. The shipped code bounds it, and says why the text could not:
#
#   8df: push %ecx ; push %ecx                  <- 2 padding words
#   8e7: push %esi ; push %eax ; push %edi ; push %ebx      <- 4 arguments
#   8f7: push $0x280   R_386_32 .rodata.str1.1  <- "...expected %d strings..."
#   8fc: push <buffer> ; call sprintf           <- THE SHARED TAIL
#   915: sub $0xc,%esp                          <- 3 padding words, other path
#   91e: push %edx ; push %edx ; push %eax                  <- 3 arguments
#   927: push $0x2c0   R_386_32 .rodata.str1.1  <- "...greater than max..."
#   92c: jmp 8fc
#
# TWO call sites sharing a tail, each pushing its format as an IMMEDIATE. Eight
# pushed words either way, and at most FOUR of them are ever read. Ghidra merges
# the two into one call whose format is a variable and whose argument list is
# the union — six — so `_resolve_arity`'s max-over-literals gives 4, which is
# exactly right, and `pcVar4, pcVar6` are the two `push %ecx`.
#
#   sprintf(file->error,pcVar7,iVar1,iVar2,uVar8,puVar9,pcVar4,pcVar6);
#   ->  sprintf(file->error,pcVar7,iVar1,iVar2,uVar8,puVar9);
#
# WHAT WAS DECLINING IT was `pcVar7 = x;` earlier in the same function —
# readToNextTag's OUTPUT BUFFER, a non-literal whose arity cannot be bounded at
# all. The rule refused any site where the function held one. It does not reach
# this call, and `_reaching_formats` is what says so rather than assuming it:
# Ghidra writes its control flow with labels and gotos, so the reaching
# definitions are the nearest assignment before the call plus the nearest
# assignment before every `goto` to a label between them. One level, no fixed
# point; a predecessor whose own nearest definition is not a literal declines.
#
#   controls, and the second and third are the ones that matter:
#     both reaching definitions literal (4 and 3)   -> drops 2, keeps 4
#     the GOTO path's definition non-literal        -> drops 0
#     the FALL-THROUGH definition non-literal       -> drops 0
#
# AND THIS ONE IS MEASURED IN THE ENGINE, not argued from reachability — the
# first of the session's asset-path releases where the repaired function itself
# runs:
#
#   gdb, test-karma-1, xDeathMatch, ~130 s, 134 objects substituted
#     MeXMLElementProcess   1,462 calls
#     MeXMLParseMeReal      2,167 calls
#   engine, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER
#     exit 124 (ran to the timeout), START MATCH reached, 0 faults
#     stock control, same map and URL: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#   substitution verified: MeXMLParseStringArray 107 vs 101 instructions,
#   MeXMLElementProcess 231 vs 229
#
#   134 objects · 134/134 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 in the build
#   bindings 0 · difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 134/134 · armv7 134/134, 0 truncations · arm64 7,125 across 84
#   drop-in gap 12 -> 11 members, 58 -> 46 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: two reachability routes CLOSED, recorded so nobody re-walks --
#
# The reachability argument released MeFAsset, MeAssetFactory and (in effect)
# MdtBcl. It does NOT work for the two singletons it looks like it should, and
# both near-misses are worth having written down.
#
# MdtLOD — THE ROUTE IS CLOSED, and the check that said otherwise was broken.
#   All 18 kd_argslot_ references are in `ResizeConstraint`, and the engine needs
#   only `MdtLODLastPartition`. Searching the corpus for a relocation naming
#   ResizeConstraint finds NOTHING — no R_386_PC32, no R_386_32 — which reads
#   exactly like MeFAssetCreateCopy's answer and is worthless here:
#
#     ResizeConstraint is `t`, a FILE-STATIC, so a call from its own object
#     needs no relocation at all. It is a PC-relative displacement fixed at
#     assembly time.
#
#   Disassembling instead of grepping relocations:
#
#     4da: e8 81 07 00 00    call c60 <ResizeConstraint>     inside MdtLODLastPartition
#
#   So the frame IS reachable, from the one symbol the engine imports. MdtLOD
#   needs a measurement or a repair, not an argument. ANY reachability claim
#   about a static must be made from the DISASSEMBLY.
#
# IxBoxTriList — the route exists and is REFUSED on the merits.
#   The 12 kd_argslot_ references are in `McdBoxTriangleListIntersect`, and the
#   engine needs only `McdBoxTriangleListRegisterInteraction`. §3a proves
#   Box x TriangleList is intercepted by KIntersect before Karma is consulted —
#   "NEVER CALLED and CANNOT be, from the engine source" — so the argument is
#   available. It is not taken, because the difference from MeFAssetCreateCopy
#   is material: the registration function REGISTERS this one in a live dispatch
#   table, so its ADDRESS IS TAKEN, and difftest measures it diverging on
#   139,961 of 200,000 pairs. An object that is merely unreachable is one thing;
#   a known-wrong collision function sitting in a dispatch table is what the
#   detectors are for. One symbol is not worth it.

# --- 2026-08-26: a plain data reference at a NON-ZERO addend -----------------
#
# §5's inversion, applied to the one shape it had never been given somewhere to
# put the answer. `_resolve_external`'s docstring has always said that a plain
# READ of a mislabelled slot "has no calling convention to get wrong, so the
# exact four-byte access Ghidra meant is always available" — and the code only
# implemented the addend-ZERO case.
#
# MeProfileStartHardwareTimer fills a 24-byte exported object one word at a
# time, and every store is the SAME relocation:
#
#   57: mov  %edx,0x0     R_386_32 frameTime   addend 0
#   5d: mov  %ecx,0x4     R_386_32 frameTime   addend 4
#   63: movl $0x0,0x8     R_386_32 frameTime   addend 8
#   6d: movl $0x0,0xc                          addend 12
#   77: movl $0x0,0x10                         addend 16
#   81: movl $0x0,0x14                         addend 20
#
# Ghidra names each by whichever EXTERNAL slot the addend landed in, so the six
# come out as `frameTime`, `_select`, `_clockSpeed`, `_DAT_0001100c`,
# `_DAT_00011010`, `_DAT_00011014` — five borrowed from unrelated undefined
# symbols. `relocation_targets` already inverted it exactly and had been doing
# so all along:
#
#   _select        -> [('frameTime', 4)]
#   _clockSpeed    -> [('frameTime', 8)]
#   _DAT_0001100c  -> [('frameTime', 12)]   ... and so on
#
# The slot is four bytes wide BY CONSTRUCTION — that is how Ghidra lays its
# block out — so the access width is not a guess, and the rule is only reached
# where the name is never CALLED, because a call needs a prototype and this
# gives it none.
#
#   McdGjkPenetrationDepth RELEASED on it — its `_DAT_0001205c` is the same
#   shape, at 0x1205c in an object whose sections end at 0x110f4. That was the
#   §3c row this file listed as "needs the relocation, not the address"; it
#   needed neither a new tool nor a re-dump, only the branch that was missing.
#
#   135 objects · 135/135 clean on three scenes · McdGjkPenetrationDepth
#   trajectory bit-identical · frame bounds 0 in the build · bindings 0
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   drop-in gap 11 -> 10 members, 46 -> 45 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: MeProfile_linux — an instruction, a tag name, a first word --
#
# 6 symbols, four causes, and the addend fix above had already removed two of
# them. What was left:
#
#   rdtsc()          NOT A FUNCTION — the x86 instruction that reads the cycle
#                    counter, which Ghidra renders as a call returning 64 bits.
#   timeval t;       a tag name where C wants `struct timeval`.
#   frameTime = ...  a four-byte store to the first word of a 24-byte struct,
#                    spelled with the struct's own name because at addend ZERO
#                    the name Ghidra prints is the real one — so nothing flagged
#                    it until the compiler did. The five stores AFTER it are
#                    already `*(int *)((char *)&frameTime + K)`; this makes the
#                    first the same access.
#
# THE rdtsc SHIM IS A STAND-IN OFF x86 AND THAT IS SAID OUT LOUD, in kd_compat.h
# and here. On x86 it is the real instruction, so i386 — where every gate in
# this project runs — is exact. Elsewhere it is a monotonic counter, not a cycle
# count. What makes that acceptable is reachability, and it was checked rather
# than assumed:
#
#   * the ENGINE references no MeProfile symbol directly. All ten arrive
#     through the link closure.
#   * but they are not dead: MeProfileStartSectionFn is a real function (`T`)
#     and MstUniverseStep CALLS it twice at the top of every step —
#     `1bd: R_386_PC32 MeProfileStartSectionFn`. So the profiler runs.
#   * what rdtsc's value reaches is `MeProfileTimerResult.cpuCycles` and
#     nothing else. No physics depends on it. A profiler reporting the wrong
#     UNITS on a target where nothing has ever executed is not a defect worth
#     blocking the port for — but it is a difference, so it is on the record.
#
#   136 objects · 136/136 clean on three scenes · MeProfile_linux trajectory
#   bit-identical · frame bounds 0 in the build · bindings 0
#   wasm32 136/136 — the non-x86 branch of the shim compiles
#   armv7 136/136, 0 truncations · arm64 7,149 across 86
#   drop-in gap 10 -> 9 members, 45 -> 39 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: MeProfile — seven causes, and two near-misses caught --------
#
# 4 symbols and the last FAIL outside McdSpace/MeMath. Seven distinct causes,
# each a generator fix; zero objects changed by any of them except the two
# below, which were reverted.
#
#  1. `weightingData` USED AS A TYPE. MathEngine writes `typedef struct _X {...}
#     X;` so Ghidra uses `_X` for the tag and `X` for the typedef, and the
#     recovered source uses BOTH. gen_typedb emitted only `typedef struct _X _X`.
#     THE CONDITION FOR ADDING THE BARE NAME IS DELIBERATELY BLUNT: it must
#     appear NOWHERE in the public headers, in any form. Of the 27 bare names
#     the corpus uses this way, 25 DO appear — MeHeap, MeXMLInput, McdGjkSimplex
#     — and a typedef for those would collide with whatever shape the header
#     already gives them, including `typedef struct _X *X;`, which is a
#     different type. Two do not: `weightingData` and `McdContactLink`.
#
#  2. `declared_under` PARSED PAST A `#define`. Its statement boundary is `;`
#     and a `#define` has none, so the chunk ending at KD_MANGLED("frameTime")
#     ran back through the prelude's tail and `_DECLARATOR` matched
#     `MeProfileOutputResults__size64` — a macro name, which is followed by `(`
#     where `kd_frameTime` is followed by a space. `frameTime` therefore mapped
#     to it, `fix_exported_data_name` was handed an identifier that appears
#     nowhere on the line, and renamed nothing. Comments and preprocessor lines
#     are stripped first now. ONE mapping changes corpus-wide, and it is that one.
#
#  3. THE RENAME HAS TO BE FILE-WIDE, and per-line it could not converge: GCC
#     reports an undeclared identifier once per FUNCTION and `frameTime` appears
#     twenty-odd times, so a line-at-a-time rename needs one compile per
#     occurrence and the loop runs out first.
#
#  4. AND FILE-WIDE WENT STRAIGHT THROUGH KD_MANGLED("..."). The payload of that
#     macro is the ELF symbol the unit EXPORTS. `keaRbdCore_unified` — validated,
#     bit-identical on all three scenes — started exporting `kd_gDebug`,
#     `kd_gDebugDataFile` and `kd_gPartition` in place of the three names the
#     shipped object exports. **check_symbol_bindings reported ZERO interface
#     errors while it did**, which is a hole worth knowing about: it compares
#     what the recovered object exports against the SHIPPED object's list and
#     did not notice three names replaced by three others. Caught by the
#     blast-radius diff and by `nm`, not by the gate. `_sub_in_code` now skips
#     string literals and comments.
#
#  5. THE RENAME UNMASKS `X.member._4_4_`. While `frameTime` was undeclared GCC
#     said nothing about member accesses on it; renaming makes six appear at
#     once, so the edit costs +5 in one step and the loop reverts it — correctly.
#     The rule performs the subfield rewrite itself, on expressions containing
#     the renamed identifier only.
#
#  6. `clockSpeed` IS `.data`, so it got the byte blob rather than its DWARF
#     type, and `fix_exported_data_name` refuses to rename onto a definition
#     whose type is wrong (the McduDebugDraw guard). A `.data` export whose
#     bytes are all zero can have its DWARF type and still be byte-exact —
#     **BUT ONLY IF NO RELOCATION COVERS IT**. Zero bytes do not mean zero
#     value: MeMessage's ten `.data` exports are zero bytes plus a relocation
#     each (`MeInfoShow` -> `MeShowStderr`), and without that second condition
#     they moved to `.bss` and lost their initialisers. That one the gate DID
#     catch, precisely: ".data -> .bss but the shipped bytes are not zero — the
#     initialiser is LOST", 11 errors across 3 objects. `clockSpeed` has no
#     relocation at all.
#
#  7. libgcc's 64-bit helpers. On i386 a `long long` divide is a CALL, and each
#     operand arrives as two pushed words, low first. The ORDER is read, not
#     assumed: MeProfileGetClockSpeed computes `(clockSpeed + 100000) / 1000000`
#     — cycles to MHz — so the dividend is the first pair; reversed it returns
#     zero. The DIVISOR'S HIGH WORD is sometimes missing from Ghidra's
#     rendering, and defaulting it to zero is read from the machine code:
#     MeProfile.o+0x2ac9 is `xor %eax,%eax; push %eax; push %edx; push %esi;
#     push %ebx` — four words, the top one an explicit zero, of which Ghidra
#     recovered three. Verified against real division at 32/64-bit values, with
#     the 3-argument, 4-argument and padded forms, and with each argument
#     evaluated exactly once.
#
#     AND THAT BROKE fix_compat_macro_extra_args, which drops arguments past a
#     variadic kd_compat.h macro's named count. Right for
#     `__strtod_internal(s, e, ...)`, which DISCARDS them; wrong for
#     `__divdi3(al, ah, ...)`, where the "extras" are the divisor. The
#     discriminator is whether the macro BODY mentions __VA_ARGS__.
#
#  8. `_DAT_000136c0` is at 0x136c0, inside `.rodata.cst16` — a real section of
#     that object, not the EXTERNAL block, which starts at 0x14000. DATA_REF
#     matched only the bare spelling, so it fell through to the
#     mislabelled-external rule, which had nothing to say about it.
#
# A CORRECTION TO THE PREVIOUS ENTRY, and it is the kind this file exists for.
# MeProfile_linux's note said "they are not dead: MeProfileStartSectionFn is a
# real function and MstUniverseStep CALLS it twice at the top of every step. So
# the profiler runs." That was read off a RELOCATION and is wrong as a statement
# about UT2004. Measured:
#
#   gdb, test-karma-1, xDeathMatch, 137 objects substituted
#     MeProfileStartSectionFn   0 calls
