#     MeProfileGetTimerValue    0 calls
#     MstUniverseStep           0 calls
#     MstBridgeUpdateContacts   0 calls
#   and the engine imports MstUniverseStep 0 times
#
# `MstUniverseStep` is a SIXTH member of the §3a family — code UT2004 links and
# reimplements, alongside MdtWorldStep, KIntersect's two intercepts,
# MdtKeaIntegrateSystem and MeAssetInstanceCreate. So the whole profiler chain
# links and never runs, and the rdtsc stand-in is even less consequential than
# that entry claimed. The claim was still wrong when it was made.
#
#   137 objects · 137/137 clean on three scenes · MeProfile and MeProfile_linux
#   both trajectory bit-identical · frame bounds 0 in the build · bindings 0
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 137/137 · armv7 137/137, 0 truncations · arm64 7,457 across 87
#   engine, test-karma-1, 260 s: exit 124, START MATCH, 0 faults
#   drop-in gap 10 -> 8 members, 45 -> 35 symbols

# --- 2026-08-26: the hole in check_symbol_bindings, closed -------------------
#
# Found by walking into it. A file-wide rename went through the payload of
# KD_MANGLED("gDebugDataFile") — which IS the ELF symbol the unit exports — and
# `keaRbdCore_unified` began exporting `kd_gDebug`, `kd_gDebugDataFile` and
# `kd_gPartition` in place of the three names the shipped object exports. The
# gate whose entire job is "does each recovered object export the same INTERFACE
# as the shipped one" reported ZERO errors.
#
# The reason was one line:
#
#     if name not in r:
#         continue          # absent is a link error, not ours
#
# Every per-symbol check below it — binding, section, size — only ever ran on
# symbols present in BOTH objects, so a name that VANISHED was skipped before
# any of them looked at it.
#
# A missing shipped GLOBAL is now reported, and whether it is an ERROR or a
# warning is decided by whether anything in the corpus IMPORTS it — which is
# read, not assumed:
#
#   currently missing and imported by nothing (warnings, 5):
#     CxSmallSort   _ZN11CxSmallSortC2Eii, D1Ev, D2Ev   (C++ ABI variants)
#     McdBox        McdBoxGetXYAABB
#     McdTriangleList  McdTriangleListGetBoundingBox
#   gDebug: 1 metoolkit importer and 14 engine objects  -> ERROR
#
# CONTROL, and it is the defect itself rather than a stand-in: take the real
# keaRbdCore_unified.o, `objcopy --redefine-sym` the three names exactly as the
# bug did, and run the gate.
#
#     [ERROR ] keaRbdCore_unified.o: gDebug (B) is EXPORTED by the shipped
#              object and MISSING from the recovered one
#     interface errors : 1        exit 1
#
#   137 objects · 0 interface errors · 7 tolerated (the 5 above plus the two
#   documented .rodata -> .data warnings)

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): ReadWriteKeaInputToFile — one symbol -----
#   typed two ways, and a variadic family nobody had counted
#
# THREE symbols of the drop-in gap, closed by two generator fixes and one
# prelude fix. Neither was written for this object; both are about a shape.
#
# 1. `_gDebug` — ONE EXTERNAL, TWO INCOMPATIBLE TYPES, IN ONE FILE.
#
#      *(void **)(_gDebug + 4)                        <- byte arithmetic
#      (*_gDebug != 0) && (_gDebug[8] == _gDebug[9])  <- element arithmetic
#
#    No single C declaration makes both right, and THAT is the finding: it is a
#    contradiction visible in the text. Declaring the import `char *` makes the
#    first correct and silently turns `_gDebug[8]` into byte 8; giving it the
#    real `MdtKeaDebugDataRequest *` makes `+ 4` mean byte 176. Either way it
#    COMPILES and reads the wrong memory.
#
#    Ghidra folds the load of a pointer variable away, so the element SIZE is
#    nowhere in the file. It is in the machine code, and
#    `extern_load_displacements` reads it — the byte displacements each function
#    uses off the register the symbol was loaded into:
#
#      checkPrintDebugInput   text {*0, [8], [9]}   code {0, 0x20, 0x24}  -> 4
#      writeKeaInputToFile    text {+4}             code {0x4}            -> 1
#      readKeaInputFromFile   text {+0xc}           code {0xc}            -> 1
#      writeLambdaToFile      text {+0x1c}          code {0x1c}           -> 1
#
#    Two scales for one name is the contradiction; the scale is ACCEPTED only
#    when text and code agree exactly as sets. Every offset lands on a real
#    member of MdtKeaDebugDataRequest (MdtKea.h): 0 writeKeaInputData,
#    4 writeKeaInputDataFilename, 0xc readKeaInputDataFilename,
#    0x1c writeKeaOutputDataFilename, 0x20 frame, 0x24 badFrame — and the
#    sibling object keaRbdCore_unified, validated and bit-identical, spells the
#    same comparison `kd_gDebug->frame == kd_gDebug->badFrame`. Six independent
#    confirmations of one reading.
#
#    FIVE CONTROLS, because the first version of this rule DECLINED ON THE ONE
#    OBJECT IT EXISTS FOR and passed every negative control while doing it — a
#    rule that never fires passes them all. A byte-only function admits every
#    scale, so demanding a unique scale rejected it. The controls only became
#    evidence once the real run returned 1:
#      A  index 8 -> 7          disagrees with the code   -> declines
#      B  byte offset 4 -> 8    disagrees with the code   -> declines
#      C  a use in an unaccounted shape                   -> declines
#      D  the element form removed, no contradiction      -> declines
#      E  keaLCPSolver, validated and in the build        -> untouched
#    E is the one that matters: `_vanillaQMatrix` is the corpus's ONLY other
#    two-typings site and it is not an external at all. Running after
#    fix_vptr_store means that spelling is already gone.
#
# 2. `sscanf` — THE VARIADIC PADDING WORD, ON THE READING SIDE.
#    `fix_printf_extra_args` has always known that gcc's alignment filler comes
#    back as an argument; its family table had no scanf in it. The machine code
#    says outright that the fourth word is not an argument:
#
#      62a: push %eax ; push %ecx ; push $0x100 ; push %edi
#      632: call MeStreamReadLine
#      637: add $0xc,%esp          <- cleans THREE of the four
#      63a: push %esi ; push $fmt ; push %edi
#      644: call sscanf
#      650: add $0x10,%esp         <- FOUR words for a THREE-argument call
#
#    gcc pushed a filler word before the previous call, cleaned only three, and
#    reused the leftover as sscanf's alignment slot. Ghidra recovers it as an
#    argument sourced from whatever register was live — which is where this
#    object's `extraout_ECX`/`extraout_EDX` come from. prove_inert says they are
#    NOT inert (the object code differs for 0 / -1 / 0x5a5a5a5a), and it is
#    right: the word is really pushed. It is simply never fetched.
#
#    COUNTED WITH ITS OWN ARITY FUNCTION. `%*d` consumes an int in printf and
#    SUPPRESSES in scanf, so sharing `_printf_arity` would over-count — which
#    only ever makes the rule keep an argument, i.e. it would have been wrong in
#    the silent direction. Ten-case table, including that divergence: 0 failures.
#
# 3. THE PRELUDE DECLARED A TYPE THAT DOES NOT EXIST.
#    `extern struct MdtKeaDebugDataRequest *gDebug;` — MdtKea.h writes
#    `typedef struct {...} MdtKeaDebugDataRequest;`, so the `struct` spelling is
#    an unrelated incomplete type. `anonymous_struct_typedefs` has existed since
#    the sixth session and was applied to the two EXPORT paths and never to the
#    IMPORT path. One object corpus-wide, and it was this one.
#
#   138 objects · 138/138 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 in the build
#   bindings 0 · difftest byte-identical to the 137-object baseline (diffed
#   run-to-run, not read off the documented numbers) · wasm32 138/138
#   armv7 138/138, 0 truncations · arm64 7,670 across 88 · vptr_ab ok
#   BLAST RADIUS: every one of the 137 pre-existing .o BYTE-IDENTICAL
#   engine, build-subst138, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER:
#     exit 124, START MATCH, 0 SIGSEGV, 0 Critical Error, 0 NaN
#     stock control on the same map: identical on every one of those
#     substitution verified in the BINARY, not from the link —
#       writeKeaInputToFile  3198 -> 3065 insns, readKeaInputFromFile 2974 -> 2542
#   drop-in gap 8 -> 7 members, 35 -> 32 symbols
ReadWriteKeaInputToFile  RELEASED 2026-08-26 (seventh session). Three symbols of the
#                     drop-in gap. `_gDebug` was typed two ways by Ghidra and the
#                     scale that settles it was read out of the machine code and
#                     checked against the text in all four functions; the sscanf
#                     padding words are gcc's alignment filler, proven by
#                     `add $0x10,%esp` cleaning four words for a three-argument
#                     call. All nine gates green, 137 pre-existing objects
#                     byte-identical, and the engine runs on it against a stock
#                     control on the same map.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): MdtPartition — a chain of allocas ---------
#
# FOUR symbols of the drop-in gap, and the object was not merely unbuilt: what
# it compiled to before this was a stack smash the detector was holding back.
#
# TWO DEFECTS, ONE CAUSE — materialise_alloca_frame modelled ONE alloca shift,
# and MdtUpdatePartitions has two. The prologue says so outright:
#
#    20: sub %esi,%esp    addedBodies      = &local_3c + iVar16   (maxBodies*4)
#    39: sub %edx,%esp    addedConstraints = &local_3c + iVar8 + iVar16
#    3e: sub $0xc,%esp    the outgoing argument area
#
#   (a) THE SECOND BLOCK WAS NEVER ALLOCATED. Only the one-shift defining use
#       was recognised, so the generic collapse dropped `+ iVar8` and left
#
#           addedConstraints = (MdtBaseConstraint **)((int)&local_3c);
#
#       — a FOUR-BYTE local, written `maxConstraints` times at line 130. It
#       compiles. This is the 64 KB-buffer defect of the function's own
#       docstring, in a second spelling.
#
#   (b) THE ARGUMENT AREA KEPT A DANGLING SHIFT. The five slots are
#       `(int)aiStack_50 + iVar8 + iVar16 + {0,4,8,0xc,0x10}`; the collapse
#       removed one shift and KEPT the other, leaving a large negative DYNAMIC
#       index into `int aiStack_50[5]`. check_frame_bounds cannot see it — the
#       index is a variable and it only reads constants.
#
# WHICH VAR SIZES WHICH BLOCK IS DERIVED, NOT GUESSED. The shift sets are
# nested by construction — each is the running total of the `sub`s so far — so
# sorting a base's sets by length makes each introduce exactly one new var, and
# that var is the `sub` that created the block. A set introducing none or more
# than one is not a chain and is left alone.
#
# AND THE BOUND THAT MAKES (b) SAFE IS "MORE THAN ONE SHIFT", measured the hard
# way. Written first without it, the area rule fired on SINGLE-shift sites too
# and changed five validated objects — IxConvexTriList, IxCylinderTriList,
# IxSphereTriList, IxSphylPrimitives, MeAssetFactory — and knocked MstUtils out
# of the build. With one shift the existing collapse removes it outright and the
# slot lands on its own base local, which is exactly what those seven rely on.
# With two, one shift survives. That is the whole difference, and it is why
# HANDOVER.md's "survey the RAW dumps, not the recovered output" warning is
# right: the shape is everywhere, the DEFECT is not.
#
# An area is told from a pointer derivation by having MORE THAN ONE SLOT in it
# — the group must carry at least one explicit constant. That is what keeps this
# off MeAssetDBXMLOutput_1_0, validated and in the build, whose twenty-two
# multi-shift sites are every one of them a bare `base + shifts` with no
# constant. Measured across the corpus, not assumed.
#
# WHAT THE ENGINE EVIDENCE IS, AND WHAT IT IS NOT — and this is the part worth
# reading, because the naive reading of it is wrong.
#
#   `MdtUpdatePartitions` is imported by MathEngine's own MdtWorld.o and by
#   NOTHING ELSE. The engine replaces MdtWorldStep with KWorldStepSafeTime, so
#   the partitioner is a FIFTH MEMBER OF THE 3a FAMILY: linked, in the drop-in
#   closure, and never executed in normal play. A gdb breakpoint on it reads
#   ZERO over a 200 s match — and that is not the instrument failing, which was
#   CHECKED: a second breakpoint on MdtKeaAddConstraintForces in the same run
#   stops immediately, at KDynStep.cpp:849.
#
#   THREE of the five functions ARE called by the engine, directly, from its own
#   KDynStep.cpp — and they execute, in a live match, as recovered code:
#     MdtPartOutCreateFromChunk  KWorldStepSafeTime, KDynStep.cpp:761
#     MdtPartInfoReset           KBuildPartitions,   KDynStep.cpp:93
#     MdtPartInfoAddConstraint   KBuildPartitions,   KDynStep.cpp:144
#   `KBuildPartitions` is the engine's own reimplementation of the partitioner,
#   which is why the MathEngine one is dead here.
#
#   So the behavioural evidence for MdtUpdatePartitions itself is the SCENE.
#
# >>> CORRECTION, same day, and it is the important part of this entry. <<<
#
#   The first version of this paragraph said "900 calls in scene_chain with a
#   BIT-IDENTICAL trajectory". THAT WAS WRONG, and the mistake is one this file
#   warns about in general terms and had not yet caught anyone doing:
#   `substitute_test.sh`'s SUMMARY line reads
#
#       substituted and ran cleanly : 140    crashed / NaN / short : 0
#
#   and "ran cleanly" is the CRASH check — `pass` is incremented in ALL THREE
#   branches of the verdict, `[ diverg]` included, so that number cannot go down
#   for a numerical difference at all. And there are three tiers, so `[  ok  ]`
#   is not bit-identity either: delta 0 is "bit-identical", delta < 1e-3 is a
#   TOLERANCE band that still prints `[  ok  ]`. MdtPartition's line says the
#   opposite of what the summary implied:
#
#       [ diverg] MdtPartition — max delta 8.744e+02 m first@1=3.941e-02   (ragdoll)
#       [ diverg] MdtPartition — max delta 4.351e+00 m first@1=3.391e-04   (chain)
#       [ diverg] MdtPartition — max delta 1.045e+03 m first@52=6.988e-02  (boxes)
#
#   It diverges on ALL THREE scenes, and 3.9e-02 at step ONE is not rounding —
#   the other five divergers in the build are 1e-6 to 1e-8 at their first step.
#   The gate_sensitivity row I quoted as proof was the same number read out of
#   the "as recovered" COLUMN, which is the divergence, not the sensitivity.
#
# WHAT bisect_object.sh SAYS, and it is why the release still stands — on
# better evidence than was originally claimed. Both controls read identical:
#
#       --none--   (control)                  identical
#       --abi-only-- (control)                identical
#       FindNextUnadded                       identical
#       MdtPartInfoAddConstraint              identical   <- the engine calls this
#       MdtPartInfoReset                      identical   <- the engine calls this
#       MdtPartOutCreateFromChunk             identical   <- the engine calls this
#       MdtUpdatePartitions   first@1=3.941e-02 max=8.744e+02
#
#   EVERY FUNCTION THE ENGINE REACHES IS BIT-IDENTICAL. The one that diverges is
#   the 3a-family member the engine cannot reach, and the import counts say so
#   with their own control in the same measurement:
#
#       MdtPartOutCreateFromChunk   1 engine object imports it
#       MdtPartInfoReset            1
#       MdtPartInfoAddConstraint    1
#       MdtUpdatePartitions         0
#       MdtWorldStep                0   (so the route through it does not exist)
#
#   SO THE RELEASE IS CORRECT AND THE ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS NOT. What is
#   NOT known is why MdtUpdatePartitions diverges — a different but valid
#   partition ORDER would do it, since the order constraints are solved in
#   changes the summation order — and nobody should assume that without
#   measuring. It is unreachable, so it is out of scope by this file's own rule;
#   it is recorded because "we shipped it and never found out" is worse.
#
#   139 objects · 139/139 no crash on three scenes (NOT the same as
#   bit-identical — see the correction above) · frame bounds 0 in the build
#   bindings 0 · difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   wasm32 139/139 · armv7 139/139, 0 truncations · arm64 7,771 across 89
#   BLAST RADIUS: all 138 pre-existing .o BYTE-IDENTICAL
#   engine, build-subst139, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER:
#     exit 124, START MATCH, 0 SIGSEGV, 0 Critical Error, 0 NaN
#     substitution verified in the BINARY: MdtUpdatePartitions 487 -> 470 insns,
#     MdtPartOutCreateFromChunk 45 -> 38
#   drop-in gap 7 -> 6 members, 32 -> 28 symbols
MdtPartition          RELEASED 2026-08-26 (seventh session). Four symbols of the drop-in
#                     gap. Two allocas in one function; only one was modelled, so
#                     `addedConstraints` pointed at a four-byte local and the five
#                     argument slots kept a dangling dynamic shift. Three of its
#                     five functions are called by the engine's own KDynStep.cpp
#                     and execute in a live match, and bisect_object.sh says ALL
#                     THREE ARE BIT-IDENTICAL with both controls passing.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): McdContact — ATTEMPTED, REVERTED, and the -
#   frame is now READ. The next attempt is cheap; this one shipped a smash.
#
# HANDOVER.md said the blocker was "an alloca shift whose rounding is + 0x10
# rather than + 0xf & ~0xf, which is why ALLOCA_SIZE does not match it". That is
# TRUE, it was fixed, and IT IS NOT THE WHOLE BLOCKER. Recording both halves.
#
# THE ROUNDING HALF — correct, and worth keeping when this is retried.
# `sub %esp` must leave the stack 16-byte aligned, so where the size is ALREADY
# a multiple of 16 gcc omits the mask entirely:
#
#     7c6: mov 0x18(%ebp),%ebx   ; inMaxContactPointCount
#     7cc: shl $0x4,%ebx         ; * 16
#     7d0: add $0x10,%ebx        ; + 16     -> (count+1) * 16
#     7d3: sub %ebx,%esp         ; the alloca, no rounding needed
#     7d7: mov %esp,-0x48(%ebp)  ; and THIS is list.link
#
# Ghidra spells it `iVar30 = -(inMaxContactPointCount * 0x10 + 0x10);`. Matching
# it needs the multiplier AND the addend to be multiples of 16 — the exact
# condition under which the mask is redundant, so the only condition under which
# its absence means an alloca. One object corpus-wide satisfies that test.
#
# THE HALF THAT IS NOT DONE, AND WHY THE OBJECT IS STILL HELD:
# **GHIDRA'S ANCHOR IS NOT THE BLOCK BASE.** It is the block base PLUS 0x10.
#
#     452:  list.link = (McdContactLink *)(auStack_8c + iVar30 + -0x10);
#     465:  *(undefined1 **)(auStack_8c + iVar30 + -8) = auStack_8c + iVar30;
#
# and line 465 is line 458 of the same file written out longhand —
# `list.link[0].next = list.link + 1` — with `next` at offset 8 and
# sizeof(McdContactLink) 0x10. Ghidra confirms its own anchor for us.
#
# So the group's constants are {-0x10, -8, 0} and THE BLOCK BASE IS THE MINIMUM,
# not the bare form. Every existing rule assumes the bare form is the base:
#   * sub_alloca fired on `auStack_8c + iVar30` and allocated 0x10 too high;
#   * sub_below claimed `+ -0x10` as "argument scratch below the block" and gave
#     `list.link` a `void *[2]`, which the code then indexes as (count+1)
#     sixteen-byte structs.
#
# THE RESULT COMPILED AND EVERY GATE READ CLEAN. check_frame_bounds reports 0
# because `list.link[i]` is a VARIABLE index and it only reads constants — the
# same blind spot recorded for MeAssetFactory. This is the shape HANDOVER.md
# warns about in those words: it compiles, it passes the substitute scenes, and
# it corrupts. REVERTED rather than shipped; a detector holding an object back
# is the detector working, and one symbol does not buy a stack smash.
#
# WHAT THE NEXT ATTEMPT NEEDS, stated so it is not re-derived: the alloca model
# must allow Ghidra's anchor to sit at a CONSTANT OFFSET above the block base,
# and that offset is the minimum constant over the (anchor, shift) group. Then
#     list.link            -> kd_alloca + 0
#     anchor + shift + -8  -> kd_alloca + 8
#     anchor + shift       -> kd_alloca + 0x10
# Guard it and measure the blast radius: this rule family regressed five
# validated objects once already today (see the MdtPartition entry), so the
# acceptance test is every pre-existing .o byte-identical, and sub_below must
# stop claiming a site whose value is ASSIGNED rather than dereferenced.
#
# SECOND LOOK, same session, and it found TWO MORE THINGS that make the plan
# above insufficient. Neither was visible until the references were listed in
# full, so they are listed in full here:
#
#     452:  list.link = (McdContactLink *)(auStack_8c + iVar30 + -0x10);
#     465:  *(undefined1 **)(auStack_8c + iVar30 + -8) = auStack_8c + iVar30;
#     605:  *(McdContact **)((int)aiStack_b0 + iVar30 + 0x10) = pMVar23;
#     607:  *(MeReal **)((int)aiStack_b0 + iVar30 + 0xc) = normal;
#     608:  *(McdContact **)((int)aiStack_b0 + iVar30 + 8) = pMVar23;
#     609:  *(McdContactList **)((int)aiStack_b0 + iVar30 + 4) = &list;
#     612:  *(undefined4 *)((int)aiStack_b0 + iVar30) = 0x10ae8;
#     678:  iVar30 = list.ct;                          <-- !!
#     716:  } while (iVar24 < iVar30);
#
#   1. TWO ANCHORS, NOT ONE. `auStack_8c` is the alloca block (constants
#      -0x10, -8, 0) and `aiStack_b0` is the OUTGOING ARGUMENT AREA (constants
#      0, 4, 8, 0xc, 0x10). They are different regions at the same depth and a
#      rule that takes "the minimum constant" across both computes nonsense.
#
#   2. **THE SHIFT VARIABLE IS REASSIGNED AND REUSED.** At line 678, inside the
#      SAME function, `iVar30 = list.ct` and it becomes a loop bound. So the
#      name means the alloca shift for the first half of McdContactSimplify and
#      a contact count for the second. Any rule keyed on the variable name alone
#      rewrites the wrong thing, and any rule that checks "every use of the var
#      is a frame reference" correctly DECLINES — which is what happens today.
#
# So this is the fourth independent reason McdContact is not the small fix the
# old one-line diagnosis suggested. It is ONE symbol. Whoever takes it needs a
# rule that is anchor-aware, offset-aware AND live-range-aware, and the burden
# of proof is a byte-identical blast radius plus difftest, because
# McdContactSimplify is on the live contact path.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): arm64 — 7,771 truncations to 363 ---------
#
# HANDOVER.md §6b has said since the fourth session that arm64 "compiles and is
# not trustworthy", that the fix "is a generator-wide change, not a flag", and
# that no behavioural gate here can see the defect because none of them executes
# arm64 code. This is that change. **95.3% of the defect is closed and every
# i386 object is BYTE-IDENTICAL.**
#
#     7,771 across 89 objects        the sixth session's figure, at 139 objects
#     6,919 across 89                after dropping dead decompiled fragments
#       363 across 42                after widening the punned casts
#
# PART ONE — FIVE "FUNCTIONS" THAT ARE FRAGMENTS OF THREE REAL ONES (852 sites).
# `not_code` already refused a FUN_ outside .text and one that is nothing but
# `halt_baddata()`. A third case was never covered: Ghidra cannot always follow
# the control flow of a large function and slices interior blocks off as
# separate "functions". Read from the symbol table rather than judged —
#
#     FUN_00020000, FUN_00020030, FUN_000201a0  inside MdtBclAddRPROJoint
#     FUN_00020720                              inside LimitSingleAxis
#     FUN_00020ee0                              inside MdtContactWriteRow
#
# — all three parents decompiled under their own names, all five fragments
# `static` and referenced by nothing. They are entered mid-frame, which is why
# they read `unaff_EBP + -0x25c`: `%ebp` was set by the PARENT's prologue, so
# from the fragment's view the frame base arrives in a register nobody assigned.
# `initialise_unmodelled_locals` then honestly sets it to 0 and the fragment
# dereferences address -604.
#
#   AND THIS EXPLAINS A prove_inert RESULT THAT SHOULD HAVE LOOKED ODD. MdtBcl's
#   four `unaff_` values were reported PROVEN INERT while being dereferenced,
#   which reads like a contradiction. It is not: the functions are static and
#   unreferenced, gcc discards them whole, and the emitted object never depended
#   on them. That is also why dropping them is byte-identical BY CONSTRUCTION.
#
#   TWO CONTROLS, both required, because the test could easily delete real code:
#   a fragment whose parent is NOT decompiled is KEPT (dropping it would lose
#   the only copy), and a FUN_ whose address is exactly a function's ENTRY is
#   kept (it is that function under another name, not a fragment). Both checked
#   directly against MdtBcl.o.
#
# PART TWO — 3,498 NARROW CASTS WIDENED, AND THE COMPILER CHOSE EVERY ONE.
# `tools/fix_ptrwidth.py`. The rewrite is only correct where the integer holds
# an ADDRESS; widening an ordinary `int` changes truncation on arm64 and nowhere
# else, and NO GATE HERE EXECUTES ARM64 CODE, so a heuristic that is wrong 1% of
# the time would be undetectable. Textually `(int)x` says nothing about whether
# `x` is a pointer — so the site set is taken from clang's own
# `-Wpointer-to-int-cast`, which fires exactly when a pointer is narrowed and
# names the column of the cast. It is a real compiler's judgement about types.
#
#   WHAT MAKES IT SAFE ON THE TARGETS THAT WORK is that it is not a widening
#   there at all: `intptr_t` is not merely the same SIZE as `int` on i386, it is
#   the same TYPE — `intptr_t *p = &some_int;` compiles clean. So the acceptance
#   test passes by construction, and was run anyway:
#
#     139 of 139 objects recompiled from the rewritten sources are BYTE-IDENTICAL
#
#   `-Wint-to-pointer-cast` is deliberately NOT rewritten. It is the other half
#   of the same round trip and most of it disappears once the inner cast is
#   width-correct: 4,214 -> 322.
#
# WHAT THE REMAINING 363 ARE, so the next attempt does not start by measuring.
# They are ALL the int-to-pointer direction, and they are NOT more casts to
# widen — they are an integer LOCAL OR STRUCT FIELD that holds an address:
#
#     iVar19 = ...;  *(float *)(iVar19 + 0xb0)      an int local used as a base
#     pool_ptr = (MdtKeaInverseMassMatrix *)iVar10;
#     (**(code **)(*(int *)this->A + 0x18))         an int FIELD used as a base
#
# Closing them means widening a DECLARATION, which is type inference and not a
# textual rewrite. And it must not be done blind, because the same bucket
# contains sites where widening would be WRONG — `pMVar3 =
# (McdGeometryID)KD_FBITS((dy * 0.5))` stores a FLOAT in a pointer-typed slot,
# and `local_3c = (MdtBody *)(iVar10 + 1)` is a body INDEX in a pointer-typed
# local. Both are Ghidra type confusion, not pointer punning. 207 distinct
# shapes; they need judgement, one at a time.
#
# STATUS OF THE ARTEFACT, stated plainly: the widened sources are produced by a
# POST-PASS that needs the NDK, so `/tmp/kd_out` from §4's 95-second pipeline is
# NOT the arm64-correct source. An arm64 build must run tools/fix_ptrwidth.py
# first. i386, wasm32 and armv7 are unaffected either way — that is the point of
# the typedef.
#
#   139 objects · 139/139 clean on three scenes · frame bounds 0 (both source
#   trees) · bindings 0 · wasm32 139/139 with identical exported symbol sets
#   armv7 139/139 with 0 truncations · arm64 363 across 42 (was 7,771 across 89)
#   BLAST RADIUS: all 139 .o BYTE-IDENTICAL after BOTH changes
#   difftest is unchanged BY CONSTRUCTION — the objects it runs are the same
#   bytes as the baseline that passed it — and was re-run anyway

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): MdtLOD — the guard cannot be true --------
#
# HANDOVER.md said this needed "a measurement or a repair", and recorded that an
# earlier reachability check had LIED — `ResizeConstraint` is a file-static, so
# a call from its own object needs no relocation, and grepping relocations for
# it found nothing and read like an answer. Disassembling settled that: it IS
# called, from `MdtLODLastPartition`, the one symbol the engine imports.
#
# ALL OF THAT IS TRUE AND IT ANSWERED THE WRONG QUESTION. Nobody had asked
# whether `MdtLODLastPartition` itself can execute. It cannot.
#
#   KDynStep.cpp:309   if(po->info[p].rowCount > params->maxMatrixSize)
#   KDynStep.cpp:310       MdtLODLastPartition(po, params);
#
#   KDynStep.cpp:764   KBuildPartitions(..., KAutoDisableLastPartition,
#                                       &w->partitionParams, ...)
#   KDynStep.cpp:281   MdtPartitionParams* params = (MdtPartitionParams*)pcbdata;
#
# so `params->maxMatrixSize` IS `w->partitionParams.maxMatrixSize`, and
# `MdtWorldCreate` sets it to
#
#   MdtWorld.c:150   (w->partitionParams).maxMatrixSize = 0x7ffffffc;   /* 2,147,483,644 */
#
# NOTHING ANYWHERE LOWERS IT, and every route was checked rather than assumed:
#
#   engine source, any spelling of SetMaxMatrixSize          0 hits
#     CONTROL: the same grep for MdtWorldSetGravity/SetEpsilon   2 hits
#     (a search that cannot find what it is looking for returns 0 too — the
#      2026-08-24 lesson; the control is what makes the 0 mean something)
#   engine object files importing MdtWorldSetMaxMatrixSize   0
#   metoolkit members importing MdtWorldSetMaxMatrixSize     0
#
# `rowCount` counts constraint ROWS in one partition, accumulated one constraint
# at a time by MdtPartInfoAddConstraint. Reaching 2^31-4 needs on the order of
# 350 million constraints in a single partition. **The comparison cannot be
# true, so the call cannot execute.** This is a §3a-family result — code UT2004
# LINKS and cannot reach — not a census zero.
#
# AND THE ONE UNPROVEN THING IN THE OBJECT SITS BEHIND EXACTLY THAT BRANCH:
#   all 17 kd_argslot_ lines are in `ResizeConstraint`, which is static and
#   called from ONE place, `MdtLODLastPartition` (line 513);
#   `ComparePenetration` is exported but the engine imports it 0 times — it is
#   ResizeConstraint's qsort comparator.
#
# MEASURED AS WELL AS ARGUED, because the argument alone is what MeAssetDBXMLIO
# should have taught us to distrust:
#   * stock engine, test-karma-1, 8 bots, 235 s: a breakpoint on
#     MdtLODLastPartition fires ZERO times while the control breakpoint on
#     MdtKeaAddConstraintForces fires more than 100,000 times in the same run.
#     The instrument works; the function does not run.
#   * scene_census: MdtLOD is `*NEVER RAN` — 0 of 7 functions — on BOTH
#     scene_chain and scene_ragdoll, so the substitute gate's verdict for it is
#     about the LINK and nothing else, and gate_sensitivity agrees at
#     0.000e+00 ("not demonstrated"). Neither is evidence FOR the object and
#     neither is claimed as such.
#
# SO THIS IS A REACHABILITY RELEASE, the MeFAsset/MeAssetFactory precedent, and
# it is NOT the IxBoxTriList situation: MdtLODLastPartition's address is never
# taken, it is in no dispatch table, and nothing measures it diverging. What is
# shipped is a symbol the linker needs and a body the engine cannot enter.
#
# LIMIT, STATED: if a future engine build ever calls MdtWorldSetMaxMatrixSize —
# it is a public API — this release is void and ResizeConstraint's frame becomes
# live and unproven. That is one grep, and it is written here so it gets run.
#
# NOT RELEASED, AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY WAS WRONG ABOUT WHY.
#
# It said "all 17 unproven frame slots are in ResizeConstraint". The 17
# `kd_argslot_` references are — but they are not the detector that holds this
# object. `stack_guesses` counts a DIFFERENT shape, `(int)aXStack_NN + var`, and
# MdtLOD has **243 of them, 237 in MdtLODLastPartition itself**:
#
#     MdtLODLastPartition  aiStack_90  176      ResizeConstraint  aiStack_2c  6
#     MdtLODLastPartition  auStack_78   61
#
# THIS IS THE DIFFERENCE FROM MeFAsset AND MeAssetFactory, and it is the whole
# reason those two could be released on reachability and this one cannot:
#
#     MeFAsset        stack_guesses 0   argslot_refs 169   IN THE BUILD
#     MeAssetFactory  stack_guesses 0   argslot_refs 155   IN THE BUILD
#     MdtLOD          stack_guesses 243 argslot_refs  18   HELD
#
# Their frames were MATERIALISED — the reachability argument answered the
# `nrecon` detector, which fires on a materialised frame, and there was nothing
# guessed left. MdtLOD's frame was never materialised at all. `proven.txt` only
# clears `nrecon` (recover.py), and NO object has ever been pushed past
# `nguess` — the detector whose docstring records IxSphereTriList compiling,
# passing the substitute gate, and segfaulting on its first real call.
#
# Forcing it through for ONE symbol is the McdContact mistake in a second
# costume. The reachability argument above is sound and is not wasted: it is
# what will make the release cheap once MdtLODLastPartition's frame is
# materialised the way MdtPartition's was.
#
# THAT WAS THEN DONE, AND IT TOOK 243 GUESSES TO 6. One cause, one line:
#
#     iVar6 = -(iVar2 * 4 + 0x13U & 0xfffffff0);
#
# `(x + 15) & ~15` is round-up-to-16, so a request of `n*K + EXTRA` bytes is
# spelled `n*K + (EXTRA + 15) & ~15`. MdtLODLastPartition asks for `(n+1)*4`, so
# EXTRA is 4 and the addend is 19. Every alloca pattern here hardcoded 0xf, so
# this was not an alloca to any of them — and the whole frame of the one
# function the engine imports went with it. The machine code says it plainly:
#
#     4e: lea 0x13(,%eax,4),%ecx ; 56: and $0xfffffff0,%ecx ; 5c: sub %ecx,%esp
#
# The extra is DERIVED as `C - 15`, not guessed, and `C < 15` is not this idiom.
# ONE site corpus-wide, and the blast radius is zero: all 139 pre-existing .o
# byte-identical.
#
#     MdtLODLastPartition  aiStack_90 176 + auStack_78 61  ->  0
#     ResizeConstraint     aiStack_2c   6                  ->  6
#
# WHAT THE LAST SIX NEED, read from the disassembly so nobody re-derives it:
# `ResizeConstraint` allocates TWICE WITH THE SAME SIZE —
#
#     c8c: lea 0xf(,%ebx,4),%edx ; c95: and $0xfffffff0,%edx
#     c98: sub %edx,%esp      <- block 1, spelled `iVar3 = -uVar7`
#     ca9: sub %edx,%esp      <- block 2, spelled `uVar7 * -2`
#
# so the same size variable names two DIFFERENT blocks by its multiplier. Block
# 1 is already materialised (`kd_alloca_iVar3`); block 2 has no rule. The six
# sites are all `(int)aiStack_2c [+ iVar8 * 4] + uVar7 * -2`. Note the `&stack0x
# ... + uVar7 * -2` sites at the same depth are the OUTGOING ARGUMENT AREA and
# are already handled by materialise_shifted_frame — do not consume those.
#
# **Until block 2 has real storage MdtLOD stays out of the build and in the
# gap**, and the release line goes back only after it does.
#
# AND THE OBVIOUS RULE FOR IT WAS WRITTEN, MEASURED, AND REVERTED — twice-bitten
# territory, so here is exactly how it failed. "Every `(int)BASE [+ i*4] +
# var * -K` with K >= 2 is the K-th block; give it storage" is TRUE of
# ResizeConstraint and takes its six guesses to zero. It also:
#
#   * BROKE MeFAsset, which is validated, in the build and on the .ka path.
#     Its `uVar24 * -2` sites are NOT all block references — `*(int *)(BLOCK +
#     -4) = iVar14` and `*(undefined4 *)(BLOCK) = 0x101bf` are the outgoing
#     ARGUMENT AREA and a return-address artefact, at a base that is not a
#     `stack0x` name, so excluding stack0x bases is not enough of a guard;
#   * and hoisted `alloca((size_t)(group->count) * 4)` to the top of the
#     function, where `group` is not yet in scope. The size expression has to be
#     emitted AT THE DEFINING USE, the way sub_alloca does it inline, not as a
#     declaration initialiser.
#
# So the rule needs a real discriminator between "the K-th block" and "the
# argument area below it", and the multiplier alone is not one. That is the
# third time this rule family has matched a SHAPE without the fact that makes
# the shape mean something (see the MdtPartition and McdContact entries).
# Blast radius is the acceptance test: 139 objects byte-identical, or revert.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): keaMatrix_PcSparse_vanilla ---------------
#   an alloca whose block is never given a name — and a correction to
#   "five of six functions are exact"
#
# THE LAST STILL-SHIPPED libMdtKea MEMBER. With it in the build, every object in
# that library is recovered and the engine runs on all of them.
#
# THE CAUSE, and it is one shape nothing here had a rule for. Every alloca rule
# in ghidra_clean keys on a DEFINING USE — `dest = (T)(&base + negVar)` — which
# is where the block gets a name. `solveUnits` never gives it one:
#
#     iVar3 = -(numClamped * 4 + 0xfU & 0xfffffff0);
#     *(int *)((int)aiStack_4c + iVar3) = iVar7;            /* block[0] */
#     *(int *)((int)aiStack_4c + t * 4 + iVar3) = iVar7;    /* block[t] */
#
# and the machine agrees — ONE `sub %ecx,%esp` at 0x108d, sized
# `lea 0xf(,%edx,4)` off `numClamped`. With no defining use the repair block
# never ran at all and the seven references stayed a large NEGATIVE dynamic
# index into `int aiStack_4c[4]`, sixteen bytes.
#
# FOUR GUARDS, and the third is the McdContact lesson paying for itself:
#   * the var must have no defining use;
#   * every reference is `(int)ANCHOR [+ terms] + negVar` naming ONE anchor;
#   * NO reference carries a negative constant — in McdContact the group runs
#     {-0x10, -8, 0} and the base is the MINIMUM, so the bare form is 0x10 high;
#   * every use of the var accounted for: references + size assignment +
#     DECLARATION. Leaving the declaration out of that count is what made the
#     first version decline on the one object it exists for, silently, while
#     passing all three negative controls — because A RULE THAT NEVER FIRES
#     PASSES EVERY NEGATIVE CONTROL. That is twice in one session.
#
# The alloca is emitted AT THE SIZE ASSIGNMENT, not at the opening brace. A
# neighbouring attempt earlier the same day hoisted one to the top of a function
# where its size expression was not yet in scope; here the expression is on the
# line above by construction.
#
# >>> AND A CORRECTION: "FIVE OF SIX FUNCTIONS ARE EXACT" WAS SCENE-SPECIFIC. <<<
#
# HANDOVER.md said five of six were exact and `factorize` was the sixth. That is
# true ON scene_chain AND ONLY THERE. bisect_object.sh, both controls identical
# on all three scenes:
#
#   function        scene_chain        scene_ragdoll        scene_boxes_on_plane
#   solve           identical          identical            identical
#   allocate        identical          identical            identical
#   multiply        identical          identical            identical
#   solveUnits      identical (0 runs) first@0=2.830e-07    first@65=6.000e-08
#   makeFromJMJT    identical          first@0=4.770e-07    first@99=3.000e-08
#   factorize       first@10=3.800e-09 first@0=1.072e-06    first@66=3.000e-08
#
# So THREE functions are imperfect, not one, and `solveUnits` reads identical on
# scene_chain because the census says it runs ZERO times there. Every divergence
# is ULP-scale at the FIRST differing step — 3e-08 to 1.1e-06 — which is the
# same class as the five objects already in the build and released with it
# (GeomUtils, IxSphylPrimitives, McdSphyl, IxBoxBox, IxBoxPlane). The max deltas
# are metres because these scenes amplify an ULP into metres; read the first
# step, not the maximum.
#
# IT IS IN THE BUILD AND IT IS NOT EXACT, and both halves are deliberate. The
# goal is a drop-in replacement with no shipped members; holding this one keeps
# a shipped member forever to avoid a rounding difference the project already
# accepts in five other live objects. `factorize` remaining exact is still worth
# doing — HANDOVER.md MOVE 1 — and the arithmetic is in `vc_strip_Cholesky` /
# `vc_dstrip_Cholesky`, which the census confirms are separate functions in the
# recovery (6,305 and 5,406 calls on scene_chain), not inlined as MOVE 1 assumed.
#
#   140 objects · no crash on three scenes · frame bounds 0 · bindings 0
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline · wasm32 140/140
#   armv7 140/140 with 0 truncations · arm64 6,977 raw across 90
#   BLAST RADIUS: all 139 pre-existing .o BYTE-IDENTICAL
#   census: 10 of 10 functions run on scene_ragdoll, 8 of 10 on scene_chain
#   gate_sensitivity: PROVEN SENSITIVE (rounded 4.787e+00) — the scene can see it
#   engine, build-subst140, test-karma-1, 260 s, -SOFTWARERENDERER:
#     exit 124, START MATCH, 0 SIGSEGV, 0 Critical Error, 0 NaN; stock control
#     on the same map reads the same on every one
#     AND IT EXECUTES: a breakpoint on the recovered factorize is hit more than
#     500 times, from keaLCPSolver::PrincipalSubmatrix <- solveLCP
#     substitution verified in the BINARY: factorize 87 -> 603 instructions
#   drop-in gap 6 -> 5 members, 28 -> 27 symbols
keaMatrix_PcSparse_vanilla  IN THE BUILD 2026-08-26 (seventh session), and NOT exact.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): the association defect, BOUNDED ----------
#
# WHAT REMAINS row 1a has said since the sixth session that this is "a
# corpus-wide lead and nothing here can see it", that "three sites were found
# and fixed in one object" and that "**nobody has looked at the other 152**".
# `tools/assoc_scan.py` is the look. It does not fix anything and it does not
# claim to: it turns an unbounded risk into a finite, ranked list.
#
#   575 candidate sites across 51 objects
#   498 of them in objects that are IN THE BUILD
#    66 are INDEX-PERMUTED dot products, 59 of those in the build
#
# WHAT A CANDIDATE IS. A `+` chain at bracket depth zero with THREE OR MORE
# terms, at least one mentioning a float local declared in the same function.
# Two-term chains are excluded — `a + b` has only one tree. Ghidra prints
# `a + b + c` for both `(a+b)+c` and `a+(b+c)`, so **the association half has no
# textual fingerprint** and no scan can do better than enumerate.
#
# WHY THE PERMUTED COLUMN IS WORTH RANKING BY. The one site in this project with
# GROUND TRUTH has exactly that shape. Epic copied MathEngine's integrator
# verbatim into KDynStep.cpp, so the original is readable:
#
#     Ghidra        myw[2]*axis[2] + myw[0]*axis[0] + myw[1]*axis[1]
#     KDynStep:647  myw[0]*axis[0] + myw[1]*axis[1] + myw[2]*axis[2]
#
# a dot product accumulated in INDEX ORDER that Ghidra printed permuted. The 66
# hits are the same signature — mostly a strict 2,1,0 descent — in
# `IxSphylPrimitives` (13), `ConvexGeomUtils` (6), `MdtBcl` (5),
# `McdPlaneIntersect` (3), `IxConvexTriList` (3) and twenty more.
#
# THE CONTROLS, AND THE DEFECT THEY CAUGHT IN THE TOOL ITSELF. Run against the
# real keaIntegrate_pc.c:
#     the site as it stands now (repaired)          -> ordered
#     the same line reverted to Ghidra's order      -> permuted
# The FIRST version of the scanner reported NEITHER, because it took the right
# hand side as `s.split('=', 1)[1]` and the chunk between semicolons carries the
# control flow that preceded it — `if ((flags & 1U) != 0) { fVar1 = ...` — so it
# split inside the `!=` and the whole statement became one unparsable term. **It
# was silently blind to the one site the tool exists to be able to see**, and
# the corpus number it printed (594/69) was wrong in both directions. Fixed by
# taking the LAST ` = ` at depth zero. Same lesson as the two rules earlier in
# this session, in a third costume: RUN THE POSITIVE CASE FIRST.
#
# WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO, stated plainly:
#   * it does not decide a single site. `permuted` means "this is the shape of
#     the one known defect", not "this is wrong";
#   * ON i386 A REPAIR CANNOT BE FALSIFIED. A float product needs 48 mantissa
#     bits and the x87 register has 64, so these sums are exact whatever the
#     association — 0 differences in 2,000,000 samples. Reversing all 41 flat
#     chains in keaMatrix_PcSparse_vanilla changed the object file and changed
#     nothing measurable. Under storage precision — wasm32, armv7, arm64 — the
#     same probe differs in 31%. So the gate that would settle these is not on
#     this machine;
#   * the count is a LOWER BOUND: a chain over struct fields is not counted,
#     because their types are in the DWARF and this reads only local
#     declarations.
#
# NEXT STEP FOR WHOEVER TAKES IT, in the order that costs least: the 59
# in-build permuted sites are the shortlist; the machine-code oracle
# (`fadd`/`faddp` order in the shipped disassembly) settles them one at a time;
# and executing on wasm settles all of them at once, which is why HANDOVER-WEB's
# item 3 and this are the same problem seen from two ends.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): McdSpace — "LEAVE LAST" WAS ANSWERING ----
#   THE WRONG QUESTION. The struct layout is never needed.
#
# HANDOVER.md has said since the sixth session, in §3c, §13 and WHAT REMAINS:
#
#   "McdSpace 16 — LEAVE LAST. The struct _McdSpace layout is in nobody's
#    DWARF, including its own object, where the DIE is DW_AT_declaration: 1.
#    Inferring it is the guess the detectors exist to stop."
#
# Every word of that is true. It is also irrelevant, and it has been parked at
# the top of the gap — 16 of 27 remaining symbols, 59% — on that basis.
#
# **GHIDRA NEVER ASKS FOR THE LAYOUT.** With no struct to name fields from, it
# emits BYTE OFFSETS, and all 22 of the object's errors were one diagnostic:
#
#     return *(int *)(space + 0x18) - *(int *)(space + 0x1c);
#     error: invalid use of undefined type 'struct _McdSpace'
#
# which is a constraint violation only because `McdSpaceID` is a pointer to an
# incomplete type. THERE IS NOTHING TO INFER: arithmetic on a pointer to an
# incomplete type has no meaning in C at all, so the offsets CANNOT be
# element-scaled and byte arithmetic is the only well-formed reading. The
# machine code is the check, not the reason:
#
#     830 <McdSpaceGetModelCount>:
#       837: mov 0x18(%edx),%eax     <- `space + 0x18` is byte 0x18
#       83a: mov 0x1c(%edx),%ecx
#       83d: sub %ecx,%eax
#
# `fix_incomplete_pointer_arithmetic` writes `(char *)space + 0x18`. It names no
# field and cannot be wrong about one. It resolves the identifier set through
# the public headers' typedef chain — `typedef struct _McdSpace McdSpace;
# typedef McdSpace *McdSpaceID;` — and rewrites ONLY an identifier that is the
# left operand of `+`/`-`. Three controls: a tag that IS defined declines, a tag
# with no typedef declines, another diagnostic declines. It also VERIFIES the
# type is incomplete rather than believing the message, so the first control is
# real rather than a formality.
#
#   McdSpace: FAIL -> review. Blast radius ZERO — all 140 objects byte-identical.
#
# WHAT ACTUALLY BLOCKS IT, now that the wall is gone, is ONE MIS-DECLARED
# PROTOTYPE. The detector holds it on `extraout_AL`, and that value is the
# dropped RETURN of a call:
#
#     prelude:  extern void CxSmallSort__Remove(void *)     /* Remove(int) */
#     recovered: CxSmallSort__Remove(cm->mSpace);
#                if (extraout_AL != '\0') { ... }
#
#     machine:  6b2: mov 0x1c(%ebx),%ecx    ; cm->mSpaceID
#               6b5: push %ecx              ; TWO arguments pushed
#               6b6: push %eax              ; cm->mSpace
#               6b7: call CxSmallSort::Remove
#               6c1: test %al,%al           ; and a RETURN VALUE read
#
# and the DWARF of the defining object says outright what it is:
#     DW_AT_MIPS_linkage_name: _ZN11CxSmallSort6RemoveEi
#     DW_AT_type: <0x1ede>  ->  base_type "bool", byte_size 1
#     formal_parameter <0x2b07> artificial:1   (this)
#     formal_parameter <0x4e>   ->  int
# i.e. `bool CxSmallSort::Remove(CxSmallSort *, int)`.
#
# `kd_protos10.h` carries `void _ZN11CxSmallSort6RemoveEi(void *);` — wrong in
# BOTH the return type and the arity — so the mangled re-declaration inherited a
# BARE-NAME COLLISION: `Link::Remove()` is `void (this)` and the database is
# keyed on `Remove`.
#
# MEASURED, so the size of the defect class is known and not guessed: of 44 C++
# method prototypes the corpus emits, exactly TWO disagree with the arity their
# own demangled name implies, and one of those is a false positive —
# `CxSmallSort::New` is STATIC, so it has no `this` and 4 args is correct. **One
# genuine defect, in one prototype, and it is the one blocking 16 symbols.**
#
# WHAT IT WOULD TAKE, so the next attempt does not re-derive it:
#   1. gen_prelude must prefer the DEFINING OBJECT'S DWARF over the bare-name
#      protos entry when the two disagree — the artificial-parameter flag is
#      what distinguishes a static method from an instance one, so the arity
#      check is decidable;
#   2. the CALL SITE still says `CxSmallSort__Remove(cm->mSpace)` with one
#      argument, because Ghidra emitted it from the same wrong prototype. Fixing
#      the declaration alone leaves a one-argument call to a two-argument
#      function, which is an ABI defect, not an improvement. Either re-dump with
#      a corrected kd_protos (§5, ~75 min, background) or restore the argument
#      and the return value from the pushes, the way §5a does for virtual calls.
#
# **DO NOT ship McdSpace on the declaration fix alone**, and do not read
# "compiles" as progress here: the detector holding it is the only thing between
# a one-argument call and the build.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): McdSpace RELEASED — 16 symbols ----------
#
# The largest single member of the gap, parked since the sixth session on a
# premise that was true and irrelevant (see the previous entry). Two changes,
# and NEITHER of them invents anything about `struct _McdSpace`:
#
#  1. `fix_incomplete_pointer_arithmetic` — byte arithmetic on a pointer to an
#     incomplete type is the only well-formed reading there is. 22 errors gone,
#     FAIL -> review, blast radius zero.
#  2. THE PROTOTYPE, AND THIS IS THE HALF WORTH READING. gen_prelude looked
#     prototypes up by the C++ method's SHORT name, and short names are not
#     unique: `Link::Remove()` is `void (this)` and `CxSmallSort::Remove(int)`
#     is `bool (this, int)`, and both key on `Remove`. McdSpace got the wrong
#     one. `kd_protos*.h` already carries every C++ function a SECOND time under
#     its ELF symbol (§5b) — a mangled name cannot collide, that is what
#     mangling is for — so the lookup now tries the mangled name first and falls
#     back to the short one.
#
# WHY THE DECLARATION FIX ALONE WOULD HAVE BEEN A DEFECT, and this is why the
# previous entry said do not ship it: Ghidra emitted the CALL from the same
# wrong prototype, so it dropped an argument and the result —
#
#     old dump:  _ZN11CxSmallSort6RemoveEi(cm->mSpace);
#                if (extraout_AL != '\0') { ... }
#
# and a one-argument call to a two-argument function is an ABI defect that
# compiles. Correcting the declaration only turns it into "too many arguments",
# which is honest and is not progress.
#
# SO THE PROTOTYPE WAS CORRECTED AT THE SOURCE AND GHIDRA RE-RUN, which is the
# documented way (§5) and cost 50 seconds for one object rather than the 75
# minutes a full re-dump takes:
#
#     kd_protos11.h = kd_protos10.h with ONE line changed
#         void _ZN11CxSmallSort6RemoveEi(void *);
#      -> char _ZN11CxSmallSort6RemoveEi(void *, int);
#     out14 = out13 with McdSpace.o.c and McdSpace.o.locals replaced
#
#     new dump:  cVar1 = _ZN11CxSmallSort6RemoveEi(cm->mSpace,cm->mSpaceID);
#                if (cVar1 != '\0') { ... }
#
# which matches the machine code exactly — `mov 0x1c(%ebx),%ecx; push %ecx;
# push %eax; call; test %al,%al` — and the DWARF of the defining object:
# `DW_AT_type -> base_type "bool"`, two formal parameters, the first artificial.
# **The re-dump changed that ONE function and nothing else**: 16 diff lines, all
# in McdSpaceRemoveModel, and only McdSpace.o imports the symbol at all.
#
# THE CORRECTION IS CHECKED CORPUS-WIDE, not asserted: of 44 C++ method
# prototypes the corpus emits, exactly two disagree with the arity their own
# demangled name implies, and one is a false positive (`CxSmallSort::New` is
# static, so it has no `this` and four arguments is right). One genuine defect.
#
# EVIDENCE. The offsets are read, never inferred — three spot checks against the
# shipped disassembly, chosen from different functions:
#     space + 0x18 / 0x1c   837: mov 0x18(%edx),%eax ; 83a: mov 0x1c(%edx),%ecx
#     s + 200 (0xc8)        2b9: mov %eax,0xc8(%ecx)

#
#   141 objects · **McdSpace trajectory BIT-IDENTICAL on ALL THREE scenes**

#     verdict is about the CODE and not the link

#   drop-in gap 5 -> 4 members, 27 -> 11 symbols

# AND THE ENGINE NEVER CALLS IT — a 3a-family member, stated so nobody reads the

McdSpace              RELEASED 2026-08-26 (seventh session). Sixteen symbols, the largest

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): MeMath — the refusal STANDS, and the -----
#   reason this file gives for it is WRONG
#
# Checked because McdSpace's "LEAVE LAST" turned out to be answering the wrong
# question, and the same discipline says test the stated reason of anything that
# has sat for two sessions. This one is the opposite result: the conclusion is
# right, the evidence for it is not.
#
# §5c says, of `MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocities`:
#
#   "the mapping IS exact — the nine accesses are exactly `MeReal eR[3][3]` at
#    -60 — and `eR` is DECLARED AND NEVER WRITTEN, because Ghidra emitted
#    fcos/fsin and discarded the results. Repairing the name would buy a compile
#    at the price of reading uninitialised memory"
#
# **THE SHIPPED CODE WRITES eR TEN TIMES.** `eR[3][3]` is 36 bytes at -0x3c, and
# the disassembly fills exactly that range:
#
#   fsts  -0x3c(%ebp)   fstps -0x38   fstps -0x34   fstps -0x30   fstps -0x2c
#   fstps -0x28         fstps -0x24   fstps -0x20   fstps -0x1c   fstps -0x18
#
# So "never written" is a fact about GHIDRA'S OUTPUT, not about the program, and
# the file states it as though the original discarded the work. It did not. The
# recovery is INCOMPLETE, not the program impossible — a distinction that
# decides whether anyone ever picks this up.
#
# WHAT IS ACTUALLY MISSING, so it is not re-derived: Ghidra models the x87
# `fcos` and `fsin` INSTRUCTIONS as pseudo-function calls and throws the results
# away, so everything between them and the ten stores is gone. What survives is
# the consumer — a three-iteration loop that reads eR row by row and multiplies:
#
#     fcos(lVar9);  fsin(lVar9);            <- results discarded
#     puVar5 = &stack0xfffffff4;            <- -12; eR row i is at puVar5-0x30
#     ... (*paMVar4)[0] = fVar3 * aTransform[2][0] + fVar2 * aTransform[1][0]
#                       + fVar1 * (*aTransform)[0];   /* aTransformReturn = eR * aTransform */
#
# i.e. the function integrates an angular velocity into a rotation matrix —
# Rodrigues' formula — and the ~60 x87 instructions that build eR from cos and
# sin are the recovery job. It is not a naming fix and `fix_stack_address_name`
# is right to refuse it.
#
# AND THE REFUSAL STANDS ON A REASON THIS FILE CAN DEFEND: **nothing here could
# verify a reconstruction.** The function is exercised by no scene and by no
# engine path measured so far, so a rebuilt rotation matrix would ship with no
# gate able to tell it from a wrong one — the same position as the association
# defect (§11 item 2a), and the same answer.
#
# IT CANNOT SIMPLY BE DROPPED EITHER, which was the other thing worth checking:
# 0 engine objects import it, but TWO IN-BUILD members do —
#     McdModel.o          from McdModelUpdatePath
#     McdInteractions.o   from McdIntersectAt   (twice)
# so the symbol has to exist for the link. The engine needs only three MeMath
# symbols directly — MeMatrix4TMInvert, MeQuaternionFiniteRotation,
# MeQuaternionSlerp — and none of them is this one; the other five in the gap
# are closure.
#
# SO: 8 symbols, REFUSED, and the entry to read before trying again is this one
# rather than §5c's row, which is corrected here.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): MeMath — REFUSED ON A MEASUREMENT, and ---
#   the gate that makes it a measurement now exists
#
# Two corrections in one entry, one of them to something written earlier TODAY.
#
# CORRECTION 1 — already recorded above: §5c's "eR is declared and never
# written" is a fact about Ghidra's output. The shipped code writes it ten times.
#
# CORRECTION 2 — MINE, from earlier this session: I wrote that the refusal
# stands because "nothing here could verify a reconstruction". **That was wrong
# in exactly the way McdSpace's "leave last" was wrong**: the gate did not
# exist, which is not the same as being impossible. `difftest_pair.sh` and
# `ab_integrate.sh` are both this instrument for other objects, and these
# functions are PURE — inputs in, a transform out — so they are EASIER to A/B
# than the collision pairs. `test/standalone/ab_matrix.sh` is that gate.
#
#     control  SELFTEST (shipped vs shipped): 200000 cases, 0 differ
#     measured recovered vs shipped: 200000 cases, 200000 differ (100.000%),
#              worst |delta| 190.63, non-finite 0, first@0
#
# **THE FUNCTION MEASURED IS THE ONE WITH NO DIAGNOSTIC.** Of the two MeMath
# functions whose fcos/fsin results Ghidra discards, only
# `MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocities` produces a compile error (the `stack0x`).
# Its sibling `...AndAcceler` COMPILES CLEANLY, no detector holds it, and it is
# wrong on every single input — it reads `MeReal eR[3][3]` uninitialised. So the
# price §5c predicted for repairing the name is already real and already silent
# in the other half of the object, and nobody had measured it.
#
# AND SEVEN OF THE NINE fcos/fsin USERS ARE FINE, which is the other thing the
# old note got wrong by implication. Ghidra models the x87 instructions as
# functions and CAPTURES the result almost everywhere:
#     MeQuaternionSlerp 3 captured · MeQuaternionFiniteRotation 2 ·
#     MeMatrix3MakeRotationX/Y/Z 2 each · MeMatrix3FromEulerAngles 6 ·
#     MeMatrix4TMFromEulerAnglesAndPosition 6
# `MeMatrix3MakeRotationX` comes out as a textbook [[1,0,0],[0,c,-s],[0,s,c]].
# Only the two `...UpdateFromVelocities*` functions discard, and both are the
# ones that build eR through a loop Ghidra could not follow.
#
# REACHABILITY, measured rather than assumed:
#     ...AndAcceler                imported by NOBODY — 0 metoolkit, 0 engine
#     ...FromVelocities            McdModel.o (McdModelUpdatePath) and
#                                  McdInteractions.o (McdIntersectAt, twice)
#     McdModelUpdatePath           0 engine objects import it; NEVER RAN in
#                                  scene_ragdoll (McdModelUpdatePathCompatible,
#                                  a DIFFERENT function, ran 8,104 times)
#     McdIntersectAt               0 engine imports; never ran
# so the whole chain is dead in practice and alive at LINK time — the MdtLOD
# and 3a pattern again. The engine needs only three MeMath symbols directly:
# MeMatrix4TMInvert, MeQuaternionFiniteRotation, MeQuaternionSlerp, and all
# three are among the seven that are correct.
#
# SO WHY NOT RELEASE IT ON REACHABILITY, the way MeFAsset was? Because this is
# the IxBoxTriList situation and the precedent is settled: an object that is
# merely unreachable is one thing, and one that is MEASURED WRONG is another.
# IxBoxTriList was refused at 139,961 of 200,000; this is 200,000 of 200,000.
# Naming the `stack0x` would buy a compile and ship two functions that are wrong
# on every input, one of them with nothing to hold it back.
#
# **THE JOB IS NOW ONE FUNCTION AND IT HAS AN ORACLE.** Reconstruct
# `MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocities`'s eR — Rodrigues' formula, ~60 x87
# instructions between the `fcos` at 0xcd8 and the ten stores at -0x3c..-0x18,
# with heavy fxch juggling — and `ab_matrix.sh` says bit-exact or not. Do
# `...AndAcceler` the same way by moving the stub; the driver names the function
# under test in one place. **Do not ship either on "it is unreachable" while the
# A/B reads 100%.**

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): MeMath's eR RECONSTRUCTED and PROVEN ------
#   bit-exact for the reachable function. The other one is still open.
#
# With `ab_matrix.sh`'s oracle in hand the reconstruction stopped being a guess.
# `MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocities` is **SOLVED, BIT-EXACT OVER 1,000,000
# RANDOM CASES**, and the formula is below so nobody has to re-derive it.
#
# WHAT THE DISASSEMBLY GIVES, which is what made the hypothesis cheap:
#     bd0: fsqrt                       |w|
#     c80: fld1 ; c88: fdiv %st(3),%st 1/|w|   -> the unit axis
#     lVar9 = lVar8 * lVar9            theta = dt * |w|
#     cd8: fcos ; ce0: fsin            and both results discarded by Ghidra
# i.e. Rodrigues' rotation formula. The only thing not readable at a glance was
# the SIGN convention, and the oracle settled it in one run.
#
#   theta = dt * |w| ;  n = w / |w| ;  c = cos(theta) ;  s = -sin(theta)
#   C = 1 - c
#     eR[0][0] = c + n0*n0*C     eR[0][1] = n0*n1*C + n2*s   eR[0][2] = n0*n2*C - n1*s
#     eR[1][0] = n0*n1*C - n2*s  eR[1][1] = c + n1*n1*C      eR[1][2] = n1*n2*C + n0*s
#     eR[2][0] = n0*n2*C + n1*s  eR[2][1] = n1*n2*C - n0*s   eR[2][2] = c + n2*n2*C
#
#   **NOTE THE `s = -sin`** — the TRANSPOSE of the textbook form. With `+sin`
#   the same code reads 100% different at worst |delta| 1.59; with `-sin` it is
#   bit-identical. That one sign is the entire difference between right and
#   wrong here, and no amount of reading the C would have settled it.
#
#   Intermediates in `long double` (the x87 keeps 80 bits and the stores to
#   eR are `fstps`, so the truncation happens at the store and nowhere else).
#
# THREE CONTROLS, and all three are load-bearing:
#     SELFTEST shipped vs shipped   1,000,000 cases   0 differ
#     candidate  s = -sin           1,000,000 cases   0 differ
#     candidate  s = +sin              20,000 cases   20,000 differ (100%)
# The third is what makes the second mean something: the test CAN fail.
#
# `MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocitiesAndAcceler` IS NOT SOLVED, and the search is
# recorded so it is not repeated. Same shape, same matrix multiply, but
#     lVar10 = |w| + |alpha| * dt      (a MAGNITUDE sum, not a vector sum)
#     theta  = lVar10 * dt
# and the axis is neither of the two obvious readings:
#     axis = w/|w|            50,000 cases, 100.000% differ, worst 0.217
#     axis = (w + alpha*dt)   50,000 cases,  99.998% differ, worst 0.221
# Both are FAR closer than the object as it stands (worst 190.63), so the
# structure is right and the axis or the angle is not. Whoever takes it should
# read the stack from `c80` forward rather than guess a third time.
#
# **NOT INTEGRATED, DELIBERATELY.** Landing only the solved half makes MeMath
# COMPILE, which would put `...AndAcceler` in the build measured 100% wrong.
# It is imported by nobody, so that is the MdtUpdatePartitions situation rather
# than the IxBoxTriList one — but it is a decision to take on purpose with both
# functions understood, not a side effect of fixing the one that had the
# diagnostic. Solve the sibling, then ship both.

# --- 2026-08-26 (seventh session): MeMath RELEASED — 8 symbols, gap 11 -> 3 --
#
# Both discarded-x87 functions are now RECONSTRUCTED AND BIT-EXACT, so the
# reason for holding the object is gone. The earlier entries above stand as the
# record of how it was refused twice on reasons that were wrong; this is what it
# took to stop refusing it.
#
# `...AndAcceler` was the hard one, and it fell to READING THE X87 STACK rather
# than to a sixth hypothesis. Simulating f55..1042 gave three facts that no
# amount of guessing had produced, and each one was worth a factor:
#
#   1. THE AXIS IS NOT NORMALISED. The code divides by `eff = |w| + |alpha|*dt`
#      — a MAGNITUDE sum — which is not |w + alpha*dt|. Every normalisation
#      hypothesis failed for that reason and no other.
#   2. THE DIAGONAL IS `1 - Cc*(vj^2 + vk^2)`, not `c + Cc*vi^2`. Those are the
#      same thing only when |v| == eff, which here it is not. This one took the
#      error from 0.20 to 1.19e-07 — one float ULP.
#   3. THE ROUNDING POINTS ARE IN THE INSTRUCTIONS. `inv = 1/eff` is a
#      RECIPROCAL (fc5), `inv*inv` is truncated to FLOAT (feb `fstps`), and so
#      is `(1-cos)*that` (ff7). And the `fsts` copies of v at -0x58/-0x54/-0x50
#      are DEAD STORES, never read back — so v stays 80-bit. Using the float
#      copies instead is 68% wrong at 1 ULP, which is the control that says so.
#
# THE VERIFIED FORMULAE, so they are never re-derived:
#
#   MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocities
#     theta = dt*|w| ; n = w/|w| ; c = cos ; s = -sin ; C = 1-c
#     eR[i][i] = c + n_i^2*C        eR[i][j] = n_i*n_j*C +/- n_k*s
#
#   MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocitiesAndAcceler
#     eff = |w| + |alpha|*dt ; theta = eff*dt ; inv = 1/eff
#     inv2 = (float)(inv*inv) ; Cc = (float)((1-cos theta)*inv2)
#     S = -(sin theta * inv) ; v = w + alpha*dt        (all 80-bit but Cc/inv2)
#     eR[i][i] = 1 - Cc*(vj^2+vk^2) eR[i][j] = Cc*vi*vj +/- S*vk
#
#   BOTH use -sin, the TRANSPOSE of the textbook Rodrigues form. With +sin each
#   is 100% wrong. That sign is not readable from the decompiled C at all.
#
# CONTROLS, and every one of them fails when it should:
#     shipped vs shipped                      1,000,000   0 differ
#     ...FromVelocities  candidate            1,000,000   0 differ
#     ...FromVelocities  +sin variant            20,000   100%, worst 1.59
#     ...AndAcceler      candidate            1,000,000   0 differ
#     ...AndAcceler      +sin variant            20,000   100%, worst 1.59
#     ...AndAcceler      v truncated to float   100,000   68%,   worst 1.19e-07
#     and re-run against the PIPELINE-PRODUCED MeMath.o: 500,000, 0 differ, both
#
# THIS IS THE ONLY CODE IN THE PROJECT THAT IS WRITTEN RATHER THAN RECOVERED,
# and `X87_RECONSTRUCTIONS` says so at the top of the table. It RAISES rather
# than declining if a re-dump moves the anchors, because a silent no-op here
# leaves the object reading uninitialised memory and looks exactly like success.
#
#   142 objects · MeMath trajectory bit-identical on all three scenes
#   census: 1 of 28 functions runs in scene_ragdoll, so the SCENE verdict is
#     mostly about the link — **the A/B is the evidence, not the scene**
#   frame bounds 0 · bindings 0 · wasm32 142/142 · armv7 0 truncations
#   difftest byte-identical to the documented baseline
#   BLAST RADIUS: all 141 pre-existing .o BYTE-IDENTICAL
#   engine, build-subst142, test-karma-1, 260 s: exit 124, START MATCH,
#     0 SIGSEGV, 0 Critical Error, 0 NaN; stock control identical on each
#     AND IT EXECUTES: the recovered MeQuaternionFromTM is hit from
#     UpdateCOMTransform <- KInitActorDynamics (KarmaSupport.cpp:782)
#     substitution verified in the BINARY: MeMatrix4TMUpdateFromVelocities
#     249 -> 288 instructions, MeQuaternionFiniteRotation 121 -> 156
#   The two RECONSTRUCTED functions are themselves not reached in a match —
#   their callers McdModelUpdatePath and McdIntersectAt never run — so their
#   evidence is the A/B, which is stronger than a match would have been.
#   drop-in gap 4 -> 3 members, 11 -> 3 symbols
MeMath                RELEASED 2026-08-26 (seventh session). Eight symbols. Refused twice
#                     on reasons that were both wrong, then solved by simulating the
#                     x87 stack: the axis is not normalised, the diagonal is
#                     1 - Cc*(vj^2+vk^2), and the rounding points are in the
#                     instructions. Both functions bit-exact vs the shipped ones
#                     over 1,000,000 cases with controls that fail.
